### 3. [HIGH] [baseline:normwear] MODELS-9 marked FIXED but its prose+window-cell edits were never applied — doc still overstates NormWear as rate-adaptive
- **area:** regression
- **issue:** MODELS-9 is logged '✅ FIXED', but its fix had two parts and only one landed. The fix text explicitly said: 'In the prose (52-55), drop/qualify "length/rate-adaptive"' and (adjacent) relabel the window/session cell to 'fixed 6 s (pad)'. Neither prose edit was made. The document is now internally self-contradicting: the matrix sampling-rate cell and the § footnote correctly say NormWear resamples to a fixed 65 Hz (lossy), yet the 'How each handles heterogeneity' prose still asserts NormWear's tokenizer is rate-adaptive, and the Window/session-length matrix cell describes the tokenizer instead of the fixed 6 s window. This re-asserts the exact flexibility overstatement MODELS-9 was opened to remove (which understates HALO's 'only-model-native-on-every-axis' uniqueness claim).
- **evidence:** docs/baselines/baseline_flexibility.md:53 still reads '**NormWear** — **CWT scalogram** tokenization is length/rate-adaptive'. Line 29 Window/session-length column reads 'fixed (65 Hz-tuned CWT)' (describes the CWT, not the 6 s window) rather than 'fixed 6 s'. Contradicted by the SAME file's :29 sampling cell ('fixed 65 Hz (resample, lossy)') and :34 footnote ('resamples every signal to a fixed 65 Hz'). Ground-truthed against released main_model.py:57 (self.sampling_rate=65) and :66-68 (resample only if sampling_rate>256) and paper App. B.1 ('uniform sampling rate and interval length to 65 HZ and 6 seconds'). Resolution table row (adversarial_sweep_findings.md:338) claims MODELS-9 fully FIXED.
- **fix:** Complete MODELS-9: rewrite baseline_flexibility.md:53-55 to drop 'length/rate-adaptive' and state NormWear resamples every signal to a fixed 65 Hz grid and runs CWT on fixed sample-unit scales (~1-64), so rate is handled in preprocessing exactly like the other fix-upstream baselines (ssl-wearables/LiMU); and change the :29 Window/session-length cell to 'fixed 6 s (pad)'. Then the row can honestly stay marked FIXED.
- **files:** /home/alex/code/HALO/code/docs/baselines/baseline_flexibility.md, /home/alex/code/HALO/code/docs/v2/adversarial_sweep_findings.md
- **verify:** CONFIRMED(repro): Both unfixed items are directly observable in /home/alex/code/HALO/code/docs/baselines/baseline_flexibility.md. Line 53 still reads "**CWT scalogram** tokenization is length/rate-adaptive" — exactly the phrase the MODELS-9 fix was supposed to drop. Line 29 Window/session-length cell still reads "fixed (65 Hz-tuned CWT)", describing the CWT tokenizer rather than the actual fixed 6 s window the fix was supposed to label as "fixed 6 s (pad)". The resolution log at adversarial_sweep_findings.md:338 records only "NormWear sampling-rate cell → 'fixed 65 Hz (resample, lossy)'" — confirming that only the sampling-rate cell change landed. The document is internally self-contradictory: line 29 sampling-rate cell and footnote § (line 34) correctly state fixed 65 Hz preprocessing, yet line 53 still asserts rate-adaptive tokenization. The original MODELS-9 fix specification (lines 197-198 of the same file) listed three actions; only action 1 was applied. Ground truth (paper App. B.1 quoted in the finding: "uniform sampling rate and interval length to 65 HZ and 6 seconds") confirms both the fixed 65 Hz and the fixed 6 s window, consistent with the footnote already in the doc. NormWear source code was not available locally, but the contradiction is fully established from the document's own content without needing external code. || CONFIRMED(repro): The prose omission is real and verified. /home/alex/code/HALO/code/docs/baselines/baseline_flexibility.md line 53 still reads "**CWT scalogram** tokenization is length/rate-adaptive" — unchanged from before MODELS-9 was opened. The MODELS-9 fix text explicitly required this prose edit ("In the prose (52-55), drop/qualify 'length/rate-adaptive'"), but the resolution log entry only records the matrix cell change ("NormWear sampling-rate cell → 'fixed 65 Hz (resample, lossy)'"). The result is a genuine internal contradiction: the matrix cell (line 29) and the § footnote (line 34, "resamples every signal to a fixed 65 Hz") now correctly describe fixed-rate preprocessing, while the prose directly below still asserts rate-adaptability. No compensating mechanism was found — the § footnote makes the contradiction sharper, not resolved. The finding's secondary claim about the window/session-length cell is overstated: the original MODELS-9 text labeled that cell change "outside this finding's stated scope," so it was never a required part of the MODELS-9 fix. That nuance does not affect the verdict. The prose omission alone constitutes an incomplete fix on a finding marked fully FIXED, which is a regression. Severity remains HIGH.

### 4. [HIGH] [dataset:harth] Subject S006 recorded at 100 Hz is mislabeled/used as 50 Hz -> its windows are 2x time-warped in eval
- **area:** sampling-rate
- **issue:** The released HARTH archive stores subject S006 at 100 Hz while all 21 other subjects are at 50 Hz. convert.py hard-codes SAMPLE_RATE = 50.0 for every file and has no per-file rate detection: it overwrites timestamps with np.arange(len)/50 and calls create_variable_windows(sample_rate=50). So S006's true 100 Hz samples are stored and later evaluated as if 50 Hz. dataset_config and the processed eval tensor both declare sampling_rate_hz=50 with window_size=300 (6.0 s); an S006 window that really spans 3 s of real time is treated as 6 s, and the eval's 50Hz->20Hz resample under-samples it 2x, compressing all of S006's activity dynamics (e.g. gait cadence) by a factor of two. This corrupts a full test subject.
- **evidence:** Measured over the whole file: data/raw/harth/harth/S006.csv -> 408709 rows / 4172.9 s span = 97.94 Hz, median dt = 0.0100 s, p10=p90=0.0100 s; S008/S021 and the other 20 files -> median dt = 0.0200 s (~48.6 Hz). Paper says released rate is 50 Hz (harth_paper.txt lines 410-411 'sampling rate of 100 Hz (which we later downsampled to 50 Hz)' and 432 'sampling rate was set to 50 Hz'). Code: datascripts/harth/convert.py:93 SAMPLE_RATE=50.0, :181 timestamp=np.arange/SAMPLE_RATE, :270 reset timestamp /SAMPLE_RATE, :285 create_variable_windows(sample_rate=50). Effect measured in stored parquet: harth_S006_walking_0001_0000 has 170 rows, stored_dt=0.0200, dur_s=3.38 (labeled 50Hz) though the underlying samples are 100Hz. S006 = 2548/39199 = 6.5% of harth windows (labels.json). eval metadata: sampling_rate_hz=50, window_size=300, window_duration_sec=6.0.
- **fix:** Detect each subject file's true rate from its raw timestamps (or hard-code the known 100 Hz files) and resample S006 to 50 Hz (e.g. scipy resample_poly by 1/2) during conversion so every converted window is genuinely 50 Hz, OR store a per-session native rate and honor it in the eval resampler instead of the global 50. As-is, add a per-file rate assertion so a non-50Hz file can never be silently ingested at the wrong rate.
- **files:** datascripts/harth/convert.py, data/raw/harth/harth/S006.csv, benchmark_data/dataset_config.json, benchmark_data/processed/tsfm_eval/harth/metadata.json
- **verify:** CONFIRMED(repro): All core claims independently reproduced from primary sources. (1) S006.csv measures at 97.94 Hz (median_dt=0.0100s, p10=p90=0.0100s) vs all other 21 subjects at ~50 Hz (median_dt=0.0200s) — confirmed by parsing raw timestamps directly. (2) convert.py hard-codes SAMPLE_RATE=50.0 at line 93 with no per-file rate detection; lines 181, 270, 285 apply this constant unconditionally to every file including S006. (3) S006 processed parquets confirm the mislabeling: stored_dt=0.0200s for every window, e.g. harth_S006_walking_0001_0000 has 170 rows stored as 3.38s duration though the underlying samples cover only ~1.69s of real time (2x time-warp). (4) S006 accounts for 2548/39199=6.5% of harth windows. (5) Eval metadata (benchmark_data/processed/tsfm_eval/harth/metadata.json) declares sampling_rate_hz=50, window_duration_sec=6.0 for all harth windows; evaluate_tsfm_v2.py line 339 resamples from this declared 50 Hz, propagating the wrong rate into the model. The 2x temporal distortion of S006 is real and unmitigated. || CONFIRMED(repro): Every adversarial refutation attempt fails. Empirically: data/raw/harth/harth/S006.csv has median inter-sample dt=10.00ms (100.0Hz), while all 21 other subjects have dt=20.00ms (50Hz). datascripts/harth/convert.py hardcodes SAMPLE_RATE=50.0 (line 93), overwrites timestamps with np.arange(len)/50 (lines 181, 270), and calls create_variable_windows(sample_rate=50) (line 285) — no per-file rate detection exists anywhere in the file. The downstream benchmark_data/raw/harth/subject_S006.csv (input to preprocess_tsfm_eval.py) carries synthetic 50Hz timestamps (dt=20ms confirmed); its 984,397 rows vs. the expected ~482,559 (if S006 had been correctly downsampled to 50Hz before windowing, using the 2.361× windowing overhead measured from S008) gives a ratio of 2.04×, which is exactly consistent with the 100Hz signal being stored un-downsampled with synthetic 50Hz timestamps. preprocess_tsfm_eval.py reads metadata.json (declares sampling_rate_hz=50), sets window_size=300, and creates non-overlapping 6-second windows with no rate verification. The eval tensor (benchmark_data/processed/tsfm_eval/harth/data_native.npy + metadata.json) confirms window_size=300, n_windows=51443, and S006 contributes 3,281/51,443=6.4% of windows. evaluate_tsfm_v2.py line 339 resamples using sr=50→20Hz (factor 2/5), but the correct factor for S006 is 100→20Hz (factor 1/5); the resampler under-samples S006 by 2×, compressing all of its temporal dynamics (e.g. gait cadence) by a factor of two. The resolution log in docs/v2/adversarial_sweep_findings.md has no entry for this harth S006 finding. No compensating mechanism was found in the eval, export, or preprocessing scripts. Severity HIGH is appropriate: a full test subject (6.4% of harth eval windows) has its frequency content corrupted by a factor of 2, and harth is one of the six benchmark test datasets.

### 5. [HIGH] [dataset:inclusivehar] convert.py create_manifest() still emits false 'pocket or hand-held' placement into the manifest description that feeds the model's channel text (placement fix only partially applied)
- **area:** regression / placement semantics
- **issue:** InclusiveHAR's authoritative on-body placement is an iPhone 14 Pro worn vertically in a WAIST POUCH (Mendeley description in references/datasets/inclusivehar/webpage.html <meta description> and citation.json). The placement discrepancy was already logged and marked verified in docs/v2/CROSSCHECK.md:78-83, and the fix was applied in 3 of 4 places: dataset_config placement='waist' (dataset_config.json:1409), convert.py docstring line 16 ('worn vertically in a WAIST POUCH'), and the committed data/inclusivehar/manifest.json:3 ('the phone worn vertically in a waist pouch'). But the code body that GENERATES the manifest — convert.py create_manifest() line 191 — was missed and still writes '...with the phone carried in a pocket or hand-held.' This both contradicts the file's own docstring (internal contradiction) and would silently overwrite the hand-patched artifact on any re-run of the converter. Critically, eval_common.py:139 reads manifest['description'] and line 165 prepends it verbatim to EVERY per-channel text string fed to HALO's frozen-SentenceBERT text encoder for this held-out TEST set, so regenerating the manifest would tell the model this dataset was collected 'in a pocket or hand-held' — a materially different IMU mount/orientation than a vertical waist pouch. The CROSSCHECK.md:81 safety note ('does not currently reach the model's eval channel text ... which omits placement') is now stale: the corrected description DOES carry placement and eval_common feeds it, so the mislabel is no longer harmless on regeneration.
- **evidence:** Paper truth (webpage.html <meta description>, citation.json notes): 'iPhone 14 Pro smartphone worn vertically in a waist pouch'. datascripts/inclusivehar/convert.py:191 generates description ending '...with the phone carried in a pocket or hand-held.' vs convert.py:16 docstring 'worn vertically in a WAIST POUCH' and committed data/inclusivehar/manifest.json:3 '...worn vertically in a waist pouch'. dataset_config.json:1409 placement='waist'. eval_common.py:139 dataset_desc=manifest.get('description',''); :165 full_desc=f'{dataset_desc} {ch_desc}' -> every channel text string. CROSSCHECK.md:78 '[HIGH] inclusivehar — placement ✅verified' with fix that missed convert.py:191.
- **fix:** Change convert.py create_manifest() line 191 from 'with the phone carried in a pocket or hand-held.' to 'with the phone worn vertically in a waist pouch.' so the code that regenerates the manifest matches the paper, the docstring, dataset_config placement='waist', and the committed manifest artifact. Add a regeneration/consistency check so the converter output cannot drift from the corrected artifact again.
- **files:** /home/alex/code/HALO/code/datascripts/inclusivehar/convert.py, /home/alex/code/HALO/code/data/inclusivehar/manifest.json, /home/alex/code/HALO/code/val_scripts/human_activity_recognition/eval_common.py, /home/alex/code/HALO/code/docs/v2/CROSSCHECK.md
- **verify:** CONFIRMED(repro): All core claims independently verified from primary sources. convert.py create_manifest() at line 191 generates "with the phone carried in a pocket or hand-held." while the paper (waist pouch), docstring line 16 (WAIST POUCH), and committed manifest.json line 3 (waist pouch) all say otherwise. dataset_config.json line 1409 has placement='waist'. eval_common.py line 139 reads manifest['description'] and line 165 prepends it verbatim to every channel text string, confirming placement reaches the model on eval. The CROSSCHECK.md line 81 safety note claiming placement "does not currently reach the model's eval channel text" is empirically stale: the committed manifest already encodes placement in its description field, and eval_common uses it. If the converter is re-run, the hand-patched correct artifact is silently overwritten with the wrong "pocket or hand-held" description, which flows into the frozen SentenceBERT encoder for this held-out TEST set. || CONFIRMED(repro): Every material claim survives adversarial scrutiny. (1) convert.py:191 is empirically confirmed to contain the wrong text "with the phone carried in a pocket or hand-held." — the only occurrence in datascripts/inclusivehar/. (2) The committed data/inclusivehar/manifest.json:3 already has the correct "worn vertically in a waist pouch" text, so the current eval is unaffected; but raw data (InclusiveHAR.csv) is present locally, making a re-run a live, zero-effort risk that would silently overwrite the correct artifact. (3) eval_common.py:139 reads manifest['description'] and :165 prepends it verbatim to every per-channel text string fed to the frozen SentenceBERT encoder — confirmed directly in the code. (4) The CROSSCHECK.md:81 safety note ("does not currently reach the model's eval channel text ... which omits placement") is confirmed stale: the committed manifest description now includes placement and eval_common does forward it, so "pocket or hand-held" from a regenerated manifest would reach the model. (5) No test, CI check, or guard anywhere in tests/ references inclusivehar or validates manifest content against the converter output. (6) The resolution log in adversarial_sweep_findings.md contains no entry for this finding. All attempted refutations failed: not dead code (raw data present), not already fixed (not in resolution log), no compensating guard, safety note genuinely stale, and no documented rationale for the "pocket" deviation given that docstring, manifest, and dataset_config all agree on "waist pouch".

### 6. [HIGH] [dataset:mhealth] mhealth "magnetometer" channels are motion-scaled artifacts, not magnetic field; manifest text is false and they feed the model as real 'magnetic field'
- **area:** channel-semantics
- **issue:** The 6 magnetometer channels (ankle_mag_{x,y,z}, arm_mag_{x,y,z}) do NOT behave like a magnetometer: their magnitude tracks activity intensity instead of being the (motion-invariant) Earth field. At rest |mag| is a plausible Earth-field magnitude (~0.5-0.9 local-flux/Gauss units), but during vigorous motion it explodes ~200x. A genuine magnetometer's ||B|| is rotation/motion-invariant, so this is physically impossible for magnetic-field data — these channels carry a motion/vibration-coupled artifact. Yet the manifest describes them as plain 'Left ankle magnetic field X-axis' / 'Right wrist magnetic field X-axis' with no caveat, they pass the IMU filter (multi_dataset_loader IMU_PATTERNS includes 'mag', line 423) as 6 of mhealth's 21 IMU channels (29%), and are CLIP-aligned to the frozen-SBERT 'magnetic field' text. This teaches the model a false physical relation (large 'magnetic field' magnitude => running) and pollutes the shared cross-dataset 'magnetometer' text token (dsads/pamap2 have real magnetometers). This directly refutes the DATA-13 resolution-log claim 'acc + mag verified clean (1.2% hold)', which only tested sample-and-hold, not physical validity.
- **evidence:** Measured over data/mhealth parquets (CPU): per-activity ankle |mag| mean = lying 0.76, sitting 0.75, standing 0.96, walking 89.8, jogging 130.9, running 188.0, jump 107.7 (arm |mag| identical pattern: standing 1.6 -> running 201). Over 120 random windows corr(ankle |mag| mean, ankle |acc| within-window std [motion proxy]) = 0.87; |mag| mean ranges 0.51 (rest) to 260.85 (vigorous) = 507x swing. Raw rows: lying window subject1_seg002_0000 ankle_mag=(0.38,0.55,-0.59) |mag|=0.92; running window subject1_seg009_0000 ankle_mag=(118.9,-71.8,14.4)... per-axis x in [-352.8,266.5], |mag| mean=198.08. Manifest text: data/mhealth/manifest.json:214-271 (e.g. line 215 'Left ankle magnetic field X-axis'); regenerated by datascripts/mhealth/convert.py:213-271. Fed as IMU via datasets/imu_pretraining_dataset/multi_dataset_loader.py:423. Contradicts docs/v2/adversarial_sweep_findings.md:312 resolution ('mag verified clean').
- **fix:** Do not describe these as bare 'magnetic field'. Either (a) exclude the 6 mhealth mag channels from the IMU set (they are droppable via channel_dropout) since they carry no valid magnetic information during dynamic activity, or (b) flag them in the channel text exactly as the gyro was flagged (e.g. 'low-reliability magnetometer, magnitude tracks motion, not Earth-field') so the CLIP text token is not corrupted. Add a converter sanity check asserting magnetometer |mag| is roughly motion-invariant (static≈dynamic) and fail/flag when it correlates with activity intensity, mirroring the gyro check.
- **files:** data/mhealth/manifest.json, datascripts/mhealth/convert.py, datasets/imu_pretraining_dataset/multi_dataset_loader.py, docs/v2/adversarial_sweep_findings.md
- **verify:** CONFIRMED(repro): All core claims independently reproduced from primary data. Over 2029 windows (all 10 subjects): per-activity ankle |mag| mean = lying 0.75, sitting 0.77, standing 0.96, walking 84.45, jogging 133.97, running 196.90, jump 109.07; overall range 0.493-260.852 = 529x swing. Pearson r between per-window ankle |mag| mean and ankle |acc| std = 0.9155 (even stronger than the 0.87 the finding claimed over 120 windows). Specific windows match exactly: subject1_seg002_0000 (lying) ankle_mag=(0.38,0.55,-0.59) |mag|_mean=0.92; subject1_seg009_0000 (running) ankle_mag=(118.88,-71.83,14.36) |mag|_mean=198.08, x-range [-352.8, 266.5]. These magnitudes are physically impossible for a magnetometer: Earth's field is ~25-65 uT (or ~0.5-1.3 in the dataset's apparent units at rest), yet running produces 196-261 units — a 200x excursion that is motion-coupled, not magnetic. The manifest (/home/alex/code/HALO/code/data/mhealth/manifest.json lines 61-74, 105-119) gives bare "Left/Right ankle/wrist magnetic field X/Y/Z-axis" with no caveat, while the gyro channels were already patched with a low-reliability warning. IMU_PATTERNS at multi_dataset_loader.py:423 includes 'mag', so all 6 channels feed the model as legitimate magnetometer data aligned to the SBERT 'magnetic field' text token. The DATA-13 resolution ("acc + mag verified clean, 0.0%/1.2% hold") only tested sample-and-hold rate, not physical plausibility — it does not address this finding. Severity HIGH is appropriate: 6 of 21 mhealth IMU channels (29%) carry a false physical signal that teaches the model large |B| => running, directly corrupting the shared cross-dataset 'magnetic field' CLIP token used by dsads and pamap2 which contain real magnetometers. || CONFIRMED(repro): All refutation attempts fail. Empirical measurements on the converted parquets reproduce the finding: ankle|mag| mean = lying 0.91, sitting 0.96, standing 0.99, walking 95.77, jogging 153.29, running 183.37 (200x rest-to-running swing); arm|mag| running ~205; corr(ankle|mag|, ankle_acc_std) = 0.906 over 120 windows; range 0.55–252.65 (~450x). A real magnetometer's |B| is rotation/motion-invariant, so a 200–450x swing with activity intensity is physically impossible for Earth-field measurement regardless of sensor units. The on-disk manifest.json (lines 61-73, 106-118) and converter datascripts/mhealth/convert.py:213-271 describe all 6 mag channels as bare "Left ankle magnetic field X-axis" / "Right wrist magnetic field X-axis" with no caveat (gyro channels have a caveat, mag channels do not). The loader sample confirms these 6 mag channels appear in the 21-channel output with text descriptions feeding CLIP alignment, making the text-token corruption active. The DATA-13 resolution log's "acc + mag verified clean (1.2% hold)" tests only temporal sample-and-hold repetition rate, not physical plausibility under motion — a different property. Per-patch z-score normalization (model/preprocessing.py:191-198) partially mitigates signal-amplitude corruption in the encoder input but does not prevent the model from learning that "magnetic field" text corresponds to motion-coupled artifact patterns, and does not protect the shared SBERT embedding space where dsads and pamap2 contribute real magnetometer signals. No channel_filter, exclusion, or special-casing for mhealth mag exists anywhere in the codebase. The module docstring saying "6 channels (acc + gyro)" is a stale comment, not a compensating mechanism.

### 7. [HIGH] [dataset:recgym] DATA-14 marked FIXED but recgym gravity/yaw augmentation still misfires on ~19% of sessions (up to 88% of rope_skipping)
- **area:** regression
- **issue:** The resolution log (docs/v2/adversarial_sweep_findings.md:313) marks DATA-14 as '✅ FIXED ... recgym no longer misfires gravity/yaw aug.' It is NOT fixed. The hardened _gravity_present guard (augmentations.py:836 `return dc > 0.5*rms and axis_conc > 0.6`) was tuned assuming recgym's min-max-normalized acc sits at a symmetric [0.5,0.5,0.5] diagonal giving axis_conc≈0.577<0.6. But for dynamic activities the normalized per-axis distribution is skewed, so one axis's mean exceeds the others and axis_conc crosses 0.6. recgym's operating point (0.578–0.613) straddles the threshold, so a large minority of sessions still trip the guard. When it fires, the default_v2 gravity augmentation high-pass-filters the dimensionless [0,1] signal and rewrites the channel text to '(gravity removed)' — a FALSE physical claim (the removed content is the 0.5 normalization offset, not a gravity vector), poisoning the frozen-SBERT CLIP text-alignment target — and yaw_rotation rotates the co-located acc+gyro triads about the physically-meaningless diagonal axis. This is exactly the pollution DATA-14 claimed to eliminate.
- **evidence:** Called the REAL _gravity_present(acc_triad, acc_descs) with the on-disk manifest descriptions ('Accelerometer X-axis (wrist)', which contain no gravity keyword, so the text branch is silent and the signal heuristic runs) over 800 random sessions: returns True on 155/800 = 19.4%. By activity: rope_skipping 15/17=88.2%, walking 65/131=49.6%, running 44/127=34.6%, squat 19/75=25.3%, arm_curl 11/57=19.3% (bench_press/leg_press/leg_curl/adductor/stairclimber 0%). End-to-end run of IMUAugmenter(default_v2) with gravity+yaw forced (enabled=True at augmentations.py:759-760) on 3 firing sessions rewrote all 3 acc channel descriptions to 'Accelerometer X-axis (wrist) (gravity removed)' and mutated the signal with max|Δ|=0.68–1.22 in normalized units. Root cause: measured acc_mean per session ≈[0.51,0.48,0.48] for firing sessions vs [0.50,0.50,0.50] for non-firing, giving axis_conc 0.578–0.613 straddling the 0.6 guard.
- **fix:** Do what DATA-14's own proposed fix said (but the axis_conc guard failed to achieve): gate the gravity + yaw_rotation augmentations on an ABSOLUTE physical-units gravity magnitude (acc DC ≈ 1 g ≈ 9.8 m/s^2, or ≈1.0 in g-units) rather than the unit-agnostic dc>0.5*rms + axis_conc heuristic, so dimensionless [0,1]-normalized data can never satisfy it; OR carry a per-dataset `normalized`/`gravity_removed` flag in data/recgym/manifest.json or dataset_config and opt recgym out of the physics augmentations entirely. Additionally re-open DATA-14 in the resolution log (it is not FIXED for recgym). File: datasets/imu_pretraining_dataset/augmentations.py:836 (_gravity_present) and the default_v2 gravity/yaw enablement at :759-760.
- **files:** datasets/imu_pretraining_dataset/augmentations.py, docs/v2/adversarial_sweep_findings.md, data/recgym/manifest.json
- **verify:** CONFIRMED(repro): Independently reproduced on all 7150 recgym sessions. Core claims hold:

CODE VERIFIED:
- augmentations.py:836: `return dc > 0.5 * rms and axis_conc > 0.6` — exact threshold as claimed
- augmentations.py:759-760: default_v2 enables gravity.enabled=True and yaw_rotation.enabled=True
- data/recgym/manifest.json: acc channel descriptions are "Accelerometer X-axis (wrist)" etc. — none contain "gravity removed", "includes gravity", "linear acceleration", or any other keyword that would trigger the text-branch early return in _gravity_present. The text branch is always silent for recgym, so the signal heuristic always runs.

EMPIRICAL MEASUREMENT (all 7150 sessions, not a sample):
- Overall firing: 1546/7150 = 21.6% (finding claimed 19.4% on 800-session random sample — consistent)
- rope_skipping: 121/152 = 79.6% (finding claimed 88.2% on 17 sessions — same order of magnitude)
- walking: 647/1245 = 52.0% (finding claimed 49.6%)
- running: 495/1176 = 42.1% (finding claimed 34.6%)
- squat: 207/731 = 28.3% (finding claimed 25.3%)
- arm_curl: 71/461 = 15.4% (finding claimed 19.3%)
- adductor_machine: 0/424 = 0%, bench_press: 0/389 = 0%, leg_curl: 0/394 = 0%, leg_press: 0/471 = 0%, stairclimber: 1/579 = 0.2% (finding claimed 0% for all these — confirmed)

MECHANISM CONFIRMED:
- Non-firing sessions have acc_mean ≈ [0.4998, 0.4989, 0.5006] → axis_conc ≈ 0.578 (below 0.6 threshold)
- Firing sessions have skewed acc_mean e.g. [0.4805, 0.4875, 0.5152] → axis_conc = 0.601 (above threshold)
- dc for ALL sessions is ~0.85–0.87 (= the [0.5,0.5,0.5] normalization offset magnitude, ≈0.5*sqrt(3)), confirming min-max normalization is the root cause — the guard cannot distinguish this from real gravity
- Dynamic activities (rope_skipping, walking, running) produce a dominant axis due to wrist motion, tipping axis_conc above 0.6; static/machine exercises (bench_press, leg_press, adductor) keep axes near-equal at ≈0.578

RESOLUTION LOG STATUS: Line 313 of docs/v2/adversarial_sweep_findings.md marks DATA-14 as "✅ FIXED — recgym no longer misfires gravity/yaw aug." This is demonstrably incorrect. The fix (adding the axis_conc>0.6 guard) does reduce misfires from 100% to ~21.6% overall but does not eliminate them. The fix works only for static exercises; dynamic activities with any wrist-axis dominance still cross the threshold. The item should be re-opened.

SEVERITY: HIGH is appropriate — this is a regression (FIXED item that remains broken), affects 21.6% of all recgym sessions and up to 79.6% of rope_skipping, and the misfire produces false channel-text mutations ("gravity removed") that directly poison the frozen-SBERT CLIP alignment targets used in training. || CONFIRMED(repro): Every refutation angle was exhausted and none succeeded. Empirical reproduction on 800 random recgym sessions (seed 42) shows _gravity_present=True on 188/800 = 23.5% of sessions, closely matching the finding's claimed 19.4%. Per-activity rates also match: rope_skipping 92.9% (claimed 88.2%), walking 58.8% (49.6%), running 45.6% (34.6%), squat 28.2% (25.3%), all machine exercises 0% (exact match). The text branch of _gravity_present is confirmed silent for all recgym channels: the full description the loader constructs (line 496: dataset_desc + ch_desc) is "RecGym: Gym Workouts Recognition Dataset. 10 volunteers... Accelerometer X-axis (wrist)" — no keyword from the false-list or true-list triggers, so the signal heuristic always runs. The recgym manifest (/home/alex/code/HALO/code/data/recgym/manifest.json) still has plain "Accelerometer X-axis (wrist)" descriptions with no gravity annotation; DATA-17's partial annotation fix only reached kuhar (as stated in its resolution row: "remaining datasets deferred"). No per-dataset gravity opt-out, skip_physics flag, or normalized flag exists in dataset_config.json, manifest.json, multi_dataset_loader.py, or anywhere else in the pipeline. The augmentations.py code at line 836 — `return dc > 0.5 * rms and axis_conc > 0.6` — contains no absolute-magnitude guard despite DATA-21's resolution note claiming one was added; that note is misleading (kuhar is rescued by the text branch via its DATA-8 manifest fix, and the axis_conc guard only helps uci_har body_acc with near-zero DC). The mathematical root cause is confirmed: the symmetric [0.5,0.5,0.5] diagonal gives axis_conc = 0.5/0.866 = 0.577, safely below 0.6, but dynamic activities produce slight per-axis skew (e.g. m=[0.546, 0.493, 0.456] for rope_skipping), pushing axis_conc to 0.600-0.650 and crossing the threshold. The claimed fix — axis_conc > 0.6 — was correctly motivated by recgym's diagonal offset not being axis-concentrated, but the 0.6 threshold sits too close to the symmetric value (0.577) to provide adequate margin for dynamic sessions, exactly as the finding states. The DATA-14 resolution note claim "recgym no longer misfires gravity/yaw aug" is demonstrably false.

### 8. [HIGH] [design:data-strategy] Channel text is a per-dataset fingerprint, and 64-token right-truncation deletes the per-channel semantics for 8/11 datasets (all of capture24)
- **area:** channel-text conditioning / shortcut-learning
- **issue:** The full manifest dataset description (dataset name, subject count, device list, activity list) is prepended to EVERY channel description, then TokenTextEncoder tokenizes with max_length=64 and default right-truncation. For 8 of 11 training datasets the concatenated string exceeds 64 tokens, so the discriminative tail (which axis, placement, gravity state, and the '(sampled at NHz, Xs window)' rate suffix the legacy/headline path relies on as its ONLY rate cue) is cut off, while the dataset-identifying prose prefix always survives. capture24 (42% of loaded hours) is the worst case: its 3 accelerometer axes all collapse to the identical truncated dataset-prose string, so text gives zero per-axis, gravity, or rate discrimination for nearly half the corpus. Because each dataset owns a near-unique label set (see the group-vs-dataset finding), this surviving fingerprint lets ChannelTextFusion act as a dataset-ID embedding that shortcuts to the dataset's label prior instead of reading the signal. This behaviour is baked into the reported headline numbers, which use --channel-text native.
- **evidence:** multi_dataset_loader.py:494-500 prepends dataset_desc to every channel; :590-594 appends the rate suffix LAST (first to be truncated); token_text_encoder.py:28 max_length=64; :93-99 tokenizer(truncation=True,max_length); eval_common.py:162 same prepend at eval; tokenizer.truncation_side=='right'. Measured token lengths (desc+channel+rate suffix): capture24 103, hhar 106, kuhar 101, hapt 99, dsads 71, mhealth 69, recgym 66, unimib_shar 66 all >64 (truncated); capture24 desc alone=74 tok so the whole channel-specific text+rate suffix is dropped and all 3 capture24 axes decode to one identical string. Even untruncated, per-channel-discriminative tokens are only 22-36% of the string (mhealth 22%, wisdm 28%, pamap2 36%).
- **fix:** Stop prepending the full dataset_desc into per-channel text (multi_dataset_loader.py:494-500 and eval_common.py:162). Feed channel text that is purely per-channel-semantic and short: sensor family + placement + axis + gravity/units, e.g. 'wrist accelerometer x-axis, m/s^2, gravity removed' — this fits well under 64 tokens so the rate suffix and gravity/axis cues actually reach the encoder. Raise max_length or left-pad the rate suffix so it is never truncated. To test the shortcut causally: (a) report the existing --channel-text neutral parity row next to native for every dataset — a large native>>neutral gap concentrated on training-similar datasets is the fingerprint signature; (b) swap a held-out sample's dataset description for a DIFFERENT dataset's description and measure whether the predicted-label distribution shifts toward the swapped dataset's vocabulary; (c) linear-probe the pooled channel embedding to predict dataset identity (expected ~100% today). Expected benefit: removes a train-only shortcut that cannot transfer, and restores the rate/gravity/axis conditioning the architecture was designed around.
- **files:** /home/alex/code/HALO/code/datasets/imu_pretraining_dataset/multi_dataset_loader.py, /home/alex/code/HALO/code/model/token_text_encoder.py, /home/alex/code/HALO/code/val_scripts/human_activity_recognition/eval_common.py
- **verify:** CONFIRMED(repro): Independently reproduced from primary sources. Code: multi_dataset_loader.py:494-500 prepends full dataset_desc to every channel description; :590-594 appends the '(sampled at NHz, Xs window)' rate suffix LAST (legacy/spectral_temporal branch); token_text_encoder.py:28 max_length=64 and :93-99 truncation=True with right truncation_side; eval_common.py:162-171 mirrors the prepend+suffix. Re-ran the actual all-MiniLM-L6-v2 tokenizer on real manifests: desc+channel+suffix token lengths matched the finding exactly — capture24 103, hhar 106, kuhar 101, hapt 99, dsads 71, mhealth 69, recgym 66, unimib_shar 66 (all >64, truncated) vs pamap2 55, uci_har 63, wisdm 61 (not truncated), giving the claimed 8/11 split. capture24 description ALONE = 74 tokens (>64), so at max_length=64 all three accelerometer axes decode to a byte-identical truncated string with zero per-axis/gravity/rate content and the rate suffix fully dropped (verified identical). Headline checkpoint hyperparameters.json confirms feature_extractor_type=spectral_temporal with no dft_size/filterbank, i.e. the legacy path where the (now-truncated) suffix is the only rate cue. This distinct issue is NOT in the resolution log (which only covers the embedded-Hz-literal-vs-suffix contradiction). Severity HIGH upheld: the truncation deletes the rate/gravity/axis conditioning for 8/11 datasets (incl. capture24 = ~42% of hours) and is baked into reported numbers. The one inferential part is the causal 'dataset-ID shortcut inflates headline' claim — its proposed a/b/c tests were not executed here — but the confirmed structural deletion alone warrants HIGH. || CONFIRMED(repro): Core mechanism reproduced exactly and not refuted. Verified with the real all-MiniLM-L6-v2 tokenizer (max_length=64, truncation_side=='right'): dataset_desc is prepended to every channel in BOTH train (multi_dataset_loader.py:494-500) and eval (eval_common.py:162), and the rate suffix is appended LAST so it truncates first. Measured token lengths match the finding (capture24 103, hhar 106, kuhar 101, hapt 99, dsads 71, mhealth 69, recgym 66, unimib_shar 66) and actually understate it: 9/11 train datasets exceed 64 (not 8). capture24, hhar, kuhar, and hapt collapse all 3 accel axes to one IDENTICAL truncated token-id sequence; capture24 desc-alone = 74 tokens, so per-axis/gravity/rate text is entirely deleted for ~42% of loaded hours. The headline path is the legacy spectral path where a code comment (multi_dataset_loader.py:588-594) explicitly calls the suffix 'the ONLY rate signal', yet it is truncated away. No compensating mechanism: filterbank mode intentionally drops the suffix but is not the headline checkpoint; the resolution log (DATA-17/20/22/26) only stripped 'at N Hz' literals and never stopped the dataset_desc prepend or raised max_length, so this is not an already-fixed item. The proposed short-per-channel-text fix is low-cost and restores the rate/gravity/axis conditioning the architecture depends on. Downgraded HIGH->MEDIUM because the specific 'dataset-ID shortcut baked into the reported headline numbers' framing is unproven and internally inconsistent: held-out eval datasets receive their OWN novel prepended descriptions (mobiact/inclusivehar also collapse axes), so a train-only fingerprint cannot transfer to those novel strings and therefore cannot inflate held-out zero-shot numbers by the finding's own 'cannot transfer' logic; the causal shortcut tests (a/b/c) are proposed, not run. The demonstrable defect is the deleted conditioning, not a proven results-inflating shortcut.

### 9. [HIGH] [design:deployment-extensibility] No deployable inference API — inference exists only as forward_from_raw() buried in the 2800-line training module, requiring hand-built channel-description strings, masks, and rate lists
- **area:** deployability/adoption
- **issue:** There is no halo/ package, no HALO.from_pretrained(), no predict(), and no inference-only entrypoint. The sole way to run the model is SemanticAlignmentModel.forward_from_raw (defined inside training_scripts/.../semantic_alignment_train.py), which every eval script calls with five hand-assembled arguments: a (B,T,C) tensor, a per-sample list of per-channel description strings, a boolean channel_mask, a per-sample sampling_rates list, and a per-sample patch_sizes list. A developer wanting to label a phone window must import the training module, reconstruct the exact training-format channel strings (see get_dataset_metadata), build masks, and mean-pool per-patch embeddings, then cosine-match to a separately SBERT-encoded label bank. The design doc §2 specs exactly the missing HALO.from_pretrained().predict() contract and calls it the #1 adoption lever, but none of it is implemented ('All of §2/§3 is greenfield').
- **evidence:** training_scripts/human_activity_recognition/semantic_alignment_train.py:668 (forward_from_raw is the only inference path); val_scripts/human_activity_recognition/eval_common.py:227-247 shows the 5-arg call + manual channel_mask/attention_mask construction; `ls halo/` -> no package; find for api.py/predict*.py/model_card -> none; docs/v2/design_application.md:76-88 specs the unimplemented predict() API and :130 confirms 'grep ... finds nothing usable'.
- **fix:** Ship a small halo/api.py with HALO.from_pretrained(name) and predict(window, rate_hz, labels, placement, channels, timestamps) returning (label, confidence, per_label, embedding, abstained), wrapping the existing forward_from_raw + label-bank cosine + abstain-threshold. This is a thin wrapper over code that already exists; it is the single highest-leverage change for the 'useful/deployable' thesis.
- **files:** training_scripts/human_activity_recognition/semantic_alignment_train.py, val_scripts/human_activity_recognition/eval_common.py, docs/v2/design_application.md
- **verify:** CONFIRMED(repro): Independently reproduced all claims. (1) No halo/ package: ls halo/ fails, no __init__.py/api.py/model card. (2) No HALO.from_pretrained() and no HALO predict(): grep finds none; only def predict* are in baseline evaluators (crosshar/limubert) plus a bare argmax helper predict_from_similarity in eval_v2. (3) forward_from_raw is defined exactly once at semantic_alignment_train.py:668 inside the training module (2623 lines) and is the sole HALO inference path -- all 5 callers (eval_common.py x4, fig_embedding_umap.py) use it. (4) The 5-arg hand-assembled call is confirmed at eval_common.py:239-243 with manual channel_mask (228-230), attention_mask (231), and per-sample channel_descs/sampling_rates/patch_sizes lists (233-235). (5) The training module imports AdamW, LambdaLR, GradScaler, DataLoader, DistributedSampler, so importing the inference path drags in full training infrastructure. (6) load_model is only a checkpoint loader, not a from_pretrained that bundles label banks + abstain threshold. (7) Design doc corroborates: design_application.md:76-88 specs the unimplemented predict() contract and :130 states grep finds nothing usable / all greenfield. File:line citations all match. Severity kept HIGH: not a correctness bug (forward_from_raw works), but for a deployability-thesis paper the lack of any inference API and reliance on importing a 2600-line training module with hand-built masks/strings is a real adoption blocker, ranked #1 lever by the design doc itself. || CONFIRMED(repro): Every factual claim in the finding was empirically verified; no compensating mechanism was found.

Empirical checks:
- `ls halo/` → directory does not exist.
- `grep "def from_pretrained"` across the full repo → zero hits.
- `grep "def predict"` scoped to HALO inference → zero hits (only `conse_predict` in eval_v2.py, a closed-vocab ConSE bridge unrelated to the public API question).
- `forward_from_raw` has exactly one definition: `training_scripts/human_activity_recognition/semantic_alignment_train.py:668`, inside the `SemanticAlignmentModel` class that lives alongside ~2800 lines of training logic. All callers (eval_common.py:239/292/299/399, figures/fig_embedding_umap.py:154) confirm it is the single inference path.
- Resolution log at `docs/v2/adversarial_sweep_findings.md` has no entry for "deploy", "inference API", "forward_from_raw", "halo/api", "HALO.from_pretrained", or "adoption" — this finding was not previously audited.

Best refutation angle tried — `val_scripts/human_activity_recognition/model_loading.py`:
`load_model(checkpoint_path, device)` + `load_label_bank(checkpoint, device, hyperparams_path)` do abstract architecture instantiation from the checkpoint and hyperparameters.json. This is a genuine partial mitigation: a caller no longer needs to instantiate `IMUActivityRecognitionEncoder`, `SemanticAlignmentHead`, etc. by hand. However, it does not refute the finding because: (1) `load_model()` returns a raw `SemanticAlignmentModel` imported directly from the training module; (2) the caller must still call `model.forward_from_raw(data, channel_descs, channel_mask, sampling_rates, patch_sizes)` with all five hand-assembled arguments; (3) `model_loading.py` provides no label-resolution step — no cosine-to-label mapping, no abstain threshold, no `Prediction` return type. It is a checkpoint hydration helper, not a `predict()` API.

Second angle tried — `eval_common.py`'s `extract_tsfm_embeddings()`:
Wraps `forward_from_raw` with batch logic and sensible 6-channel defaults, but still requires the caller to pass pre-assembled `channel_descriptions` strings, and returns raw L2-normalized embeddings `(N, D)`, not classified labels. Label resolution (cosine vs label bank, argmax) lives elsewhere in the eval pipeline.

Internal design documents explicitly corroborate the gap: `docs/v2/design_application.md:130` states "grep for `from_pretrained|def predict|streaming` finds nothing usable… All of §2/§3 is greenfield"; `docs/v2/data_diversity_audit.md:178` states "`halo/` package, `from_pretrained/predict` do not exist (greps empty)." These are independent self-audits reached before this finding was written, providing strong corroboration.

Value-of-fix attack: the proposed `halo/api.py` is a thin wrapper over existing code (`load_model` + `load_label_bank` + `forward_from_raw` + `label_bank.encode(labels)` + cosine argmax + abstain threshold), which is genuinely implementable in ~100–150 lines. Multiple internal priority rankings (design_application.md §2, REDESIGN_PLAN.md, data_diversity_audit.md) independently rank it the #1 adoption lever. The value claim survives scrutiny.

### 10. [HIGH] [design:deployment-extensibility] No model-export path of any kind (ONNX/CoreML/TFLite/ExecuTorch/TorchScript) and the forward path is non-traceable per-sample Python
- **area:** on-device/export
- **issue:** Despite the 'runs on a phone' thesis, there is zero export or quantization code. The only *_export.py in the repo is benchmark_data/scripts/export_raw.py, which exports per-subject data CSVs, not a model. The inference path cannot be traced: _preprocess_raw_batch loops over the batch in Python, calls encoder.preprocess per sample over dicts/lists, trims by attention_mask.sum().item(), and appends to Python lists — none of which survives torch.jit.trace / torch.export. There is no fp16/int8 path and no numeric-parity test. So '26M fp32 -> on device' has no realization.
- **evidence:** find for *.onnx/*.mlpackage/*.pte/*.tflite/torch.jit/torch.onnx/coreml/executorch -> none (outside .venv/baselines); benchmark_data/scripts/export_raw.py:1-12 is a data exporter; training_scripts/.../semantic_alignment_train.py:611-666 _preprocess_raw_batch is per-sample Python over lists; docs/v2/design_application.md:106 confirms 'forward_from_raw ... not exportable'.
- **fix:** Add a pure-tensor forward_export(patches, channel_emb_const, label_matrix_const) with static/dynamic axes, move preprocessing to a batched tensor op (the PHz filterbank matmul enables this), and add scripts/export/ producing ONNX + CoreML + a PyTorch<->exported |Δcos|<1e-3 parity test. Report size/latency for a 1s window on a mid phone.
- **files:** training_scripts/human_activity_recognition/semantic_alignment_train.py, benchmark_data/scripts/export_raw.py
- **verify:** CONFIRMED(repro): Every specific claim in the finding was independently reproduced from primary sources.

1. No model export infrastructure. A grep for torch.onnx, torch.jit.trace/script, coremltools, executorch, tflite, torch.export across all .py files outside .venv and baselines returns zero hits. find for *.onnx, *.mlpackage, *.pte, *.tflite, *.torchscript returns zero files. scripts/export/ does not exist.

2. The sole *_export.py is a data exporter. /home/alex/code/HALO/code/benchmark_data/scripts/export_raw.py writes df.to_csv() per-subject CSVs. Its docstring says "Export TSFM session data to standardised per-subject raw CSVs." There are no torch imports and no model references anywhere in that file.

3. _preprocess_raw_batch is per-sample Python. Verified at lines 611–666 of /home/alex/code/HALO/code/training_scripts/human_activity_recognition/semantic_alignment_train.py: a for i in range(batch_size): loop, attention_mask[i].sum().item() to compute a Python int, self.encoder.preprocess called on a single 2-D tensor per iteration, results appended to Python lists (all_patches, all_channel_descs, all_meta). encoder.preprocess (model/encoder.py:209) accepts shape (num_timesteps, num_channels) — a single sample, not a batch. This control flow (Python loop, .item(), list appends) cannot survive torch.jit.trace or torch.export.

4. Docs confirm non-exportability. /home/alex/code/HALO/code/docs/v2/design_application.md line 106 states verbatim: "forward_from_raw (semantic_alignment_train.py:611) does per-sample CPU Python preprocessing over lists/dicts — not exportable." This is not a stale comment; it accurately describes the code as read.

5. No fp16/int8 inference path or numeric parity test. No quantization code, no forward_export function, no parity test file found anywhere outside .venv. The single fp16 reference in the repo (semantic_alignment_train.py:2440) is a comment about GradScaler during training, not an inference precision path.

Severity HIGH is correct. The paper's on-device thesis has zero engineering realisation: no export path, no quantized checkpoint, no parity validation, and the current preprocessing pipeline is structurally non-traceable. || CONFIRMED(repro): All factual claims verified empirically. (1) Repo-wide find shows zero model-export artifacts (no *.onnx/*.mlpackage/*.pte/*.tflite/*.ptl) outside .venv. (2) Repo-wide grep for torch.jit|torch.onnx|torch.export|coremltools|executorch|quantize_dynamic|ExportedProgram returns nothing outside .venv/auxiliary_repos/baselines — no export or quantization code exists. (3) benchmark_data/scripts/export_raw.py:1-12 is confirmed a per-subject data-CSV exporter, not a model exporter. (4) semantic_alignment_train.py:586-666 _preprocess_raw_batch is a per-sample Python `for i in range(batch_size)` loop using attention_mask[i].sum().item(), per-sample encoder.preprocess() over dicts, and Python-list accumulation — non-traceable by torch.jit.trace/torch.export. (5) Even forward() at line 508 is non-traceable as-is: it iterates Python channel-desc lists, pads with '[PAD]' strings, and calls text_encoder.encode() on raw strings (SBERT). (6) Docs confirm rather than refute the gap: design_application.md:7/:106 state forward_from_raw is 'not traceable'/'not exportable' and :130 states export/predict/stream is greenfield with no prior art. No numeric-parity/fp16/int8 path exists. Not in the resolution log. Every refutation angle (dead code, handled-elsewhere, documented-as-done, measurement artifact) fails; the docs are a plan to fix, not evidence the gap is closed. Severity HIGH upheld: the model's headline thesis is on-phone HAR, yet '26M fp32 -> on device' has zero realization or latency/size evidence; the proposed forward_export + batched tensor preprocessing + ONNX/CoreML + parity test is justified and feasible at this scale, not cargo-culting.

### 11. [HIGH] [design:objective] Headline ships the worst negative-sampling strategy: hard-negative MoCo queue is net-negative in the repo's own ablation (-8pp unseen acc vs no queue), and GradCache large-batch is disabled
- **area:** infonce-negatives
- **issue:** The headline model is trained with USE_MEMORY_BANK=1 (semantic_alignment_train.py:276; hyperparameters.json loss.use_memory_bank=true, size 256) and USE_GRAD_CACHE=0 (line 275, use_grad_cache=false). Two compounding problems: (1) The MoCo queue is pure hard-negative — memory_bank.py stores no labels and semantic_loss.py:171-174 explicitly zeros the soft-target mass for all queue columns, so a queued same-label ('walking' vs 'strolling') patch appears in the i2t denominator with target 0 and is actively pushed apart (false-negative pushing), on top of the queue being stale. The repo can ONLY do this: grep for queue_mode across training_scripts/model/datasets returns nothing, so the semantic-queue variant tested on the fork does not exist in this codebase. (2) Because GradCache is off, the 'effective batch 512' (line 280) is gradient-accumulation ONLY; the InfoNCE loss per step still only back-props through one 32-session micro-batch of fresh negatives (forward_cached / large-batch machinery at semantic_loss.py:488 is unused). Negative count is the dominant driver of CLIP-style contrastive quality, and the shipped config both starves it of gradient-bearing negatives and pollutes it with harmful stale ones.
- **evidence:** WAVE2_RESULTS_SUMMARY.csv, matched 80-epoch runs, val_acc/unseen_acc: P1_none (use_memory_bank=false) = 0.7938/0.6578; P1_semantic = 0.7456/0.6244; P1_hard_neg (current default behavior) = 0.7164/0.5710; P6_headline (hard_neg queue on) s43/s44 = 0.7093/0.5442, 0.7068/0.5334. Removing the queue entirely is +7.7pp val, +8.7pp unseen over hard_neg, and even beats the semantic-queue fix by +3.3pp unseen. Confirmed loss configs in each run's hyperparameters.json (queue_mode none/semantic/hard_neg). Current repo passes imu_queue/text_queue straight into criterion as hard negatives (semantic_alignment_train.py:973-995) with the zero-mass path at semantic_loss.py:171.
- **fix:** Immediately set USE_MEMORY_BANK=0 for the headline (free +~8pp unseen acc, ~0 extra compute) rather than porting the semantic-queue fix, since no-queue empirically beats semantic-queue. To actually grow the negative set, enable the existing GradCache path (forward_cached) so hundreds of fresh, gradient-bearing in-batch negatives replace the detached queue. Re-run the P6 headline with queue off before publishing any number produced with it on.
- **files:** training_scripts/human_activity_recognition/semantic_alignment_train.py, training_scripts/human_activity_recognition/semantic_loss.py, training_scripts/human_activity_recognition/memory_bank.py, training_output/runpod_ablations/WAVE2_RESULTS_SUMMARY.csv
- **verify:** CONFIRMED(repro): All core claims reproduced from primary sources. (1) semantic_alignment_train.py lines 275-276 and the headline checkpoint's hyperparameters.json confirm USE_MEMORY_BANK=true (size 256) and use_grad_cache=false. (2) memory_bank.py stores only embeddings, no labels. semantic_loss.py lines 164-176 confirm queue columns receive torch.zeros soft-target mass — same-label queue entries contribute to the denominator with target 0 (false-negative pushing). (3) grep queue_mode across all code directories returns zero hits; the semantic-queue variant is absent from the current repo. The current training loop unconditionally passes imu_queue/text_queue as hard negatives (lines 973-995). (4) GradCache dispatches to a completely separate function (line 1495); the standard loop never calls forward_cached, so gradient-bearing negatives are capped at the 32-session micro-batch. (5) WAVE2_RESULTS_SUMMARY.csv confirms verbatim: P1_none val=0.7938/unseen=0.6578; P1_hard_neg val=0.7164/unseen=0.5710 (delta: +7.7pp val, +8.7pp unseen); P6_headline seeds 43/44 unseen=0.5442/0.5334. A diff of the two ablation hyperparameters.json files shows the only differences are loss.use_memory_bank and loss.queue_mode, making this a clean controlled comparison. || CONFIRMED(repro): Core finding confirmed by both code and a properly matched ablation. CONFIG: shipped headline hyperparameters.json has loss.use_memory_bank=true, memory_bank_size=256, training.use_grad_cache=false, per_patch_prediction=true (matches train script lines 275-276). MECHANISM: memory_bank.py stores no labels; semantic_loss.py:164-174 with frozen_text_embeddings (used by headline, train line 971/991) computes soft targets in-batch only and pads queue columns with torch.zeros, so with soft_target_weight=1.0 every queue column sits in the log_softmax denominator (line 189) with target 0 -> same-label queue entries are real false negatives pushed apart. queue_mode grep across training_scripts/model/datasets is empty, so the semantic-queue variant genuinely does not exist in this repo. EMPIRICAL: the cleanest evidence is the matched pair P1_none_s42 vs P1_hard_neg_s42 (identical except queue: same seed 42, 80ep, mb_size 512, soft_target_weight 1.0, grad_cache off): unseen 0.6578 vs 0.5710 = +8.7pp for removing the queue; this directly isolates the queue effect. TWO PARTIAL WEAKENINGS (do not refute): (1) The P6_headline rows cited as the shipped model are 80ep/mb_size-512 proxies, whereas the actual shipped checkpoint is 200ep/mb_size-256, so the exact magnitude on the shipped config is not directly measured (smaller queue = less pollution; more epochs may recover some) - but the matched P1 pair isolates the effect independently of this. (2) The GradCache 'starves negatives to one 32-session micro-batch' claim is overstated: per_patch_prediction=true means the loss runs on flattened per-patch embeddings with TARGET_EFFECTIVE_PATCHES=2048, giving hundreds-to-thousands of in-batch negatives per step (already CLIP-scale), so the 'starved of gradient-bearing negatives' argument and the GradCache half of the proposed fix are cargo-culted for this model. Net: the primary claim (headline ships a hard-neg queue its own matched ablation shows is net-negative; USE_MEMORY_BANK=0 is a better free config) stands; the GradCache portion of the fix does not. Severity remains HIGH because a matched, seed-controlled ablation shows the shipped default is strictly worse.

### 12. [HIGH] [design:text-alignment] Headline ships the LearnableLabelBank (use_mean_pooling=False), which the team's own ablation shows is -5.83pp vs frozen mean-pool and structurally cannot generalize open-vocab
- **area:** label-bank
- **issue:** The headline checkpoint recognizes activities by cosine to labels encoded through LearnableLabelBank's learned attention pooling (LabelAttentionPooling), not frozen mean-pool. This is worse by the team's own evidence and is the wrong mechanism for zero-shot: the pooling queries are fit to the 87 TRAIN-label token distributions, then applied to unseen TEST vocab; and (LiT / research_embedding_collapse.md 2.3) a learned pool over frozen tokens cannot manufacture separation absent in the shared frozen tokens, it can only over-specialize to the training label set.
- **evidence:** hyperparameters.json label_bank.use_mean_pooling=False and eval calls label_bank.encode(labels_ld) (evaluate_tsfm_v2.py:118,206) -> pooling(...) (token_text_encoder.py:508-513), i.e. LabelAttentionPooling, NOT _mean_pool. docs/v2/data_diversity_audit.md:217 records frozen mean-pool = +5.83pp on 7/7 datasets (debug-sweep #4); BUILD_PLAN.md:29 same. Checkpoint carries trained pooling weights (label_bank_state_dict: 11 tensors, pooling.queries |mean|=0.12).
- **fix:** Set use_mean_pooling=True / ABLATION_LEARNABLE_LABEL_BANK=0 for the headline (a retrain/flag flip, +5.83pp already measured). If a learned text head is kept at all, make it a small projection on the POOLED frozen vector (ablatable) rather than a token-pool fit to train labels, and validate it never regresses unseen-vocab zero-shot.
- **files:** model/token_text_encoder.py, val_scripts/human_activity_recognition/evaluate_tsfm_v2.py, training_output/semantic_alignment/small_deep_v2_4b3fdd6/hyperparameters.json
- **verify:** CONFIRMED(repro): Core mechanism claim reproduced beyond doubt from primary sources: hyperparameters.json line 76 sets label_bank.use_mean_pooling=false; LearnableLabelBank.__init__ (token_text_encoder.py:472-494) therefore builds LabelAttentionPooling (not None); encode() (508-513) routes to self.pooling(...) = the learned attention pool, NOT _mean_pool; and evaluate_tsfm_v2.py:118,206 recognizes activities via label_bank.encode(labels_ld). The checkpoint carries 11 trained label_bank_state_dict tensors incl. pooling.queries (6x384, mean|.|=0.1224, matching finding's ~0.12), which load_label_bank loads. So the headline definitively recognizes by cosine to learned-attention-pooled label embeddings fit over frozen SBERT tokens. I independently corroborated the over-specialization argument by measuring label-embedding geometry on the actual checkpoint over motionsense unseen vocab: learned-pool label embeddings have mean pairwise off-diagonal cosine 0.856 (labels crammed, poor separation) vs 0.393 for frozen mean-pool (well separated); the two spaces are near-orthogonal (mean self-cosine -0.03). This confirms the learned pool cannot manufacture separation and instead collapses inter-label distance on unseen vocab, the exact structural defect claimed. The one piece I could not independently re-derive is the exact -5.83pp delta: that is a team retrain result (debug-sweep #4, recorded at data_diversity_audit.md:217 and BUILD_PLAN.md:29) and reproducing it needs a full flag-flipped retrain (infeasible on CPU); a naive eval-time swap is invalid because the IMU encoder is co-trained to the learned-pool space (swap gives 3.57% vs 49.51%, as expected, not a fair comparison). Severity remains HIGH: the headline ships a recognition head that is documented net-negative by the team's own ablation and demonstrably degrades label separation on unseen vocabulary, directly undermining the paper's central open-vocab zero-shot claim. Fix is a low-cost retrain/flag flip (use_mean_pooling=True). || CONFIRMED(repro): Every factual claim survives empirical verification. hyperparameters.json carries use_mean_pooling=false; load_label_bank reads this flag directly with no override; encode() routes to LabelAttentionPooling when use_mean_pooling is false; the checkpoint label_bank_state_dict holds 11 non-trivially trained tensors (pooling.queries |mean|=0.12, std=0.13) confirming the trained attention pool is loaded and used at eval. The +5.83pp advantage for frozen mean-pool is recorded as empirically VERIFIED in halo-debug-sweep-findings memory #4 from test_output/ablation_evaluation/, on 7/7 eval datasets, on this specific checkpoint. The strongest refutation angle — that the figure was an eval-time flag flip on a checkpoint trained WITH the learnable pool, hence not a fair retrain comparison — in fact inverts: mean-pool beats LabelAttentionPooling even when the IMU encoder was trained to be optimal for the learnable target space, making the true retrain gain a lower-bounded ≥5.83pp rather than an overestimate. The "cargo-culting from large scale" attack fails because the ablation was run directly on the 26M small_deep_v2 checkpoint. No compensating mechanism exists in the eval pipeline. The resolution log contains no FIXED entry for this issue; BUILD_PLAN.md line 29 and data_diversity_audit.md line 217 both classify it as an open [MUST]-fix requiring the M4 retrain.

### 13. [HIGH] [design:tokenizer] DC removal destroys the gravity vector -> tokenizer is architecturally blind to static posture (stand/sit/lie)
- **area:** tokenizer/gravity-DC
- **issue:** Both the shipped filterbank and the legacy headline path remove per-channel DC before any feature is formed, and the preserved amplitude scalar is AC-energy only. Static postures differ almost entirely in the gravity-direction DC vector (which axis reads ~1 g), so after per-channel mean subtraction standing/sitting/lying are indistinguishable. This is corpus-wide (uci_har total_acc, dsads, pamap2, mhealth, hhar all carry gravity), not the kuhar-text issue already logged as DATA-8, and stand/sit/lie are core classes in essentially every held-out test set (realworld, harth, mobiact, inclusivehar).
- **evidence:** Filterbank DC removal: model/feature_extractor.py:606-607 (mean subtracted over valid samples), amp is AC-only at :725. Legacy z-score DC removal: model/preprocessing.py:189-198. Measured on CPU: three static postures with gravity on x/y/z axes produce per-channel tokens with pairwise cosine ~0.99995-1.0 (posture-blind).
- **fix:** Append a per-channel signed DC feature (per-channel mean over valid samples, in native units, standardized) to the token input so the cross-channel transformer can reconstruct the gravity direction from the 3 accel channels; this preserves channel-independence at the tokenizer. Optionally also gate on/keep a normalized static-tilt cue. Expected benefit: restores the dominant discriminative feature for the static-posture classes that the current design cannot represent.
- **files:** model/feature_extractor.py, model/preprocessing.py
- **verify:** CONFIRMED(repro): All three pieces of evidence independently reproduced and verified.

CODE: feature_extractor.py:606-607 explicitly subtracts the per-patch per-channel mean before FFT, and the amplitude scalar at line 725 is log1p(E.sum(dim=-1)) where E was computed from the DC-removed signal — AC energy only. No DC component enters the token or any of the [e_hat, o_bpck, amp] features concatenated at line 735-742. preprocessing.py:189,198 confirms the legacy z-score path also strips the mean.

SYNTHETIC GRAVITY TEST (actual PhysicalFilterbankTokenizer, S=128, N=64, r=50 Hz, norm="none", n_bands=32): Three patches with gravity vector along x, y, z axes respectively, zero noise. Token cosines: grav_x vs grav_y = 1.000000, grav_x vs grav_z = 1.000000, grav_y vs grav_z = 1.000000. With 0.02g realistic noise: 0.9925–0.9957. The tokenizer is provably blind to which axis carries the 1g DC.

REAL DATA (UCI HAR total_acc channels, same tokenizer): standing vs sitting = 1.000000, standing vs lying = 0.999999, sitting vs lying = 1.000000. This matches the finding's claimed ~0.99995–1.0 exactly. My earlier hand-rolled proxy (0.68–0.93) was flawed because it skipped log1p compression, per-band standardization, observability masking, and the linear projection — the actual pipeline amplifies the near-identity behaviour.

DC IS the discriminative feature for static posture: UCI HAR total_acc DC is [1.02, −0.12, 0.10] for standing vs [0.19, 0.79, 0.57] for lying (~0.9g difference per channel). DSADS accel DC is [7.97, 1.12, 5.61] for sitting vs [−4.81, −0.49, 8.61] for lying (~13 m/s² on x-axis alone). After DC removal, all these differences vanish and the tokenizer maps all three postures to essentially the same token vector. This is corpus-wide: dsads, pamap2, mhealth, hhar, uci_har total_acc all carry gravity in the DC, and stand/sit/lie are core classes in realworld, harth, mobiact, inclusivehar.

Severity HIGH is correct: static posture classes are literally unrepresentable by the current tokenizer architecture regardless of training data quantity. || CONFIRMED(repro): All technical claims verified. Headline checkpoint (hyperparameters.json) uses spectral_temporal+zscore; preprocessing.py:189-198 subtracts per-patch/per-channel mean, and SpectralTemporalExtractor then rfft().abs() the already-zero-mean signal (DC bin ~0) plus a CNN on zero-mean input, so gravity direction is gone. Filterbank path feature_extractor.py:606-607 removes per-sample mean and amp at :725 is log1p(E.sum) over f_min=0.3..15Hz bands = AC-only, DC excluded (design_tokenizer.md:26 explicitly: 'DC removal drops the raw-offset bin'). No compensating mechanism: normalize_patches means/stds are returned as metadata but never fed to the model (forward metadata carries only sampling_rate_hz/patch_len_samples/channel_descriptions). Empirically reproduced on CPU: three static postures (gravity on x/y/z) with identical noise give token cosine=1.0000000 for all pairs, matching the finding's ~0.99995-1.0. Refutations fail: not dead code (live headline path), no downstream restoration, and the DC vector never contributes to separability. Downgraded HIGH->MEDIUM because DC removal is a documented, intentional design choice (P1 GravityCfg deliberately trains gravity-DC invariance at p=0.5; f_min=0.3Hz band explicitly targets 'posture/gravity micro-motion'), and with realistic posture-specific AC micro-motion tokens do separate (cosine drops to 0.84), so 'postures indistinguishable' / core-class breakage overstates real impact absent per-class eval evidence. The architectural blindness to the gravity vector is nonetheless real, and the finding surfaces a genuine internal inconsistency: P2 YawRotationCfg's docstring claims it 'preserves which way is down so the posture cue survives', which the downstream z-score nullifies. Proposed fix (append per-channel signed DC scalar) is cheap and physically motivated but must be reconciled with the P1 gravity augmentation, which the finding does not address.

### 14. [HIGH] [pipeline:fairness-harness] mobiact baseline ground truth is decoded with a stale/mismatched label mapping -> ConSE baselines scored against wrong GT on 14.7% of windows while HALO is scored correctly
- **area:** fairness-harness / ground-truth-decode
- **issue:** base.load_gt (base.py:84-88) reads benchmark_data/processed/limubert/mobiact/label_20_120.npy (the integer activity codes that the ConSE baselines are scored against) and decodes it with the CURRENT eval_v2 idx_to_label, but that .npy was generated Feb-14 under an OLD mobiact label vocabulary and its codes actually follow a different integer->string mapping. The two mappings disagree on the fall_* classes, so the baseline ground-truth strings are silently permuted. There is no consistency guard: base.load_gt never checks the codes' own mapping.json against the eval_v2 idx_to_label it decodes with. HALO's mobiact eval was re-run against the corrected vocabulary (test_output/eval_v2/tsfm_v2_mobiact_fkl_fixed.json contains fall_forward_knees and NOT fall_backward_knees), so HALO gets correct GT and the baselines get corrupted GT for the SAME windows -> the mobiact head-to-head (one of the 6 headline test sets) is invalid for both CrossHAR and LiMU-BERT. run_baselines_v2 does no regeneration; it just np.loads the stale arrays (crosshar.py:27, limubert.py:30).
- **evidence:** mapping.json (mtime 2026-02-14) that generated the codes: fall_backward_knees=2, fall_backward_sitting=3, fall_forward=4. eval_v2/labels/mobiact.json idx_to_label (mtime 2026-07-03) used to decode: fall_backward_sitting=2, fall_forward=3, fall_forward_knees=4. raw/dataset_config/tsfm_eval/data-labels.json ALL agree with eval_v2 (fall_forward_knees, no fall_backward_knees) -> only the limubert arrays are stale. End-to-end via eval_v2.window_ground_truth: for the SAME window index (keep_idx identical between baseline and HALO; acc signals correlate 1.000 window-for-window, |acc| 9.68 vs 9.70 etc.), baseline GT string != HALO GT string for 640/4345 = 14.7% of windows: 217 windows HALO-correct 'fall_forward_knees' are decoded as 'fall_backward_sitting', 211 'fall_backward_sitting'->'fall_forward', 211 'fall_forward'->'fall_forward_knees' (+1 car_step boundary). The other 5 test sets have limubert mapping.json == eval_v2 idx_to_label (no drift). This is NOT in the resolution log (MODELS-2/-3 note baseline staleness generically but never this label-decode corruption).
- **fix:** Regenerate benchmark_data/processed/limubert/mobiact/{data,label}_20_120.npy + mapping.json (re-run benchmark_data/scripts/export_raw.py + preprocess_limubert.py --datasets mobiact from the current raw) so the codes match the current vocabulary, then re-run the CrossHAR/LiMU-BERT mobiact rows. Independently, harden the harness: in base.load_gt assert that benchmark_data/processed/limubert/{ds}/mapping.json['activity_to_idx'] (the encode mapping that produced the codes) is identical to cfg['idx_to_label'] (the decode mapping) before scoring, so any future vocabulary drift crashes loudly instead of silently mislabeling GT.
- **files:** val_scripts/human_activity_recognition/baselines/base.py, benchmark_data/processed/limubert/mobiact/label_20_120.npy, benchmark_data/processed/limubert/mobiact/mapping.json, benchmark_data/eval_v2/labels/mobiact.json
- **verify:** CONFIRMED(repro): Independently reproduced all core claims from primary sources. (1) Mapping discrepancy confirmed by direct file comparison: benchmark_data/processed/limubert/mobiact/mapping.json (mtime 2026-02-14) encodes fall_backward_knees=2, fall_backward_sitting=3, fall_forward=4; benchmark_data/eval_v2/labels/mobiact.json idx_to_label (mtime 2026-07-03) decodes 2=fall_backward_sitting, 3=fall_forward, 4=fall_forward_knees. Indices 2/3/4 are a circular permutation; all other 10 indices agree. (2) base.py:84-88 load_gt reads label_20_120.npy and decodes it via eval_v2.load_label_config(ds) which returns the current (mismatched) idx_to_label — no consistency guard against the encode mapping. (3) Empirical per-window count on the actual (4345, 120, 2) array: per-window majority vote yields 639/4345 = 14.7% of windows with a majority code in {2, 3, 4} (code=2: 217 windows, code=3: 211, code=4: 211). This matches the finding's 640/4345 = 14.7% to within the +1 car_step boundary window it mentions. All 4345 windows pass the keep_idx filter, so all are scored against corrupted GT. (4) The corruption is exclusive to mobiact: motionsense, realworld, shoaib, harth, inclusivehar all have encode==decode mappings. (5) Both adapters are affected: crosshar.py:27 and limubert.py:30-31 both call into base.load_gt which applies the wrong decode. (6) The finding is absent from the resolution log; MODELS-2/MODELS-3 address baseline corpus staleness but not this label-decode permutation. || CONFIRMED(repro): Empirically confirmed. base.load_gt (base.py:84-88) decodes benchmark_data/processed/limubert/mobiact/label_20_120.npy integer codes using eval_v2/labels/mobiact.json idx_to_label via a plain dict lookup in eval_v2.window_ground_truth, with NO check that the codes' own mapping.json matches the decode dict. The .npy + mapping.json were generated Feb-14 (mtime 2026-02-14) by preprocess_limubert.py from an OLD raw vocabulary where sorted(activities) gives fall_backward_knees=2, fall_backward_sitting=3, fall_forward=4. The eval_v2 idx_to_label is copied by generate_eval_v2_labels.py (line 80-82) from tsfm_eval/mobiact/metadata.json (regenerated Jul-3, mtime 2026-07-03) where fall_backward_sitting=2, fall_forward=3, fall_forward_knees=4 (no fall_backward_knees at all). The two pipelines are never cross-checked. Loading the actual .npy shows its codes follow the OLD mapping: code2=217, code3=211, code4=211 windows out of 4345 total. Decoding those with the new dict yields a 3-way permutation of the fall classes (fall_backward_knees->fall_backward_sitting, fall_backward_sitting->fall_forward, fall_forward->fall_forward_knees) for 639/4345 = 14.7% of windows. Refutation attempts all fail: no remapping/normalization exists in the path; the keep-filter (c in idx_to_label) does not drop codes 2/3/4 since they remain valid keys, so windows are silently mislabeled rather than skipped; mobiact is a live EVALUATED_DATASET, not dead code; nothing in the resolution log (docs/v2/adversarial_sweep_findings.md) addresses label-decode corruption (MODELS-2/-3 are generic baseline-staleness deferrals). Since HALO's mobiact eval uses the corrected vocabulary while the ConSE baselines are scored against these corrupted GT strings for the same windows, the mobiact head-to-head (1 of 6 headline test sets) is invalid for CrossHAR and LiMU-BERT. HIGH severity is appropriate.

- **area:** references / reproducibility

Dropout: Not specified' (inference-only notebook). citation.json has no 'notes' field recording the recipe. Distinct from deferred MODELS-15 (which pins the recipe but assumes a source exists).

(3) citation.json notes field exists but records only the model's eval role ("Canonical CNN+LSTM HAR architecture... from-scratch supervised lower bound"); it does not record the training recipe (optimizer, LR, dropout). The spirit of "no recipe in notes" is confirmed even though the field itself exists. (4) No deepconvlstm.py adapter exists under val_scripts/human_activity_recognition/baselines/ (only base.py, __init__.py, crosshar.py, limubert.py). (5) auxiliary_repos/papers_txt/ contains 4 files (2402.03885v3, 3770652, 3485730.3485937, 3659597); none is the Ordonez & Roggen 2016 Sensors paper. (6) The inference-only notebook claim could not be independently re-fetched from this environment, but the local evidence (no training code, no adapter, citation.json pdf_url points to an open-access MDPI page) is consistent with it. (7) The finding is not already covered in the adversarial_sweep_findings.md resolution log — MODELS-15/16 are about recipe pinning and input format at adapter-build time; this finding targets the absent paper.pdf and missing recipe in the references folder, which is a distinct gap not listed in any resolution row.
