### 17. [MEDIUM] [baseline:normwear] Build plan invents a HAR query template, but NormWear already ships an 'activity' sentence template — the plan should reuse it
- **area:** paper-fidelity / undocumented-deviation
- **issue:** MODELS-11's documented build plan proposes to INVENT a HAR prompt ('add a HAR entry to NormWear's sentence_template: query "What activity is the subject doing?", answer template "This subject is currently {}"'). This is factually unnecessary and undocumented as a deviation: NormWear's released zero_shot/sentence_template.py already contains an 'activity' entry (under the PRETRAIN section, i.e. the exact template family the MSiTF alignment was trained with). A faithful adapter must reuse NormWear's own 'activity' template (pass task='activity' to txt_encode), not invented strings. Invented prompts are off-distribution relative to the released alignment templates AND are precisely the prompt-engineering that the cosine-tier fairness rule (MODELS-7) forbids on shared datasets.
- **evidence:** Released zero_shot/sentence_template.py:220 defines 'activity' with question_template ['What is the current activity?', ...] and 20 answer_template variants ('This subject is presently {}.', 'The individual is involved in {}.', ...). msitf_fusion.py:184/210 (txt_encode(task,label) -> model.txt_encode(questions+answers)) consumes these templates by task key. MODELS-11 fix text (adversarial_sweep_findings.md:208) says to ADD a new HAR entry — unaware one already exists.
- **fix:** In the NormWear adapter, set task='activity' and feed NormWear's shipped 'activity' question/answer templates into txt_encode; do not author new prompt strings. Document that the template is NormWear's own (not curated by us), and record it in the adapter-build note so the cosine/L1-tier fairness clause (MODELS-7) is satisfied.
- **files:** /home/alex/code/HALO/code/docs/v2/adversarial_sweep_findings.md, /home/alex/code/HALO/code/docs/baselines/baseline_flexibility.md
- **verify:** CONFIRMED(repro): Independently verified from primary sources. (1) NormWear's released zero_shot/sentence_template.py on GitHub (Mobile-Sensing-and-UbiComp-Laboratory/NormWear) contains an 'activity' key under PRETRAIN with exactly 5 question_template variants ("What is the current activity?", "Can you identify the current activity?", "What activity is happening right now?", "Please tell me what the current activity is.", "What is the activity being performed currently?") and exactly 20 answer_template variants — including "This subject is presently {}." and "The individual is involved in {}." as cited by the finding. (2) msitf_fusion.py's txt_encode(task, label, ...) indexes sentence_template[key]['question_template'] and sentence_template[task[0]]['answer_template'] by task key, confirming that passing task='activity' would invoke NormWear's own trained templates without any new string authoring. (3) adversarial_sweep_findings.md line 208 (the MODELS-11 fix text) explicitly says "e.g. add a HAR entry to NormWear's sentence_template: query 'What activity is the subject doing?', answer template 'This subject is currently {}'" — this instruction is factually wrong, since the entry already exists under the exact template family the MSiTF alignment was trained with. (4) The resolution log entry for MODELS-11 (line 340) marks it "FIXED (doc)" covering the high-level architecture issues (not plain cosine, L1 scoring, TinyLlama labels) but does NOT correct the specific erroneous "add a HAR entry" directive in the fix text, which remains misleading at line 208. This is a confirmed sub-finding within MODELS-11 that the resolution left unaddressed: an implementer following the fix text would author new off-distribution query strings rather than passing task='activity' to txt_encode, violating NormWear's own pretraining distribution and the MODELS-7 prompt-engineering fairness rule. || CONFIRMED(repro): The finding is factually correct on every point. (1) NormWear's released sentence_template.py has an 'activity' key at line 220, sitting in the PRETRAIN section (lines 2-250, before the DOWNSTREAM section at line 251), with 5 question templates and 20 answer templates — verified from a scratchpad copy that exactly matches the finding's claimed line numbers and content. (2) msitf_fusion.py:184 txt_encode(task, label, model) looks up sentence_template[task[0]] by direct string key in all code paths, including the inference-only query-sentence path at lines 193-196; PRETRAIN is a section comment, not a runtime gate. Calling txt_encode(task=['activity'], ...) would correctly access the existing entry. (3) The MODELS-11 fix text (adversarial_sweep_findings.md line 208) says to "add a HAR entry to NormWear's sentence_template: query 'What activity is the subject doing?', answer template 'This subject is currently {}'", proposing invented strings that differ from NormWear's shipped templates — the author was unaware the 'activity' entry exists. (4) The resolution log marks MODELS-11 FIXED (doc), but that addresses only the adapter-type error; the erroneous "add a HAR entry" text at line 208 was not corrected and remains live guidance. (5) No NormWear adapter file exists (baselines/ has only crosshar.py, limubert.py), so no compensating code overrides this. Best refutation attempted: the "(e.g.)" qualifier might mean the text is merely illustrative. This fails — "add a HAR entry" implies the author believed no such entry existed, and a developer following the text would add a new key with invented strings instead of passing task=['activity'], producing embeddings off-distribution from the MSiTF alignment checkpoint that was trained on the shipped templates.

### 18. [MEDIUM] [baseline:normwear] Plan mis-attributes L1 scoring as 'NormWear's native metric, NOT cosine' — the paper's alignment is cosine-primary
- **area:** eval-clarity / paper-fidelity
- **issue:** MODELS-11 documents NormWear's 'home-turf metric' as L1/Manhattan 'NOT cosine' and instructs the driver to score by L1. Choosing L1 is faithful to the RELEASED INFERENCE code, but the plan's justification mischaracterizes the paper: NormWear's MSiTF alignment objective (paper Eq 3, App B.5) is cosine-similarity-primary — Loss = (1 - cosΘ(Ŷ,Y)) + λ·|Ŷ-Y| — and Fig 1 frames zero-shot as 'matching of sentence similarity' (cosine). The 'Manhattan distance (MD)' the paper actually reports is for the CLAP baseline, not NormWear's MSiTF. Presenting L1 as 'the paper's native metric' invites a reviewer who reads the paper (cosine) to flag the L1 head-to-head as unfaithful.
- **evidence:** Released zero_shot_inference.py:147 'distances = torch.abs(sensor_embeds[:,None,:]-choice_embeds[None,:,:]).sum(dim=-1)' with :156 argmin (L1) — and :148 shows a commented-out dot-product alternative, i.e. the metric is a code choice, not fixed by the model. Paper Eq 3 (refine loss) = '(1 - Ŷ·Y/||Ŷ||||Y||) + λ|Ŷ-Y|' (cosine-primary + L1 penalty). Paper §experiments: 'For CLAP, we experimented with both Manhattan distance (MD) and dot product (DP)' — Manhattan is CLAP's metric, not NormWear's.
- **fix:** Keep L1 (faithful-to-released-inference) but re-document the rationale as a paper-vs-code discrepancy: paper Eq 3/Fig 1 align by cosine (primary term) with an L1 auxiliary penalty; released zero_shot_inference.py evaluates by L1 argmin. Justify L1 as 'matching NormWear's released inference default' and, ideally, report cosine as a sensitivity check. Do not state L1 is 'the paper's metric' or that cosine is wrong.
- **files:** /home/alex/code/HALO/code/docs/v2/adversarial_sweep_findings.md, /home/alex/code/HALO/code/docs/baselines/baseline_flexibility.md
- **verify:** CONFIRMED(repro): Paper Appendix B.5 Eq3 (page 21) reads: Loss_refine = (1/N) Σ[(1 − cosine_sim(Ŷ_i, Y_i)) + λ|Ŷ_i − Y_i|], and the text explicitly labels this "essentially cosine similarity loss, with vector distance as supplemental penalty." Cosine is the primary alignment term; L1 is auxiliary. Paper §4.2 (page 7) mentions Manhattan distance exclusively in the context of the CLAP baseline: "For CLAP, we experimented with both Manhattan distance (MD) and dot product (DP)." NormWear's own MSiTF scoring metric is never identified as Manhattan/L1 in the paper. MODELS-11's fix text instructs "note NormWear's home-turf metric is L1 (not cosine)" — that framing inverts the paper's hierarchy (cosine primary, L1 supplemental) and misattributes Manhattan distance to NormWear rather than to CLAP. The released zero_shot_inference.py does use L1 argmin, confirming a genuine paper-vs-code discrepancy, but the MODELS-11 documentation treats L1 as the paper's native metric rather than disclosing the discrepancy. The proposed fix (keep L1 for code faithfulness, reframe rationale as paper-vs-code gap) is well-supported. Severity MEDIUM is appropriate: no methodology error in the L1 choice itself, but the misattribution creates a concrete reviewer-scrutiny risk. || CONFIRMED(repro): Paper evidence directly corroborates both factual claims. Eq 3 (App B.5) extracted from the PDF reads "essentially cosine similarity loss, with vector distance as supplemental penalty: Lossrefine = (1/N)Σ[(1 − ŶᵢᵀYᵢ/‖Ŷᵢ‖‖Yᵢ‖) + λ|Ŷᵢ−Yᵢ|]" — cosine is the primary term, L1 is auxiliary. The zero-shot §4.2 passage reads "For CLAP, we experimented with both Manhattan distance (MD) and dot product (DP)" and NormWear's own inference is described only as "closest distance" — Manhattan is explicitly CLAP's metric, and NormWear's inference metric is unspecified in the paper. Three refutation angles were attempted: (1) "home-turf metric = code's inference metric, not the paper's preference" fails because the unqualified "NOT cosine" language in the MODELS-11 Fix text (adversarial_sweep_findings.md) does not make the paper-vs-code distinction, so a reader cross-checking Eq 3 would find the framing misleading; (2) "paper's vague 'closest distance' makes L1 paper-faithful" fails because the paper explicitly defined its refine objective as cosine-primary and reserved Manhattan discussion for CLAP, so silence on NormWear's inference metric is not endorsement of L1; (3) "current baseline_flexibility.md doesn't say L1 is the paper's metric" is partially true — the footnote § says "not a plain cosine" (referring to query-conditioned architecture, which is architecturally correct), but the adversarial_sweep_findings.md plan document (one of the two named files) contains "NormWear's home-turf metric is L1 (not cosine)" in the MODELS-11 Fix instructions without the paper-vs-code distinction, and the resolution note says "L1 scoring" was documented. Additionally, citation.json still says "cosine/text-aligned tier" — the MODELS-11 update was only partially applied, leaving an internal inconsistency. The finding's proposed fix (keep L1, document as released-inference-code default rather than paper's stated metric, add paper-vs-code note) is correct and directly supported by the paper text.

### 19. [MEDIUM] [baseline:ssl-wearables] harnet's fixed 10 s (300-ts) input is unreconciled with the 6 s ConSE eval grid; MODELS-13's "(N,3,300) from raw" directive breaks the ConSEAdapter 1:1-alignment contract
- **area:** preprocessing/integration
- **issue:** The ssl-wearables build plan tells the (unbuilt) adapter to derive input from raw and emit channels-first (N, 3, 300) 10-second windows (adversarial_sweep_findings.md:220; baseline_flexibility.md:26 'fixed 10 s (300 ts)'). But the entire ConSE eval harness is locked to a 6-second grid: preprocess_limubert.py:40 fixes WINDOW_SIZE=120 (6 s @ 20 Hz); base.py:57 mandates window_probs return rows 'aligned 1:1 with .../label_20_120.npy'; base.py:87 loads that 6 s label file; run_baselines_v2.py:41-43 derives keep_idx from the 6 s grid and indexes the adapter output as window_probs(...)[keep_idx]. A real 10 s re-windowing yields a DIFFERENT window count than the 6 s grid, so [keep_idx] mis-indexes or IndexErrors. harnet is architecturally rigid on window length (not length-flexible): its Downsample factors multiply to exactly the input length (harnet10: 2*2*5*5*3=300; harnet30: 3*3*5*5*4=900, verified), and Resnet.forward does feats.view(N,-1) into EvaClassifier's Linear(input_size=out_channels), which only works when the feature map collapses to length 1 — i.e. exactly 150/300/900 samples. The OxWearables-faithful reconciliation is downstream_task_evaluation.py's forward_by_batches, which linearly interpolates (resize()) every window to cnn_input_size; feeding a 6 s window resized to 300 preserves 1:1 alignment but temporally STRETCHES 6 s into the 10 s slot (~1.67x), so harnet sees activities at ~0.6x their true cadence — an undocumented off-distribution distortion. Neither branch (real 10 s vs resize-stretch) is documented for ssl-wearables, and none of MODELS-13/14/21/22 addresses it. By contrast UniMTS's identical 10 s requirement HAS its reconciliation documented in the same matrix column (baseline_flexibility.md:28: '@20 Hz: first-10 s truncate + wrap-pad'), making the ssl-wearables omission a concrete doc/build gap.
- **evidence:** Verified: label_20_120.npy is (N,120,6)=6 s@20 Hz for every dataset (measured). preprocess_limubert.py:40 WINDOW_SIZE=120. base.py:57,87; run_baselines_v2.py:41-43 (keep_idx from 6 s grid; window_probs[keep_idx]). accNet.py Resnet.forward: feats.view(x.shape[0],-1) -> EvaClassifier(input_size=out_channels); downsample products harnet10=300, harnet30=900 (computed). downstream_task_evaluation.py forward_by_batches -> resize() = scipy interp1d linear stretch to cnn_input_size. adversarial_sweep_findings.md:220 says output (N,3,300). baseline_flexibility.md:26 vs :28 (UniMTS reconciliation documented, ssl-wearables not).
- **fix:** Before building baselines/ssl_wearables.py, document the window reconciliation explicitly in baseline_flexibility.md/EVALUATION_PROTOCOL_V2.md and reconcile MODELS-13's (N,3,300) directive with the ConSEAdapter contract. Either (a) resize each 6 s eval window to the model input length via the repo's own linear-interp resize() (preserves keep_idx 1:1 alignment) and disclose the temporal-stretch distortion as an inherent handicap, or (b) if feeding native 10 s windows, add per-baseline ground-truth grid support to run_baselines_v2/load_gt (currently hardcoded to label_20_120) and re-derive keep_idx/gt on the 10 s grid. Do NOT ship an adapter that emits a 10 s window count against the 6 s keep_idx.
- **verify:** CONFIRMED(repro): All primary claims independently verified from source files.

1. label_20_120.npy shapes: empirically loaded for all 6 test datasets — all are (N,120,2) = 6s@20Hz (motionsense 12080, realworld 27138, mobiact 4345, shoaib 5537, harth 51443, inclusivehar 3370).

2. preprocess_limubert.py:40: WINDOW_SIZE=120 confirmed; file header explicitly states "(N,120,6) - 6-second windows at 20Hz."

3. base.py:57: ConSEAdapter.window_probs docstring reads "aligned 1:1 with benchmark_data/processed/limubert/{ds}/label_20_120.npy" — confirmed verbatim.

4. base.py:87: load_gt() calls np.load(BENCH_LIMU / ds / "label_20_120.npy") — confirmed.

5. run_baselines_v2.py:41-43: line 41 unpacks keep_idx from B.load_gt(ds) (which reads from the 6s label file); line 43 does probs = adapter.window_probs(ds, state, device)[keep_idx] — confirmed. Any adapter returning N_10s rows (10s windows) would be indexed by a keep_idx derived from N_6s > N_10s, causing an IndexError on ~40% of indices (N_10s/N_6s = 0.6).

6. harnet10 downsample factor product: epoch_len==10 in accNet.py has factors [2,2,5,5,3] — product 300 — confirmed. harnet30 epoch_len==30 has [3,3,5,5,4] — product 900 — confirmed; the code comment at line 685 even states "3*3*5*5*4 = 900 (input size)."

7. Resnet.forward (line 845): self.classifier(feats.view(x.shape[0], -1)) — confirmed. EvaClassifier is constructed with input_size=out_channels (last layer, 1024 for both harnet10 and harnet30), so the feature map must collapse to time-length=1, which only occurs when input length equals exactly the downsample product (300 or 900). Any other input length breaks the view.

8. adversarial_sweep_findings.md line 220 says "output channels-first (N, 3, 300)" — confirmed in the MODELS-13 Fix block.

9. baseline_flexibility.md:26: ssl-wearables row has "fixed 10 s (300 ts)" — confirmed. baseline_flexibility.md:28: UniMTS row has "@20 Hz: first-10 s truncate + wrap-pad" — confirmed. ssl-wearables has no analogous reconciliation note — confirmed.

10. Temporal stretch arithmetic: a 6s window (120 samples @20Hz) resized to 300 samples is interpreted by harnet as 10s@30Hz. Activities that occupy fraction f of the 6s window are perceived by the model as occupying fraction f of a 10s window, so their apparent duration is f*10s = f*6s * (10/6) — a 10/6 ≈ 1.67x stretch; cadence at 6/10 = 0.6x true. This matches the finding's numbers exactly.

11. downstream_task_evaluation.py forward_by_batches + resize() could not be directly confirmed (file not in the local HALO codebase — it is from the OxWearables upstream repo). However, the core finding does not depend on this file: the eval-harness mismatch is fully established from points 1-5 above, and the architectural rigidity from points 6-7.

Severity MEDIUM is correct: the ssl-wearables adapter is not yet built, so this is a pre-build design/doc gap rather than a live eval regression. However, it is a real and concrete gap — any attempted implementation following the current docs will either IndexError at runtime (real 10s windows) or apply an undisclosed 1.67x temporal distortion (resize path), both of which undermine result validity. The comparison with UniMTS (whose identical 10s requirement has documented reconciliation) confirms it is a concrete omission, not theoretical. MEDIUM is appropriate. || CONFIRMED(repro): All core claims verified empirically. (1) label_20_120.npy is (N,120,2) = 6s@20Hz for every dataset, measured directly from benchmark_data/processed/limubert/. (2) base.py:57 and run_baselines_v2.py:41-43 hard-wire the 1:1 alignment contract and keep_idx against the 6s grid. (3) harnet10 empirically crashes on 6s inputs: 120/150/180 samples all raise "Padding value causes wrapping around more than once" from circular Conv1d before reaching the classifier — direct 6s feed is not just distorted, it errors. (4) True 10s re-windowing produces ~0.6×N windows (e.g. motionsense 12080→7248); keep_idx from the 6s grid reaches indices up to 12079, which is out-of-bounds for a 7248-row array → IndexError confirmed. (5) The OxWearables-faithful resize path (resize 120→300 via scipy interp1d linear) works without crash but introduces real temporal distortion: 120 samples = 6s of real activity stretched to fill harnet's 10s slot, so harnet observes activity cadence at 0.6× true rate (1.67× temporal dilation), which is undisclosed in HALO's docs. (6) baseline_flexibility.md:26 ssl-wearables row carries no reconciliation text; UniMTS:28 has an explicit "@20 Hz: first-10 s truncate + wrap-pad" entry — the contrast is real and confirmed. (7) baselines/ssl_wearables.py does not exist; MODELS-13 resolution is DEFERRED for units/gravity only, not for window reconciliation. One minor overstatement: the finding says harnet is rigid to 'exactly 150/300/900 samples'; empirically harnet10 also accepts 360 and 450-sample inputs (the minimum-working length is not exactly 300). This does not weaken the finding because no 6s input (120-180 samples) works at any plausible HAR rate, and the keep_idx/window-count mismatch for true 10s re-windowing is unaffected. Severity MEDIUM is correct: the adapter is not yet built, so no live code is silently wrong, but all three possible implementation paths (direct 6s feed → crash, true 10s re-window → IndexError, resize → undisclosed distortion) are empirically confirmed traps.

### 20. [MEDIUM] [baseline:unimts] UniMTS custom-eval input contract (joint_list + 6-ch/sensor layout) is a required but undocumented adapter input
- **area:** preprocessing / input-contract
- **issue:** The released UniMTS eval path for non-native datasets (evaluate_custom.py + data.load_custom_data) has TWO mandatory inputs that no HALO doc records: (a) --joint_list, an explicit mapping of every sensor to one of 22 SMPL skeleton joints (evaluate_custom.py:103 'required=True'; README 'Prepare Custom Dataset'), and (b) a channel layout of 6 values/sensor in (acc_x,acc_y,acc_z,gyro_x,gyro_y,gyro_z) order, hard-coded by the stride-6 indexing X[:,:,6*i:6*i+3]/[6*i+3:6*i+6] (data.py:268-269). baseline_flexibility.md:28 only says placement is 'native (SMPL joint)' and gives no joint indices; there is NO joint_list/joint-assignment/SMPL-index text anywhere in docs/. The choice is judgment-laden: HALO's cosine eval collapses each test set to a SINGLE core acc+gyro sensor (BENCH_LIMU arrays are (N,120,6) — one sensor), so exactly ONE joint index per dataset must be picked, yet UniMTS's own author mappings cover the multi-sensor originals (MotionSense joints 5&1 data.py:163-164; Shoaib 1,5,21,20,0 :174-178; Harth 9&6 :138-139; realworld 7 joints :195-201; Opp_g :114-119) and so are NOT directly reusable for a single-sensor window; mobiact and inclusivehar have no author mapping at all and must be read off the joint_assignment figure. Also: because the checkpoint is accel-only, feeding a 3-ch array would misalign the stride-6 loader, so the adapter must build a 6-ch array with zero-filled gyro (harmless, sliced by --gyro 0) or rewrite the loader.
- **evidence:** Ground-truth UniMTS source (HF xiyuanz/UniMTS, mirrored in scratchpad): evaluate_custom.py:103 (--joint_list required), data.py:260-283 (load_custom_data: 6*i stride, joint fill into zeros(N,T,22,6)), data.py:138-201 (per-dataset author joint maps for MotionSense/Shoaib/Harth/realworld/Opp_g). Repo: /home/alex/code/HALO/code/docs/baselines/baseline_flexibility.md:28 (only 'native (SMPL joint)'); grep of docs/ finds NO 'joint_list'/'joint index'/'SMPL joint' occurrence beyond that one cell; /home/alex/code/HALO/code/val_scripts/human_activity_recognition/baselines/base.py:87 (GT aligned to BENCH_LIMU/<ds>/label_20_120.npy, single 6-ch sensor).
- **fix:** Before building the adapter, pre-register in EVALUATION_PROTOCOL_V2.md/baseline_flexibility.md a per-test-set table of {core physical placement -> chosen SMPL joint index}. Reuse the author's joint choice for the placement HALO actually feeds (e.g. Harth back->9, realworld waist->3, MotionSense front-pocket->thigh joint used by UniMTS), and derive mobiact/inclusivehar from the joint_assignment figure. Document that the adapter builds a 6-ch (acc, zero-gyro) array per sensor and runs --gyro 0 --stft 0 to match the released accel-only checkpoint.
- **files:** /home/alex/code/HALO/code/docs/baselines/baseline_flexibility.md, /home/alex/code/HALO/code/val_scripts/human_activity_recognition/baselines/base.py
- **verify:** CONFIRMED(repro): All claims independently verified from primary sources. (a) evaluate_custom.py:87 confirms --joint_list is required=True. (b) data.py:268-269 confirms the stride-6 layout: X[:,:,6*i:6*i+3] / X[:,:,6*i+3:6*i+6] — input must be exactly 6 values/sensor in acc+gyro order. (c) Author joint maps for Harth (9,6), MotionSense (5,1), Shoaib (1,5,21,20,0), and realworld (14,16,13,3,1,15,9) confirmed in data.py at the cited lines; none of these apply directly to HALO's single-sensor eval layout. (d) Full grep of docs/ yields exactly one "SMPL joint" hit (the matrix cell in baseline_flexibility.md:28) and zero hits for "joint_list", "joint index", or "joint assignment" — no per-dataset joint index table exists anywhere. (e) No UniMTS adapter exists in val_scripts/human_activity_recognition/baselines/ (only base.py, crosshar.py, limubert.py); __init__.py marks UniMTS as "Planned". The documentation gap and the mismatch between multi-sensor author mappings and HALO's single-sensor eval window are real and unaddressed. || CONFIRMED(repro): The finding's core claim is confirmed by exhaustive evidence. A repo-wide grep for "joint_list", "joint_index", "SMPL joint", and "joint_assign" returns exactly two hits, both in baseline_flexibility.md: the matrix cell at line 28 ("native (SMPL joint)") and the prose at line 51 ("22-joint SMPL skeleton graph"). No per-dataset joint index table exists anywhere in the HALO repo or its docs. The UniMTS adapter is unbuilt (no unimts.py in val_scripts/human_activity_recognition/baselines/). The scratchpad mirror of UniMTS source (unimts_data.py) confirms load_custom_data takes joint_list as a required positional parameter and uses stride-6 indexing X[:,:,6*i:6*i+3] / X[:,:,6*i+3:6*i+6] (lines 268-269), exactly as claimed. The multi-sensor author maps in data.py cover MotionSense (joints 5&1), Shoaib (joints 1,5,21,20,0), Harth (joints 9&6), and realworld (7 joints) — all multi-sensor originals not directly reusable for HALO's single-sensor (N,120,6) eval windows. mobiact and inclusivehar have no author mapping in the source. This finding does not appear in the resolution log (MODELS-5 through MODELS-8 address accel-only correction, unit scaling, label normalization, and window handling respectively; none address joint index assignment). One element of the proposed fix is overstated: BENCH_LIMU arrays are already (N,120,6) six-channel windows, so load_custom_data with joint_list=[j] and i=0 reads X[:,:,0:3] as acc and X[:,:,3:6] as gyro directly — no zero-filled gyro construction is needed. The gyro columns are silently discarded by the default --gyro 0 slice, so feeding the 6-ch array as-is is sufficient. This is a minor correction to the proposed fix, not a refutation of the core finding. Severity MEDIUM is appropriate and matches the pattern of MODELS-6 (per-dataset unit scaling, also a pre-build documentation requirement for the same unbuilt adapter, also marked MEDIUM and DEFERRED to build time). An additional side-observation (not the subject of this audit): baseline_flexibility.md:52 still reads "Expects 6-axis (acc+gyro) per placed sensor," contradicting the MODELS-5 FIXED claim that was supposed to correct that line — the matrix cell was fixed but the prose was not.

### 22. [MEDIUM] [dataset:mobiact] manifest num_subjects=31 is wrong (actual 24); on-disk data is the MobiFall v2.0 TXT release, not the 57-subject MobiAct the paper/citation describe
- **area:** subject-identity / provenance
- **issue:** data/mobiact/manifest.json declares "num_subjects": 31, but the converted corpus contains only 24 distinct subjects (IDs {1-21,29,30,31}; 22-28 absent). The converter was run through its MobiFall (TXT) branch — gyroscope is interpolated onto accelerometer timestamps (convert.py:194-212), a MobiFall-only artifact, and create_manifest() emits num_subjects=31 exactly for the mobifall branch. So the dataset is effectively the MobiFall v2.0 24-subject release, whereas references/datasets/mobiact/paper.pdf (Vavoulas 2016, MobiAct) describes 57 subjects / 2500+ trials and the citation.json flags the second-release ambiguity (66 subjects). The manifest description even self-contradicts: it calls the data "MobiAct (v2.0) ... (Derived from but distinct from MobiFall.)" while it IS the MobiFall path. create_manifest() can never reproduce the true count (it hardcodes 31 for mobifall / 66 for mobiact, never 24). Signal content is protocol-identical (same S3 phone, same trouser-pocket protocol, same activity codes), so nothing is corrupted — but the dataset is mislabeled/miscounted, which matters when a paper cites 'MobiAct, N subjects'.
- **evidence:** data/mobiact/manifest.json: "num_subjects": 31; datascripts/mobiact/convert.py:487 (mobifall→31), :491 (mobiact→66); measured 24 distinct subjects from 3646 labels.json sessions = {1..21,29,30,31}; benchmark_data/eval_v2/labels/mobiact.json n_subjects=24 and benchmark_data/processed/tsfm_eval/mobiact/metadata.json subject_to_idx has exactly 24 keys (both correct, contradicting the manifest); paper.pdf p.4 'total of 57 subjects with more than 2500 trials'; convert.py:194-212 gyro np.interp onto acc timestamps (mobifall-only).
- **fix:** Set data/mobiact/manifest.json num_subjects to the true 24 (and fix create_manifest() to count distinct subjects rather than hardcode 31/66). Resolve provenance: either relabel the source as 'MobiFall v2.0 subset (24 subjects)' in the description, or re-fetch the actual MobiAct release. Note raw is absent (data/raw/mobiact missing), so a re-run is blocked — flag like the other raw-absent datasets.
- **verify:** CONFIRMED(repro): All core claims independently reproduced from primary sources. (1) manifest.json line 5 confirms num_subjects=31. (2) My script on labels.json keys measured exactly 24 distinct subjects: {1..21, 29, 30, 31}; IDs 22-28 absent; total 3646 sessions. (3) convert.py create_manifest() lines 487/491 hardcode 31 for mobifall and 66 for mobiact — it never counts actual subjects. (4) The gyro interpolation at lines 198-211 (np.interp of gyro onto acc timestamps) is only reachable via the mobifall trial converter, confirming the data was processed through the MobiFall TXT branch. (5) benchmark_data/eval_v2/labels/mobiact.json shows n_subjects=24, and benchmark_data/processed/tsfm_eval/mobiact/metadata.json subject_to_idx has exactly 24 keys — both correctly reflect the actual count and directly contradict the manifest. The manifest description also self-contradicts by calling the data "Derived from but distinct from MobiFall" when the TXT pipeline and hardcoded count confirm it IS the MobiFall path. No data corruption — the eval pipeline uses the correct 24-subject count — but the manifest mislabels provenance and subject count, which matters for paper claims. || CONFIRMED(repro): All empirical evidence confirms the finding. Three independent sources agree on 24 actual subjects ({1-21, 29, 30, 31}): labels.json session key parsing, benchmark_data/processed/tsfm_eval/mobiact/metadata.json subject_to_idx (24 keys), and benchmark_data/eval_v2/labels/mobiact.json n_subjects=24. The manifest.json at /home/alex/code/HALO/code/data/mobiact/manifest.json declares num_subjects=31, and create_manifest() at convert.py:487 hardcodes 31 for the mobifall branch with no dynamic counting. The MobiFall TXT code path is confirmed by session naming ("FKL_21_03_0000" = activity_subjectID_trial_window) and by the gyro np.interp logic at convert.py:194-212 (parse_mobifall_txt-based, mobifall-only). The MobiAct CSV path (parse_mobiact_filename / load_mobiact_csv) was never exercised. The resolution log (DATA-1 through DATA-26) has no entry for mobiact. The manifest description "Subset of subjects converted here" partially hints at the discrepancy but still calls the dataset "MobiAct (v2.0)", still says num_subjects=31, and the parenthetical "(Derived from but distinct from MobiFall.)" contradicts reality — this IS the MobiFall TXT code path. No compensating mechanism or documentation correctly labels the true state. Signal content is not corrupted (correct protocol, correct timestamps, correct activities), so severity remains MEDIUM.

### 23. [MEDIUM] [dataset:realworld] convert.py silently drops RealWorld multi-part recordings: 8 subject×activity cells empty in the held-out TEST set
- **area:** data-completeness / labels
- **issue:** load_sensor_from_zip() reads exactly one file per (sensor,activity): target_filename = f"{sensor_prefix}_{activity}_{TARGET_POSITION}.csv" (e.g. acc_walking_waist.csv), and does `if target_filename not in zf.namelist(): return None`. RealWorld recorded several probands' long/interrupted activities (walking, climbing up/down, jogging) in numbered parts inside the zip (acc_<activity>_1_waist.csv, _2_waist.csv, ...) with NO plain acc_<activity>_waist.csv. Those recordings match nothing, return None, and the entire activity for that subject is skipped without warning. Net effect on this TEST set: whole (subject,activity) cells contain zero windows, concentrated exactly in the split-prone activities.
- **evidence:** Subject×activity window matrix from data/realworld/labels.json: s04 MISSING walking+stairs_up+stairs_down; s07 MISSING sitting+stairs_up+stairs_down; s14 MISSING stairs_up+stairs_down; s13 MISSING walking; s06 MISSING sitting; s08 MISSING standing. So stairs_up/stairs_down have 0 support for 3/15 subjects (s04,s07,s14) and walking 0 for 2/15. Mechanism is confirmed in datascripts/realworld/convert.py load_sensor_from_zip (single-filename match, return None on miss) with no glob/concat of numbered parts and no warning; the all-or-nothing-per-cell pattern rules out the <10-sample filters. Raw data/raw/realworld is absent locally so I cannot open the raw zips to byte-confirm the split files exist, but the pattern + code path are conclusive that any multi-part recording is discarded.
- **fix:** In load_sensor_from_zip, match all parts (e.g. re.compile(rf'{sensor_prefix}_{activity}(_\d+)?_{TARGET_POSITION}\.csv$') over zf.namelist()), read and concatenate/segment every matching part instead of a single hard-coded name, and log a warning for every (subject,activity) that yields no waist file so silent drops surface. Then re-run to restore the missing stairs/walking/sitting/standing cells before using realworld as a headline test set.
- **files:** datascripts/realworld/convert.py, data/realworld/labels.json
- **verify:** CONFIRMED(repro): Both pillars of the finding independently reproduced. (1) Code path: load_sensor_from_zip in datascripts/realworld/convert.py line 82 constructs a single hard-coded filename and line 86 returns None silently when it is absent — no glob over numbered parts, no concatenation, no warning. (2) Data: labels.json yields exactly 11 missing (subject, activity) cells matching the finding's list: s04 missing climbingdown/climbingup/walking; s06 missing sitting; s07 missing climbingdown/climbingup/sitting; s08 missing standing; s13 missing walking; s14 missing climbingdown/climbingup. The specific activities affected (stairs_up, stairs_down, walking, sitting, standing) are exactly the ones prone to multi-part recordings. The finding is not previously logged in the adversarial_sweep_findings.md resolution log (DATA-1 through DATA-26). Raw zip files are absent locally so byte-level confirmation of numbered parts is not possible, but the code mechanism and missing-cell pattern are conclusive. Severity MEDIUM is appropriate: 11/120 cells missing in a held-out test set is material but not catastrophic; most subjects still cover all activities. || CONFIRMED(repro): The code path in datascripts/realworld/convert.py load_sensor_from_zip() (lines 82-86) is exactly as described: it constructs a single exact filename and returns None with no warning, glob, or numbered-part fallback. The zero-cell pattern in data/realworld/labels.json is directly confirmed: 11 subject×activity cells have 0 windows (s04: climbingdown/climbingup/walking; s06: sitting; s07: climbingdown/climbingup/sitting; s08: standing; s13: walking; s14: climbingdown/climbingup). The small-sample filter explanation is ruled out — affected subjects produce 193–332 windows for other activities (e.g., s04 running=332), proving their sensors were functional. No compensating mechanism exists anywhere in the codebase (grep confirms no glob/regex/concat logic for numbered parts). This finding does not appear in the adversarial_sweep_findings.md resolution log. The one unverifiable element is whether the raw zip files specifically contain numbered-part filenames (e.g., acc_walking_1_waist.csv) vs. simply being absent — raw data is not present locally — but this does not weaken the finding: both causes (multi-part naming and missing zips) are equally silent-drop failures from the same code bug, and the zero-cell evidence is real and directly measured. Severity MEDIUM is appropriate: 11/120 cells (9.2%) are zero in a held-out TEST set, including 3/15 subjects missing both stair-climbing activities.

### 24. [MEDIUM] [dataset:unimib_shar] UniMiB 'sitting_down' (a stand-to-sit transition) is mis-grouped as static 'sitting'; the intended transition token is dead
- **area:** labels / label-grouping (training soft-targets + sampling)
- **issue:** The paper defines UniMiB class SittingDown as the postural transition "From standing to sitting on a chair" (paper.pdf extracted line 417), a brief dynamic movement — the sibling of standing_up_from_sitting and lying_down_from_standing. In label_groups.py the actual dataset label 'sitting_down' is listed inside the STATIC 'sitting' group (LABEL_GROUPS line 50; LABEL_GROUPS_SIMPLE line 175), while the transition groups reference a non-existent token 'sitting_down_from_standing' ('transition_to_sitting' line 60; 'postural_transition' line 185-186). A data scan shows 'sitting_down' is produced ONLY by unimib_shar and 'sitting_down_from_standing' is produced by NO dataset, so the transition mapping is dead and the sit-down transition leaks into static sitting. label_augmentation.py mirrors the same naming bug: a dead 'sitting_down_from_standing' synonym block (line 179) plus a separate live 'sitting_down' block (line 189). Because semantic_loss.py:_build_same_label_mask (lines 23-28) uses get_label_to_group_mapping() to build the contrastive positive/soft-target mask (and multi_dataset_loader.py:802/857 uses groups for balanced sampling), UniMiB 'sitting down' windows are trained as POSITIVES against static-sitting windows from 10 other datasets (hapt, mhealth, pamap2, dsads, capture24, hhar, harth, opportunity, shoaib, inclusivehar) and as NEGATIVES against UniMiB's own sibling transitions — the opposite of the intended grouping.
- **evidence:** paper.pdf (pdfplumber extract) line 417: 'Sittingdown Fromstandingtosittingonachair SittingDown'; label_groups.py:50 'sitting':[...,'sitting_down',...]; label_groups.py:60 'transition_to_sitting':['sitting_down_from_standing','stand_to_sit']; label_groups.py:185-186 'postural_transition' lists 'sitting_down_from_standing'; label_augmentation.py:179 (dead key) vs :189 (live key); grep of data/*/labels.json: 'sitting_down' produced only by unimib_shar, 'sitting_down_from_standing' produced by none; semantic_loss.py:23-28 builds same-group positive mask from get_label_to_group_mapping(). Not in the resolution log (DATA-22 only covers the Hz literal).
- **fix:** Rename the transition-group / synonym entries from 'sitting_down_from_standing' to the actual emitted label 'sitting_down' (label_groups.py lines 60 and 186, label_augmentation.py line 179), and remove 'sitting_down' from the static 'sitting' groups (lines 50 and 175) so UniMiB's sit-down is grouped with its transition siblings, matching the paper semantics.
- **files:** datasets/imu_pretraining_dataset/label_groups.py, datasets/imu_pretraining_dataset/label_augmentation.py, training_scripts/human_activity_recognition/semantic_loss.py
- **verify:** CONFIRMED(repro): All five sub-claims independently reproduced from primary sources.

1. GROUP MAPPING (label_groups.py): `get_label_to_group_mapping()` assigns `'sitting_down' -> 'sitting'` (confirmed empirically: script output "Group for 'sitting_down': sitting"). The `'sitting'` group is `['sitting', 'sitting_down', 'sitting_chair', 'transport_sit']` — a pure static-posture group. The transition group `'transition_to_sitting'` (fine) and `'postural_transition'` (simple) reference only the non-existent token `'sitting_down_from_standing'`, confirmed by the script returning `'sitting_down_from_standing' -> 'transition_to_sitting'`/`'postural_transition'`, a mapping that never fires because the label is never emitted.

2. DEAD TOKEN IN DATA (grep of all labels.json): `'sitting_down'` appears only in `data/unimib_shar/labels.json`. `'sitting_down_from_standing'` appears in no dataset's labels.json — the grep returned no output, confirming the transition-group entry is dead.

3. LABEL_AUGMENTATION (label_augmentation.py): Line 179 contains the dead key `"sitting_down_from_standing"` in `UNIMIB_SHAR_SYNONYMS`; line 189 contains the live key `"sitting_down"`. The augmentation for the actual emitted label is technically fine (it produces plausible natural-language), but the dead synonym block wastes a slot and is evidence the naming mismatch permeates multiple files.

4. TRAINING LOSS IMPACT (semantic_loss.py + multi_dataset_loader.py): `_build_same_label_mask` (lines 18-29) calls `get_label_to_group_mapping()` imported via `val_scripts.human_activity_recognition.evaluation_metrics`, which itself imports the same `get_label_to_group_mapping` from `datasets.imu_pretraining_dataset.label_groups` (confirmed by grep). `multi_dataset_loader.py` lines 801-817 call `get_group_for_label` from the same `label_groups` module for group-balanced sampling. Both paths use the buggy `LABEL_GROUPS` dict, so the semantic inversion is active in both the contrastive positive-mask and the sampling weights.

5. RESOLUTION LOG: DATA-22 (the only unimib_shar entry) covers only the "50Hz" channel-description literal. No entry covers the `sitting_down` grouping bug.

Net effect: every `sitting_down` window from unimib_shar is treated as a contrastive POSITIVE against static-sitting windows from 10 other training datasets, and as a NEGATIVE against its own transition siblings — precisely the opposite of the paper-intended semantics. The finding is accurate in all material details. Severity MEDIUM is appropriate: the error corrupts the learned representation for one transition class but does not invalidate the full evaluation benchmark or corrupt other classes. || CONFIRMED(repro): The structural code defect is fully confirmed: 'sitting_down' is in LABEL_GROUPS['sitting'] (line 50) and LABEL_GROUPS_SIMPLE['sitting'] (line 175), while both transition groups use the dead key 'sitting_down_from_standing' that no dataset ever emits (lines 60, 185-186). label_augmentation.py's UNIMIB_SHAR_SYNONYMS also contains the dead key at line 179 alongside the live 'sitting_down' key at line 189.

One key part of the claimed mechanism is factually wrong and weakens the finding: semantic_loss.py's _build_same_label_mask is called ONLY in _compute_metrics (line 300), where it is used to compute the monitoring metric 'negative_similarity' (excluding same-group pairs from the negative set). It does NOT affect the actual training targets. The real soft-target distribution is computed from torch.matmul(frozen_sbert_embeddings, frozen_sbert_embeddings.T) — raw cosine similarities between frozen SentenceBERT text embeddings. Since label_augmentation.py's live UNIMIB_SHAR_SYNONYMS['sitting_down'] provides transition-like phrases ("sitting down", "taking a seat", "sit down"), SBERT will naturally pull the soft-target weight toward other transition labels rather than static-sitting. So the "trained as POSITIVES against static-sitting windows from 10 other datasets" claim is incorrect for the loss gradient.

The real and confirmed impact is through balanced sampling: multi_dataset_loader.py lines 802 and 857 call get_group_for_label (which uses the same LABEL_GROUPS dict) to assign sampling weights. Because 'sitting_down' falls into the large 'sitting' group spanning 10+ datasets, UniMiB sitting_down sessions receive a very low relative weight (diluted by the enormous static-sitting pool), while the 'transition_to_sitting' group is starved of any unimib_shar contribution. This under-represents the only sit-down transition example in training. The 'negative_similarity' monitoring metric is also miscategorized (wrongly treats static-sitting windows as same-group with sitting_down), but this is purely a metrics-display artifact. Net assessment: code defect is real and confirmed, sampling impact is genuine but the dominant claimed mechanism (loss target corruption) does not apply.

### 25. [MEDIUM] [dataset:wisdm] phone_accel stream runs at ~25 Hz (irregular), not the declared uniform 20 Hz — undisclosed rate error on the designated core accelerometer
- **area:** sampling-rate
- **issue:** The manifest, dataset_config, and converter all declare/hardcode a uniform 20 Hz for WISDM, and the loader consumes samples at that declared rate with no timestamp-based resampling. Empirically 3 of the 4 sensor streams are genuinely ~20 Hz, but the phone_accel stream — which is the designated core accelerometer and ~31% of all WISDM sessions — is sampled substantially faster and irregularly (~25 Hz median). Because windows are sized by sample count assuming 20 Hz and the model is fed a hard '20 Hz' text token, phone_accel windows are time-compressed ~25% relative to their label: walking/jogging cadence appears slower than reality and the per-channel rate token is wrong for this stream. The paper itself acknowledges this: 'the sampling rate is only taken as a suggestion, so actual sampling rates sometimes differed.'
- **evidence:** Measured pooled inter-sample intervals from data/wisdm/sessions/*/data.parquet timestamp_sec (60 windows/stream): phone_accel median delta = 40.11 ms -> 24.9 Hz (p10=20.1ms/50Hz, p50=40ms/25Hz, p90=50.4ms/20Hz; only 47% of intervals near 50ms). By contrast watch_accel=49.92ms/20.03Hz, phone_gyro=49.99ms/20.0Hz, watch_gyro=49.93ms/20.03Hz (all clean 20Hz). phone_accel = 50626/164623 = 30.7% of WISDM sessions. data/wisdm/manifest.json all channels sampling_rate_hz=20.0; benchmark_data/dataset_config.json wisdm sampling_rate_hz=20 and core_channels.acc_x=phone_accel_x; datascripts/wisdm/convert.py:107,151 hardcode sample_rate=20.0 for create_variable_windows; multi_dataset_loader.py:471 takes rate from manifest (20 Hz) with no timestamp resample. Paper (Weiss 2019, references/datasets/wisdm/paper.pdf p.3) confirms nominal 20 Hz but actual rates differed. Not in the resolution log (DATA-9/DATA-23 cover kuhar/mhealth rates, not wisdm).
- **fix:** In datascripts/wisdm/convert.py, resample phone_accel to a true uniform 20 Hz from its timestamp_sec (the other 3 streams are already clean 20 Hz and need no change), OR persist the true per-stream effective rate so the loader's window sizing and the '(sampled at NHz)' text token reflect ~25 Hz for phone_accel. Do not silently keep uniform 20 Hz for the core accelerometer.
- **files:** datascripts/wisdm/convert.py, data/wisdm/manifest.json, benchmark_data/dataset_config.json, datasets/imu_pretraining_dataset/multi_dataset_loader.py
- **verify:** CONFIRMED(repro): Independent measurement of inter-sample intervals from 60 sampled sessions per stream yielded: phone_accel median=39.84ms -> 25.10 Hz (p10=20.1ms, p90=50.4ms, 44.4% near 50ms); phone_gyro=50.35ms/19.86Hz; watch_accel=49.92ms/20.03Hz; watch_gyro=49.93ms/20.03Hz. These match the finding's reported values closely (their 40.11ms/24.9Hz vs my 39.84ms/25.10Hz; identical p10 and p90). Session fraction: phone_accel=50626/164623=30.8% (vs claimed 30.7%). Code confirms: convert.py lines 107 and 151 hardcode sample_rate=20.0 for all streams with no per-stream rate estimation; manifest.json declares sampling_rate_hz=20.0 for every channel including phone_accel. The finding is not present in the adversarial sweep resolution log (DATA-9/DATA-23 cover kuhar/mhealth, not wisdm). All numerical claims reproduced from primary sources. || CONFIRMED(repro): Core finding holds under empirical verification. Pooled delta stats reproduce the finding precisely: phone_accel median delta=39.91ms→25.1Hz (p10=20.1ms, p90=50.4ms), exactly matching the reported 40.11ms/24.9Hz. No compensating mechanism exists: timestamp_sec is referenced in multi_dataset_loader.py only to be excluded from channel data (lines 420, 426, 766); model/preprocessing.py:46 computes patch_timesteps=int(sampling_rate_hz*patch_size_sec) using the manifest's declared 20Hz; no timestamp-based resampling anywhere in the pipeline. Finding is not in the resolution log (DATA-9=kuhar, DATA-23=mhealth).

Strongest refutation attempt found two inaccuracies that complicate but do not eliminate the finding: (1) Per-session analysis shows phone_accel is bimodal, not uniformly ~25Hz — approximately 50% of sessions run at ~50Hz (20ms intervals) and ~38% at ~20Hz; the reported "~25Hz median" is a pooled-delta artifact. For the 50Hz sessions the time-compression is 2.5×, not the ~25% stated, so the finding understates severity for half the affected sessions. (2) The finding claims phone_gyro is clean 20Hz (49.99ms) to contrast with phone_accel, but empirically 42% of phone_gyro sessions are at ~25Hz per-session median — phone_gyro is also affected, widening the scope of the problem rather than refuting it. Both points strengthen rather than undermine the core claim of an undisclosed, uncompensated rate error on the designated core accelerometer stream.

### 26. [MEDIUM] [design:data-strategy] temp=0 group-balancing silently overrides the '400h / 42% free-living' corpus design and spends batch capacity on non-activity labels
- **area:** sampling (double-balancing interaction)
- **issue:** Two balancing stages compose without accounting for each other. Stage 1 (per-dataset HOURS caps) was tuned to lift free-living capture24 to 42% of loaded hours. Stage 2 (group-balanced WeightedRandomSampler at sampling_temperature=0.0, i.e. uniform-over-groups) then re-weights by label group, which largely erases stage 1: because capture24 owns only a few groups, its effective sampling share falls back to ~22%, and scripted wisdm becomes the single most-sampled source. Worse, uniform-over-groups gives every group ~1/38 (~2.6%) of every batch, including capture24's non-activity labels mixed_activity and household_chores — the model devotes as much capacity to 'mixed activity' text as to 'walking'. The headline claim that the model trains on '~42% free-living' is therefore not what the sampler actually presents.
- **evidence:** semantic_alignment_train.py:310 MAX_OVERSAMPLE_RATIO=20.0, :311 SAMPLING_TEMPERATURE=0.0, :2251-2253 compute_group_weights wired into ChannelBucketBatchSampler; data_quantity_report.md:155-170 claims capture24 '~42% of the mix'. Measured effective per-dataset sampling share under temp=0 + 20x cap vs loaded-hours share: capture24 21.7% (loaded 42.0%), wisdm 24.3% (loaded 21.3%, now #1), kuhar 16.3% (loaded 10.1%). mixed_activity = 5672 windows, 100% capture24, a semantically non-discriminative label receiving ~2.6% of every batch.
- **fix:** Set sampling_temperature to ~0.3-0.5 (between sqrt and balanced) so the deliberately-chosen loaded composition is largely respected while rare classes are still mildly upweighted; or explicitly reconcile the hours-caps and the group weights so the intended free-living share survives sampling. Exclude or down-weight non-activity labels (mixed_activity, and optionally household_chores/manual_work) from the alignment target. Report the effective post-sampler dataset/group share in data_quantity_report.md, not just loaded hours. Expected benefit: the model actually trains on the free-living-heavy mix intended for deployability, and stops spending capacity aligning to noise labels.
- **files:** /home/alex/code/HALO/code/training_scripts/human_activity_recognition/semantic_alignment_train.py, /home/alex/code/HALO/code/datasets/imu_pretraining_dataset/multi_dataset_loader.py, /home/alex/code/HALO/code/docs/v2/data_quantity_report.md
- **verify:** CONFIRMED(repro): Independently reproduced from primary sources. Config confirmed: semantic_alignment_train.py:310 MAX_OVERSAMPLE_RATIO=20.0, :311 SAMPLING_TEMPERATURE=0.0, :362 USE_GROUP_BALANCED_SAMPLING=True; :2253 wires compute_group_weights into ChannelBucketBatchSampler. Verified the sampler math: P(sample i)=w_i/sum(w) because bucket_probs are proportional to per-bucket weight sums and within-bucket sampling is weight-proportional, so channel-bucketing cancels and the marginal share is driven purely by compute_group_weights (w=count^(temp-1)=1/count at temp=0, capped at 20x min). Ran the exact train split (127288 sessions, wisdm capped, hhar capped) and computed effective per-dataset shares: capture24 21.8% effective vs 42.9% loaded hours (finding: 21.7% vs 42.0%); wisdm 25.3% and is the #1 sampled source though loaded hours only 21.8% (finding: 24.3%, #1); kuhar 15.7% vs loaded 9.7% (finding: 16.3% vs 10.1%). Small ~1pp differences trace to the random session shuffle at cap time. Confirmed mixed_activity (4232 train sessions, 100% capture24) and household_chores (5417, 100% capture24) are NOT in any LABEL_GROUPS entry, so each is a singleton group receiving 3.68% of every batch — identical to walking's 3.68%; the finding's ~2.6% is if anything understated (17 capped groups redistribute weight to 21 uncapped groups). Mechanism confirmed: stage-1 hours caps that lifted capture24 to ~42% of hours are largely undone by stage-2 uniform-over-groups balancing because capture24's free-living data mostly lands in shared groups (walking/standing/sitting/lying) diluted by scripted datasets, owning only a few singletons exclusively. Severity MEDIUM: not a correctness/crash bug, but a genuine mismatch between the documented '42% free-living' headline and what the sampler presents by session (~22%), plus concrete capacity spent aligning to non-discriminative labels. Not downgraded to LOW because the doc-vs-actual mismatch is a results-integrity concern and the wasted-capacity effect is concrete and measurable. || CONFIRMED(repro): Mechanism and measurement reproduced from labels.json with real caps. SAMPLING_TEMPERATURE=0.0 is the default and compute_group_weights is wired unconditionally into ChannelBucketBatchSampler; because bucket_probs is proportional to summed sample weights, the two-stage sampler collapses to marginal P(window)=w_i/sum(w), i.e. pure group-balancing. Effective per-dataset sampling share matches the finding to the decimal: capture24 21.7% (loaded 42.0%), wisdm 24.3% (now #1, loaded 21.3%), kuhar 16.3% (loaded 10.1%). So the '~42% free-living' figure is loaded-hours, not what the sampler presents (21.7%), and the two balancing stages are genuinely unreconciled. The mixed_activity group receives 3.54% of every batch (equal to walking) -- the finding's ~2.6% estimate was conservative because the 20x cap on rare groups pushes uncapped groups above 1/38. Refutations fail: not dead code (it is the default path), not a measurement artifact (independently reproduced), and no compensating reconciliation exists in the loader or train script. The only partial defense is that group-balancing across ~38 activity groups is an intentional, commented class-balance choice and the doc labels 42% honestly as loaded hours -- this makes the proposed temp=0.3-0.5 change contestable (uniform-over-groups is arguably better for zero-shot class discrimination than deployment-marginal matching), but it does not refute the factual claims. Severity MEDIUM stands: real unreconciled sampling interaction plus capacity spent equally on a non-discriminative 'mixed activity' target, with cheap valid remedies (report post-sampler share; down-weight non-activity labels).

### 27. [MEDIUM] [design:data-strategy] Sensor placement is entangled with dataset identity and never balanced, so zero-shot to unseen placements is under-supported
- **area:** corpus composition / placement coverage
- **issue:** Body placement is highly correlated with dataset (it is one axis of the fingerprint) and is not balanced anywhere in sampling. Wrist dominates via capture24 (42% loaded / ~22% effective) plus recgym/mhealth-wrist/wisdm-watch; waist via uci/hapt/hhar/kuhar; pocket via unimib/wisdm-phone; torso via dsads; hand/chest/ankle via pamap2. The held-out test sets deliberately vary placement (motionsense=pocket, realworld=7 positions, shoaib=5 positions, harth=back+thigh), so placement generalization is the core zero-shot challenge — yet training places nearly all wrist mass in a single free-living, accel-only, coarse-label source and couples every placement to a distinct label vocabulary and fingerprint.
- **evidence:** Placement read from manifests: capture24 'dominant wrist' (accel-only, 3ch), unimib 'trouser pocket', uci_har/hapt/hhar 'waist', kuhar 'waist', dsads 'torso', recgym 'wrist', pamap2 'hand/chest/ankle', mhealth 'chest/ankle/wrist', wisdm 'pocket+wrist'. compute_group_weights (multi_dataset_loader.py:775-847) balances only by label group; no placement term. Effective wrist share is dominated by one accel-only source.
- **fix:** Add placement-balanced (or placement-stratified) sampling as a second balancing axis, and expose placement as an explicit, short conditioning token decoupled from the dataset-fingerprint prose (ties into finding 1). Prioritize adding multi-position free-living sources (e.g. ExtraSensory / RealWorld-style) so wrist is not monopolized by one coarse accel-only dataset. Expected benefit: placement invariance is learned rather than memorized per dataset, directly targeting the held-out placement shift.
- **files:** /home/alex/code/HALO/code/datasets/imu_pretraining_dataset/multi_dataset_loader.py, /home/alex/code/HALO/code/training_scripts/human_activity_recognition/semantic_alignment_train.py
- **verify:** CONFIRMED(repro): Independently reproduced the core claim from primary sources. (1) Placement is entangled with dataset identity: channel-text prose embeds placement per dataset (capture24='Wrist accelerometer', dsads='Accelerometer (torso)', wisdm='Phone accelerometer (pocket-worn)'), and each training dataset maps to a single dominant placement. (2) No placement balancing exists: compute_group_weights (multi_dataset_loader.py:775-847) balances only by LABEL_GROUPS; the training sampler (semantic_alignment_train.py:2253) uses only group weights + channel bucketing. Zero placement term in loader or training script. (3) Wrist dominates and is monopolized by one accel-only free-living source: I measured raw wrist share = 42.6% of sessions (matches finding's '42% loaded'); capture24 = 46.2% of the effective group-balanced wrist pool and is accel-only (3ch, verified against manifest). (4) Held-out test sets deliberately vary placement (shoaib=5 positions, harth=back+thigh, motionsense/mobiact=pocket), confirming placement is the core zero-shot challenge. Two evidence errors in the finding that do NOT overturn the verdict: 'realworld=7 positions' is wrong (the converted realworld here is waist-only per manifest + all channel descriptions); and the '~22% effective' wrist figure does not reproduce (I measure 41.8% effective — group balancing slightly increases wrist mass, not decreases it). Severity MEDIUM is fair: a genuine corpus/training-strategy gap on exactly the generalization axis the eval tests, but it does not cause incorrect behavior or invalidate existing results. || CONFIRMED(repro): Factual claims reproduced. compute_group_weights (multi_dataset_loader.py) balances only by label group via get_group_for_label; grep of the loader and semantic_alignment_train.py finds no placement/position term in any sampler (only ChannelBucketBatchSampler by channel count + WeightedRandomSampler by group). Placement is fixed per dataset in manifests (capture24=wrist accel-only 3ch, recgym/wisdm-watch=wrist, uci/hapt/hhar/kuhar=waist, unimib/wisdm-phone=pocket, dsads=torso, pamap2/mhealth=multi), and wrist mass is dominated by capture24 (48.5k sessions, accel-only, coarse WillettsSpecific labels), so placement is entangled with dataset+label-vocabulary+fingerprint and is never balanced. Held-out test sets deliberately shift placement (motionsense=pocket, realworld=7 pos, shoaib=5 pos, harth=back+thigh), so this is the core zero-shot axis. Best refutations only partially mitigate, not contradict: (1) placement words ARE in channel-text descriptions for most sets, so ChannelTextFusion already exposes placement as a language token and gives a SentenceBERT similarity path to unseen placements — this pre-implements half the fix but enables placement identification, not sampling balance or invariance, and is inconsistent (hhar/uci_har have no placement text); (2) default yaw_rotation + random_rotation augs address sensor orientation, not body-location signal differences, so they do not compensate. Not in the Resolution log. Severity MEDIUM (not HIGH) because placement is visible via channel text and rotation augs partly help orientation. Caveat on the proposed fix: placement-balanced sampling alone cannot create invariance when placement is confounded with label vocabulary and no multi-position same-activity data exists; the finding's own multi-position-data recommendation is the stronger lever. That weakens the remedy, not the diagnosis.

### 28. [MEDIUM] [design:deployment-extensibility] SBERT text tower runs on every forward() at inference — the frozen/precomputed text bank the config docstring promises does not exist
- **area:** on-device/doc-vs-impl
- **issue:** model/config.py:11-13 states 'At inference, precomputed embeddings are stored as constants, so the text encoder is never deployed on-device.' No code does this. SemanticAlignmentModel.forward() unconditionally calls self.text_encoder.encode(all_descs) on the per-channel description strings every single forward pass, and ChannelTextFusion consumes those live token embeddings; label scoring at eval also runs SBERT (LearnableLabelBank.encode -> TokenTextEncoder -> SentenceTransformer, and eval_v2 sbert.encode). So the real deployable footprint is ~26M encoder + the full all-MiniLM-L6-v2 transformer (~22M params) + its tokenizer vocab, invoked on the hot path — making the advertised '<10 ms on-device' and 'text encoder never deployed' false for the current weights.
- **evidence:** model/config.py:11-13 (claim); training_scripts/.../semantic_alignment_train.py:571 `self.text_encoder.encode(all_descs, device)` inside forward() and :581 channel_fusion uses the tokens; model/token_text_encoder.py:46-48 TokenTextEncoder loads SentenceTransformer at construction; :508 LearnableLabelBank.encode calls text_encoder.encode; val_scripts/.../eval_v2.py:416 sbert.encode for labels.
- **fix:** Implement the promised freeze: precompute the label matrix [num_labels x D] and the enumerated channel-text embeddings (placement x sensor x rate) as buffers, add a fusion path that consumes constant channel embeddings instead of running SBERT, and gate SBERT behind set_labels()/set_channels() so the deployed graph is SBERT-free. Until then, correct the config docstring.
- **files:** model/config.py, training_scripts/human_activity_recognition/semantic_alignment_train.py, model/token_text_encoder.py
- **verify:** CONFIRMED(repro): Independently reproduced from primary sources. config.py:11-13 claims precomputed text embeddings are stored as constants and the text encoder is never deployed on-device; config.py:79,102 repeat this. No code implements it: forward() (semantic_alignment_train.py:508) unconditionally calls self.text_encoder.encode(all_descs, device) at line 571 with no gate (the use_fusion flag at :579 only gates fusion application, not encoding). There is no register_buffer, no set_labels()/set_channels(), and no precomputed label/channel matrix. The only cache is a plain Python dict (_cache) that token_text_encoder.py:52 explicitly keeps OUT of state_dict, so it is not persisted; a fresh deployment must load and run the full SentenceTransformer via _init_model(). LearnableLabelBank.encode (:508) and encode_frozen (:529) also route through text_encoder.encode, and eval label scoring runs SBERT. I empirically confirmed all-MiniLM-L6-v2 = 22,713,216 transformer params + 30,522-token vocab, matching the ~22M claim, so the deployable footprint really is ~26M encoder + full MiniLM + tokenizer. One nuance: the per-string cache skips the SBERT forward for repeated descriptions after warmup, so it does not literally recompute on every pass — but the model must still be resident and is invoked for any new string, so the 'stored as constants / never deployed' claim is false regardless. Severity corrected to MEDIUM: it is a genuine, verified doc-vs-impl overstatement of on-device footprint confined to an aspirational docstring (already contradicted by the team's own docs/v2/design_application.md:100), not a defect that corrupts results or crashes. || CONFIRMED(repro): The docstring claim in config.py:11-13 ("At inference, precomputed embeddings are stored as constants, so the text encoder is never deployed on-device") is demonstrably false. No register_buffer, no set_labels()/set_channels(), no serialized embedding matrix, and no buffer-based precompute mechanism exists anywhere in the codebase. The SBERT transformer is instantiated by TokenTextEncoder._init_model() on first use, occupies ~22M params in memory, and runs on every cold start (fresh process) to warm the cache. The "precomputed constants" described in the docstring simply do not exist.

However, the finding's specific severity framing — "SBERT runs every single forward pass / hot path" — is materially wrong and warrants a severity downgrade. TokenTextEncoder maintains a persistent in-memory dict cache (_cache, line 39); encode() only invokes the underlying transformer for strings not already present (lines 88–110). Channel descriptions are fixed enumerated strings that are identical across all batches from the same dataset, so after the very first forward call they are all cached and subsequent batches perform zero SBERT computation. The practical runtime cost is a one-time cold-start warmup per inference session, not a per-window or per-batch overhead. The eval_v2.py sbert.encode cited as evidence is exclusively in get_sbert_encoder() for the ConSE baseline bridge, not HALO's own inference path; label_bank.encode() for HALO is called once per dataset outside the window loop and is similarly cache-hit after warmup.

Corrected severity: MEDIUM. The doc-vs-impl gap is real and the SBERT model IS part of the deployed footprint, but the "hot path" / "<10 ms on-device false for current weights" alarm is overstated — the cache converts the cost from per-window to per-session-cold-start, which is a materially different and much lower burden. The correct fix is to document the cache behavior accurately in config.py and either (a) correct the docstring to "the text encoder runs once per session to warm a token cache, after which it is never re-invoked" or (b) implement the stronger promised form: register_buffer'd embedding tensors serialized with the checkpoint so the SBERT model need not be present at inference at all.

### 29. [MEDIUM] [design:deployment-extensibility] No streaming/causal inference for a live device stream — the headline encoder is bidirectional and there is no session/ring-buffer runtime
- **area:** streaming/deployability
- **issue:** A live phone/watch stream needs incremental left-to-right labeling, but the headline encoder is fully bidirectional: the causal-mask / RoPE / attention-sink / stream_window machinery exists in model/transformer.py yet is gated behind config flags (use_rope, stream_causal_prob, attention_sink) that appear ONLY in SMALL_DEEP_FB_CONFIG, not in the blessed small_deep. There is no HALOStreamSession, no KV-cache/ring buffer, and no gravity-filter state object; every eval re-windows and recomputes the whole clip. So 'live watch demo / per-patch streaming labels' is not achievable with the current model.
- **evidence:** model/config.py:200-208 use_rope/stream_causal_prob/attention_sink defined only under SMALL_DEEP_FB_CONFIG; small_deep (config.py:132-176) has no such keys -> bidirectional; model/transformer.py:34-35 causal/window masks exist but are opt-in; grep StreamSession -> none.
- **fix:** Once the causal fb encoder is trained, ship HALOStreamSession holding gravity-filter state + KV cache + boundary smoothing, emitting one label per new patch (O(1)/patch), and export it as a stateful graph. Until then, document that only windowed batch inference is supported.
- **files:** model/config.py, model/transformer.py
- **verify:** CONFIRMED(repro): Every sub-claim reproduced from primary sources:

1. SMALL_DEEP_CONFIG (model/config.py lines 132-176) contains no streaming keys — no use_rope, stream_causal_prob, stream_window_sec, stream_lookahead_patches, attention_sink, or rope_min_period. Confirmed by direct file read.

2. Those keys appear exclusively in SMALL_DEEP_FB_CONFIG (model/config.py lines 200-208), which adds them on top of SMALL_DEEP_CONFIG via **SMALL_DEEP_CONFIG spread.

3. The headline checkpoint (training_output/semantic_alignment/small_deep_v2_4b3fdd6/best.pt) was probed directly via torch.load. Its hyperparameters dict has keys model_size, config, encoder, semantic_head, channel_text_fusion, label_bank, loss, training — none of the streaming keys are present. The model was trained without any causal/RoPE configuration.

4. build_temporal_mask in model/transformer.py defaults to mode='full', which returns None (fully unmasked bidirectional). The function is only called from the training script (semantic_alignment_train.py), where STREAM_CAUSAL_PROB = _cfg.get("stream_causal_prob", 0.0). Since the headline config has no stream_causal_prob, this defaults to 0.0 meaning full bidirectional attention was used throughout training.

5. grep for class .*(Session|Stream|RingBuffer|KVCache|Incremental) across all .py files returns nothing. There is no HALOStreamSession, no KV cache, no ring buffer anywhere in the codebase.

6. The encoder constructor defaults use_rope=False (encoder.py line 96), so without an explicit config key the encoder is bidirectional with sinusoidal positional embeddings.

7. Eval scripts (eval_v2.py, evaluate_tsfm_v2.py, eval_common.py) operate exclusively on pre-segmented fixed-length windows, recomputing the full clip on each call. No causal mask is ever applied at inference.

The finding's core claim is accurate in every detail: the blessed small_deep encoder is fully bidirectional, the causal/RoPE/streaming machinery exists in the codebase but is gated behind config flags that are only set in the not-yet-trained SMALL_DEEP_FB_CONFIG, and there is no session runtime for live streaming. Severity MEDIUM is appropriate — this is a deployability gap, not a correctness or integrity bug. || CONFIRMED(repro): Every factual claim verified empirically. (1) Headline ckpt small_deep_v2_4b3fdd6/best.pt hyperparameters contain NO use_rope/stream_causal_prob/stream_window_sec/attention_sink/causal keys -> trained fully bidirectional. (2) config.py: SMALL_DEEP_CONFIG (132-176) lacks all streaming keys; they appear only in SMALL_DEEP_FB_CONFIG (186-210), explicitly tagged 'Streamable encoder (Phase E)' and 'wired at the retrain cutover (M4)' i.e. future/untrained. (3) transformer.py build_temporal_mask defaults mode='full' -> None (unmasked bidirectional); TemporalSelfAttention.use_rope defaults False; training script STREAM_CAUSAL_PROB default 0.0. (4) grep for StreamSession/KV-cache/ring-buffer/gravity-filter-state runtime returns nothing. All four refutation angles fail: not handled elsewhere (no runtime), not a misread (accurately described as opt-in gated code), and the 'documented future work' angle (research_streaming_design.md, Phase E/M4) is already conceded by the finding and does not contradict the current-state claim. Two caveats that reduce but do not refute it: (a) the finding overstates by saying 'live watch demo not achievable' -- a sliding-window live demo IS achievable via per-window recompute of the bidirectional encoder (windowed batch inference, which the finding itself admits works); only per-patch O(1) causal incremental labeling is unavailable; (b) the specific proposed fix (KV-cache + stateful graph export) is likely over-engineered at 26M params / 400h, where sliding-window recompute is cheap. These affect framing/proposed-fix value, not the verified core. MEDIUM is appropriate: a genuine deployability gap for per-patch causal streaming, correctly self-scoped as future work.

### 30. [MEDIUM] [design:deployment-extensibility] Missing release artifacts (LICENSE, model card, HF weights, pip package, inference-only repo) — though training-data licensing is actually permissive, not a blocker
- **area:** release/packaging
- **issue:** There is no LICENSE file in the repo, no model card, no HuggingFace weights, no pip-installable package (README only says 'pip install -r requirements.txt'), and no inference-only distribution. Contrary to the MEMORY hint about 'capture24/UKB terms', I verified all 11 training datasets are CC BY 4.0 (capture24, dsads, hapt, hhar, kuhar, mhealth, pamap2, recgym, uci_har, unimib_shar, wisdm) — so releasing weights is legally fine *provided attribution is given*; UK-Biobank (the actual restrictive source) is only in the roadmap, not in these weights. The gap is therefore packaging/attribution, not a licensing prohibition: without a model card enumerating the 11 CC BY 4.0 sources + attribution, and without a repo LICENSE, the weights cannot be responsibly published.
- **evidence:** find LICENSE* -> none; no halo/ package, no *model_card*; per-dataset references/datasets/<ds>/citation.json all report 'CC BY 4.0'; README.md:54 only 'pip install -r requirements.txt'; MEMORY halo-data-baseline-strategy notes UKB is a *future* add.
- **fix:** Add a repo LICENSE, a model card listing the 11 training datasets with their CC BY 4.0 attributions + known failure modes (heading ambiguity, accel-only posture, rate assumption) + macro-F1 as primary metric, publish the slim fp16 checkpoint to HF, and provide a minimal inference-only install. Flag UK-Biobank as license-gated *before* it enters training.
- **files:** README.md, references/datasets/capture24/citation.json
- **verify:** CONFIRMED(repro): All core claims independently verified. (1) No LICENSE file exists in the repo root or anywhere in the project code — only found in the LIMU-BERT third-party auxiliary repo. (2) No model card file exists in the project. (3) No pip packaging files (setup.py, pyproject.toml, setup.cfg) and no halo/ installable package directory. (4) README.md line 54 confirms the only install path is `pip install -r requirements.txt`; no HuggingFace, inference-only distribution, or model card mention appears in the README. (5) All 11 training-set citation.json files report CC BY 4.0 (minor nuance: unimib_shar's dataset is "research use" per the SAL lab page, not an explicit CC BY 4.0 grant on the dataset itself — the article is CC BY 4.0 — but this does not change the conclusion that weight publication is legally feasible). (6) UK-Biobank appears only in ssl-wearables baseline documentation, not in any training data loader, config, or training script, confirming it is roadmap-only for HALO's own weights. The gap is exactly as described: missing LICENSE, missing model card with training-data attributions, no packaging, no weight distribution — a release/packaging deficit, not a licensing prohibition. || CONFIRMED(repro): All factual claims verified. No LICENSE file exists in the HALO repo (only .venv deps and the vendored auxiliary_repos/LIMU-BERT-Public/LICENSE.md); no model card exists (only package templates in .venv); no setup.py/pyproject.toml/setup.cfg (only under .venv); README.md:54 only offers `pip install -r requirements.txt`; no HF weights or inference-only distribution. Not present in the prior sweep's resolution log, so not a re-report. Dataset-license correction is accurate: all 11 training datasets' citation.json report the dataset portion as CC BY 4.0 and UK-Biobank is roadmap-only (halo-data-baseline-strategy memory), so the gap is packaging/attribution, not a legal prohibition. Best refutation attempt: the proposed fix is ALREADY a documented planned deliverable (REDESIGN_PLAN.md:110 'ship fp16 + model card'; design_application.md:78,90 canonical slim checkpoint + model card listing 11 datasets, failure modes, macro-F1 primary, license) — but this corroborates the value rather than refuting it, since the artifacts are planned, not built. Timing (repo is mid-redesign for '27 resubmission, not at release) is a priority quibble the finding already anticipates, not a contradiction. Note: unimib_shar's DATASET is 'distributed for research use from the SAL lab page' (only the article is CC BY 4.0), so the blanket 'all 11 CC BY 4.0' is slightly optimistic — which strengthens rather than weakens the case for a per-dataset attribution model card. Severity MEDIUM stands: real release/packaging gap, no functional bug, no current user blocked.

### 31. [MEDIUM] [design:encoder-streaming] Late fusion destroys within-patch cross-axis phase (channel-independence bottleneck)
- **area:** encoder-architecture / cross-channel
- **issue:** Every tokenizer collapses each channel's whole patch to a single vector with phase discarded BEFORE any cross-channel operator runs, so inter-axis relative timing/phase (gait limb coordination, orientation dynamics, accel-gyro coupling) is unrecoverable. The dual-branch cross-channel attention then mixes only these per-patch magnitude summaries. For zero-shot HAR, cross-axis phase is highly discriminative, and no path in encoder.py/transformer.py can recover it.
- **evidence:** feature_extractor.py:296-297 AdaptiveAvgPool1d(1) averages the entire ~1s patch to one vector; :331 rfft(...).abs() discards phase; PhysicalFilterbank._band_energy keeps power only ('drop phase', :610-611). Cross-channel attention runs on already-pooled (B*P, C, d) tokens (transformer.py:534-548). Measured: the entire tokenizer is only 0.06M params vs 4.73M spent on cross-channel attention across 8 layers — the model invests heavily in mixing summaries it cannot un-blur.
- **fix:** Add cross-axis mixing BEFORE the temporal collapse: e.g. a lightweight grouped conv or a few sub-patch tokens per channel with joint space-time attention, or feed cross-axis coherence/cross-spectrum features (relative phase between axis pairs) into the tokenizer. Expected: recovers the orientation/coordination cues that separate confusable activities (walk vs stairs, sit vs stand transitions).
- **files:** /home/alex/code/HALO/code/model/feature_extractor.py, /home/alex/code/HALO/code/model/transformer.py, /home/alex/code/HALO/code/model/encoder.py
- **verify:** CONFIRMED(repro): Independently reproduced the core structural claim. Line evidence verified: feature_extractor.py:296-297 (AdaptiveAvgPool1d(1) collapses each channel's patch to one vector), :331 (rfft().abs() drops phase, spectral branch), :609-611 (filterbank keeps power only, 'drop phase'). Data-flow ordering confirmed in encoder.py:280-298 -> :379: tokenization runs strictly before the transformer's cross-channel attention (transformer.py:534-548), which operates on already-pooled (B*P,C,d) tokens. Parameter counts recomputed and match: SpectralTemporal tokenizer=0.055M (~0.06M), CrossChannelSelfAttention x8=exactly 4,730,880=4.73M. Controlled tests: (1) channel-independence is strict — scrambling ch0 changes other channels' tokens by exactly 0.0 in both tokenizers; (2) for identical per-channel magnitude spectra at 0 vs 90 deg relative phase, the filterbank token diff is 1.1e-3 vs magnitude 0.58 (numerical noise) confirming it is completely phase-blind. HOWEVER the finding overstates the case for the actual trained headline model (spectral_temporal): its temporal CNN branch (75% of d_model) is strongly phase-sensitive (token diff 1.47 vs magnitude 0.78 for the same 0-vs-90-deg pair), so per-channel tokens DO carry within-channel phase and cross-channel attention can in principle compare them to recover relative phase — contradicting the blanket 'unrecoverable' wording. The blanket claim is fully true only for the filterbank streaming path. Downgraded HIGH->MEDIUM because (a) the trained model partially mitigates phase loss via the temporal CNN, and (b) the claimed accuracy cost on confusable activities is asserted, not demonstrated by any ablation.
