The architectural bottleneck (no explicit cross-axis phase/coherence inductive bias; fine temporal detail lost to pooling; fully phase-blind filterbank) is real and the proposed fix is reasonable. || CONFIRMED(repro): The architectural claim is verified in the active headline path (spectral_temporal / SpectralTemporalExtractor). Within each channel the whole ~1s patch is collapsed to one vector before any cross-channel operator: temporal branch uses AdaptiveAvgPool1d(1) (feature_extractor.py:296, applied :327) discarding sub-patch timing; spectral branch uses rfft(...).abs() (:331) discarding phase. Cross-channel attention (transformer.py:534-548) then mixes only these phase-free per-channel summaries at (B*P,C,d). Patches are non-overlapping (stride=patch_size, PATCH_SIZE_SEC=1.0), and temporal attention is per-channel across patches, so no path in encoder/transformer can recover within-patch cross-axis relative phase. I searched the repo for coherence/cross-spectrum/relative-phase handling and found none in the active path. Evidence largely checks out: the tokenizer is 0.055M params (finding's 0.06M is correct). One error: the finding attributes 4.73M to cross-channel attention, but 4.73M is temporal_attn; cross_channel_attn is actually 7.10M — so the summary-mixing-vs-tokenizer imbalance is even larger than claimed, not smaller. The PhysicalFilterbank:610-611 citation is for the unused small_deep_fb tokenizer, but the same phase drop holds in the active one, so it is redundant not disqualifying. Best refutations fail: numbers are essentially right, no recovery path exists, not dead code. Severity lowered HIGH->MEDIUM because the performance impact is asserted, not measured (no ablation shows accuracy loss; static-posture orientation survives as accel DC/FFT-bin-0), and the proposed fix, while cheap and plausible at 400h/26M scale, has undemonstrated benefit. The limitation is real and verified; its consequence for results is unquantified.

### 32. [MEDIUM] [design:objective] Purely contrastive-to-label objective with no auxiliary self-supervised (masked-reconstruction / predictive) signal — 26M transformer trained from scratch leaves capture24-scale unlabeled structure and low-level motion modeling unused
- **area:** missing-objective
- **issue:** The entire training signal is a single InfoNCE term aligning per-patch IMU embeddings to text-label embeddings; there is no masked-patch reconstruction, forecasting, or JEPA-style predictive auxiliary anywhere (grep for reconstruct/mlm/masked-modeling/denoise/jepa across training + model returns only unrelated hits). The legacy Stage-1 MAE is removed (PRETRAINED_ENCODER_PATH=None) so the encoder learns all of its low-level motion structure purely from noisy activity labels. This couples representation quality to label quality/coverage, gives the model no incentive to model intra-patch dynamics, and wastes the one genuinely large, diverse corpus (capture24, ~48.5k windows of free-living data) whose value is mostly self-supervised, not label-supervised.
- **evidence:** Only InfoNCE/SemanticAlignmentLoss is constructed and called (semantic_alignment_train.py:2170, standard loop 989-995); no second loss term is summed except the (inactive) distillation at 1009. Zero-shot macro-F1 is very low (21.0 per memory audit) despite accuracy ~42, the signature of representations that separate coarse clusters but not fine/confusable motion — consistent with label-only supervision. capture24 dominates the corpus by window count (48,518 sessions) yet contributes only label-contrastive gradient.
- **fix:** Add a lightweight masked-patch reconstruction (or contrastive predictive / JEPA) head co-trained with the InfoNCE term on the raw tokenizer output, weighted modestly. This is the standard recipe for IMU/TS foundation models and decouples encoder representation learning from label noise; expected benefit is higher zero-shot macro-F1 and better few-shot data efficiency, at the cost of one extra decoder head and a loss-weight to tune.
- **files:** training_scripts/human_activity_recognition/semantic_loss.py, training_scripts/human_activity_recognition/semantic_alignment_train.py
- **verify:** CONFIRMED(repro): Independently reproduced all core claims. (1) The sole constructed loss is SemanticAlignmentLoss (semantic_alignment_train.py:2169), which wraps InfoNCELoss/SigLIPLoss; the headline checkpoint's own hyperparameters record loss_type='infonce'. The only other additive term (loss + DISTILL_WEIGHT*_distill, line 1009) is a full-context->causal teacher-student consistency distillation gated on is_filterbank AND STREAM_CAUSAL_PROB>0; the headline model uses feature_extractor_type='spectral_temporal' (not filterbank) and STREAM_CAUSAL_PROB defaults to 0.0, so it never fires, and it distills label-derived per-patch embeddings, not a masked/predictive SSL signal. (2) No auxiliary SSL: __init__.py states the legacy Stage-1 MAE (masked reconstruction + patch contrastive) was removed in the V2 cleanup; grep for mae/masked/jepa/recon/mlm/denoise yields only dead infrastructure. encoder.mask_token exists and the encoder forward handles mae_mask/channel_dropout_mask, but NO training or model forward call ever passes those arguments (empty grep) - a dead parameter, reinforcing the claim. (3) PRETRAINED_ENCODER_PATH=None (line 256): encoder trains from scratch. (4) capture24 has exactly 48,518 session dirs, matching the ~48.5k claim; it is the largest corpus and contributes only label-contrastive gradient. (5) Stored eval JSONs show the accuracy>>macro-F1 signature (mobiact 54.7 acc/20.7 macroF1; harth 47.6/20.5; inclusivehar 30.0/20.5), consistent with label-only supervision separating coarse but not fine motion. Minor imprecision: finding says 26M params vs checkpoint's 35.4M total (trainable-encoder vs total-with-frozen-SBERT counting) - does not affect the objective claim. || CONFIRMED(repro): All attempts to refute the finding fail against the concrete evidence in the checkpoint and training code.

WHAT THE HEADLINE CHECKPOINT ACTUALLY USES

The saved hyperparameters in training_output/semantic_alignment/small_deep_v2_4b3fdd6/best.pt confirm: loss.loss_type="infonce", per_patch_prediction=True, feature_extractor_type="spectral_temporal", no stream_causal_prob or distill_weight fields saved. The only criterion constructed and called is SemanticAlignmentLoss, which wraps InfoNCELoss exclusively (semantic_alignment_train.py line 2172, semantic_loss.py lines 475-476). There is no second loss term added to `loss` for this model.

WHY THE DISTILLATION BRANCH DOES NOT HELP HERE

There is a dual-forward distillation mechanism in the codebase (_distillation_loss, lines 1001-1009) that constitutes a predictive self-supervised auxiliary. The finding's grep claim that "zero unrelated hits" is therefore slightly overstated. However, this branch is triple-gated: it fires only when PER_PATCH_PREDICTION=True AND encoder.is_filterbank=True AND STREAM_CAUSAL_PROB > 0. The headline encoder sets is_filterbank = (feature_extractor_type == 'physical_filterbank') (encoder.py line 141), and the headline checkpoint uses spectral_temporal, so is_filterbank=False. STREAM_CAUSAL_PROB defaults to 0.0 (line 219) and was not set for this run. Both gates are closed. The distillation path was designed for the future physical filterbank tokenizer, not the current production model.

WHY THE PRETRAINED ENCODER ARGUMENT FAILS

PRETRAINED_ENCODER_PATH=None is hardcoded with the comment "d=768 != d=384 checkpoint," and the training/__init__.py explicitly states: "The legacy Stage-1 self-supervised pretraining (masked reconstruction + patch contrastive) was removed in the V2 cleanup — the headline model trains the encoder from scratch during alignment." There is no compensating prior SSL stage.

ON VALUE OF THE PROPOSED FIX

The proposed masked-patch reconstruction head is not cargo-culting. Capture24 supplies 48,518 windows of free-living data whose label diversity is limited but whose raw motion diversity is high; at 400h/26M parameters, the encoder has significant capacity that contrastive supervision on ~30 coarse activity labels cannot fill. The zero-shot macro-F1=21.0 vs accuracy~42% gap directly reflects the known failure mode of label-only contrastive training: coarse cluster separation without fine discriminability. The maintainers' own redesign plan (per memory notes) independently converges on adding a "dense per-patch segment+stream objective," confirming the direction is both recognized and valued at project scale. The cost — one lightweight decoder head and a loss weight to tune — is minimal relative to the expected gain in representation quality and few-shot data efficiency.

SEVERITY ASSESSMENT

MEDIUM is correct. This is a design limitation in the objective function, not a correctness bug or data integrity issue. The model does produce useful representations; the gap is in how fully it exploits the available unlabeled structure.

### 33. [MEDIUM] [design:text-alignment] Zero-shot recognition uses one raw (underscored) label string per class: no de-underscoring, no prompt/synonym ensembling
- **area:** eval-open-vocab
- **issue:** Open-vocab robustness is a core deliverable, but eval feeds each class's single verbatim label string (with underscores, e.g. 'walking_downstairs') straight to the text encoder. Underscored forms are only ~0.86-0.90 cosine to their natural phrasing, and the learnable bank was trained on de-underscored synonym-augmented text, so there is also a train/eval text distribution mismatch. Unlike CLIP zero-shot (which averages many prompt templates for robustness), HALO commits to one bare string, maximizing sensitivity to phrasing.
- **evidence:** evaluate_tsfm_v2.py:82 labels_ld=cfg['labels']; :118/:206 label_bank.encode(labels_ld) with no .replace('_',' '); grep for de-underscore/replace in evaluate_tsfm_v2.py and eval_common.py is empty. motionsense.json labels contain 'walking_downstairs' and state 'no rephrasing at evaluation time'. data_diversity_audit.md:138 confirms only the ConSE baseline de-underscores (eval_v2.py:418).
- **fix:** De-underscore/normalize eval labels before encoding, and add prompt/synonym ensembling (encode a few short content-dominant templates per class and average, CLIP-style) so recognition is robust to the exact class-name phrasing that varies across held-out datasets.
- **files:** val_scripts/human_activity_recognition/evaluate_tsfm_v2.py, benchmark_data/eval_v2/labels/motionsense.json
- **verify:** CONFIRMED(repro): All core claims independently verified from primary sources. (1) evaluate_tsfm_v2.py:118 calls label_bank.encode(labels_ld, normalize=True) where labels_ld=cfg["labels"] with zero de-underscoring anywhere in the chain — confirmed by reading the file and grepping both evaluator files for any replace/_-space transformation. (2) model/token_text_encoder.py:508 passes label_texts directly to self.text_encoder.encode() with no internal normalization. (3) motionsense.json labels contain 'walking_downstairs' / 'walking_upstairs' verbatim; its source note explicitly says "no rephrasing at evaluation time." (4) Cosine similarity measured independently via all-MiniLM-L6-v2: underscored vs. de-underscored forms score 0.868–0.912 (mean 0.895), confirming the ~0.86–0.90 claim. (5) Train/eval mismatch confirmed: label_augmentation.py:476 (fallback) and :489 (no-synonym branch) both apply label.replace("_", " ") so training never passes raw underscored strings to SentenceBERT, while eval always does for multi-word labels. (6) eval_v2.py:415 confirms only the ConSE bridge de-underscores (texts = [l.replace("_", " ") for l in labels]); HALO's own ZS path has no equivalent. Prior sweep MODELS-7 (PARTIAL) already documents the evaluate_tsfm_v2.py:118/206 locus; the resolution note "eval de-underscores labels" refers only to ConSE, and "Symmetric HALO-side normalization finalized at build" means the HALO-side fix was deferred — the code is unchanged. Severity MEDIUM is appropriate: every underscored class name enters similarity scoring at ~10% cosine deficit, directly suppressing ZS F1 on datasets with compound labels. No reason to escalate to HIGH since the leaked gap (~0.10 cosine headroom) is a systematic but bounded degradation, not a correctness collapse. || CONFIRMED(repro): Core claim verified empirically. evaluate_tsfm_v2.py:118 (ZS) and :206 (FS) both call label_bank.encode(labels_ld) with labels_ld=cfg['labels'] verbatim; no de-underscoring anywhere in the HALO path. LearnableLabelBank.encode (token_text_encoder.py:496) forwards straight to text_encoder.encode + pooling with no normalization, so the raw string (e.g. 'walking_downstairs') reaches the SBERT tokenizer as-is. The only de-underscore in eval is eval_v2.py:415, which is the ConSE bridge path, NOT the HALO cosine path. Training text is naturalized (label_augmentation.py:476/489 de-underscore fallback; configured datasets substitute natural synonyms), so a genuine train/eval text mismatch exists for the underscored classes. motionsense.json holds underscored labels and states 'no rephrasing at evaluation time'. Repo-wide search found no compensating mechanism, so the best refutation fails. CAVEATS lowering my emphasis: (1) This substantially duplicates already-logged MODELS-7 (status PARTIAL — 'Symmetric HALO-side normalization ... finalized at build' is explicitly deferred) and the training-side DATA-15 (FIXED). It is a known, tracked, still-unfixed gap, not a new bug or a regression. (2) Impact is bounded: only multi-word underscored classes are affected (motionsense: 2 of 6 labels), single-word labels like jogging/sitting/standing/walking are untouched. (3) The proposed fix splits: de-underscoring is a solid, near-zero-cost fix for a real mismatch, but the CLIP-style prompt/synonym ensembling half is weaker at 400h/26M with a learnable attention bank (and optional multi-prototype) already trained on the synonym distribution — averaging arbitrary eval templates risks leaving the learned manifold and is closer to web-scale cargo-culting than a justified change here. Severity MEDIUM retained: real train/eval mismatch on a core open-vocab deliverable, but scoped to a subset of classes and already tracked.

### 34. [MEDIUM] [design:text-alignment] Text side aligns to bare labels+synonyms, not LLM-generated descriptions - the field-standard fix for label collapse, and bigger encoder is a proven wash
- **area:** text-encoder
- **issue:** On whether frozen MiniLM is the right text encoder: keeping it frozen is defensible (mpnet-768 upgrade was a statistical wash and unfreezing a HAR-tiny text tower risks overfitting), so the lever is NOT a bigger/unfrozen encoder. But aligning to bare class names + synonym templates is the specific choice that leaves fine axes (up/down, ascend/descend, transitions) degenerate. Every comparable system (UniMTS, LanHAR, GOAT, IMU2CLIP, Ghosh'26) aligns to LLM-generated rich activity/placement descriptions instead; HALO injects none of that discriminative physics content.
- **evidence:** config keeps sentence_bert_model=all-MiniLM-L6-v2; label augmentation is synonyms/templates only (no physics content). research_embedding_collapse.md 4/5.2.8 documents mpnet wash (up/down 0.868->0.909) and that the discriminative direction is genuinely absent from bare labels, curable only by adding real content words. Probe: 'someone is walking' cut confusable cosine 0.483->0.444 vs verbose boilerplate 0.801.
- **fix:** Generate short, content-dominant LLM physics-descriptions offline for labels AND channels (e.g. 'walking upstairs: ascending steps, lifting the body against gravity' vs 'descending, controlled lowering'); keep phrases short. This adds tokens the frozen encoder CAN separate, unlike synonym reshuffling of the same tokens.
- **files:** datasets/imu_pretraining_dataset/label_augmentation.py, docs/v2/research_embedding_collapse.md
- **verify:** CONFIRMED(repro): Independently reproduced every core claim from primary sources. (1) Frozen text encoder: config.py:154 sets sentence_bert_model=all-MiniLM-L6-v2 for the headline small_deep block (with an untried mpnet TODO). (2) Text side aligns to bare labels+synonyms only: read all 588 lines of label_augmentation.py; augment_label does synonym-swap + template-wrap ('{}','person {}','{} activity' etc.) with zero physics/content tokens (no gravity/ascend/descend/against/controlled anywhere). (3) Fine-axis label collapse reproduced on MiniLM: walking-up vs walking-down 0.868, ascend vs descend 0.930, sit-to-stand vs stand-to-sit 0.966, turn-left vs turn-right 0.875, while coarse pairs separate (walk/run 0.495, sit/stand 0.425). (4) mpnet is a wash: up/down 0.868->0.909 matches the doc exactly and mpnet is worse-or-equal on all antonym axes, so a bigger/unfrozen encoder is NOT the lever. (5) The short-phrase probe reproduced near-exactly with the pair 'walking' vs 'climbing stairs': bare 0.482 (doc 0.483), 'someone is walking' 0.440 (doc 0.444), verbose boilerplate inflates confusable cosine to ~0.73-0.80 (doc's 0.801 reproduces on 'walking vs standing' verbose). Honest caveat that does not refute: on the actually-deployed frozen MiniLM, physics descriptions only modestly lower the collapse (up/down 0.868->0.836; mpnet larger 0.909->0.732), matching the doc's own 'partially recoverable' framing. This is a genuine, field-standard design gap (UniMTS/IMU2CLIP/LanHAR/GOAT all align to LLM-generated descriptions), not a correctness/results-integrity bug -> MEDIUM is correct. Not a re-report: this is a design-audit item, absent from the 51-item sweep resolution log. || CONFIRMED(repro): Code path is live and correctly read: label_augmentation.py contains only synonyms + templates; augment_label() is invoked by multi_dataset_loader.py:31, and eval aligns IMU embeddings to raw label strings (evaluate_tsfm_v2.py:118/206). A repo-wide grep found NO LLM-generation or physics-description mechanism in training/model/datasets, so there is no compensating path. Channel descriptions carry sensor/placement physics but are injected at signal-tokenization level, not as the contrastive label target. The comparable-systems claim and the frozen-MiniLM/mpnet-wash claim are both independently validated by the team's own research_embedding_collapse.md, which also documents the collapse and lists this exact fix (§5.2.8/§5.4). Strongest refutation: the synonym lists DO contain directional content words (ascending 13, descending 14, climbing 19, lifting/lowering/controlled), so 'injects none of that discriminative physics content' is a slight overstatement for the up/down axis; but the doc's own probe shows up/down cosine stays at 0.87-0.93 despite those words, so they do not rescue the collapse and the conclusion holds. Severity stays MEDIUM: it is a real, well-evidenced design gap, but already self-documented and classified by the team as a research bet requiring ablation (not a guaranteed win) rather than a shipped bug.

### 35. [MEDIUM] [design:tokenizer] Legacy headline path (small_deep) caps zero-shot: per-patch z-score kills amplitude+gravity; linear interpolate-to-64 aliases and makes the FFT rate-inconsistent
- **area:** legacy-path/headline
- **issue:** The headline checkpoint uses feature_extractor_type=spectral_temporal with normalization_method=zscore and linear interpolation to 64. Per-patch z-score removes both amplitude (running vs walking collapse - the design doc's Bottleneck 3) and DC/gravity. Interpolation to 64 is linear with no anti-alias filter (aliases high-freq content). Critically, the spectral branch runs rfft over the interpolated 64 samples (fft_size falls back to target_patch_size=64), so bin m maps to m/D Hz where D varies 1.0-2.0 s across datasets - the same MLP input index means different physical frequencies per dataset, so the spectral features are neither rate- nor duration-invariant and force per-dataset memorization, directly hurting zero-shot transfer to unseen rates.
- **evidence:** config small_deep: feature_extractor_type=spectral_temporal, normalization_method=zscore, interpolation_method=linear, target_patch_size=64 (model/config.py:143-146). Interp is linear/no-AA: model/preprocessing.py:106-115. z-score: preprocessing.py:189-198. SpectralTemporalExtractor built with no fft_size -> fallback to 64 (feature_extractor.py:273; encoder.py:159-168); FFT over interpolated window at :331 -> bin m = m/D Hz.
- **fix:** Cut over the headline model to the filterbank tokenizer (already built) so results no longer rest on aliased, amplitude-destroyed, rate-inconsistent features; if the legacy path must stay, at minimum define fft over native (non-interpolated) samples with an explicit physical-Hz mapping and stop z-scoring away amplitude/gravity. Expected benefit: removes a per-dataset spectral-scale confound that inflates train-set fit but caps zero-shot macro-F1.
- **files:** model/feature_extractor.py, model/preprocessing.py, model/config.py
- **verify:** CONFIRMED(repro): All three sub-claims independently reproduced from primary sources. (1) Per-patch z-score: normalize_patches (preprocessing.py:189-198) subtracts per-patch/per-channel mean and divides by std. Empirically the mean captures gravity DC (9.80 for both walk/run, subtracted to ~0) and std captures amplitude (0.70 vs 2.80, a 4x intensity gap, normalized to std=1.0); same-frequency signals differing only in DC+amplitude collapse to L2 diff 7e-6 — walk vs run made indistinguishable (design-doc Bottleneck 3). (2) Linear interpolation aliases: interpolate_patches (preprocessing.py:106-115) uses F.interpolate(mode='linear') with no anti-alias filter; a 40 Hz tone at 100 Hz native, downsampled to 64 samples over 1.5 s (Nyquist 21.3 Hz), folds to a 2.7 Hz peak. (3) Rate-inconsistent FFT: preprocess interpolates to 64 first (preprocessing.py:274), SpectralTemporalExtractor is built with no fft_size so it falls back to target_patch_size=64 (encoder.py:159-168, feature_extractor.py:273), and rfft(n=64) at feature_extractor.py:331 makes bin k map to k/D Hz. Verified patch duration D spans 1.0-2.0 s (2x) across train datasets (PATCH_SIZE_PER_DATASET in semantic_alignment_train.py:135-157), so the same 33-bin MLP input index means different physical frequencies per dataset. Headline checkpoint hyperparameters confirm feature_extractor_type=spectral_temporal, zscore, linear, target_patch_size=64. Severity held at MEDIUM (not HIGH): it is an architectural limitation with plausible-but-unmeasured zero-shot impact, and the 6 zero-shot sets are all 1.5 s so their bin mapping coincidentally matches the dominant training bucket, softening the rate-inconsistency and aliasing harm at eval time; not a correctness or results-integrity bug. || CONFIRMED(repro): All mechanical claims verified on the ACTIVE headline path (not dead code). (1) fft_size falls back to target_patch_size=64: SpectralTemporalExtractor is constructed without fft_size (encoder.py:159-168) and defaults to target_patch_size (feature_extractor.py:273). (2) The spectral rfft runs on the already linearly-interpolated 64-sample window: torch.fft.rfft(x.squeeze(1), n=64) at feature_extractor.py:331. (3) Interpolation is linear with no anti-alias filter (preprocessing.py:113-115); this genuinely aliases the downsampled high-rate sets (kuhar/capture24 @100Hz -> effective 64/1.5=42.7Hz < native Nyquist). (4) Per-patch/per-channel z-score subtracts the mean (DC/gravity) and divides by std (amplitude): preprocessing.py:189-198. (5) patch_size_sec (D) truly varies 1.0s (uci_har/hhar) to 2.0s (pamap2/dsads) in PATCH_SIZE_PER_DATASET (config:135-157); since the 64-sample window spans duration D, FFT bin m maps to m/D Hz, so the same MLP input index encodes a different physical frequency per dataset. My best refutations reduce but do not overturn the finding: the 25% spectral_ratio + LayerNorm (feature_extractor.py:309-315) and patch-size augmentation (config:408-425) MITIGATE, not eliminate, the confound. Two overstatements to note for the record: (a) the finding's "neither rate- nor duration-invariant" is half wrong -- interpolating to a fixed 64 samples per fixed D actually MAKES the spectral branch rate-invariant at equal D; only duration-invariance is violated; (b) augmentation jitters D per-sample, which undercuts the literal "forces per-dataset memorization" wording. Crucially, the headline impact ("caps zero-shot macro-F1") is an unproven causal hypothesis (design-doc Bottleneck 3) with no A/B evidence at 400h/26M scale, and the proposed fix (filterbank) already exists as SMALL_DEEP_FB_CONFIG and is the already-planned M1 redesign. Because no concrete contradicting evidence overturns the verified defects on the live path, this is CONFIRMED; MEDIUM stands given the real-but-bounded, unmeasured impact.

### 36. [MEDIUM] [pipeline:augmentations] channel_text_phrase paraphrase injects "linear acceleration" onto gravity-PRESENT acc channels, flipping the gravity semantics its own docstring/detector rely on
- **area:** text co-variation / augmentation correctness
- **issue:** The channel-description paraphrase substitution `(r"\bacceleration\b", ["acceleration", "linear acceleration"])` (augmentations.py:701) rewrites gravity-PRESENT accelerometer descriptions to say 'linear acceleration'. In this codebase 'linear acceleration' is a load-bearing gravity-REMOVED marker: `_gravity_present` (augmentations.py:824-825) returns False (== gravity removed) whenever the text contains 'linear acceleration'/'user acceleration'. So the paraphrase manufactures channel text that (a) contradicts the actual gravity-bearing signal, (b) is internally self-contradictory when the base desc keeps '... including gravity' (pamap2), and (c) directly violates the ChannelTextPhraseCfg docstring's explicit guarantee that 'units, and gravity state are left verbatim, so the load-bearing semantics are provably preserved' (augmentations.py:681-682). This corrupts the frozen-SBERT channel-text conditioning target — the model's core language-alignment signal — for a large fraction of training samples, teaching it that gravity-present and gravity-removed acc share the same text.
- **evidence:** augmentations.py:701 substitution; :824-825 `_gravity_present` treats 'linear acceleration' as gravity-removed (verified: `_gravity_present(ones, ['linear acceleration x-axis (m/s^2 including gravity)'])`==False); :681-682 false 'provably preserved' claim; :1079-1083 `_channel_text_phrase` applies it. Standalone on pamap2 gravity-present desc 'Acceleration x-axis (... m/s^2 including gravity)...': 'linear acceleration' injected in 997/2000 (50%) paraphrases, ALL 997 self-contradictory ('linear acceleration'+'including gravity'). Chained via `\baccelerometer\b`->'acceleration sensor'->'linear acceleration': hhar 'Accelerometer X-axis' 16%, mhealth 'Chest acceleration ...' 48%. END-TO-END through the real train loader (multi_dataset_loader.py:523-535 augment then :591 build descriptions) with default_v2 on pamap2: 743 contradictory acc channel descriptions across 150 sampled train windows reached metadata['channel_descriptions']. Training default is preset 'v2'=default_v2 with channel_text_phrase enabled p=0.5 (semantic_alignment_train.py:382-389).
- **fix:** Remove 'linear acceleration' from the `\bacceleration\b` synonym list in `_CH_SYNONYMS` (and 'acceleration sensor' from `\baccelerometer\b`, which chains into it) — these are NOT meaning-preserving surface forms, they encode gravity state. Alternatively skip the acceleration/accelerometer substitutions entirely, or gate them so 'linear'/'acceleration sensor' can never be introduced when the description lacks a gravity-removed marker. Then the docstring's 'gravity state left verbatim' claim actually holds.
- **files:** /home/alex/code/HALO/code/datasets/imu_pretraining_dataset/augmentations.py
- **verify:** CONFIRMED(repro): All core claims independently reproduced from primary source augmentations.py (/home/alex/code/HALO/code/datasets/imu_pretraining_dataset/augmentations.py).

VERIFIED FROM SOURCE:

Line 701 — _CH_SYNONYMS entry exists exactly as claimed: (r"\bacceleration\b", ["acceleration", "linear acceleration"]). The comment at line 697-698 even calls these "meaning-preserving substitutions" — which the finding correctly identifies as false for the gravity-semantics axis.

Lines 681-682 — ChannelTextPhraseCfg docstring states "Placement, units, and gravity state are left verbatim, so the load-bearing semantics are provably preserved." This claim is directly contradicted by the synonym table.

Lines 822-826 — _gravity_present returns False when any of ("gravity removed", "gravity-removed", "user acceleration", "useracceleration", "linear acceleration") appear in the description text. Verified empirically: _gravity_present_text(["linear acceleration x-axis (wrist, m/s^2 including gravity)"]) == False; _gravity_present_text(["Acceleration x-axis (wrist, m/s^2 including gravity)"]) == True.

Lines 1079-1082 — _channel_text_phrase calls _paraphrase_channel on every channel description with no guard on gravity state.

EMPIRICAL REPRODUCTION (N=2000, seed=0):
- pamap2 gravity-present desc "Acceleration x-axis (wrist, m/s^2 including gravity)": linear acceleration injected in 1010/2000 (50.5%), all 1010 self-contradictory ("linear acceleration" + "including gravity"). Finding claimed 997/2000 (50%) — difference is RNG seed only; rate confirmed.
- hhar "Accelerometer X-axis": 333/2000 (16.6%) via chain accelerometer -> "acceleration sensor" (1/3 prob) -> "linear acceleration" (1/2 prob). Finding claimed ~16% — confirmed.
- mhealth "Chest acceleration - x-axis (m/s^2 including gravity)": 1010/2000 (50.5%). Finding claimed ~48% — confirmed within RNG variation.

ORDERING NOTE: AugmentationConfig.ORDER places channel_text_phrase after gravity (line 753-755). This means gravity augmentation fires on original descriptions, then channel_text_phrase may paraphrase the result. The problematic case is when gravity aug does NOT fire (p=0.5) and channel_text_phrase does fire (p=0.5): roughly 12.5% of gravity-present acc channel descriptions in pamap2/mhealth reach the frozen SBERT encoder per training step with self-contradictory text. This does not reduce severity below MEDIUM — it is systematic and affects the core CLIP alignment target.

NOT ALREADY IN SWEEP LOG: The adversarial_sweep_findings.md resolution log (DATA-1 through DATA-26, MODELS-1 through MODELS-23, EVAL-1/EVAL-2) contains no entry addressing _CH_SYNONYMS channel_text_phrase "linear acceleration" injection onto gravity-PRESENT descriptions. This is a new finding.

SEVERITY: MEDIUM confirmed. Corruption is systematic but subsets to ~12.5% of gravity-present acc channel descriptions per step under default_v2. Not HIGH because the model still trains and can learn from the majority of unparaphrased or correctly-paraphrased samples; not LOW because it's a direct, systematic corruption of the codebase's stated core invariant ("gravity state left verbatim") and the CLIP text conditioning target for a significant fraction of the largest train sets (pamap2, mhealth, hhar). || CONFIRMED(repro): All verifiable claims reproduced. (1) _CH_SYNONYMS[701] maps \bacceleration\b -> ["acceleration","linear acceleration"] and \baccelerometer\b[700] -> "acceleration sensor", which chains into it. On real base descriptions: pamap2 gravity-present 'Acceleration x-axis (m/s^2 including gravity)' gets 'linear acceleration' in ~50% of paraphrases (all self-contradictory with the surviving 'including gravity' clause); mhealth 'Chest acceleration' ~50%; hhar 'Accelerometer X-axis' ~16-18% via the chain. (2) _gravity_present[824-826] checks the 'linear acceleration' False-keyword set BEFORE the 'including gravity' True-keyword set, so contradictory text returns False (gravity-removed) even on a gravity-bearing signal (verified: _gravity_present(ones,['linear acceleration x-axis (m/s^2 including gravity)'])==False). (3) The ChannelTextPhraseCfg docstring[681-682] 'gravity state left verbatim ... provably preserved' is provably false. (4) End-to-end through multi_dataset_loader (aug at 523-535 -> channel_descriptions built at 585/591 -> metadata['channel_descriptions'] at 608/635), default preset v2=default_v2 has channel_text_phrase enabled p=0.5, so corrupted descriptions reach frozen SBERT with no downstream sanitization (checked model/positional_encoding.py encode_channel_descriptions and token_text_encoder). Refutation attempts all failed: the gravity aug (ORDER before text) only marks '(gravity removed)' when it fires at p=0.5, so it does not compensate on the ~50% of samples where it does not fire; the pipeline's own _gravity_present calls run before the text aug so they are uncorrupted, but that does not protect the SBERT conditioning target which is the finding's actual claim; SBERT is NOT insensitive to the phrase (cos 0.94/0.74/0.96 for the three base descs; the induced shift ~0.06 is on the same order as the real gravity-present<->gravity-removed distinction ~0.08); no resolution-log row covers channel_text_phrase, and the nearest one (DATA-8) reinforces that 'linear acceleration' is a deliberate gravity-removed marker. Kept at MEDIUM (not raised): it corrupts a conditioning target (not eval/leakage/crash), is bounded in frequency (text-aug p=0.5 x gravity-present-acc subset x ~50% injection), and SBERT keeps the corrupted string closer to gravity-present (0.94) than gravity-removed (0.85), so the finding's 'teaches the two states share the same text' framing is a moderate perturbation rather than a full state collapse.

### 37. [MEDIUM] [pipeline:augmentations] jitter uses a fixed absolute sigma=0.05 on raw, pre-normalization signals whose units span g to m/s^2 — effective augmentation SNR varies ~150x across the corpus
- **area:** units-awareness / augmentation fairness
- **issue:** `_jitter` adds Gaussian noise `s.data + randn_like(s.data)*spec.sigma` with a fixed sigma=0.05 (augmentations.py:913, JitterCfg default :582), applied to the RAW session in the loader (multi_dataset_loader.py:523-532) BEFORE per-patch z-score normalization (model/preprocessing.py:191-198). Because different train datasets are stored in different units (g ~1.0 for uci_har/kuhar body_acc vs m/s^2 ~10 for pamap2/hhar/dsads/mhealth) and different motion amplitudes, a single absolute sigma means wildly different augmentation strengths per dataset, and for low-amplitude (static-posture / gravity-removed / g-unit) windows the injected noise exceeds the signal, so the subsequent per-patch z-score returns a near-noise input. Augmentation strength is thus dataset-dependent (a fairness/units-awareness violation) rather than a consistent perturbation.
- **evidence:** Measured median per-channel acc std over real sessions and the ratio jitter/std=0.05/std: uci_har standing 0.0165 -> 3.04 (noise >> signal), hhar stand 0.0361 -> 1.38, mhealth sitting 0.128 -> 0.39, pamap2 standing 0.198 -> 0.25, kuhar sitting 0.89 -> 0.06, wisdm sitting 2.80 -> 0.02. That is a ~150x spread in effective augmentation SNR. jitter is enabled (p=0.5) in the training default default_v2. Contrast: `_scale` is multiplicative so unit-invariant; only additive `_jitter` has this defect.
- **fix:** Make the noise relative to the per-channel signal scale (e.g. sigma * per-channel std of the window) or apply jitter AFTER per-patch normalization so sigma=0.05 is a consistent ~5% perturbation across all datasets/units, as in TS-TCC (which jitters unit-variance normalized signals).
- **files:** /home/alex/code/HALO/code/datasets/imu_pretraining_dataset/augmentations.py, /home/alex/code/HALO/code/datasets/imu_pretraining_dataset/multi_dataset_loader.py
- **verify:** CONFIRMED(repro): Independently reproduced the core claim from primary sources. CODE: JitterCfg default is enabled=True/p=0.5/sigma=0.05 (augmentations.py:580-582); _jitter does s.data + randn_like(s.data)*spec.sigma, a fixed ABSOLUTE additive noise (line 913); augmentation runs on raw timestep data at multi_dataset_loader.py:523-532 BEFORE patching (line 541+), and per-patch z-score normalization happens afterward (preprocessing.py:191-198). Jitter is active in default_v2 (inherited default, not disabled). _scale is multiplicative (line 918-919) hence unit-invariant — only additive _jitter has this defect, as claimed. Not previously logged in the adversarial sweep. DATA (my own script over real static-posture sessions, per-channel acc std, sigma=0.05): uci_har standing std=0.0059 -> sigma/std=8.45 (noise >> signal, WORSE than finding's 3.04); hhar stand 0.047 -> 1.06 (finding 1.38); mhealth 0.118 -> 0.42 (finding 0.39); pamap2 0.176 -> 0.28 (finding 0.25); kuhar 0.358 -> 0.14 (finding 0.06); wisdm 0.085 -> 0.59. The mechanism, direction, and the noise-dominant low-amplitude cases (esp. uci_har gravity-removed body_acc) are confirmed. DISCREPANCY: the finding's wisdm figure (std=2.80, sigma/std=0.02) does NOT reproduce — wisdm is m/s^2 and static-sitting per-axis std is ~0.08, so the finding's claimed ~150x spread is overstated; my measured spread is ~60x (8.45/0.14). That is still a very large fairness/units-awareness violation and does not weaken the finding. SEVERITY: MEDIUM is correct — this is a training-time augmentation-fairness defect (fix = relative sigma or jitter-after-normalization, needs retraining), not a results-integrity or data-corruption bug; reported results were produced consistently under this behavior. || CONFIRMED(repro): Core mechanism verified in code and empirically. JitterCfg.sigma=0.05 is a fixed absolute value (augmentations.py:582); _jitter adds randn_like(s.data)*sigma with no relative/std scaling (912-914). It is applied to the RAW full-session tensor in the loader (multi_dataset_loader.py:523-532); the only step before it is NaN ffill/bfill (460-468) so NO normalization precedes it, and per-patch z-score runs AFTER inside preprocess_imu_data (542-574 -> preprocessing.py:191-198). jitter is live in the training default: ABLATION_SIGNAL_AUG defaults to 1 so the kill-switch at 392-393 does not fire, and default_v2() inherits JitterCfg(enabled=True,p=0.5); docs/comments (374-376,729-731) explicitly list jitter as part of v2. _scale is multiplicative (unit-invariant), confirming the additive-only defect. No compensating normalization exists upstream (rate/gravity augs do not rescale amplitude). Not dead code, not in the resolution log, not a documented deviation. Refutation attempts all failed. CAVEAT on evidence magnitude: my reproduction does NOT match the finding's high-amplitude figures (wisdm sit measured 0.039 not 2.80; kuhar sit 0.125 not 0.89), so the headline '~150x spread' is overstated - observed spread ~48x. But the consequential low-amplitude end reproduces and is worse than claimed: uci_har standing body_acc median std 0.0046 => jitter noise ~11x the signal (finding said 3x), so those static-posture windows are z-scored to near-noise 50% of the time. The unit-dependent, pre-normalization additive sigma is a genuine fairness/units-awareness defect. MEDIUM is appropriate: it degrades training quality for low-amplitude/g-unit datasets but half the windows are unaugmented and it does not corrupt evaluation; the inflated spread figure argues against escalating to HIGH.

### 38. [MEDIUM] [pipeline:dataloader] Session-index cache keyed only on (datasets, cap, seed) silently serves STALE manifest text + labels after any re-conversion
- **area:** caching / regression-vector
- **issue:** _load_datasets_cached() pickles the FULL dataset_info snapshot (manifest text, per-channel descriptions, labels, sampling_rates) plus the session list, but _get_cache_key() hashes only sorted(datasets) + max_sessions + seed. Content changes to data/<ds>/manifest.json or labels.json do NOT invalidate the cache. On a cache hit, self.dataset_info is restored wholesale from the pickle (line 275), so the channel-text conditioning the model trains on comes from the CACHED manifest, not the on-disk one. This is a latent regression vector for a large class of the resolution-log fixes that live in manifest.json / labels.json: DATA-1/DATA-4 (hhar reconvert 318k->47,961), DATA-7 (dsads lying_right_side), DATA-6/DATA-8/DATA-11 (pamap2/kuhar channel-text + gravity-removed wording), and DATA-17/20/22/26 ('at N Hz' strips). Any pre-fix cache with the same (datasets,cap,seed) key silently reinstates the pre-fix data/text at training time. Currently latent: data/.cache holds only 3 single-dataset audit caches, no active 11-dataset training cache. The docstring's stated invalidation ('Cache is invalidated when datasets, max_sessions, or seed change') is the entire logic and is insufficient for what is cached.
- **evidence:** multi_dataset_loader.py:254-259 (_get_cache_key hashes only datasets+max_sessions+seed); :293-300 (save_info stores full 'manifest'/'channel_info'/'channels'/'sampling_rates'); :275 (self.dataset_info = cached['dataset_info'] on hit). Demonstrated in scratchpad: built loader on a toy dataset (channel desc 'ORIGINAL-DESC acc x'), edited on-disk manifest.json to 'FIXED-DESC acc x (gravity removed)', rebuilt with same key -> sample still returned 'Toy dataset. ORIGINAL-DESC acc x' while on-disk manifest read 'FIXED-DESC acc x (gravity removed)'. Cache hit printed 'Loaded session index from cache'.
- **fix:** Fold a content hash into the cache key -- e.g. mtime or md5 of each dataset's manifest.json + labels.json -- so any re-conversion or manifest edit invalidates it; or cache only the lightweight session index (paths+labels) and always re-read manifests fresh from disk into dataset_info.
- **files:** datasets/imu_pretraining_dataset/multi_dataset_loader.py
- **verify:** CONFIRMED(repro): Independently reproduced end-to-end. _get_cache_key() (line 259) hashes only sorted(datasets)+[msd_key, seed] — no mtime or content hash of manifest.json/labels.json. On a cache hit, line 275 restores self.dataset_info wholesale from the pickle, including 'manifest', 'channel_info', 'channels', 'sampling_rates', 'labels' (all written by _load_datasets() lines 329-336 from on-disk manifests at cache-creation time). Live scratchpad test confirmed: built toy loader with channel desc "ORIGINAL-DESC acc x", pickled it, mutated on-disk manifest to "FIXED-DESC acc x (gravity removed)", reinstantiated with identical (datasets, cap, seed) — output "Loaded session index from cache" and dataset_info still held "ORIGINAL-DESC acc x" while the on-disk file clearly had "FIXED-DESC". The claim that data/.cache holds only single-dataset audit caches (finding says 3, actual count is 4: uci_har, mhealth, pamap2, wisdm) is correct in substance — no 11-dataset training cache exists, so the vulnerability is currently latent. The docstring's stated invalidation condition ("Cache is invalidated when datasets, max_sessions, or seed change") exactly matches the code and is confirmed insufficient for what is cached. || CONFIRMED: The code mechanism is exactly as described and no compensating logic exists anywhere in the codebase. _get_cache_key() (lines 254-259) hashes only sorted(datasets)+max_sessions_per_dataset+seed; on a cache hit (lines 271-283) self.dataset_info is restored wholesale from pickle and the function returns without touching manifest.json or labels.json; __getitem__ (lines 415, 471, 488-496) derives all channel descriptions, sampling rates, and dataset text from self.dataset_info, so cached text is what the model trains on. The resolution log has no entry for this finding — no fix was ever applied. The current data/.cache holds 4 single-dataset audit caches (uci_har Jul 9, pamap2 Jul 9, wisdm Jul 9, mhealth Jul 10); spot-checks of pamap2 and wisdm show their cached descriptions happen to match the current on-disk manifests (those manifests were in their post-fix state before the Jul 9 caches were created), so no stale content is demonstrably present in existing caches, and no 11-dataset training cache exists. The risk is therefore latent rather than active today — consistent with what the finding itself states. No refutation is possible: there is no content-hash in the key, no post-hit re-read of manifests, and no training-script override. Any future 11-dataset training run that hits a pre-manifest-edit cache will silently train on the cached (pre-fix) channel text, which is the stated hazard.

### 39. [MEDIUM] [pipeline:train-loop] best.pt selection signal is a per-sample-pooled val accuracy dominated by capture24/hhar/wisdm, on in-distribution subjects, not the zero-shot HAR objective
- **area:** val-selection
- **issue:** best.pt is saved on `val_acc = val_metrics.get('accuracy',0.0)` (semantic_alignment_train.py:2579-2606), where accuracy comes from compute_group_accuracy_majority_vote over the pooled val loader with shuffle=False and NO per-dataset/group reweighting (validate() lines 1509-1609; metric is per-sample correct/N, evaluation_metrics.py:246). Val is 15% of the (capped) training datasets, so the selection metric is sample-weighted: capture24 ~28.4%, hhar ~19.7%, wisdm ~14.0% (=62% combined) vs mhealth/hapt/pamap2 <6% combined. capture24 is free-living wrist-accel, distributionally far from the six held-out scripted-HAR test sets. Additionally unimib_shar has no recoverable subject id so it splits per-session (_subject_of returns None, loader :379), allowing within-subject train/val leakage that further inflates its ~6.9% share. Net: the checkpoint chosen as headline is selected by a signal dominated by the largest/most-OOD training datasets on in-distribution (and partly subject-leaky) val, which need not track zero-shot macro-F1 on the test sets.
- **evidence:** semantic_alignment_train.py:2579 (val_acc=accuracy), :2604-2606 (best.pt on val_acc>best); validate() :1599-1609 uses compute_group_accuracy_majority_vote (per-sample, evaluation_metrics.py:246 correct/N). Measured est. val composition (15% of hours-cap counts): capture24 28.4%, hhar 19.7%, wisdm 14.0%, mhealth 1.2%, hapt 1.5%. _subject_of returns None for unimib_shar (verified none_frac=1.000) -> per-session split.
- **fix:** Select best.pt on a per-dataset macro-averaged (or group-macro) val accuracy so small scripted sets are not swamped by capture24, and/or track a zero-shot proxy (the existing unseen-motionsense eval) in the selection criterion; exclude the per-session-split unimib_shar from the selection metric or note its leakage.
- **verify:** CONFIRMED(repro): Every numerical and code claim in the finding was independently reproduced from primary sources.

1. best.pt selection signal: semantic_alignment_train.py:2579 reads `val_acc = val_metrics.get('accuracy', 0.0)`, and lines 2604-2606 save best.pt exactly when `val_acc > best_val_acc`. Confirmed verbatim.

2. Per-sample accuracy: evaluation_metrics.py:246 returns `{'accuracy': correct / N}` — pure per-sample count, no per-dataset reweighting. Confirmed verbatim.

3. Val loader shuffle=False: DataLoader at line 2305 has `shuffle=False`. Confirmed.

4. Val composition (session counts, which is what per-sample accuracy weights by): I counted all sessions in data/<ds>/sessions/ directories, applied the caps from TRAIN_HOURS_PER_DATASET (wisdm capped at 24,000 sessions, hhar at 33,750; all others uncapped), and computed each dataset's share of the 171,079 total sessions. Results exactly reproduce the finding's numbers:
   - capture24: 28.4% (48,518 sessions)
   - hhar: 19.7% (33,750 sessions)
   - wisdm: 14.0% (24,000 sessions)
   - Combined top-3: 62.1%
   - mhealth+hapt+pamap2 combined: 5.3% (below the claimed "<6%")
   - unimib_shar: 6.9%

5. unimib_shar per-session split: multi_dataset_loader.py:34-52 shows _subject_of has no branch for unimib_shar and falls through to `return None` at line 52. The grouping logic at line 379 then uses `("unimib_shar", "__sess__" + session_id)` as the group key — one group per session — meaning train/val split is on individual sessions rather than subjects. Within-subject data appears in both train and val for unimib_shar.

6. The resolution log (adversarial_sweep_findings.md lines 289-366) has no entry for val-selection; this finding is not previously logged there.

Severity assessment: MEDIUM is correct. The checkpoint chosen as headline is selected by a signal dominated by the three largest training datasets (capture24 is free-living wrist-only, distributionally far from the scripted multiposition test sets). The selection criterion does not track zero-shot macro-F1 on the held-out test sets, and unimib_shar's 6.9% val share is slightly inflated by subject leakage. These are genuine methodological concerns for the validity of model selection, but they do not constitute data fabrication and are partially mitigated by the fact that the training objective (CLIP-style alignment) is dataset-agnostic. MEDIUM is the right severity. || CONFIRMED(repro): All core claims survive the strongest refutation attempt. Empirically verified:

1. val_acc = val_metrics.get('accuracy', 0.0) at line 2579; best.pt saved on val_acc > best_val_acc at lines 2604-2606. No other metric is used for checkpoint selection.

2. compute_group_accuracy_majority_vote returns accuracy = correct / N (evaluation_metrics.py:246) — this is per-sample, not per-class or per-dataset. f1_macro IS also computed and returned in the metrics dict (evaluation_metrics.py:249), which is a partial refutation attempt: the val metrics do contain a macro-balanced signal. However, f1_macro is never plumbed through to the checkpoint-selection logic; the training loop keys exclusively on val_metrics.get('accuracy', 0.0).

3. Val DataLoader is constructed at line 2301-2312 with the explicit comment "no balancing needed", plain shuffle=False, no sampler. No per-dataset reweighting.

4. Val composition empirically reproduced from labels.json counts + TRAIN_HOURS_PER_DATASET caps: capture24 28.4% (48,518 sessions), hhar 19.7% (33,750 capped), wisdm 14.0% (24,000 capped) = 62.1% combined; mhealth 1.2% (2,029), hapt 1.5% (2,546). Matches the finding's numbers exactly. After the DATA-1 hhar re-conversion (318,543 → 47,961 sessions), the effective count is still 33,750 because the hours cap (24 h / 2.56 s = 33,750) sits below 47,961 — so percentages are unchanged.

5. unseen_loader (motionsense, a held-out test set) IS evaluated every 5 epochs and logged to the plotter, providing a zero-shot proxy. However, it is never connected to best.pt selection — unseen_metrics is computed after the checkpoint-save block and only passed to plotter.add_scalar. This is the most meaningful refutation angle, but it fails: the proxy exists in the logs but is not wired to the save criterion.

6. _subject_of returns None for unimib_shar (line 52, confirmed by comment and fallthrough), so unimib_shar gets per-session groups → within-subject train/val leakage confirmed.

One minor overstatement in the finding: the split IS subject-disjoint within each dataset (cross-subject), so calling it "in-distribution subjects" is slightly imprecise — it is out-of-distribution subjects but same-dataset distribution. This does not change the core validity of the claim about dataset-level composition bias.

The finding is not in the resolution log (which covers DATA-1 to DATA-26, MODELS-1 to MODELS-23, EVAL-1 to EVAL-2 only).

### 40. [LOW] [baseline:deepconvlstm] Undocumented 'reimplementation, not port': released code is deprecated Lasagne/Theano with no released weights for our datasets
- **area:** faithful-port / docs
- **issue:** The docs treat DeepConvLSTM like the other adapters, but its released code is Lasagne/Theano (both abandoned/unmaintained, unrunnable on modern CUDA) and inference-only, and there are NO released weights for any HALO test dataset (it is trained per-target from scratch). Consequently a faithful 'port' is necessarily a from-scratch PyTorch REIMPLEMENTATION in which weight-init, optimizer internals, and exact framework behaviors cannot be matched to the original. This deviation-in-kind (reimplementation vs port; no released checkpoint to verify against) is not disclosed anywhere, unlike the released-weights baselines whose provenance is documented.
- **evidence:** DeepConvLSTM.ipynb imports confirmed via curl: 'import lasagne', 'import theano', 'import theano.tensor as T'. citation.json data_or_code_url = github.com/sussexwearlab/DeepConvLSTM (resolves; raw files fetched OK). No PyTorch/released-weights path; role='supervised from-scratch few-shot floor'. baseline_flexibility.md row 27 and 'How each handles heterogeneity' (line 47) give no reimplementation/framework note.
- **fix:** At build time, add a one-line provenance note (baseline_flexibility.md / EVALUATION_PROTOCOL_V2.md): 'DeepConvLSTM is a from-scratch PyTorch reimplementation of the Ordonez-Roggen Lasagne/Theano architecture (no released weights for these datasets); architecture (4x conv-64 5x1, 2x LSTM-128, softmax, dropout 0.5) matched to released code, init/optimizer per paper.'
- **files:** docs/baselines/baseline_flexibility.md
- **verify:** CONFIRMED(repro): All three core claims independently verified:

1. Lasagne/Theano code confirmed. Fetched raw GitHub notebook (github.com/sussexwearlab/DeepConvLSTM, master branch, DeepConvLSTM.ipynb). Cell 11 contains: `import lasagne`, `import theano`, `import time`, `import theano.tensor as T` — no PyTorch or TensorFlow anywhere. The notebook also loads pre-existing weights with `cp.load(open('weights/DeepConvLSTM_oppChallenge_gestures.pkl'))` via the deprecated `cPickle` module, confirming the code is not runnable on modern stacks without a full rewrite.

2. No released weights for HALO test datasets. The only released checkpoint is `DeepConvLSTM_oppChallenge_gestures.pkl` — for the Opportunity Challenge gesture task, not for any of the six HALO test datasets (motionsense, realworld, mobiact, shoaib, harth, inclusivehar). A per-target HALO evaluation necessarily trains from scratch.

3. Documentation gap confirmed but the claim "not disclosed anywhere" is slightly imprecise. `baseline_flexibility.md:47` already says "from-scratch supervised floor" and `references/baselines/deepconvlstm/citation.json` role field says "supervised from-scratch few-shot floor". So the from-scratch nature IS disclosed. What is NOT documented anywhere is the reason: the released code is unmaintained Lasagne/Theano (Python 2 / `cPickle`), making a faithful port impossible and requiring a full from-scratch PyTorch reimplementation. The prior sweep (MODELS-15, MODELS-16) addresses training-recipe fairness and input-format pre-registration but neither flags the Lasagne/Theano framework provenance explanation specifically; both are DEFERRED to build-time, not FIXED.

The documentation gap is real and narrow: the word "from-scratch" appears, but the reason (original framework is abandoned Lasagne/Theano, no cross-framework weight transfer possible) is not stated, unlike the released-weights baselines whose exact checkpoint provenance is logged. LOW severity is correct — there is no eval correctness, leakage, or result inflation issue; it is purely a provenance transparency gap in two doc files.

### 41. [LOW] [baseline:deepconvlstm] citation.json overstates the code license as 'MIT-style' — the repo has no LICENSE file
- **area:** citation-metadata
- **issue:** citation.json states 'license: CC BY 4.0 (paper); code MIT-style (Sussex Wearlab)'. The sussexwearlab/DeepConvLSTM repository has no LICENSE file, so 'MIT-style' is unsupported; absent an explicit license the code is all-rights-reserved by default. Since we reimplement the architecture (rather than vendor the code) the practical risk is low, but the metadata claim is inaccurate and should not be relied on.
- **evidence:** curl -o /dev/null -w %{http_code} https://raw.githubusercontent.com/sussexwearlab/DeepConvLSTM/master/LICENSE -> 404. README grep for 'license' -> no match. citation.json line 12: '"license": "CC BY 4.0 (paper); code MIT-style (Sussex Wearlab)"'.
- **fix:** Correct the citation.json license field to reflect reality, e.g. 'paper CC BY 4.0; code repo has no LICENSE file (all rights reserved) — architecture reimplemented from the paper, not vendored'.
- **files:** references/baselines/deepconvlstm/citation.json
- **verify:** CONFIRMED(repro): Independently verified three points. (1) /home/alex/code/HALO/code/references/baselines/deepconvlstm/citation.json line 12 reads exactly: "license": "CC BY 4.0 (paper); code MIT-style (Sussex Wearlab)". (2) curl of https://raw.githubusercontent.com/sussexwearlab/DeepConvLSTM/master/LICENSE returned HTTP 404 — no LICENSE file exists in the repo. (3) The README.md contains no mention of the word "license". Without an explicit license file the repo is all-rights-reserved by default; the "MIT-style" claim is unsupported metadata.

### 42. [LOW] [baseline:limubert] realworld LiMU-BERT input is acc-only (gyro zero-padded) though realworld natively provides gyroscope — an undocumented channel deviation from the paper's 6-ch acc+gyro model
- **area:** faithfulness / input-format
- **issue:** For the realworld test set the LiMU-BERT 6-channel array carries zeros in all three gyro channels, because the benchmark raw export (benchmark_data/raw/realworld/metadata.json) is accelerometer-only, so preprocess_limubert marks has_gyro=False and zero-pads gyro. But realworld natively has gyroscope (and magnetometer): HALO's own data/realworld/manifest.json declares gyro_x/y/z and mag_x/y/z at 50 Hz. This means (a) LiMU-BERT runs realworld on a degenerate half-zero input, deviating from the paper/repo's 6-ch acc+gyro model and from the flexibility-doc row that lists LiMU-BERT input as 'fixed acc+gyro', and (b) there is a potential channel-parity mismatch if HALO's own realworld evaluation consumes the native gyro. This is not disclosed in baseline_flexibility.md or EVALUATION_PROTOCOL_V2.md.
- **evidence:** benchmark_data/processed/limubert/realworld gyro channels are all-zero (gyro_rms=0.000, gyro_absmax=0.00 over first 2000 windows); realworld/mapping.json has_gyro=False; benchmark_data/raw/realworld/metadata.json channels=[acc_x,acc_y,acc_z]; data/realworld/manifest.json channels include gyro_x/y/z + mag_x/y/z @50Hz. (For contrast motionsense/mobiact/shoaib/inclusivehar gyro RMS ~0.8-1.2 rad/s, non-zero.)
- **fix:** Confirm and document whether realworld is intentionally acc-only for the whole benchmark (in which case HALO must also be fed acc-only realworld for channel parity, and the flexibility doc should note realworld's gyro is padded), or regenerate the realworld raw export with gyro so LiMU-BERT/CrossHAR receive the same 6-ch input the model expects. Either way, record the realworld channel decision in EVALUATION_PROTOCOL_V2.md.
- **files:** /home/alex/code/HALO/code/benchmark_data/scripts/preprocess_limubert.py, /home/alex/code/HALO/code/benchmark_data/raw/realworld/metadata.json, /home/alex/code/HALO/code/data/realworld/manifest.json, /home/alex/code/HALO/code/docs/baselines/baseline_flexibility.md
- **verify:** CONFIRMED(repro): All core claims independently verified from primary sources.

1. benchmark_data/raw/realworld/metadata.json channels = ["acc_x","acc_y","acc_z"] — acc-only, no gyro present in the raw benchmark export.

2. data/realworld/manifest.json declares gyro_x/y/z and mag_x/y/z at 50 Hz — the native HALO parquet for realworld does include gyroscope.

3. preprocess_limubert.py line 202: `has_gyro = any("gyro" in ch for ch in metadata["channels"])` reads from the raw benchmark metadata.json, so has_gyro resolves to False for realworld. extract_core_channels() then fills channels 3-5 with zeros when has_gyro=False.

4. benchmark_data/processed/limubert/realworld/mapping.json confirms "has_gyro": false.

5. Direct numpy check on benchmark_data/processed/limubert/realworld/data_20_120.npy (27138 windows, shape (27138,120,6)): gyro_rms=0.0, gyro_absmax=0.0, all-zero confirmed. Acc_rms=6.08 (non-degenerate), confirming the degenerate half-zero pattern is confined to gyro channels only.

6. adversarial_sweep_findings.md resolution log contains no entry for this specific issue — it is not a previously-logged or already-resolved finding.

The channel mismatch is real: LiMU-BERT's 6-ch model receives a degenerate half-zero input for realworld while the native HALO pipeline has gyro available, creating an undisclosed channel-parity asymmetry. Severity remains LOW: the discrepancy is consistent (not random), affects one dataset, and is a documentation/fairness gap rather than a results fabrication issue.

### 43. [LOW] [baseline:limubert] v2 adapter's sub-window softmax mean-pooling (sub-window -> per-window) is an undocumented aggregation deviation from the paper/original scoring
- **area:** faithfulness / documentation
- **issue:** The v2 adapter (val_scripts/human_activity_recognition/baselines/limubert.py, window_probs) runs the GRU on 20-step sub-windows, softmaxes each, then MEAN-POOLS the per-sub-window softmaxes back to a per-parent-window distribution over the 87 labels (np.add.at over parent ids). The original LiMU-BERT scores each reshaped 20-step sub-window as an independent example (no window-level aggregation), and the repo's own legacy path (evaluate_limubert.py majority_vote_subwindows) used hard majority vote of sub-window argmax. The mean-pool-of-softmaxes choice is a reasonable HALO-benchmark adaptation needed to align with eval_v2.window_ground_truth, but it is a genuine deviation that appears nowhere in EVALUATION_PROTOCOL_V2.md or baseline_flexibility.md (the protocol documents HALO's own patch soft-voting, not the baselines' sub-window aggregation).
- **evidence:** limubert.py window_probs: F.softmax(clf(sub)) then np.add.at(win, parent, sub_probs); win[nz] /= cnt[nz] (mean-pool). evaluate_limubert.py:239-264 majority_vote_subwindows (legacy hard vote). Grep of EVALUATION_PROTOCOL_V2.md / baseline_flexibility.md for 'sub-window/mean-pool/aggregat' finds only HALO-patch soft-voting and SBERT mean-pool, no baseline sub-window->window rule.
- **fix:** Add one line to EVALUATION_PROTOCOL_V2.md (ConSE section) stating that closed-vocab baselines whose classifier operates on sub-windows (LiMU-BERT) aggregate sub-window softmaxes to the parent 6 s window by mean-pooling before the ConSE bridge, so the per-window score aligns with eval_v2.window_ground_truth. Note this differs from the paper's per-sub-window scoring.
- **files:** /home/alex/code/HALO/code/val_scripts/human_activity_recognition/baselines/limubert.py, /home/alex/code/HALO/code/docs/baselines/EVALUATION_PROTOCOL_V2.md
- **verify:** CONFIRMED(repro): All three evidence pillars independently verified. (1) limubert.py window_probs lines 42-57: F.softmax per sub-window batch, then np.add.at + divide-by-count mean-pool back to parent windows; the file's own docstring states it. (2) evaluate_limubert.py lines 239-264: legacy majority_vote_subwindows uses hard argmax vote via np.bincount — a genuine deviation from the v2 mean-pool path. (3) EVALUATION_PROTOCOL_V2.md ConSE section (lines 69-91) and baseline_flexibility.md (lines 38-43) both mention LiMU-BERT sub-windows but neither documents the sub-window→window aggregation rule. The adversarial_sweep_findings.md entries for LiMU-BERT cover corpus staleness and result reproducibility, not this gap. Documentation omission confirmed; no correctness defect.

(3) PDF text extracted from references/baselines/normwear/paper.pdf confirms Table 14 text verbatim: "NormWear-Medium and NormWear-Large refer to NormWear's pretrained checkpoints trained on 2.58 million and 8.97 million signal segments, respectively." ### 46. [LOW] [baseline:ssl-wearables] MODELS-14 pins harnet10 (10 s) as the checkpoint, but the 6 s eval grid makes harnet5 (5 s) the lower-distortion faithful choice; the tradeoff is undocumented
- **area:** checkpoint-selection
- **issue:** MODELS-14's fix recommends pinning 'the default UK-Biobank harnet10'. Given the ConSE eval grid is 6 s windows and harnet is rigid on window length, resizing a 6 s window into harnet10's 10 s / 300-sample slot stretches the signal by 1.67x, whereas harnet5's native 5 s / 150-sample slot is only a 1.2x compression from 6 s — materially less temporal distortion and thus a more faithful feed for this benchmark. All three (harnet5/10/30) are released with identical pretraining, so the choice is free. The docs pin harnet10 without stating this window-distance consideration, so an adapter author following MODELS-14 verbatim silently maximizes the resize distortion.
- **evidence:** hubconf.py docstrings: harnet5 = N x 3 x 150 (5 s @ 30 Hz), harnet10 = 300, harnet30 = 900. Eval windows are 6 s (label_20_120.npy = (N,120,6) @ 20 Hz, measured). adversarial_sweep_findings.md:225-227 (MODELS-14) pins harnet10. resize distortion: 6->10 s = 1.67x stretch vs 6->5 s = 1.2x compression.
- **fix:** In the ssl-wearables build-time doc, either switch the pinned checkpoint to harnet5 (closest native window to the 6 s eval grid, minimizing resize distortion) or explicitly justify keeping harnet10 and record the exact stretch factor in the results-JSON provenance. Keep the UK-Biobank (not Capture-24) variant per MODELS-14 to avoid train-set overlap.
- **verify:** CONFIRMED(repro): All core claims independently verified from primary sources. Eval windows confirmed 6.0s from real .npy shapes (N,120,6) @ 20Hz across all five current test sets. harnet window specs confirmed from citation.json notes: harnet5=5s/150 samples, harnet10=10s/300 samples at 30Hz. Resize distortion factors recomputed: 6s-to-harnet5 = 1.200x, 6s-to-harnet10 = 1.667x — both figures in the finding are exact. adversarial_sweep_findings.md line 227 confirms MODELS-14 recommends "the default UK-Biobank harnet10" with no window-distance justification documented. One minor imprecision in the finding: "identical pretraining" is loose — all three share the UK-Biobank corpus but were pretrained with different window lengths, so their learned temporal scales differ. This does not undermine the resize-distortion argument; it adds a secondary reason to prefer harnet5 (closer native temporal scale). Severity LOW is appropriate: the ssl-wearables adapter is not yet built (PLANNED), the practical F1 impact of the 1.2x vs 1.67x difference is unquantified, and the concern is about an undocumented documentation choice rather than a broken implementation.

### 47. [LOW] [baseline:unimts] Accel units are already m/s^2 in the 20 Hz eval arrays; the outstanding UniMTS unit issue is gravity-convention, affecting motionsense AND inclusivehar
- **area:** preprocessing / units-gravity
- **issue:** MODELS-6 (FIXED-doc) warns a single global accel scale mis-scales g-unit sets ~9.8x. Empirically that hazard is already neutralized IF the UniMTS CosineAdapter consumes BENCH_LIMU (the 20 Hz arrays the cosine framework aligns to), because preprocess_limubert already converts every test set to m/s^2 — so the adapter must apply NO further accel scaling (the ~9.8x risk only bites if it re-reads raw parquet). The genuinely un-fixed part is the gravity convention: UniMTS pretrains on IMUSim-synthesized specific force (gravity-INCLUSIVE), but two held-out sets are gravity-REMOVED and stay so after limubert conversion — motionsense (|acc|mean 3.74, no ~9.8 static baseline) and inclusivehar (|acc|mean 2.43). MODELS-6 named only motionsense; inclusivehar is additionally implicated and undocumented.
- **evidence:** Measured on /home/alex/code/HALO/code/benchmark_data/processed/limubert/*/data_20_120.npy (CPU): |acc|mean motionsense=3.74, inclusivehar=2.43 (gravity-removed) vs realworld=10.05, shoaib=10.58, harth=10.12, mobiact=10.11, opportunity=9.86 (gravity-inclusive m/s^2); realworld & harth gyro all-zero (accel-only). UniMTS applies NO input z-score (model.py:302-337 data_bn uses FROZEN pretraining running stats, so physical scale AND gravity convention are load-bearing). Author scaling is non-uniform: data.py:163 motionsense acc*9.80665 vs data.py:174 shoaib no scale. base.py:87 confirms cosine GT aligns to BENCH_LIMU label_20_120.npy.
- **fix:** In the adapter plan state explicitly: (1) read BENCH_LIMU m/s^2 arrays and apply NO accel rescale; (2) re-add gravity for the gravity-removed sets (motionsense, inclusivehar) to match UniMTS's gravity-inclusive pretraining, or disclose them as a gravity-convention mismatch in RESULTS_V2; add inclusivehar to MODELS-6's gravity note (currently only motionsense).
- **files:** /home/alex/code/HALO/code/benchmark_data/scripts/preprocess_limubert.py, /home/alex/code/HALO/code/docs/baselines/baseline_flexibility.md
- **verify:** CONFIRMED(repro): All core claims independently reproduced from primary sources.

1. |acc|mean measurements from /home/alex/code/HALO/code/benchmark_data/processed/limubert/*/data_20_120.npy (first 3 channels, Frobenius per timestep, mean over all windows): motionsense=3.7401, inclusivehar=2.4303, realworld=10.0464, shoaib=10.5746, harth=10.1176, mobiact=10.1107, opportunity=9.8555. These match the claimed values to two decimal places.

2. Gravity-removed status is confirmed from manifests: motionsense channels[0-2] description="User acceleration X-axis in g units (gravity removed by Core Motion)"; inclusivehar channels[0-2] description="User acceleration X (g, gravity removed by iOS)". Both datasets explicitly carry userAcceleration / gravity-stripped signal.

3. preprocess_limubert.py:52 confirms ACC_IN_G_UNITS = {"motionsense", "inclusivehar", "capture24", "harth"} — these are multiplied by GRAVITY_MS2 (g->m/s²) at line 267, but no gravity is re-added. So BENCH_LIMU npy arrays for these datasets are in m/s² AND remain gravity-removed.

4. The MODELS-6 gravity note in adversarial_sweep_findings.md (line 183) says "match the gravity convention ... since motionsense feeds gravity-removed userAcc while shoaib/realworld include gravity" — motionsense is named, inclusivehar is not. The resolution row marks MODELS-6 as FIXED (doc) for the unit-map requirement, but does not separately call out inclusivehar's gravity-removed status. This gap is real and undocumented.

5. The claim that BENCH_LIMU arrays are already in m/s² (so the UniMTS adapter must NOT re-apply an accel scale) is directly supported by preprocess_limubert.py's explicit ACC_IN_G_UNITS conversion before saving.

The UniMTS model.py data_bn frozen-BN claim (lines 302-337) could not be verified directly — the UniMTS code is not in auxiliary_repos — but this is secondary evidence for WHY physical scale matters; the gravity-convention discrepancy stands on its own from the data measurements and manifests. Severity LOW is appropriate: the ~9.8x unit hazard is neutralized by BENCH_LIMU; the gravity issue is a known limitation for a not-yet-built adapter that should be disclosed, not a silent bug in live code.

### 48. [LOW] [baseline:unimts] Single-sensor HAR eval makes UniMTS's spatio-temporal graph (its cross-position contribution) degenerate — an off-home-turf deviation to disclose
- **area:** fairness / documentation
- **issue:** UniMTS's headline mechanism is a ST-GCN over a 22-joint skeleton that generalizes across device positions by propagating signal across joints. HALO's cosine eval feeds a SINGLE core sensor per dataset (one filled joint, the other 21 zero-filled). With one active node the graph convolution has no populated neighbors to aggregate, so UniMTS collapses to a per-joint temporal CNN on a lone node — its distinctive cross-location generalization is inactive on exactly the single-placement setting HALO tests. baseline_flexibility.md sells UniMTS placement as 'native (SMPL joint) / genuinely placement-aware' without noting that on HALO's single-sensor windows this strength is unexercised (UniMTS's paper gains come largely from multi-position sets like realworld/DSADS with many joints filled). This should be disclosed so the head-to-head is read correctly.
- **evidence:** model.py:36 (num_node=22), model.py:332-348 (graph forward aggregates over V joints; with 21 zero joints the einsum 'nkctv,kvw->nctw' mixes in only the pretrained-BN bias of empty nodes). data.py:95 all_X=zeros((N,T,22,6)); only the joint_list entries are filled. HALO single-sensor confirmed: /home/alex/code/HALO/code/val_scripts/human_activity_recognition/baselines/base.py:87 GT aligned to (N,120,6) one-sensor arrays.
- **fix:** Add a one-line disclosure in baseline_flexibility.md/RESULTS_V2.md that on single-placement test sets UniMTS's inter-joint graph is inactive (single populated node), so it runs off its multi-position home turf; keep the joint choice consistent (finding 1) and note this is inherent to single-sensor HAR, not a handicap we impose.
- **files:** /home/alex/code/HALO/code/docs/baselines/baseline_flexibility.md, /home/alex/code/HALO/code/docs/baselines/RESULTS_V2.md
- **verify:** CONFIRMED(repro): All core technical claims independently verified from primary sources. (1) UniMTS model.py: num_node=22 confirmed; graph forward einsum 'nkctv,kvw->nctw' sums over V source joints, so 21 zero-filled joints contribute no signal. (2) UniMTS data.py: all_X initialized as zeros(N,T,22,6); only joint_list indices populated — single sensor = one populated node. (3) All 6 HALO test datasets are stored as (N,120,6) single-core-sensor arrays in benchmark_data/processed/limubert/: shoaib's 5 placements collapsed to right_pocket only (dataset_config.json core_channels); harth's back+thigh collapsed to back_acc only; the other four datasets are inherently single-placement. (4) baseline_flexibility.md says "genuinely placement-aware" without disclosing that the cross-joint graph aggregation is inactive when only one of 22 joints is populated. One minor imprecision: base.py:87 is the ground-truth loading line, not a sensor-shape assertion — the single-sensor fact is confirmed through benchmark_data shapes and core_channels config, not that line. The disclosure gap is real and the fix (one-sentence caveat in the docs) is appropriate. Severity LOW is correct: the UniMTS adapter is not yet built, no scores exist, and this is a fairness/documentation issue only.

### 49. [LOW] [dataset:hapt] convert.py create_manifest() diverges from on-disk manifest: re-running would regress the deliberate 'at 50Hz' strip and never annotates units/gravity
- **area:** reproducibility/regression
- **issue:** datascripts/hapt/convert.py:271 emits the dataset description ending '...Triaxial accelerometer and gyroscope at 50Hz.', but the on-disk data/hapt/manifest.json description ends '...Triaxial accelerometer and gyroscope.' (the 'at 50Hz' literal was stripped corpus-wide during the sweep, DATA-17/20/22/26). The converter is therefore NOT the source of the committed manifest; re-generating data/hapt from source would silently re-introduce the rate literal that the loader also appends as '(sampled at 50Hz, Xs window)', recreating the rate-token duplication/contradiction (with rate-aug) that DATA-20 fixed. Additionally the converter's channel descriptions ('Accelerometer X-axis (waist)') never encode units or gravity state, so a regeneration also fails to add the '(g, includes gravity)' annotation DATA-17 mandated. This is the same converter-vs-on-disk divergence class already reported for pamap2 (DATA-6) and pamap2 seeding (DATA-18), but it is not logged for hapt. On-disk (training-time) data is currently correct; the risk is purely on regeneration.
- **evidence:** convert.py:271 string literal contains 'gyroscope at 50Hz.'; on-disk manifest.json description tail = 'elerometer and gyroscope.' (no Hz). Verified programmatically: replicated create_manifest() description string != on-disk description (match=False; tails differ 'er and gyroscope at 50Hz.' vs 'elerometer and gyroscope.'). Measured acc |mag|=1.030 g over 25 sessions (static postures 1.00-1.04 -> g, gravity present) confirming the missing '(g, includes gravity)' annotation the converter never writes.
- **fix:** Update convert.py create_manifest() (line 271) to emit the current on-disk description WITHOUT 'at 50Hz', and add '(g, includes gravity)' to the acc channel descriptions and '(rad/s)' to gyro so a regeneration reproduces the committed manifest byte-for-byte (mirrors the DATA-6 pamap2 fix). Alternatively vendor the manifest and note it is hand-authored, but the converter must not silently re-add the Hz literal.
- **files:** datascripts/hapt/convert.py, data/hapt/manifest.json
- **verify:** CONFIRMED(repro): Both core claims verified directly from source files. (1) convert.py line 271 description ends "gyroscope at 50Hz." while data/hapt/manifest.json ends "gyroscope." — the Hz literal was stripped from the committed manifest but not from the converter, so regeneration would silently re-introduce it. (2) All six channel descriptions in convert.py lack unit/gravity annotations ("Accelerometer X-axis (waist)" with no "(g, includes gravity)"; "Gyroscope X-axis (waist)" with no "(rad/s)"), matching the same bare strings in the on-disk manifest. On-disk training data is currently correct; risk is regeneration-only. Severity LOW is appropriate.
