### 52. [LOW] [dataset:hhar] hhar manifest description re-embeds Hz literals ('50-200 Hz ... a true 50 Hz') that reach model channel text and contradict the rate-aug suffix — DATA-17 'stripped corpus-wide' is false for hhar
- **area:** channel-text/regression
- **issue:** The DATA-1 re-conversion's create_manifest() writes a description containing rate literals: '...Phones sampled at their native 50-200 Hz; resampled to a true 50 Hz here...'. The loader prepends this whole description onto every channel description fed to the frozen-SBERT text encoder, and separately appends a co-varying '(sampled at {N}Hz, {w}s window)' suffix. Under rate augmentation (RateCfg default_v2, p=0.5) the suffix mutates (e.g. 40Hz) while the prose still asserts 'a true 50 Hz here', producing a self-contradictory rate signal in the same text string. The DATA-17 resolution row claims "'at N Hz' stripped corpus-wide"; the sibling datasets (dsads/unimib/capture24) were stripped (DATA-20/22/26), but hhar's literal was reintroduced by the reconvert and survives.
- **evidence:** data/hhar/manifest.json description embeds '50-200 Hz' and 'a true 50 Hz' (regenerated by datascripts/hhar/convert.py:198 create_manifest). Loader: multi_dataset_loader.py:480 dataset_desc=manifest['description']; :495-496 full_desc=f'{dataset_desc} {ch_desc}'; :591-592 (and :618-620) append '(sampled at {sampling_rate:.0f}Hz, ...)'. DATA-17 resolution (adversarial_sweep_findings.md:316) asserts corpus-wide stripping.
- **fix:** Remove the '50-200 Hz' / 'a true 50 Hz' rate literals from the hhar manifest description (and from create_manifest() so a re-run stays clean), leaving rate to the co-varying suffix/metadata — same treatment already applied to dsads/unimib/capture24. Note this is separate from the still-deferred DATA-17 units/gravity annotation gap.
- **files:** data/hhar/manifest.json, datascripts/hhar/convert.py, datasets/imu_pretraining_dataset/multi_dataset_loader.py
- **verify:** CONFIRMED(repro): Independently verified all claims. data/hhar/manifest.json line 3 contains "Phones sampled at their native 50-200 Hz; resampled to a true 50 Hz here." verbatim. datascripts/hhar/convert.py line 198 hard-codes the same string in create_manifest(), so it survives any re-run. multi_dataset_loader.py line 480 reads manifest description; lines 495-496 prepend it to every channel description. Lines 590-593 confirm the legacy non-filterbank path appends a mutable "(sampled at {N}Hz, ...)" suffix under use_text_augmentation, producing a self-contradictory rate signal when rate augmentation fires. DATA-17 resolution row (line 316) is marked PARTIAL (not FIXED), and sister datasets dsads/unimib_shar/capture24 each have their own FIXED entries (DATA-20/22/26); hhar has no such entry, confirming the literal was never stripped. The contradiction is real in the legacy path; in filterbank mode the suffix is suppressed so only the prose contamination remains.

### 53. [LOW] [dataset:kuhar] Bidirectional postural-transition classes labeled as one direction (kuhar's 2 largest classes, 27% of windows)
- **area:** label-semantics
- **issue:** The KU-HAR paper/Mendeley protocol defines 'Stand-sit -> Repeatedly standing up AND sitting down (5 times)' and 'Lay-stand -> Repeatedly standing up AND laying down (5 times)'. These are inherently bidirectional (alternating up/down) transition recordings. datascripts/kuhar/convert.py collapses each to a single unidirectional label: Stand-sit -> standing_up_from_sitting, Lay-stand -> standing_up_from_laying. Windowing is blind (create_variable_windows segments the whole ~1-min 5-cycle recording), so roughly half of each class's windows physically contain the OPPOSITE transition (sitting-down / lying-down) yet carry a 'standing up from ...' CLIP text target. This directly contradicts hapt (a dataset in the SAME train corpus), which labels stand_to_sit AND sit_to_stand (and lie/stand directions) as SEPARATE classes: kuhar thus aligns ~1300 sit-to-stand-and-back windows to a text that conflicts with hapt's directional stand_to_sit examples.
- **evidence:** convert.py ACTIVITIES map: 'Stand-sit':'standing_up_from_sitting', 'Lay-stand':'standing_up_from_laying'. Mendeley description (references/datasets/kuhar/webpage.html): 'Stand-sit -> Repeatedly standing up and sitting down (5 times)', 'Lay-stand -> Repeatedly standing up and laying down (5 times)'. data/kuhar/labels.json counts: standing_up_from_sitting=2585 (14.9%), standing_up_from_laying=2132 (12.3%) = 27.2% of 17374 windows (the #1 and #2 classes). Contrast datascripts/hapt/convert.py lines 39-44 (stand_to_sit=7, sit_to_stand=8, sit_to_lie=9, lie_to_sit=10, stand_to_lie=11, lie_to_stand=12) and hapt labels.json which contains all 6 directional transition labels. label_augmentation.py synonyms reinforce one direction ('sit-to-stand', 'rising from chair', 'lay-to-stand').
- **fix:** Rename kuhar's Stand-sit/Lay-stand to direction-neutral labels (e.g. 'sit_stand_transition' / 'lie_stand_transition' or 'postural_transition') and update the synonyms to not assert a single direction, so the CLIP target matches the physically ambiguous windows and does not contradict hapt's directional stand_to_sit / lie_to_stand examples. Alternatively segment per half-cycle before labeling (not feasible without direction annotations, which KU-HAR does not provide).
- **files:** datascripts/kuhar/convert.py, data/kuhar/labels.json, datasets/imu_pretraining_dataset/label_augmentation.py
- **verify:** CONFIRMED(repro): All core claims independently verified from primary sources. (1) datascripts/kuhar/convert.py lines 57 and 59 map "Stand-sit" -> "standing_up_from_sitting" and "Lay-stand" -> "standing_up_from_laying". (2) references/datasets/kuhar/webpage.html explicitly states "Stand-sit: Repeatedly standing up and sitting down (5 times)" and "Lay-stand: Repeatedly standing up and laying down (5 times)" — the source protocol is bidirectional. (3) Independently recomputed from data/kuhar/labels.json: 17374 total windows, standing_up_from_sitting=2585 (14.9%), standing_up_from_laying=2132 (12.3%), combined 27.2% — exactly matching the reported numbers. (4) datascripts/hapt/convert.py lines 39–44 confirm all 6 directional transitions are separate labels (stand_to_sit, sit_to_stand, sit_to_lie, lie_to_sit, stand_to_lie, lie_to_stand). (5) label_augmentation.py KUHAR synonyms for both transition labels are exclusively unidirectional ("rising from chair", "sit-to-stand", "getting up from lying", "lay-to-stand"), compounding the mismatch. The HAPT contradiction is genuine: kuhar sends ~half its 4717 windows through a "standing up from…" CLIP target while hapt trains explicit stand_to_sit / lie_to_stand examples separately. Severity MEDIUM is appropriate because SentenceBERT embeddings for opposing postural transitions are semantically adjacent, partially absorbing the signal, and this is one dataset in an 11-dataset corpus. || CONFIRMED(repro): All three claims are empirically verified. (1) Code path: convert.py ACTIVITIES map lines 57/59 assigns "Stand-sit"->"standing_up_from_sitting" and "Lay-stand"->"standing_up_from_laying"; no relabeling or neutral override exists anywhere in the pipeline. (2) KU-HAR protocol: webpage.html lines 9/11/54/56 explicitly state "Stand-sit → Repeatedly standing up AND sitting down (5 times)" confirming bidirectional multi-cycle recordings. (3) Physical signal: sampling windows from file_idx 1862 (15 windows, all labeled "standing_up_from_sitting"), acc_z trend is strongly negative on windows _0003 (−2.435), _0007 (−0.934), and _0009 (−0.285), confirming ~3/10 windows in that file physically contain sitting-down motion under the standing-up label. label_augmentation.py kuhar synonyms are entirely unidirectional ("rising from chair", "sit-to-stand"). No compensating mechanism was found: ACTIVE_LABEL_GROUPS = LABEL_GROUPS (fine-grained, not SIMPLE), so the same-label mask in semantic_loss.py places kuhar's mislabeled sit-down windows in group "transition_to_standing" alongside hapt's sit_to_stand — not with hapt's stand_to_sit — compounding rather than neutralizing the error. The finding is not in the resolution log.

Severity correction from MEDIUM to LOW: the SentenceBERT (all-MiniLM-L6-v2) frozen encoder barely distinguishes transition directions. "Standing up from sitting" vs "sitting down from standing" = 0.9421 cosine similarity. More tellingly, hapt's own opposite-direction synonyms "sitting to standing" vs "standing to sitting" are at 0.9820. Even "standing to sitting" (hapt stand_to_sit) vs "standing up from sitting" (kuhar's label) = 0.9211. Because the CLIP text targets for both motion directions land within 0.06 of each other in the frozen embedding space, the CLIP loss gradient from a mislabeled sit-down window is nearly identical to what it would be with the correct label. The cross-dataset contradiction with hapt's directional classes is real in label space but nearly invisible in text-embedding space. This substantially limits the practical training signal damage, warranting a severity downgrade. The 27% window share and the structural mislabeling remain real code quality issues worth fixing (rename to neutral labels), but the training-time impact is smaller than the finding implies.

### 55. [LOW] [dataset:kuhar] talking_standing label/synonyms omit the walking component the paper includes
- **area:** label-semantics
- **issue:** KU-HAR's 'Talk-stand' class is defined as 'Talking with hand movements while standing OR WALKING' (Mendeley/paper). The converter maps it to 'talking_standing' and label_augmentation.py describes it only as standing ('talking while standing', 'standing conversation', 'chatting while standing'), dropping the walking component. Empirically these windows carry substantial locomotion-level motion (|acc| mean ~1.0-2.3 m/s^2, |gyro| mean ~0.5-1.1 rad/s across sampled sessions), consistent with walking-while-talking, yet are aligned to a purely 'standing' text — under-describing the signal the model must match.
- **evidence:** webpage.html: 'Talk-stand -> Talking with hand movements while standing or walking (1 min)'. label_augmentation.py:359 talking_standing synonyms = ['talking while standing','standing conversation','chatting while standing']. Measured (CPU, sampled sessions): s1036_talking_standing |acc|mean=2.273 |gyro|mean=1.105; s1034=1.281/0.859; s1019=1.080/0.537 (vs talking_sitting |acc|~0.09-0.17, |gyro|~0.02-0.06).
- **fix:** Broaden the talking_standing description/synonyms to reflect that the activity includes walking while talking (e.g. add 'talking while standing or walking', 'conversing while moving'), so the alignment text is not falsely restricted to a stationary posture.
- **files:** datasets/imu_pretraining_dataset/label_augmentation.py
- **verify:** CONFIRMED(repro): Primary sources independently verified: (1) webpage.html explicitly states "Talk-stand → Talking with hand movements while standing or walking"; (2) label_augmentation.py line 359 lists only stationary synonyms ["talking while standing", "standing conversation", "chatting while standing"] with no walking variant; (3) motion measurements on 6 subjects show talking_standing has acc mean 1.151 m/s^2 and gyro mean 0.604 rad/s vs talking_sitting acc mean 0.194 and gyro mean 0.053 — a ~6x/~11x difference consistent with locomotion. The text-signal mismatch is real and reproduced.

### 56. [LOW] [dataset:mhealth] mhealth L5 is bidirectional 'climbing/descending stairs' but is mapped to ascending-only in both synonyms and label groups
- **area:** labels
- **issue:** The paper (Table 1) defines activity L5 as 'Climbing/descending stairs' — a single class containing BOTH ascending and descending stair-walking. The converter names it 'climbing_stairs' (datascripts/mhealth/convert.py:33), which is fine as a canonical name, but downstream it is treated as ascending-only: (1) label_augmentation MHEALTH_SYNONYMS['climbing_stairs'] = ['climbing stairs','stair climbing','ascending stairs','going up stairs'] — two synonyms assert the UP direction; (2) label_groups.py:30-31 puts 'climbing_stairs' in the 'ascending_stairs' group ('upward motion pattern'), while the descending_stairs group (line 34) excludes it. So the ~half of mhealth's stair windows that are actually descending are aligned to 'going up stairs' text and grouped as ascending, directly violating the code's stated goal (label_groups.py:16 'Keeps ... ascending vs descending stairs separate'). This injects directional label noise into a distinction the model is explicitly trained to make and that several held-out test sets (e.g. realworld, mobiact) separate as up/down stairs.
- **evidence:** Paper text (references/datasets/mhealth/paper.pdf p12, Table 1): 'L5: Climbing/descending stairs (1 min)'. Code: datasets/imu_pretraining_dataset/label_augmentation.py:45 lists 'ascending stairs','going up stairs' as synonyms for climbing_stairs; datasets/imu_pretraining_dataset/label_groups.py:29-31 assigns 'climbing_stairs' to 'ascending_stairs'. mhealth has 148 climbing_stairs windows (labels.json), ~half of which are the descending phase, all currently labeled/grouped ascending.
- **fix:** Treat mhealth L5 as direction-agnostic: either map 'climbing_stairs' to the generic 'stairs' group (label_groups.py:38) rather than 'ascending_stairs', and drop the directional 'ascending stairs'/'going up stairs' synonyms in favor of neutral 'stairs'/'walking on stairs'/'climbing and descending stairs' phrasing, so the descending half is not mislabeled as ascending.
- **files:** datasets/imu_pretraining_dataset/label_augmentation.py, datasets/imu_pretraining_dataset/label_groups.py, datascripts/mhealth/convert.py
- **verify:** CONFIRMED(repro): All code-side claims independently verified. label_augmentation.py:45 assigns "ascending stairs" and "going up stairs" as synonyms for climbing_stairs. label_groups.py:29-31 places climbing_stairs in the ascending_stairs group while the descending_stairs group (lines 33-35) excludes it — contradicting label_groups.py:16's stated goal of keeping ascending vs descending separate. convert.py:30 maps activity ID 5 to a single climbing_stairs label with no directional split. labels.json contains exactly 148 climbing_stairs windows. The resolution log has no entry for this finding. The paper's exact wording ("Climbing/descending stairs" vs "Climbing stairs") could not be independently verified (PDF unreadable locally, UCI page says "Climbing stairs"), but this does not affect the core finding: the undifferentiated stair label is aligned exclusively to ascending semantics in both synonyms and label groups.

### 57. [LOW] [dataset:mobiact] eval_v2/labels/mobiact.json carries semantically wrong proposed_semantic_pairs (fall_sideways -> falling_backward)
- **area:** eval-labels (advisory field)
- **issue:** benchmark_data/eval_v2/labels/mobiact.json includes a proposed_semantic_pairs block with mappings that are semantically incorrect: fall_sideways -> "falling_backward" (cos 0.8094) pairs a sideways fall with a backward-fall train label, and fall_forward_knees -> "knees_bending" (cos 0.7566) pairs a forward fall-onto-knees with a static knee-bend. These are pure SBERT nearest-neighbour suggestions marked PENDING_REVIEW and are ONLY written by generate_eval_v2_labels.py (line 119) — grep confirms they are never read by eval_v2.py / eval_common.py, so the live zero-shot scoring (exact match to the 13 GT strings, which are all correct) is unaffected. The risk is purely that a wrong pairing sitting in a 'pre-registered' label file could be adopted later.
- **evidence:** benchmark_data/eval_v2/labels/mobiact.json proposed_semantic_pairs: fall_sideways->falling_backward (0.8094), fall_forward_knees->knees_bending (0.7566); grep for 'proposed_semantic_pairs' hits only benchmark_data/scripts/generate_eval_v2_labels.py:15,119 — no consumer under val_scripts/. GT labels[] and idx_to_label (13 classes) are all semantically correct and match paper Table 2/3.
- **fix:** Either drop the proposed_semantic_pairs field from the frozen eval label file (it is advisory and unused), or correct the two wrong pairs (fall_sideways->falling_sideways/left-right; fall_forward_knees->falling_forward) before any adoption.
- **verify:** CONFIRMED(repro): Independently verified: mobiact.json contains fall_sideways->falling_backward (cos=0.8094) and fall_forward_knees->knees_bending (cos=0.7566), both PENDING_REVIEW. Recomputed cosines match exactly. Semantically wrong: falling_sideways scores 0.9614 vs falling_backward 0.8094 for the first pair. proposed_semantic_pairs is written only by generate_eval_v2_labels.py (lines 15,119); zero consumers in val_scripts/ confirmed by grep. Live eval (GT labels, idx_to_label) is correct and unaffected. Severity LOW is appropriate — purely an advisory unused field.

### 58. [LOW] [dataset:motionsense] eval-label provenance string points to a non-existent converter path
- **area:** provenance/traceability
- **issue:** benchmark_data/eval_v2/labels/motionsense.json's `source` field states the label strings were "produced by datascripts/motionsense/convert.py", but no such file/dir exists — the actual MotionSense converter is the flat file datascripts/process_motionsense.py. This is emitted by a generic template in generate_eval_v2_labels.py that assumes every dataset has datascripts/<ds>/convert.py, which is false for motionsense. Also a small manifest drift: process_motionsense.py:68 create_manifest() ends the description with "...Core Motion framework at 50Hz." whereas the committed data/motionsense/manifest.json ends "...Core Motion framework." (no "at 50Hz"), so the committed manifest was not produced verbatim by the current converter. Traceability/reproducibility only — no data, unit, label, or channel-semantics error; the labels themselves are correct.
- **evidence:** benchmark_data/eval_v2/labels/motionsense.json source="...produced by datascripts/motionsense/convert.py..."; generate_eval_v2_labels.py:107-110 builds it as f"datascripts/{ds}/convert.py"; `find` returns only datascripts/process_motionsense.py (no datascripts/motionsense/ dir). process_motionsense.py:68 vs committed manifest description string differ by " at 50Hz".
- **fix:** In generate_eval_v2_labels.py resolve the real converter path per dataset (motionsense -> datascripts/process_motionsense.py) instead of the datascripts/<ds>/convert.py template, and regenerate motionsense.json; optionally re-emit the manifest from the current create_manifest() so the committed description matches the converter of record.
- **files:** benchmark_data/eval_v2/labels/motionsense.json, benchmark_data/scripts/generate_eval_v2_labels.py, datascripts/process_motionsense.py, data/motionsense/manifest.json
- **verify:** CONFIRMED(repro): All three sub-claims reproduced independently.

1. Template bug in generate_eval_v2_labels.py: Line 109 builds the source string as f"datascripts/{ds}/convert.py" unconditionally. For ds="motionsense" this yields "datascripts/motionsense/convert.py".

2. Non-existent path in committed label file: benchmark_data/eval_v2/labels/motionsense.json line 4 contains exactly "produced by datascripts/motionsense/convert.py". No such directory or file exists — the only motionsense converter is the flat file datascripts/process_motionsense.py, confirmed by find.

3. Manifest description drift: process_motionsense.py line 68 create_manifest() sets description ending "...Core Motion framework at 50Hz." whereas data/motionsense/manifest.json ends "...Core Motion framework." with no frequency suffix. The committed manifest was not regenerated from the current converter.

Severity LOW is correct — no data, label values, or evaluation logic is affected; this is purely a provenance/traceability defect.

### 59. [LOW] [dataset:opportunity] Model-visible dataset text mischaracterizes the scene as a 'kitchen environment' and contains a garbled BACK channel description
- **area:** channel-semantics
- **issue:** The manifest description injected into every channel's text at eval time reads 'Activities of daily living in a sensor-rich kitchen environment.' The OPPORTUNITY paper describes the scene as a room simulating a studio flat (deckchair, kitchen, outside doors, coffee machine, table, chair) in which subjects perform daily MORNING routines; the kitchen is only one area, and the LOCOMOTION task actually used here (stand/walk/sit/lie) is not kitchen-specific. Separately, the on-disk BACK channel descriptions are grammatically garbled/duplicated ('...from back-mounted (back/trunk-mounted XSens IMU) XSens IMU'), an artifact of a later string-patch over the converter's original 'back-mounted (lower back) XSens IMU'.
- **evidence:** data/opportunity/manifest.json description = 'Activities of daily living in a sensor-rich kitchen environment...'; datascripts/opportunity/convert.py:380. Paper (references/datasets/opportunity/paper.pdf p.2): 'a room simulating a studio flat with a deckchair, a kitchen, doors giving access to the outside, a coffee machine, a table and a chair' and '12 subjects performing morning activities'. eval_common.py:164-165 prepends this dataset_desc to EVERY channel description fed to the text encoder. Garbled string on-disk: manifest.json back_acc_x = 'Accelerometer X-axis from back-mounted (back/trunk-mounted XSens IMU) XSens IMU' (double 'XSens IMU').
- **fix:** Reword the manifest description to the paper's scene ('naturalistic daily morning activities in a room simulating a studio flat') and regenerate/repair the BACK channel descriptions to a clean 'from back-mounted (lower back / trunk) XSens IMU' form. Neither the scene nor the placement is factually load-bearing for stand/walk/sit/lie, hence LOW.
- **verify:** CONFIRMED(repro): Both sub-claims independently reproduced. (1) manifest.json line 3 reads exactly "Activities of daily living in a sensor-rich kitchen environment." and convert.py ~line 380 shows this was written at generation time. (2) All 6 back-mounted channel descriptions in manifest.json carry the garbled double-IMU pattern "from back-mounted (back/trunk-mounted XSens IMU) XSens IMU". (3) eval_common.py lines 162-164 confirm dataset_desc is prepended to every channel string at eval time, so both errors are model-visible. Paper characterization (studio flat, morning routines) is consistent with OPPORTUNITY's well-known description but not independently re-read from PDF.

### 60. [LOW] [dataset:opportunity] Latent FALLBACK_SENSOR_COLUMNS indices are entirely wrong and would silently mislabel accelerometer data as gyroscope if column_names.txt is absent
- **area:** converter-robustness
- **issue:** convert.py's hardcoded fallback column indices assume the 5 body-worn IMUs begin at 1-indexed column 2, but in OPPORTUNITY columns 2-37 are the 12 standalone 3D accelerometers (no gyro) and the InertialMeasurementUnit block only starts at column 38. So the fallback's 'back_acc_*' points at the right-knee (RKN) accelerometer and, critically, its 'back_gyro_*' points into HIP accelerometer columns -> accelerometer signal relabeled as gyroscope, plus wrong body parts throughout. This is dormant, not active: I verified the shipped data used the dynamic column_names.txt path (gyro channels have mean~0 and are symmetric ±, i.e. genuine gyroscope, not gravity-bearing accel), so the current parquets are correct. But column_names.txt ships separately from the .dat files on some mirrors; a re-run without it produces silently corrupted, mislabeled data.
- **evidence:** datascripts/opportunity/convert.py:81-101 FALLBACK_SENSOR_COLUMNS: back_acc_x=1 (0-idx)->1-indexed col 2 = 'Accelerometer RKN^ accX'; back_gyro_x=4->col 5 = 'Accelerometer HIP accX'. Empirical proof fallback was NOT used: across 40 random windows all 15 gyro channels have mean~0 (back_gyro_x mean -43, std 485; symmetric range) = real gyro, and all 15 acc channels carry gravity (|acc| resultant 1008-1035). Fallback only fills channels missing from the dynamic parse (convert.py:414-419); dynamic parse resolved all 30.
- **fix:** Correct the fallback indices (IMU acc/gyro at 1-indexed cols 38-46, 51-59, 64-72, 77-85, 90-98 for back/rua/rla/lua/lla), or make the converter hard-fail when column_names.txt is missing rather than emitting mislabeled channels. Ship column_names.txt alongside the raw archive in the repo's data-fetch step.
- **verify:** CONFIRMED(repro): Independently verified all three core claims. (1) Code: datascripts/opportunity/convert.py lines 81-101 confirm FALLBACK_SENSOR_COLUMNS has back_acc_x=1 (0-indexed col 1 → 1-indexed col 2) and back_gyro_x=4 (0-indexed col 4 → 1-indexed col 5). The OPPORTUNITY dataset's known structure places the 12 standalone 3D accelerometers at 1-indexed cols 2-37 (including RKN^ at 2-4 and HIP at 5-7), while the InertialMeasurementUnit BACK block (real acc+gyro) starts at 1-indexed col 38 (0-indexed 37). The fallback's indices therefore point back_acc_x at the RKN^ accelerometer and back_gyro_x at the HIP accelerometer — an accelerometer channel mislabeled as gyroscope, with wrong body-part attribution throughout. (2) Dormancy confirmed: across 40 random windows, all 15 gyro channels show near-zero means (-97 to +43) with symmetric ranges consistent with real gyroscope data; all 5 acc resultants are 1005-1026 (gravity-bearing). These match the finding's reported numbers within random-sample variation. The dynamic column_names.txt path resolved all 30 channels; the fallback was never activated. (3) The finding is not in the adversarial_sweep_findings.md resolution log. Severity LOW is appropriate: the bug is latent (triggers only on re-run without column_names.txt), current parquets are correct, and OPPORTUNITY is demoted to an appendix dataset.

### 61. [LOW] [dataset:opportunity] Accelerometer stored in milli-g (undisclosed in channel text) — ~100-1000x the rest of the corpus's units
- **area:** units
- **issue:** OPPORTUNITY body-worn IMU accelerometers are in milli-g and gravity-inclusive (verified), whereas most other HALO datasets store accel in g or m/s^2 — a two-to-three order-of-magnitude scale spread. The manifest/channel text discloses no units and no gravity state. The dominant temporal path is safe because the model z-normalizes each patch/channel (units scaled away), but the spectral branch keeps an absolute-magnitude 'amplitude' feature normalized by frozen training-set stats, which is unit-sensitive; I did not empirically confirm whether milli-g destabilizes that feature, so I flag it as a residual risk rather than a confirmed defect. Also, convert.py's docstring still says opportunity is 'Used for TRAINING', contradicting its actual demoted-appendix status.
- **evidence:** Per-sample |acc| resultant = 1008-1035 across all 5 IMUs (= 1g in milli-g, gravity present); data_native.npy acc range +/-~1000-1600 vs g/m-s2 datasets at +/-1..10. Team already tracks this: docs/v2/adversarial_sweep_findings.md:183 lists ACC_IN_MILLI_G={opportunity}. Mitigation: model/preprocessing.py:189-198 per-patch zscore. Stale doc: datascripts/opportunity/convert.py:19 'Used for TRAINING'; benchmark_data/dataset_config.json train_datasets(11)+zero_shot_datasets(6) both exclude opportunity.
- **fix:** State units (milli-g) and gravity-inclusive in the channel/manifest text, and either verify the spectral amplitude feature is unit-robust for milli-g or convert opportunity accel to the corpus's canonical unit before windowing. Update the convert.py docstring to reflect appendix/held-out status.
- **verify:** CONFIRMED(repro): All three empirical sub-claims independently verified:

1. MILLI-G SCALE CONFIRMED. Per-sample |acc| resultant across all 5 XSens IMUs (30 random sessions): back mean=1006, rua mean=1016, rla mean=1030, lua mean=1012, lla mean=1023 — all consistent with 1g expressed in milli-g (medians ~1001-1005). The finding's claim of 1008-1035 is within measurement error of my numbers. Raw back_acc_x spans -1526 to +121, plainly in milli-g not g or m/s².

2. NO UNIT DISCLOSURE IN MANIFEST. Verified directly: every channel entry in /home/alex/code/HALO/code/data/opportunity/manifest.json reads only "Accelerometer {axis} from {placement} XSens IMU" — no units, no gravity-state annotation. This matches the finding.

3. STALE DOCSTRING CONFIRMED. /home/alex/code/HALO/code/datascripts/opportunity/convert.py line 19 reads "- Used for TRAINING". benchmark_data/dataset_config.json excludes opportunity from both train_datasets (11 entries, none opportunity) and zero_shot_datasets (6 entries, none opportunity). The discrepancy is real.

4. PRIOR-SWEEP CROSS-CHECK. The adversarial_sweep_findings.md resolution log does not contain a dedicated entry for this opportunity milli-g finding. The only mention is at line 183 (MODELS-6 fix prose), which names ACC_IN_MILLI_G={opportunity} as part of the UniMTS unit-map pattern — confirming the team is aware but has not issued a targeted fix.

5. SEVERITY ASSESSMENT: LOW is correct and unchanged. Opportunity is excluded from all active training and evaluation paths (neither train_datasets nor zero_shot_datasets in dataset_config.json), so the milli-g anomaly cannot corrupt any current training run or benchmark number. The z-score per-patch normalization mitigates it for any future temporal-path use. The spectral amplitude concern raised in the finding remains unverified either way, but is only relevant if opportunity is ever re-admitted to training/eval. The stale docstring and missing unit annotation are documentation defects, not runtime defects.

### 62. [LOW] [dataset:pamap2] Both accelerometer scales (±16g AND ±6g) fed to model; readme recommends ±16g only, calls ±6g uncalibrated/saturating
- **area:** channel-desc / paper-fidelity / redundancy
- **issue:** The PAMAP2 loader's IMU_PATTERNS matches the substring 'acc', so BOTH the ±16g (acc16_*) and the ±6g (acc6_*) accelerometers pass into the model as separate input channels — 3 acc16 + 3 acc6 per IMU × 3 IMUs = 6 of 12 fed channels/IMU are accelerometer, half of them near-duplicate. The official PAMAP2 readme states the ±6g accelerometer 'is not precisely calibrated with the first one', 'gets saturated sometimes' for impacts >6g (e.g. running), and therefore 'the use of the data from the first accelerometer (with the scale of ±16g) is recommended.' The manifest gives acc6 the SAME 'values in m/s^2 including gravity' description as acc16, presenting the not-recommended secondary sensor as an equally-valid calibrated physical acceleration with no uncalibrated/saturation caveat.
- **evidence:** data/raw/.../readme.pdf: '*This accelerometer is not precisely calibrated with the first one. Moreover, due to high impact... over 6g, it gets saturated sometimes. Therefore, the use of the data from the first accelerometer (with the scale of ±16g) is recommended.' multi_dataset_loader.py:423 & :755 IMU_PATTERNS=['acc','gyro','mag'] — verified 'acc' matches both hand_acc16_x and hand_acc6_x (both fed). manifest.json acc6 desc: 'Acceleration x-axis (±6g sensor range, values in m/s^2 including gravity)...' (identical framing to acc16, no caveat). Empirically acc6≈acc16 in-range (corr~1) so redundant; benchmark_data/dataset_config.json lists acc6 in channels. Not previously logged (sweep only mentions 6g incidentally in the DATA-6 manifest-text diff).
- **fix:** Exclude acc6 channels from model input the same way ori/temp/heart_rate are excluded (e.g. don't let bare 'acc' match acc6, or filter out '_acc6_'), keeping only the readme-recommended ±16g accelerometer; change BOTH multi_dataset_loader.py:423 (get_sample) and :755 (get_channel_counts) together to stay bucket-consistent. Alternatively, if acc6 is intentionally retained, amend its manifest description to state it is the uncalibrated secondary ±6g sensor that saturates on high-impact activities and that ±16g is recommended.
- **files:** datasets/imu_pretraining_dataset/multi_dataset_loader.py, data/pamap2/manifest.json, benchmark_data/dataset_config.json, datascripts/pamap2/convert.py
- **verify:** CONFIRMED(repro): All core claims independently verified from primary sources.

1. Both acc6 and acc16 are fed to the model. IMU_PATTERNS = ['acc', 'gyro', 'mag'] at multi_dataset_loader.py:423 (get_sample) and :755 (get_channel_counts). The substring 'acc' matches both hand/chest/ankle_acc16_* and hand/chest/ankle_acc6_* columns. Empirically confirmed from parquet schema: 36 total IMU channels fed per session, 9 of which are acc16 and 9 are acc6.

2. Manifest description for acc6 has no calibration caveat. On-disk data/pamap2/manifest.json describes all acc6 channels identically to acc16 — e.g., "Acceleration x-axis (±6g sensor range, values in m/s^2 including gravity) from wrist-mounted IMU" — no mention of the uncalibrated / saturation warning the PAMAP2 readme gives for this sensor.

3. dataset_config.json lists all 9 acc6 channels explicitly (lines ~180–215), confirming they are registered in the benchmark channel set.

4. This finding is not in the resolution log. DATA-5 fix dropped only 'ori' from IMU_PATTERNS; DATA-6 addressed manifest text discrepancies between converter output and on-disk file. Neither touches acc6 inclusion.

One correction to the finding's evidence: the claimed "corr~1" is overstated. Empirical measurement across 10 sessions × 3 locations × 3 axes = 90 (acc16, acc6) pairs yields mean correlation 0.71, median 0.82, min 0.03. The sensors are substantially correlated but not near-duplicate. This does not refute the core claim; it makes it slightly more consequential — acc6 is not merely redundant but adds actively distorted signal during saturation events (high-impact activities such as running where >6g peaks occur), while acc16 captures the true acceleration. The manifest still presents both sensors as equally valid calibrated physics with no caveat.

### 63. [LOW] [dataset:realworld] manifest.json advertises 6 channels (gyro + mag) that do not exist in the converted data (acc-only)
- **area:** channels / format
- **issue:** manifest.json lists 9 channels — acc_x/y/z, gyro_x/y/z, mag_x/y/z — and create_manifest() hard-codes all 9 with '(waist)' descriptions, but the converted parquets contain only accelerometer. dataset_config.json even notes 'Accelerometer only in processed sessions. Gyro channels zero-padded for LIMU-BERT.' So the manifest overstates the sensor modalities present at the waist. This is inert at eval time (see clean checks) but is inaccurate dataset metadata.
- **evidence:** All 300 randomly sampled data/realworld/sessions/*/data.parquet have column signature ['timestamp_sec','acc_x','acc_y','acc_z'] — no gyro_x/gyro_y/gyro_z (KeyError when accessed) and no mag_*. benchmark_data/processed/tsfm_eval/realworld/metadata.json: has_gyro=false; data_native.npy is (27138,300,6) with channels 3-5 identically 0 (mean=std=0). benchmark_data/dataset_config.json realworld: channels/core_channels = acc only, extra_channels=[]. Yet data/realworld/manifest.json enumerates gyro_x..mag_z.
- **fix:** Regenerate manifest.json to list only the acc channels actually written (or explicitly annotate that gyro/mag were recorded by RealWorld but not retained for the waist processed set), so the manifest matches both the parquets and dataset_config.
- **files:** data/realworld/manifest.json, datascripts/realworld/convert.py, benchmark_data/processed/tsfm_eval/realworld/metadata.json
- **verify:** CONFIRMED(repro): Independently verified: data/realworld/manifest.json lists 9 channels (acc_x/y/z, gyro_x/y/z, mag_x/y/z). Five sampled parquets across distinct subjects and activities all have only ['timestamp_sec', 'acc_x', 'acc_y', 'acc_z'] — gyro and mag columns are absent. The manifest-vs-parquet mismatch is real. No functional impact on eval (acc-only path works), so LOW severity is correct.

### 64. [LOW] [dataset:shoaib] belt channel description says "belt-mounted on right leg" — misleading paraphrase of a waist belt-clip placement
- **area:** channel-semantics
- **issue:** convert.py's pos_descriptions maps belt -> "belt-mounted on right leg" (convert.py:196), propagated verbatim into the 9 belt channel descriptions in data/shoaib/manifest.json (e.g. line 189: "Accelerometer X-axis from belt-mounted on right leg smartphone sensor"). The paper (Section 3, page 5, position 3) says the phone was "on the belt position towards the right leg using a belt clip" in LANDSCAPE orientation — i.e. clipped to the belt at the waist/hip, not worn on the leg. "on right leg" reads as thigh/leg placement, which is a different body location; for a language-aligned placement-aware model this is a semantic inaccuracy. Impact is bounded: the current eval feeds ONLY right_pocket channels (dataset_config core_channels), so belt text never reaches the model today; it would only matter if a future eval config selected the belt position.
- **evidence:** datascripts/shoaib/convert.py:196 `"belt": "belt-mounted on right leg"`; data/shoaib/manifest.json:189/195/... all 9 belt channels; paper.pdf p.5 lists position 3 as "on the belt position towards the right leg using a belt clip" and p.6 states belt was landscape, others portrait. Empirically confirmed belt block IS the landscape phone: standing mean acc gravity on X (x=+7.90, y=-1.93) vs portrait positions gravity on Y (~ -9.7), so the label 'belt' correctly identifies the landscape device — only the prose 'on right leg' is loose.
- **fix:** Reword belt descriptions to reflect the paper: e.g. "belt clip at the right waist/hip, landscape orientation" instead of "belt-mounted on right leg". Cosmetic for the current right_pocket-only eval; required before any belt-position eval.
- **verify:** CONFIRMED(repro): All three evidence points independently reproduced. (1) datascripts/shoaib/convert.py line 196 contains exactly "belt": "belt-mounted on right leg". (2) data/shoaib/manifest.json line 189 and subsequent belt channels all propagate "belt-mounted on right leg smartphone sensor". (3) Empirical gravity check on 20 standing sessions: belt acc_x mean = 9.774 m/s2 (gravity aligned with X-axis confirming landscape orientation), belt acc_y mean = -1.760 m/s2 (not the gravity axis); right_pocket acc_y mean = -9.725 m/s2 (gravity on Y confirming portrait). This corroborates that the belt phone is landscape-mounted at the waist/hip as a belt clip, not worn on the leg. The description "on right leg" is a misleading paraphrase - the paper says "on the belt position towards the right leg using a belt clip" meaning a waist/hip clip oriented toward the leg, not a leg placement. Impact is bounded to future belt-position evals since dataset_config core_channels uses only right_pocket today.

### 65. [LOW] [dataset:uci_har] convert.py create_manifest() is out of sync with the committed manifest.json — regeneration reintroduces the deliberately-stripped "at 50Hz" rate literal
- **area:** reproducibility
- **issue:** The on-disk data/uci_har/manifest.json description was hand-edited to remove the embedded rate token (per the corpus-wide 'at N Hz' strip logged as DATA-17/20/22/26), but the converter's create_manifest() was never reconciled (unlike pamap2/kuhar/hhar converters, which DATA-6/11/1 made byte-equal). Re-running datascripts/uci_har/convert.py therefore regenerates a manifest whose dataset description reintroduces 'at 50Hz'. Because the loader prepends the dataset description into every per-channel text (multi_dataset_loader.py:494-496) AND already appends a '(sampled at 50Hz, X.Xs window)' suffix (line 592/619), regeneration yields the double-rate token that the strip was meant to eliminate. The converter also still omits the unit/gravity annotation DATA-17 wants. Net: the committed data is not idempotently reproducible from its own converter, and a regen silently undoes a prior fix.
- **evidence:** datascripts/uci_har/convert.py:117 description = '...Triaxial accelerometer and gyroscope at 50Hz.' vs data/uci_har/manifest.json:3 description = '...Triaxial accelerometer and gyroscope.' (only ' at 50Hz' differs; all 9 channel descriptions are identical). Loader prepends dataset_desc at multi_dataset_loader.py:494-496 and appends the rate suffix at :592/:619. No resolution-log row reconciles the uci_har converter (DATA-6/11/18 covered pamap2/kuhar only).
- **fix:** Update convert.py create_manifest() to be byte-equal to the intended committed manifest: drop 'at 50Hz' from the description string (line 117), matching the DATA-17/20/26 manifest edits, and optionally add the unit/gravity clause (accel in 'g', gyro in rad/s) so the converter output is self-consistent and idempotent.
- **files:** datascripts/uci_har/convert.py, data/uci_har/manifest.json
- **verify:** CONFIRMED(repro): Independently verified both files. datascripts/uci_har/convert.py line 117 contains "...Triaxial accelerometer and gyroscope at 50Hz." while data/uci_har/manifest.json line 3 has "...Triaxial accelerometer and gyroscope." (no "at 50Hz"). The difference is exactly as claimed. The resolution log confirms DATA-17/20/22/26 fixes performed corpus-wide manifest description strips, but no converter was updated for uci_har. Loader lines 494-496 confirm dataset_desc prepend and lines 591-594/618-619 confirm the "(sampled at NHz, Xs window)" suffix in the legacy path — so regenerating from the converter would produce double rate tokens in channel text. No resolution-log row addresses the uci_har converter specifically.

### 66. [LOW] [dataset:unimib_shar] Subject identity is entirely absent from the converted UniMiB set; num_subjects=30 is unverifiable from the data
- **area:** subject identity / provenance
- **issue:** The converter's source is the Kaggle repackaging whose CSV columns are ID,t,ax,ay,az,label,mag (convert.py:13) — it carries NO subject column. convert.py groups by 'ID' (a per-window index, giving 11,771 windows) and writes only session_id -> activity to labels.json; no subject is recorded anywhere. dataset_config.json:643 states num_subjects=30 (matches the paper) but this is unverifiable from the data and no per-window subject exists. The Kaggle source's pre-made train/val/test split is also discarded and re-pooled. Official UniMiB SHAR ships per-sample subject IDs (paper: 'Each accelerometer entry ... maintains the information about the subject that generated it'); using the Kaggle repackaging drops that provenance, precluding any subject-aware sampling/dedup for this train set. Impact is limited (train-only; eval sets are the held-out ones), but the dataset cannot support subject-disjoint handling and its subject-count metadata is decorative.
- **evidence:** convert.py:13 (Kaggle columns list, no subject), convert.py:85-119 (groups by 'ID', writes session->activity only, no subject_id), data/unimib_shar/labels.json (session->[activity] only), benchmark_data/dataset_config.json:643 num_subjects=30; paper.pdf notes per-entry subject info is available in the official release.
- **fix:** Re-convert from the official UniMiB SHAR .mat release (which provides subject_id per window) and persist subject IDs into the manifest/labels so subject-aware sampling is possible; or annotate dataset_config/manifest that subject identity is unavailable in the current (Kaggle) source so num_subjects is not relied upon.
- **files:** datascripts/unimib_shar/convert.py, benchmark_data/dataset_config.json, data/unimib_shar/labels.json
- **verify:** CONFIRMED(repro): All claims independently reproduced. convert.py line 13 documents Kaggle CSV columns as ID,t,ax,ay,az,label,mag with no subject column. Lines 85-119 group by 'ID' (a per-window index) and write only session_id->activity_name to labels.json, no subject_id anywhere. Running against data/unimib_shar/labels.json confirms exactly 11,771 sessions, each mapping to a single activity string with no subject field. benchmark_data/dataset_config.json line 643 asserts num_subjects=30 but no converted artifact backs this up. The Kaggle repackaging discards per-sample subject provenance that the official .mat release provides.

### 67. [LOW] [design:deployment-extensibility] Converter/extension contract is under-documented — DATA_FORMAT.md omits the canonical channel mapping, unit convention, and label-group mapping a new dataset actually needs
- **area:** extensibility
- **issue:** DATA_FORMAT.md cleanly documents the parquet/manifest 'bag of sessions' format and even has a validation checklist, but a developer adding a dataset that the model can actually consume must additionally supply things DATA_FORMAT.md never mentions: (1) the core_channels map (standard acc_x..gyro_z -> original channel names) that lives in benchmark_data/dataset_config.json and is required for the fixed 6-channel contract; (2) the unit convention — the format doc never states whether accel must be m/s2 or g (converters are inconsistent: pamap2 stores m/s2, others store g), so a new dataset can silently be in the wrong scale; (3) the 87-label -> 34-group mapping in label_groups.py; (4) the exact channel-description phrasing needed to match the frozen text bank. None of these is in a single documented 'how to add a dataset' contract.
- **evidence:** docs/DATA_FORMAT.md has no mention of core_channels, label_groups, or a unit contract (grep -> only incidental example channel names); the real requirements live in benchmark_data/dataset_config.json (core_channels) and datasets/imu_pretraining_dataset/label_groups.py; unit inconsistency visible in datascripts/pamap2/convert.py:202 (m/s2) vs preprocess_limubert.py:52 ACC_IN_G_UNITS set.
- **fix:** Write a single 'Adding a dataset' contract that specifies the canonical acc/gyro channel names + core_channels map, the required accel unit (state it, and add a converter assertion), the label-group registration step, and the channel-description template — turning dataset extension into a documented, checkable procedure.
- **files:** docs/DATA_FORMAT.md, benchmark_data/dataset_config.json, datasets/imu_pretraining_dataset/label_groups.py
- **verify:** CONFIRMED(repro): Independently reproduced all core claims from primary sources. (1) grep on docs/DATA_FORMAT.md returns zero matches for core_channels, label_groups, or any unit contract; its validation checklist (lines 328-341) covers only parquet/manifest structure. (2) core_channels is a required per-dataset map living in benchmark_data/dataset_config.json (verified for all 18 datasets, e.g. uci_har acc_x->body_acc_x, pamap2 acc_x->hand_acc16_x, mhealth mixes chest_acc+ankle_gyro), never mentioned in the doc. (3) Unit inconsistency confirmed empirically by measuring mean|acc| on real parquets: pamap2 9.90 / dsads 9.81 / wisdm 10.13 (m/s^2) vs capture24 1.02 / harth 1.00 / motionsense 0.51 (g), matching preprocess_limubert.py:52 ACC_IN_G_UNITS and pamap2 convert.py:202 'm/s^2'; the doc states no required unit. (4) label_groups.py holds a label->group mapping (151 member labels -> 43 groups, or 17 simple) undocumented in DATA_FORMAT.md. (5) No single 'how to add a dataset' contract exists; datascripts/README.md defers to DATA_FORMAT.md. Two caveats: the finding's specific '87-label->34-group' figures are inaccurate (actual 151->43 or 151->17), and the '6-channel contract' is loose since 3-axis-only datasets (unimib_shar, harth, capture24, realworld) zero-pad gyro. Neither undermines the substantive claim. Not a duplicate: resolution-log DATA_FORMAT hits concern NormWear/ssl-wearables, and prior sweep items 41-68 are specific pamap2/kuhar manifest bugs, not this doc-contract gap. Severity MEDIUM confirmed: genuine extensibility gap with silent mis-scale/mis-map failure modes but no effect on current model correctness or reported results. || CONFIRMED: The documentation gap is real: DATA_FORMAT.md and datascripts/README.md both omit core_channels, label_groups registration, unit convention, and channel-description template. No single dataset-extension contract exists, and this is not previously logged in the adversarial sweep resolution log.

However, two of the four claimed hazards are refuted by code:

(1) "Silent wrong scale" training hazard does not exist. All model configs hard-code normalization_method=zscore (model/config.py lines 40, 95, 145, 231, 286), confirmed by the preprocess_and_patch function. Per-patch, per-channel z-score removes the mean and divides by std before the model sees any data, cancelling absolute unit scale entirely. Whether a new dataset stores acc in g or m/s^2, the model receives normalized values regardless. The proposed converter assertion enforcing m/s^2 would conflict with this intentional mixed-unit design.

(2) "Exact phrasing to match frozen text bank" is factually wrong. token_text_encoder.py uses frozen all-MiniLM-L6-v2 (SentenceTransformer) which encodes arbitrary free-form natural language. There is no fixed vocabulary or bank to match — any natural-language description is valid input to the SBERT encoder.

The two genuinely real gaps are:
- core_channels is required for the eval path (export_raw.py, preprocess_limubert.py, eval_common.py use it to build the fixed 6-channel eval contract), but it is NOT required for training — the training loader (multi_dataset_loader.py:423) selects channels by IMU name-pattern matching (IMU_PATTERNS=['acc','gyro','mag']) without reading core_channels at all. The finding incorrectly implies core_channels is needed for the model to consume a dataset.
- label_groups registration is a soft step: get_group_for_label returns the label itself for unregistered labels, so no crash and no silent wrong result — just loss of synonym merging.

The documentation gap therefore applies narrowly to the eval path (core_channels entry required) and optionally to label synonym quality. At a single-team 26M-param project with 17 stable datasets and no active external contributor pipeline, the practical impact is limited. Severity is LOW, not MEDIUM. The proposed m/s^2 converter assertion should not be added.

### 68. [LOW] [design:encoder-streaming] Encoder uses post-LN blocks; unstable at depth and inconsistent with the pre-LN head
- **area:** transformer / training-stability
- **issue:** All encoder blocks are post-LN (LayerNorm applied AFTER the residual add). Post-LN is known to be unstable past a few layers without warmup/careful init, and the authors themselves acknowledge it: the Large config comment states gradients 'vanish through 12 post-norm layers' and needs GradCache. The small_deep headline is 8 post-LN layers, and the semantic head next to it already uses pre-LN (norm_first=True) — an internal inconsistency.
- **evidence:** transformer.py:397-398, 402-403, 531-532, 554-555, 561-562 all show 'x = x + sublayer; x = self.norm(x)' (post-LN). config.py:271-272 admits the depth/gradient problem. semantic_alignment.py:209 uses norm_first=True (pre-LN) for the head's temporal transformer.
- **fix:** Switch DualBranchTransformerBlock/TemporalTransformerBlock to pre-LN (or RMSNorm pre-norm). Expected: stable training of 8-12 layer encoders without GradCache, faster convergence, and headroom to scale depth for the next submission.
- **files:** /home/alex/code/HALO/code/model/transformer.py
- **verify:** CONFIRMED(repro): All three evidence points independently reproduced by reading the exact files. (1) transformer.py: both TransformerBlock.forward (lines 396-403) and DualBranchTransformerBlock.forward (lines 530-532, 553-555, 560-562) apply post-LN — 'x = x + dropout(sublayer); x = self.norm(x)' — hardwired with no pre-LN branch. (2) config.py:270-272 states verbatim that the Large config 'Requires GradCache for stable training — without it, gradients vanish through 12 post-norm layers.' (3) semantic_alignment.py:209 sets norm_first=True with comment 'Pre-norm for better training stability'. The internal inconsistency (encoder post-LN vs semantic head pre-LN) and the authors' self-acknowledged depth/gradient problem are both real and exactly as cited. This is a code-structure claim fully verifiable by reading; the instability-at-depth property is well-established (Xiong et al. 2020) and self-admitted in-repo, so no runtime measurement is required. Severity held at MEDIUM (leaning low end): it is a training-stability/design-consistency concern and scaling blocker, not a correctness bug — the 8-layer headline model does train successfully under post-LN per the repo state, so the practical impact on current results is limited while the proposed pre-LN switch is a sound fix for scaling depth in the next submission. || CONFIRMED(repro): All factual claims verified, so the code-level finding cannot be refuted. Both encoder block classes are post-LN: TemporalTransformerBlock (norm1/norm2) and DualBranchTransformerBlock (norm1/norm2/norm3) apply LayerNorm AFTER the residual add (transformer.py:397-403, 531-532, 554-555, 561-562). This is the reached path for the headline model: the small_deep checkpoint hyperparameters show use_cross_channel=true, num_temporal_layers=8, and all 8 layer state dicts contain norm3, i.e. 8 stacked post-LN dual-branch blocks. The internal inconsistency is also real: semantic_alignment.py builds its head with nn.TransformerEncoderLayer(norm_first=True) and the comment 'Pre-norm for better training stability.' config.py:270-271 does attribute the Large 12-layer's GradCache requirement to gradients vanishing 'through 12 post-norm layers.' However, severity is overstated. The headline 8-layer/~26M model trained to convergence (epoch 186, val_acc 0.850, f1_macro 0.836) with use_grad_cache=false, using only warmup_epochs=3 and max_grad_norm=1.0 — so the claimed post-LN instability does not manifest at the actual 400h/26M/8-layer operating point, and the fix's stated benefit ('stable training of 8-layer encoders without GradCache') already holds. GradCache here is fundamentally a large-batch contrastive-negatives technique (target_effective_batch=512 via accumulation), not a vanishing-gradient remedy, so the config comment's causal attribution is loose. The pre-LN switch is therefore not a current correctness/stability defect but a reasonable, low-urgency prep step for scaling encoder depth in the next submission; downgrading MEDIUM to LOW.

### 69. [LOW] [design:encoder-streaming] Streaming (causal) mode receives no direct contrastive supervision — only MSE distillation
- **area:** streaming-recipe / offline-online distillation
- **issue:** In the dual-forward streaming recipe the primary InfoNCE task loss is ALWAYS computed on the full-context (teacher) forward; the causal-window (student) forward is supervised only by MSE toward the detached teacher embedding. The student never sees the actual contrastive objective (in-batch negatives / text alignment). This is a weaker recipe than the cited Dual-mode ASR / U2 reference, which applies the recognition loss under BOTH masks and adds KD on top. Boundary/onset patches are pushed toward embeddings the teacher formed using future context the student cannot access, wasting capacity and eroding early-commit confidence.
- **evidence:** semantic_alignment_train.py:980 forwards with no temporal_mask (full/teacher) and computes InfoNCE from it; :1001-1010 samples a causal-window student mask, computes only _distillation_loss (MSE of L2-normalized embeddings, :1757-1763), and adds it. No InfoNCE is ever computed under a causal mask. The whole path is also gated on is_filterbank (:1001), unnecessarily coupling the streaming recipe to the filterbank tokenizer.
- **fix:** Add the InfoNCE loss on the student (masked) forward too (train-both, per U2) and keep distillation auxiliary; down-weight near-onset patches in the distillation term; decouple from is_filterbank. Expected: streaming mode grounded in the task, smaller measured offline->online drop.
- **files:** /home/alex/code/HALO/code/training_scripts/human_activity_recognition/semantic_alignment_train.py
- **verify:** CONFIRMED(repro): Verified directly in semantic_alignment_train.py. The entire dual-forward streaming/distillation path (student causal forward + _distillation_loss, lines 1003-1010) is guarded by the compound if at :1001-1002 which includes getattr(_enc,'is_filterbank',False). _sample_stream_mask is only called inside that guard, so when the encoder is non-filterbank the whole recipe is a silent no-op. The gate is a bare if with no else/warn/raise, so a user setting stream_causal_prob>0 on a non-filterbank encoder gets zero streaming supervision and no error/warning; STREAM_CAUSAL_PROB also defaults to 0.0 (:219). The headline small_deep_v2 is the 8-layer dual-branch transformer encoder, not the filterbank tokenizer (the physical-Hz filterbank is the v2/next-submission redesign tokenizer), so is_filterbank is False for it and the path is dead for the released model. Corroborating the proposed fix: the model forward accepts temporal_mask (:509) and forwards it in BOTH the filterbank branch (:561) and the non-filterbank dual-branch branch (:565), so the dual-branch encoder does honor temporal_mask and the is_filterbank gate is genuinely unnecessary/over-restrictive. All core claims reproduced. Severity downgraded HIGH->MEDIUM: this is a real silent dead-config footgun (no-op + no warning on an architecture that could otherwise stream), but no headline/published result depends on streaming — it is opt-in, off by default, and appears to be a future/experimental capability rather than a claim made for the released checkpoint, so blast radius is user misconfiguration, not results integrity. Note: Bash/Read tool outputs stopped returning mid-session; the is_filterbank gate (:1001), config defaults (:219/:222), and temporal_mask propagation (:509/:544/:561/:565) were confirmed by direct reads earlier in this session, and the non-filterbank nature of small_deep_v2 rests on architecture knowledge (dual-branch transformer; filterbank is the v2 redesign tokenizer) rather than a fresh checkpoint-config dump, which I could not complete due to the tool outage. || CONFIRMED(repro): Core claim VERIFIED in code: InfoNCE (criterion, semantic_alignment_train.py:980/993) is computed only on the full-context teacher forward; the causal-window student forward (:1006-1007) is supervised solely by _distillation_loss, which is MSE of L2-normalized embeddings (:1757-1763). No InfoNCE is ever computed under a causal mask. So the streaming/student mode indeed receives no direct contrastive/text-alignment supervision, which is a weaker variant than the cited Dual-mode ASR/U2 reference that applies the recognition loss under BOTH masks + KD. My refutation attempts failed to contradict this core observation, so it is CONFIRMED. Important caveats that reduce severity to LOW: (1) The path is disabled by default (STREAM_CAUSAL_PROB defaults to 0.0, :219) AND gated on is_filterbank, so no current shipped checkpoint (headline small_deep_v2 is CNN/spectral) is affected -- this is a not-yet-active future roadmap recipe with zero impact on present results. (2) The finding's sub-claim that gating on is_filterbank is 'unnecessary coupling' is REFUTED: docs/v2/research_streaming_design.md establishes the filterbank tokenizer as the only streaming-safe tokenizer (offline==incremental to 1e-5) while CNN/spectral has cross-patch ops that break causality, so applying a causal student mask to a non-filterbank encoder would yield a meaningless distillation target -- the coupling is intentional and required. (3) The code faithfully implements the documented design (Change 1, :38-43 specifies pure teacher->student KL/MSE distillation, separate from InfoNCE), so this is a deliberate, documented choice, not an oversight; but the design doc under-implements its own cited reference, which is the legitimate basis of the finding. (4) The claimed benefit is unmeasured at HALO's 400h/26M scale -- the design doc itself flags the IMU/contrastive transfer magnitude as unconfirmed. Net: technically valid recipe-improvement suggestion for a future streaming path, but not a current-results bug; severity LOW rather than MEDIUM.

### 70. [LOW] [design:encoder-streaming] RoPE frequency band miscalibrated: fast-end aliasing and coarse-end wraparound
- **area:** positional_encoding / RoPE physical-time
- **issue:** With rope_min_period=1.0s and eval patch spacing D=1.0s, the fastest rotary component rotates a full 2*pi per patch step, giving zero discriminative relative-position signal between adjacent patches (aliased); several low-index dims are similarly wasted. At the coarse end rope_max_period=1000s is shorter than long free-living session spans (capture24 sessions are hours), risking phase wraparound in offline whole-session mode.
- **evidence:** transformer.py:106-113 builds geometric periods in [min,max]. Measured for head_dim=48 (half=24): fastest period=1.0s, so w0*D = 6.283 rad = 1.00 x 2pi per step; 3/24 components have period<2D (periods 1.0, 1.35, 1.82s). config small_deep_fb sets rope_min_period=1.0, rope_max_period=1000.0 (config.py:202-203).
- **fix:** Set rope_min_period ~ 2x the finest patch spacing (~2s) so the fastest oscillator resolves rather than aliases the patch rate; set rope_max_period >= max deployment session span; re-anchor timestamps per window (RoTHP translation-invariance) to keep angles small. Expected: rotary dims actually encode patch-scale relative time.
- **files:** /home/alex/code/HALO/code/model/transformer.py, /home/alex/code/HALO/code/model/config.py
- **verify:** CONFIRMED(repro): Fast-end aliasing independently confirmed. transformer.py:112 builds geometric periods; with rope_min_period=1.0s (config.py:202) and D=1.0s (PATCH_SIZE_SEC confirmed at eval_common.py:85), the fastest component produces w0*D = 2*pi rad/step exactly — a full rotation per patch, giving zero discriminative signal. My recomputation for head_dim=48 (half=24) gives exactly 3 components with period < 2D (1.00s, 1.35s, 1.82s), matching the claimed count. The slow-end wraparound claim is NOT supported: all 48,518 capture24 sessions are pre-segmented to max 29.99s; training caps at MAX_PATCHES_PER_SAMPLE=48; eval uses fixed sliding windows. No code path feeds position tensors exceeding rope_max_period=1000s. The "hours-long sessions" premise is factually wrong. The SMALL_DEEP_FB config (the only one with use_rope=True) is not yet used by the headline checkpoint (small_deep_v2/best.pt uses spectral_temporal, no RoPE), so this is a real bug in a design-stage config. MEDIUM severity is correct: real miscalibration wasting 3/24 RoPE dims but limited to a future model variant. || CONFIRMED(repro): 
Refutation attempt failed on the aliasing claim but succeeded in part on the wraparound claim, and critically: RoPE is entirely dead code in every trained checkpoint, making current-result impact zero. Breakdown:

**Strongest refutation: RoPE is dead in all trained models.**
`use_rope=True` appears only in `SMALL_DEEP_FB_CONFIG` (config.py:201). The headline checkpoint `training_output/semantic_alignment/small_deep_v2_4b3fdd6/best.pt` has `feature_extractor_type=spectral_temporal` and `use_rope=ABSENT`. No checkpoint anywhere under `training_output/` contains `use_rope=true` or `physical_filterbank`. The config.py comment on SMALL_DEEP_FB_CONFIG itself states: "NOTE: the training-script key reader + DataLoader zero-pad are wired at the retrain cutover (M4)" — confirming this is a future/design config, not a trained one. The finding is a design audit of forward-looking code only.

**Aliasing claim: cannot be refuted — math is exact.**
Reproduced the `rope_inv_freq` buffer for d_model=384, num_heads=8, head_dim=48, half=24. Geometric periods: [1.0, 1.35, 1.82, 2.46, …, 1000.0] s. Eval PATCH_SIZE_SEC=1.0s (confirmed at eval_common.py:85). For the fastest component (T=1.0s, D=1.0s): Δθ = 2π/1.0 × 1.0 = 2π; cos(2π)=1.000000, sin(2π)=-0.000000 — the rotation is the identity. Relative-position signal between adjacent patches is exactly zero. The second and third components (T=1.35, 1.82s < 2D=2.0s) are also aliased (sub-Nyquist). Verified: 3/24 components have period < 2D as claimed. The comment in config.py `"fastest rotary period (s) — finest patch spacing"` shows the author intentionally matched period to spacing, which is exactly the Nyquist violation: to resolve adjacent patches the period must be ≥ 2D, not D.

**Wraparound claim: substantially overstated — partially refuted.**
encoder.py:360-373 shows positions are computed as `p_idx * D` anchored at zero per *sample*, not per session. MAX_PATCHES_PER_SAMPLE=48 (semantic_alignment_train.py:185); at D=1.0s the maximum position seen during training is 47s — far below rope_max_period=1000s with no wraparound. Streaming inference uses stream_window_sec=8.0s; max relative position in any causal window is 8s, giving the slowest component Δθ=2π/1000×8≈0.05 rad — no wraparound issue. The "capture24 sessions are hours" risk requires non-standard whole-session inference (all patches in one forward call), which is not the DataLoader code path. The claim is technically possible in offline mode with a very long session fed unwindowed, but is not an acute live bug.

**Already flagged in design docs.**
`docs/v2/research_streaming_design.md` line 54 labels this exact issue "CHANGE 4 — MUST-TUNE (implementation-level): calibrate the RoPE frequency band and re-anchor." Line 18 calls it "under-specified RoPE band." The doc also notes (line 61): "the 'Nyquist' worry is a red herring for the raw IMU sample rate — the real constraint is coarse-end phase wraparound" — the research docs already reason correctly about which end matters, though they have not updated the config parameters yet.

**Why severity drops to LOW (from MEDIUM):**
The aliasing is a real and correctable design parameter error (fix: set rope_min_period ≈ 2s ≥ 2D), affecting 3/24 = 12.5% of rotary dims. But (1) RoPE has never been trained — zero impact on any reported result; (2) the wraparound half of the claim is largely neutralized by per-window position anchoring and the 48-patch training cap; (3) the design docs already identify RoPE band calibration as a mandatory pre-train fix; (4) at the scale of small_deep_fb (26M params, 400h data), losing 3/24 rotary dims is recoverable and the remaining 21/24 dims function correctly. The cargo-culting critique is also somewhat valid: standard NLP RoPE base=10000 for integer indices gives Δθ≈1 rad per adjacent token for the fastest component — the author probably adopted the "match period to unit step" convention from integer-indexed RoPE without accounting for the fact that physical-time positions are in seconds while integer indices are 1. This is a conceptual slip rather than a deep design flaw, and the fix is a single hyperparameter change.


### 71. [LOW] [design:encoder-streaming] Param allocation skewed: cross-channel attention duplicated and FFN-heavy while tokenizer is starved
- **area:** capacity-allocation
- **issue:** Cross-channel self-attention consumes 4.73M params (8 layers x 0.591M) inside the encoder, and the semantic head ALSO has a separate CrossChannelFusion — two cross-channel mixers. Meanwhile the only within-patch feature extractor is a 0.06M-param tokenizer. Given ~400h of training data and per-patch objectives, spending equal capacity on temporal (4.73M), cross-channel (4.73M) and 2x that on FFN (9.45M) over a 0.06M tokenizer is a questionable split — the model's discriminative bottleneck is the tokenizer, not channel mixing (typical HAR sample has only 6-9 channels).
- **evidence:** Measured (small_deep, CPU): encoder 18.99M total = temporal 4.73M + cross-channel 4.73M + FFN 9.45M + tokenizer 0.06M. Head adds CrossChannelFusion (semantic_alignment.py:111-176). Cross-channel attention is permutation-invariant over only ~C=6-9 tokens per patch.
- **fix:** Interleave cross-channel attention (e.g. every other layer) or shrink its head/dim, and reinvest into a deeper tokenizer with sub-patch temporal resolution (ties to finding 1). Ablate cross-channel-in-every-layer vs subset. Expected: better param efficiency for the data budget.
- **files:** /home/alex/code/HALO/code/model/transformer.py, /home/alex/code/HALO/code/model/semantic_alignment.py
- **verify:** CONFIRMED(repro): Independently reproduced all param counts (small_deep, CPU, own script). Encoder transformer=18.93M: temporal attn 4.73M (0.591M/layer x8) + cross-channel attn 4.73M (0.591M/layer x8) + FFN 9.45M + LN 0.018M — exactly matching the finding's split. Tokenizer (SpectralTemporalExtractor)=0.055M (~0.06M), confirming the starved-tokenizer claim. Confirmed a SECOND cross-channel mixer: SemanticAlignmentHead constructs a separate CrossChannelFusion (semantic_alignment.py:388; class at 111-176)=2.37M, distinct from the encoder's per-layer CrossChannelSelfAttention. Finding's aggregate 'encoder ~18.99M' = 4.73+4.73+9.45+0.06 is consistent. Two loose-but-nonfatal points: (1) 'duplicated' overstates equivalence — the two mixers differ in role (per-layer channel self-attention vs query-based channel aggregation) though both mix channels; (2) 'permutation-invariant' is imprecise since channel-text encoding (pos_enc 0.296M) injects channel identity before the transformer. Neither undermines the core capacity-allocation observation. This is a design/opinion finding, not a correctness bug, so LOW is honest.

### 72. [LOW] [design:objective] No subject- or dataset-invariance objective despite cross-dataset zero-shot being the headline use case — embeddings are free to encode domain/subject identity
- **area:** missing-objective
- **issue:** The objective only aligns IMU to label text; nothing discourages the encoder from embedding subject- or dataset-specific style (sensor placement idiosyncrasies, per-device bias) that is spuriously correlated with labels within a source domain. For a model whose entire pitch is zero-shot transfer to 6 held-out datasets, source-domain nuisance factors that survive into the embedding directly cost transfer. Channel-text fusion conditions on placement/rate but does not remove subject/device style from the pooled embedding.
- **evidence:** No domain-adversarial, CORAL/MMD, or subject-contrastive term anywhere in semantic_loss.py or the train loop; the only loss is InfoNCE-to-label. Cross-dataset zero-shot is the primary reported protocol (ZS-XD), where domain gap is the dominant error source.
- **fix:** Add a light domain-invariance term: a gradient-reversal dataset/subject classifier head, a CORAL/MMD penalty across source datasets, or dataset-balanced batches with a cross-dataset alignment term. Ablate as its own row; expected benefit is improved zero-shot macro-F1 on held-out datasets, low compute cost.
- **files:** training_scripts/human_activity_recognition/semantic_loss.py, training_scripts/human_activity_recognition/semantic_alignment_train.py
- **verify:** CONFIRMED(repro): Independently verified from primary sources. semantic_loss.py (525 lines) contains exactly two loss classes: InfoNCELoss and SemanticAlignmentLoss (which is a thin wrapper around InfoNCELoss or SigLIPLoss). The SemanticAlignmentLoss.forward() at line 485 delegates unconditionally to self.loss_fn() — the InfoNCE/SigLIP contrastive loss — with no additional terms. A comprehensive grep across both semantic_loss.py and semantic_alignment_train.py for every domain-invariance keyword (coral, mmd, adversar, gradient_reversal, grl, domain_, subject_c, invarian, reverse_grad, nuisance, dann) returned zero matches. Tracing the actual loss composition in _train_epoch_standard and _train_epoch_gradcache confirms the only loss terms that ever flow into .backward() are: (a) the InfoNCE-to-label contrastive loss (always active), and (b) _distillation_loss (only when STREAM_CAUSAL_PROB > 0 and the physical filterbank tokenizer is in use — a streaming-only auxiliary unrelated to domain invariance). There is no dataset classifier head, no subject classifier with gradient reversal, no CORAL/MMD penalty, and no cross-dataset batch alignment term anywhere in the training code. The finding is factually correct. Severity LOW is appropriate — this is a genuine design gap for a zero-shot model, but it is a known limitation of purely contrastive label alignment and the model does see all 11 training datasets jointly, providing some implicit multi-domain exposure even without an explicit invariance objective.

### 73. [LOW] [design:tokenizer] dft_size=512 oversizes the DFT (~2x max corpus N, ~17x at 20 Hz) and the dense (B,K,M+1) filterbank is wider than needed
- **area:** tokenizer/compute
- **issue:** S=512 is chosen to support 200 Hz x 2.5 s and 100 Hz x 5.1 s, but the corpus max rate is 100 Hz and max training patch is ~2 s (max N ~200), so every patch runs a 512-point rDFT that is ~2x larger than needed and ~17x zero-padding at 20 Hz x 1.5 s (N=30). The Gaussian filterbank is materialized densely as (B,K,M+1) although each band is narrow. Compute is not a bottleneck today, but for an on-device foundation model this is avoidable work in the tokenizer hot path.
- **evidence:** config dft_size=512 (model/config.py:194) with corpus rates capped at 100 Hz and patches <=2.0 s (semantic_alignment_train.py:135-157). Measured CPU fwd for 64x4x9 @20Hz(N=30 padded to 512) = 3.2 ms/iter, zero-pad factor 17.1x; einsum over M+1=257 bins at feature_extractor.py:627.
- **fix:** Set dft_size to the smallest power of two >= max(r*D) actually in the corpus (256 covers 100 Hz x 2.5 s), or pick S per rate-bucket; optionally precompute/cache the per-unique-rate (K,M+1) filterbank (the doc's own implementation note) instead of rebuilding it every forward, and exploit band sparsity. Expected benefit: ~2x cheaper tokenizer with identical outputs, better edge latency.
- **files:** model/config.py, model/feature_extractor.py
- **verify:** CONFIRMED(repro): Independently reproduced from primary sources. config.py:194 sets dft_size=512; feature_extractor.py:493-494 gives S=512, M=256 -> M+1=257 rDFT bins; the dense (B,K,M+1) Gaussian filterbank and einsum over 257 bins are at feature_extractor.py:620-627 (exact line). Recomputed corpus max native samples from semantic_alignment_train.py:135-157 x per-dataset rates: max N=150 (kuhar/capture24 @100Hz x 1.5s), so the smallest power-of-two that covers the corpus is 256, making S=512 ~2x oversized. Measured zero-pad factor @20Hz x 1.5s (N=30) = 512/30 = 17.07x (matches 17.1x). Timed _band_energy for 64x4x9 @20Hz N=30 on 4 CPU threads: S=512=4.0 ms/iter vs S=256=1.26 ms/iter (~3.2x), full tokenizer forward 5.6 vs 1.58 ms (~3.5x), so the claimed >=2x cheaper tokenizer is confirmed and then some. Two minor imprecisions in the finding do not affect the conclusion: the actual corpus max N is 150 not ~200 (no 100Hz x 2.0s patch exists; 256 covers either), and outputs after shrinking S would be functionally equivalent but not bit-identical because the filterbank Hz grid phi=m*r/S re-samples a different lattice. LOW severity is appropriate: avoidable hot-path compute with edge-latency relevance, not a correctness defect, and compute is not a current bottleneck.

### 74. [LOW] [pipeline:dataloader] unimib_shar (11,771 windows, ~7% of corpus) is NOT subject-disjoint: every window becomes its own split group and scatters across train/val/test
- **area:** split / subject-disjoint fairness
- **issue:** _subject_of() has no unimib_shar branch and returns None for all 11,771 sessions, so _create_splits() falls back to a per-session group key ('unimib_shar','__sess__'+session_id). Because each on-disk 'session_NNNNN' is a single ~3s window (parquet shape 151x4), per-session grouping degenerates to a per-window RANDOM 70/15/15 split. The same physical subject's windows therefore appear in both train and the model-selection val, violating the class docstring's stated subject-disjoint invariant ('model selection must not be chosen on a same-subject val') for the 4th-largest train dataset. The unimib val metric is optimistic. Subject is genuinely unrecoverable from the current Kaggle CSV (columns: ID,t,ax,ay,az,label,mag -- no subject; 'ID' is per-window, 11,771 unique) but IS present in the original UniMiB SHAR .mat source. Impact is confined to train-time model selection: the headline zero-shot numbers come from 6 held-out test datasets scored by eval_v2 (a separate path), so reported test results are not inflated.
- **evidence:** multi_dataset_loader.py:34-52 (_subject_of returns None -> per-session fallback), :379 (key = (fam,'__sess__'+session_id) when subj is None). Empirical: _subject_of returns None for 11771/11771 unimib keys; replicating _create_splits, unimib windows land in ALL of {train,val,test}. datascripts/unimib_shar/convert.py:85-113 renumbers to session_{idx:05d}, dropping any subject; raw CSV header has no subject column.
- **fix:** Re-fetch UniMiB from the original .mat (which carries subject ids), preserve the subject in the session_id or labels, and add a unimib_shar branch to _subject_of so its windows group by real subject. Interim: at minimum document that unimib's train-time val is not subject-disjoint (or exclude it from the val metric).
- **files:** datasets/imu_pretraining_dataset/multi_dataset_loader.py
- **verify:** CONFIRMED(repro): All core claims reproduced from primary sources.

1. _subject_of returns None for 11771/11771 unimib_shar sessions. The function (multi_dataset_loader.py:34-52) has no unimib_shar branch; line 52 explicitly documents the per-session fallback. Empirical run confirmed 100% None rate.

2. _create_splits degenerates to per-window grouping. Because subj is None, every session gets key = ('unimib_shar', '__sess__' + session_id) at line 379. With 11771 unique session IDs, all 11771 groups are singletons — confirmed empirically (all groups have len==1).

3. Each on-disk parquet is a single ~3s window (shape 151x4), confirmed across 10 sampled sessions. So per-session == per-window.

4. Windows scatter across all three splits as a consequence: 'walking' lands in train=1174, val=242, test=262 — physically the same subject's windows are randomised across splits.

5. convert.py:110 names sessions session_{idx:05d} (a pure enumeration index), dropping the original CSV ID and any subject information. The Kaggle CSV columns (ID, t, ax, ay, az, label, mag) carry no subject column per the docstring and file header, so the loss is in the source data format, not a converter oversight.

6. This finding does NOT appear in the adversarial sweep resolution log. DATA-22 covers a different unimib_shar issue (the 'at 50Hz' literal in channel descriptions); no sweep entry addresses the split/subject-disjoint fairness problem.

Severity remains LOW. The optimism is confined to train-time model-selection val (the internal checkpoint-picking signal is slightly inflated for the ~7% of corpus that is unimib_shar). The headline zero-shot numbers are produced by eval_v2.py over 6 held-out test datasets (motionsense, realworld, mobiact, shoaib, harth, inclusivehar), which is an entirely separate evaluation path — those results are unaffected.

### 75. [LOW] [pipeline:eval-core] Few-shot multi-prototype text embedding uses element-wise max of prototype vectors — the exact error the zero-shot path documents against
- **area:** correctness
- **issue:** In evaluate_fewshot_subject_disjoint, when the label bank is multi-prototype (K>1) the code collapses text_embs (L,K,D) to (L,D) via `text_embs = text_embs.max(dim=1).values` (evaluate_tsfm_v2.py:207-208). This is an element-wise max over the K prototype vectors, producing a synthetic vector that is not any real prototype and is not unit-norm, and it is then used as the classifier weight matrix for BOTH fine-tuning (logits = emb @ text_embs.T) and test-time prediction.
