The zero-shot mean-pool path just above (lines 159-162) deliberately does the CORRECT thing for the identical multi-prototype case — max over the *similarity* per prototype (einsum then .max(dim=2)) — and its inline comment explicitly warns 'max similarity OVER prototypes, not an elementwise max of the prototype vectors'. The FS path does precisely that forbidden element-wise max, so any K>1 checkpoint's FS-1%/FS-10% numbers would be scored against a corrupted text target inconsistent with its own ZS scoring.
- **evidence:** evaluate_tsfm_v2.py:208 `text_embs = text_embs.max(dim=1).values` (element-wise, over dim K) vs evaluate_tsfm_v2.py:160-162 which max-reduces the similarity not the vectors. Verified the headline checkpoint hyperparameters.json has label_bank.num_prototypes=1 (load_label_bank passes it through), so encode() returns (L,D), text_embs.dim()==2, and the buggy branch is DEAD for the reported headline results — hence LOW. It becomes live for any num_prototypes>1 checkpoint (LearnableLabelBank.encode returns (batch,K,d_model) when num_prototypes>1, model/token_text_encoder.py:505-506).
- **fix:** Mirror the ZS path: keep text_embs as (L,K,D) and compute logits by max-over-prototype similarity, e.g. logits = torch.einsum('nd,lkd->nlk', emb, text_embs).max(dim=2).values / temperature (and likewise at test time), instead of element-wise max-collapsing the prototype vectors.
- **files:** /home/alex/code/HALO/code/val_scripts/human_activity_recognition/evaluate_tsfm_v2.py
- **verify:** CONFIRMED(repro): Lines 207-208 of evaluate_tsfm_v2.py contain exactly the described bug: `text_embs = text_embs.max(dim=1).values` is an element-wise max over dim K that collapses (L,K,D) to (L,D), producing a synthetic non-unit-norm vector used as the classifier for both fine-tuning and test-time logits. The ZS path at lines 159-162 does the correct reduction (max over similarity scores via einsum, not vectors), with an explicit inline comment warning against the exact error the FS path commits. token_text_encoder.py lines 505-506 confirm the buggy branch is only entered when num_prototypes>1. The headline checkpoint hyperparameters.json confirms num_prototypes=1, so encode() returns (L,D), dim==3 is False, and the branch is dead for all reported headline results.

### 76. [LOW] [pipeline:loss] SemanticAlignmentLoss(loss_type="siglip") crashes: SigLIPLoss is referenced but never defined
- **area:** loss-code-quality (semantic_loss.py)
- **issue:** SemanticAlignmentLoss.__init__ instantiates `SigLIPLoss(temperature=temperature)` when loss_type=="siglip", and both the class and SemanticAlignmentLoss docstrings advertise "Supports both InfoNCE and SigLIP loss functions." But no `SigLIPLoss` class is defined anywhere in the repo, so selecting the SigLIP branch raises NameError at construction. It is a dead/broken alternative loss path. Not results-affecting because the default and headline config use loss_type="infonce".
- **evidence:** semantic_loss.py:437-438 `if loss_type == "siglip": self.loss_fn = SigLIPLoss(...)`. `grep -rn SigLIPLoss --include=*.py .` returns only that one reference (no class def). Empirically confirmed: `SemanticAlignmentLoss(loss_type="siglip")` -> `NameError: name 'SigLIPLoss' is not defined`; `loss_type="infonce"` constructs fine (logit_scale init exp=10.0 for T=0.1). Headline hyperparameters.json: loss.loss_type="infonce", so unaffected.
- **fix:** Either implement a SigLIPLoss class, or remove the dead branch and the "Supports both InfoNCE and SigLIP" docstring claims so the module can't be constructed into a NameError.
- **files:** training_scripts/human_activity_recognition/semantic_loss.py
- **verify:** CONFIRMED(repro): Independently verified: grep -rn SigLIPLoss --include=*.py across /home/alex/code/HALO/code/ returns exactly one hit — semantic_loss.py line 438 — which is an instantiation call (self.loss_fn = SigLIPLoss(temperature=temperature)), not a class definition. No SigLIPLoss class is defined anywhere in the repo. The __init__ signature at lines 425/433 advertises loss_type="siglip" as a valid option and the branch at lines 437-438 is live code, so constructing SemanticAlignmentLoss(loss_type="siglip") would raise NameError. Headline config uses loss_type="infonce" so results are unaffected. Severity LOW is appropriate.

### 77. [LOW] [pipeline:loss] Grad-accumulation loss scaling mis-weights variable-size micro-batches (integer-floor estimate)
- **area:** loss-aggregation (train loop, per-patch/session aggregation)
- **issue:** Each micro-batch's loss is scaled by `1 / (TARGET_EFFECTIVE_BATCH // current_bs)` where current_bs = number of SESSIONS in the micro-batch, then accumulated until window_samples>=TARGET_EFFECTIVE_BATCH. Because ChannelBucketBatchSampler yields variable batch sizes, the integer floor makes each micro-batch's contribution to the accumulated gradient an inconsistent (and, in per-patch mode, doubly-mismatched: session-count-based weight applied to a per-PATCH-mean loss) fraction rather than a clean mean-of-means or patch-proportional average. E.g. a window of mb sizes [200,56] with TARGET=256 weights them 1.0 vs 0.25 (256//200=1, 256//56=4), not proportional to their true patch counts. Minor bias, not a hard bug, but it means the effective per-example loss weighting drifts with batch composition.
- **evidence:** semantic_alignment_train.py:965 `current_bs = patches.shape[0]` (session count); :1015 `estimated_micro_batches = max(1, TARGET_EFFECTIVE_BATCH // current_bs)`; :1016 `scaled_loss = loss / estimated_micro_batches`; loss itself is `-(targets*log_probs).sum(1).mean()` over N_valid PATCHES (semantic_loss.py:190), so the divisor's unit (sessions) mismatches the loss's unit (patches). Window closes on `window_samples >= TARGET_EFFECTIVE_BATCH` (:1033).
- **fix:** Track the actual accumulated micro-batch count (or total sample/patch count) for the window and divide each micro-loss by that fixed count (or weight by patches_in_mb/total_patches), instead of a per-microbatch integer-floor estimate.
- **files:** training_scripts/human_activity_recognition/semantic_alignment_train.py
- **verify:** CONFIRMED(repro): All four code claims independently verified. (1) current_bs = patches.shape[0] at line 965 is session count. (2) estimated_micro_batches = max(1, TARGET_EFFECTIVE_BATCH // current_bs) at line 1015 is an integer-floor per-microbatch estimate, not a fixed count computed after the window closes. (3) scaled_loss = loss / estimated_micro_batches at line 1016. (4) semantic_loss.py:190 loss is .mean() over N_valid_patches in per-patch mode (flat_imu from flatten_per_patch_embeddings), so the divisor's unit (sessions) mismatches the loss's averaging unit (patches). Numeric check confirms the finding's [200,56] example exactly: scale factors are 1.0 vs 0.25 rather than the correct proportional 0.78 vs 0.22. ChannelBucketBatchSampler (line 43-60) by design yields per-bucket batch sizes ranging from ~11 to ~91, so variable-window composition is the normal operating mode. The window-close condition (line 1033) uses window_samples which accumulates sessions, not patches, compounding the per-patch mismatch. Finding is not present in the adversarial sweep resolution log. Severity LOW is appropriate: training is not broken, gradients point in the right direction, but effective per-example weighting drifts with batch composition.

### 78. [LOW] [pipeline:train-loop] Per-dataset hours budget over-states trained hours: cap is on the whole pool (only ~70% trains) and wisdm mean-session is 11.2s not the assumed 12.75s
- **area:** data-budget
- **issue:** _MEAN_SESSION_SEC/_hours_to_session_cap (semantic_alignment_train.py:164-181) convert an hours budget into a fixed SESSION count that is applied to the entire dataset in _load_datasets (multi_dataset_loader.py:351-359) BEFORE the 70/15/15 subject split (_create_splits), so only ~70% of the budgeted sessions are actually trained on. The docstring calls this '~400 h of original trainable recording' / 'wisdm cap ~85 h'. Two inaccuracies: (a) 'trainable' is really the pool, ~30% of which is val/test; (b) the wisdm mean-session constant (12.75 s) is ~14% high vs measured 11.2 s, so the 24000-session cap is ~75 h, not 85 h. This is a claim/label discrepancy only; the cap is a fixed count so the trained corpus is deterministic and reproducible.
- **evidence:** semantic_alignment_train.py:164-181 (_MEAN_SESSION_SEC wisdm=12.75, hhar=2.56; cap wisdm=round(85*3600/12.75)=24000, hhar=round(24*3600/2.56)=33750). Measured: wisdm mean-session 11.2s (200-sample), hhar 2.54s. Loader applies cap pre-split (multi_dataset_loader.py:355-358) then _create_splits slices 0.7/0.15/0.15 (:387-394). Session counts: wisdm 164623>24000, hhar 47961>33750 (caps bind).
- **fix:** Either apply the hours budget to the train split only, or reword the docstring/report to say 'pool hours' (~171k windows across all splits, ~70% trained); refresh _MEAN_SESSION_SEC['wisdm'] to the measured ~11.2 s so the derived cap matches the stated 85 h.
- **verify:** CONFIRMED(repro): All three sub-claims independently reproduced. (1) Cap computation: round(85*3600/12.75)=24000 for wisdm; round(24*3600/2.56)=33750 for hhar — matches code lines 176-178 exactly. (2) Pre-split application: multi_dataset_loader.py:351-358 shuffles and truncates to the cap, then _create_splits at :363 applies 0.70/0.15/0.15 fractions — cap unambiguously covers the full pool, not the train split. wisdm labels.json=164623 sessions and hhar labels.json=47961 sessions both exceed their caps, confirming caps bind. (3) wisdm mean session duration measured from 200 random parquets (timestamp_sec column): mean=10.503s, median=7.352s. The code constant 12.75s is above my measurement (overstates by ~21%), and above the finding's 11.2s claim (~14%); the finding's exact number differs from mine (likely sample-to-sample variation) but both independently confirm the code constant is too high. Net effect: the stated 85h pool is overstated regardless of which measured value is used (finding's 11.2s gives ~75h pool; my 10.5s gives ~70h pool), and only ~70% of the pool sessions train. The finding is correctly classified as a documentation/claim inaccuracy only — the session cap is a fixed integer so the trained corpus is deterministic and reproducible. Severity LOW is appropriate: no training correctness issue, purely a docstring/comment mismatch.

### 79. [LOW] [pipeline:train-loop] LR schedule divides by zero when EPOCHS==WARMUP_EPOCHS; train DataLoader crashes when TSFM_NUM_WORKERS=0
- **area:** robustness
- **issue:** warmup_cosine_schedule computes `progress=(epoch-WARMUP_EPOCHS)/(EPOCHS-WARMUP_EPOCHS)` (semantic_alignment_train.py:2407) with no guard; setting EPOCHS==WARMUP_EPOCHS raises ZeroDivisionError inside scheduler.step() (confirmed in smoke run). Separately, the train/val/unseen DataLoaders always pass prefetch_factor=PREFETCH_FACTOR and persistent_workers=True (:2272-2276, :2306-2311, :2333-2337) regardless of NUM_WORKERS, so TSFM_NUM_WORKERS=0 raises 'prefetch_factor could only be specified in multiprocessing' before training starts (confirmed). Neither triggers under the shipped defaults (EPOCHS=100/WARMUP=3, NUM_WORKERS=8), but both are foot-guns for smoke/CI or single-process debugging.
- **evidence:** Reproduced both crashes directly: scheduler.step() -> warmup_cosine_schedule ZeroDivisionError at :2407 with EPOCHS=WARMUP=1; DataLoader ValueError at :2268 with TSFM_NUM_WORKERS=0. Defaults avoid both.
- **fix:** Guard the cosine denominator with max(1, EPOCHS-WARMUP_EPOCHS); pass prefetch_factor/persistent_workers only when NUM_WORKERS>0 (e.g. prefetch_factor=PREFETCH_FACTOR if NUM_WORKERS>0 else None).
- **verify:** CONFIRMED(repro): Both bugs independently reproduced from code + a scratch script.

1. ZeroDivisionError in warmup_cosine_schedule: semantic_alignment_train.py:2407 computes `progress = (epoch - WARMUP_EPOCHS) / (EPOCHS - WARMUP_EPOCHS)` with no guard. With EPOCHS=WARMUP_EPOCHS=1, calling warmup_cosine_schedule(1) raises ZeroDivisionError — confirmed empirically. Default values (EPOCHS=100, WARMUP_EPOCHS=3, lines 268-269) avoid the crash.

2. DataLoader ValueError with NUM_WORKERS=0: lines 290-292 define NUM_WORKERS from env TSFM_NUM_WORKERS (default 8), PREFETCH_FACTOR=4, PERSISTENT_WORKERS=True. All DataLoader constructions — bucket-sampler path (2268-2276), non-bucket path (2289-2299), val loader (2302-2311), and unseen loader hardcoded at (2328-2338) — pass prefetch_factor and persistent_workers unconditionally regardless of NUM_WORKERS. PyTorch raises "prefetch_factor option could only be specified in multiprocessing" when num_workers=0 — confirmed empirically with a minimal TensorDataset. Unseen loader (2332-2334) also hardcodes prefetch_factor=2/persistent_workers=True and has the same bug.

Neither crash is present in the adversarial_sweep_findings.md resolution log. Severity LOW is correct: both are foot-guns for smoke/CI configs only; shipped defaults avoid both paths entirely.

## DISPUTED (verifier disagreement; most repro-confirmed with a nuance)

### [LOW] [baseline:deepconvlstm] Prescribed z-score normalization silently deviates from the reference's min-max [0,1] normalization (undocumented)
- **area:** preprocessing / undocumented-deviation
- **issue:** MODELS-15's pinned recipe specifies 'per-channel z-norm on TRAIN stats only', but the released reference implementation normalizes each channel by MIN-MAX to [0,1] using fixed per-channel thresholds. For a from-scratch model the input normalization co-determines results, so this is a genuine deviation from the published method — and it is nowhere flagged as such (baseline_flexibility.md, the citation.json, and the recipe text all present z-norm as if it were the faithful choice). My mandate is to check the documented-deviations list is COMPLETE; this deviation is missing from it. (z-norm is itself defensible — the reference's thresholds are OPPORTUNITY-sensor-specific and cannot be reused for a per-dataset retrain — but a principled deviation must still be documented.)
- **evidence:** WebFetch of released preprocess_data.py: 'Min-max normalization scaled to [0,1]', formula (data[:,i]-min_list[i])/diffs[i] with diffs=max_list-min_list, hardcoded NORM_MAX_THRESHOLDS/NORM_MIN_THRESHOLDS, values clipped to [0,0.99]. adversarial_sweep_findings.md line ~232 (MODELS-15) prescribes 'per-channel z-norm on TRAIN stats only'. No mention of the z-norm-vs-minmax difference in docs/baselines/baseline_flexibility.md (DeepConvLSTM row line 27) or EVALUATION_PROTOCOL_V2.md.
- **fix:** When the recipe is pinned at build time, explicitly list 'z-score (train stats) instead of the reference min-max [0,1] with OPPORTUNITY-specific thresholds' as a principled, disclosed deviation in baseline_flexibility.md / EVALUATION_PROTOCOL_V2.md and citation.json notes, with the one-line rationale (reference thresholds are dataset-specific and not reusable for a from-scratch per-target retrain).
- **files:** docs/v2/adversarial_sweep_findings.md, docs/baselines/baseline_flexibility.md
- **verify:** CONFIRMED(repro): Independently verified from primary sources. (1) The reference implementation (sussexwearlab/DeepConvLSTM/preprocess_data.py, fetched directly) uses exclusively min-max normalization to [0,1]: formula (x - min[i]) / (max[i] - min[i]) with hardcoded 113-channel OPPORTUNITY-specific NORM_MAX_THRESHOLDS / NORM_MIN_THRESHOLDS arrays, clipped to [0.00, 0.99]. No z-score anywhere. (2) MODELS-15 fix text (adversarial_sweep_findings.md line 232) prescribes "per-channel z-norm on TRAIN stats only" for the from-scratch recipe. (3) A grep for normalize/z-norm/z-score/min-max across both docs/baselines/baseline_flexibility.md and docs/baselines/EVALUATION_PROTOCOL_V2.md returns zero hits — neither file contains any normalization text for DeepConvLSTM. (4) citation.json mentions no normalization deviation. (5) No deepconvlstm.py adapter file exists yet; MODELS-15 is DEFERRED in the resolution log. The deviation (z-norm vs reference min-max with OPPORTUNITY-specific thresholds) is real and undocumented. The finding is not already resolved: MODELS-15 DEFERRED covers the general recipe, but the specific z-norm-vs-min-max disclosure requirement is not addressed anywhere in the existing docs. || REFUTED: The finding's premise is factually incorrect in a way that makes the entire "undocumented deviation" claim moot.

CONCRETE CONTRADICTING EVIDENCE:

1. No deepconvlstm adapter exists in the repo. val_scripts/human_activity_recognition/baselines/ contains only base.py, crosshar.py, limubert.py, and __init__.py. A repo-wide search for deepconv/deep_conv/ordonez/roggen in .py files returns only datascripts/opportunity/convert.py (a reference to hardcoded channel indices from the paper, not an implementation). There is no normalization code to deviate from.

2. MODELS-15 resolution status is ⏳ DEFERRED (build-time) — adversarial_sweep_findings.md line 344: "DeepConvLSTM from-scratch recipe to be pinned in the protocol when the adapter is built." The adapter has not been built. No recipe has been pinned anywhere.

3. The "pinned recipe" the finding cites is in the Fix: recommendation section of the MODELS-15 audit item (line 232), which is an advisory proposal for future build-time action. It is not in EVALUATION_PROTOCOL_V2.md's pre-registered constants table, not in baseline_flexibility.md as an implemented spec, and not in any protocol document. Calling it a "pinned recipe" is a misreading: it is a proposed future action in a deferred finding.

4. MODELS-16 (also DEFERRED, line 345) explicitly tracks that the exact input format — including normalization — must be pre-registered at build time. This is precisely the build-time gate that will capture the normalization choice and any deviation from the reference when the adapter is written.

5. Because there is no implemented normalization at all, the claim that "this deviation is nowhere flagged" is trivially true but irrelevant: you cannot have an undocumented deviation from a specification when no implementation yet exists. The documentation gap the finding identifies (z-norm vs min-max not disclosed in baseline_flexibility.md / EVALUATION_PROTOCOL_V2.md) is expected and correct at this stage — MODELS-16's deferred build-time pre-registration is the designed mechanism for capturing exactly that choice.

SUMMARY: The finding conflates a proposed-fix recommendation in a deferred audit item with a committed protocol specification. The adapter is unbuilt, no normalization code exists, and the deferred MODELS-15/16 pair already designates build-time as the moment to pin and disclose the exact recipe (including any normalization deviation). Nothing is missing from the documentation relative to what has actually been implemented.

### [HIGH] [design:tokenizer] Power-only tokenizer discards within-patch phase and temporal direction (time-reversal-invariant tokens)
- **area:** tokenizer/information-loss
- **issue:** PhysicalFilterbankTokenizer keeps only spectral magnitude (power = X.real^2 + X.imag^2, phase thrown away) per patch/channel. A 1-1.5 s patch and its time-reversal, and a rising vs falling ramp, therefore map to identical tokens. All within-patch temporal ordering/asymmetry and all inter-axis phase coordination are lost; only the coarse power envelope + cross-patch order (via positional encoding) survive. For quasi-periodic locomotion the power spectrum is near-sufficient, but for transitions (sit->stand vs stand->sit, present as HAPT/postural-transition labels), impact asymmetry (UniMIB-SHAR falls, MobiAct falls/transitions), and gestures this is a hard representational ceiling. It is made worse by very short patches with only 2-3 patches per fixed-session dataset, so cross-patch modeling cannot compensate.
- **evidence:** model/feature_extractor.py:611 power=X.real**2+X.imag**2 (phase dropped); measured on CPU: time-reversed-patch token cosine vs forward = 1.00000; rising-ramp vs falling-ramp token cosine = 1.00000. Fixed-session datasets patch to 1.0-1.25s (train PATCH_SIZE_PER_DATASET, semantic_alignment_train.py:135-157) giving ~2 patches/session.
- **fix:** Add a compact, channel-independent within-patch temporal/phase feature concatenated to the band energies before the shared Linear: e.g. the first few DCT (or low-order raw-waveform PCA/learned depthwise-conv) coefficients of the windowed patch, which retain phase/asymmetry at ~tens of params; or re-introduce the deleted temporal CNN branch alongside the filterbank as a small dual-branch token. The transformer (use_cross_channel=True) can then recover cross-axis gravity/direction from the per-channel phase terms. Expected benefit: recovers transition/fall/gesture discriminability the power spectrum cannot express.
- **files:** model/feature_extractor.py, model/preprocessing.py
- **verify:** CONFIRMED(repro): Every claim in the finding reproduced exactly from primary sources.

CODE: feature_extractor.py:611 reads `power = X.real**2 + X.imag**2` — phase is structurally discarded after the rDFT, with no subsequent path that reintroduces it. The comment on line 609 even states "keep power (drop phase)" explicitly.

TIME-REVERSAL INVARIANCE: Measured with two signal types —
- Random Gaussian patch vs its time-reversal: cosine = 1.000000
- 3 Hz sine patch vs its time-reversal: cosine = 1.000000

RAMP DIRECTION INVARIANCE:
- Linear ramp up vs ramp down: cosine = 1.000000
- Exponential rise vs exponential fall (sit→stand / stand→sit proxy): cosine = 1.000000

All four cosines are exact 1.0 (not just near-1.0), as expected from mathematical necessity: |DFT(x_reversed)|^2 = |DFT(x)|^2 for real signals (time-reversal conjugates the spectrum, leaving power unchanged).

PATCH COUNTS (verified from PATCH_SIZE_PER_DATASET in semantic_alignment_train.py:135-157):
- uci_har: 2.56 s / 1.0 s = 2 patches/session — matches claim
- hhar: 2.56 s / 1.0 s = 2 patches/session — matches claim
- unimib_shar: 3.02 s / 1.0 s = 3 patches/session
- hapt (transition dataset): 1.48 s min / 1.25 s = 1 patch/session — worse than the finding states (~2); hapt minimum sessions produce only 1 patch so cross-patch positional modeling cannot compensate at all for that dataset

The finding's scope and severity are accurate. The tokenizer is mathematically incapable of distinguishing any patch from its time-reversal regardless of signal complexity, and the fixed-session datasets (hapt in particular) give so few patches that the transformer's cross-patch positional encoding offers no recovery path. || REFUTED(repro): The code-level claim reproduces exactly but the target and HIGH severity are wrong. (1) The headline checkpoint small_deep_v2/best.pt stores hyperparameters={'model_size':'small_deep'} -> SMALL_DEEP_CONFIG with feature_extractor_type='spectral_temporal', NOT 'physical_filterbank'; its state dict has no filterbank buffers. PhysicalFilterbankTokenizer (line 611 power=X.real^2+X.imag^2) is only instantiated for SMALL_DEEP_FB_CONFIG ('small_deep_fb') and is never reached by the deployed model. (2) That filterbank is a never-trained future design: docs/v2/design_tokenizer.md 'Implementation status' defers it to the M4 retrain cutover and its training path 'fails loudly' (missing-rate ValueError + seq_len==dft_size assert); no small_deep_fb checkpoint exists. (3) The tokenizer that IS deployed, SpectralTemporalExtractor, is 75% temporal-CNN (Conv1d k=5) + 25% FFT-magnitude and is empirically NOT time-reversal invariant: measured rising-vs-falling cosine 0.99761 and asym-impact fwd-vs-rev 0.99268, strictly <1.0 at random init with full capacity to learn direction. The finding's cosine=1.00000 'hard ceiling' is a mathematical identity holding ONLY for the power-only filterbank (verified 1.00000). Residual truth: the phase-discard/time-reversal invariance IS a real, undocumented property of the future M4 filterbank (design_tokenizer.md never calls it out), so it stands as a LOW design note for a not-yet-trained component -- not a HIGH defect in any produced model. Value of the proposed DCT/conv fix is also weak: the cited transition/fall labels (HAPT) are TRAIN-only, absent from the 6 held-out TEST sets, and the fix partly re-adds the temporal branch the redesign intentionally removed.

### [LOW] [design:tokenizer] Constant-Q Q=4 with 32 log-spaced bands is mismatched to 1-1.5 s patches: >20 bands unresolvable/redundant, discriminative 3-15 Hz under-covered
- **area:** tokenizer/band-design
- **issue:** 32 log-spaced bands put 19/32 below 3 Hz and 12/32 below 1.3 Hz, but a constant-Q Q=4 band needs a window of ~8/f_k seconds to be resolved. At the actual training patch lengths (1.0-1.5 s) 27/32 (D=1.0) or 23/32 (D=1.5) bands have Gaussian widths finer than the 1/D spectral resolution and 7-10 bands complete <1 cycle in the window, so the low bands measure heavily-overlapping, leakage-dominated, near-redundant quantities. The resolution flag only marks this, it does not stop each redundant band from consuming a Linear input dim. Meanwhile only 8 bands cover the discriminative 3-8 Hz harmonics and 5 cover 8-15 Hz (mostly masked below 30 Hz).
- **evidence:** Params/config: model/feature_extractor.py:503-504 (log-spaced centers), :480 Q=4, :622 sigma=f_k/(2Q). Measured band table (D=1.0s): 27/32 bands finer than 1/D resolution, 10/32 sub-cycle; distribution: 12 bands <1.3Hz, 19 <3Hz, 8 in 3-8Hz, 5 in 8-15Hz. Patch durations 1.0-2.0s (semantic_alignment_train.py:135-157).
- **fix:** Match band design to the window: raise f_min to ~0.5 Hz and cut the number of sub-1.3 Hz bands, or switch to constant-bandwidth (mel-style) below a ~1.5 Hz corner and constant-Q above it, or lower Q; reallocate the freed bands into 2-8 Hz. For genuinely low-frequency content use a longer (2-4 s) or dual-window/multi-resolution patch rather than more narrow low bands. Expected benefit: same or fewer bands with far higher information density in the HAR-discriminative range, less wasted projection capacity.
- **files:** model/feature_extractor.py, training_scripts/human_activity_recognition/semantic_alignment_train.py
- **verify:** CONFIRMED(repro): Independently reproduced every core number from primary sources. Code: feature_extractor.py has log-spaced centers (:503-504), f_min=0.3/f_max=15.0 (:476-478), Q=4.0 n_bands=32 (:480/:475), sigma=centers/(2Q)=centers/8 (:622); train patch sizes {1.0,1.25,1.5,2.0}s (semantic_alignment_train.py:135-157), bulk at 1.0-1.5s. My own scratch computation of the 32-band table matches the finding exactly: 12 bands <1.3Hz, 19 <3Hz, 8 in 3-8Hz, 5 in 8-15Hz; sigma<1/D for 27/32 at D=1.0s and 23/32 at D=1.5s; sub-cycle (f_k*D<1) for 10 at D=1.0s and 7 at D=1.5s (finding's 7-10). Adjacent low-band center spacing (~0.04-0.13Hz) and sigma (0.0375-0.12Hz) are far below the 1/D~=1Hz window resolution, so those bands measure heavily overlapping, unresolvable quantities. Secondary claim also confirmed in forward(): line 733 gates only the Nyquist mask into e_hat, the resolution flag is concatenated as a separate soft feature (:736-737) without gating, and proj=Linear(3*n_bands+1, d_model) (:532), so every redundant low band consumes a projection input dim. Severity kept at MEDIUM but noted as LOW/MEDIUM borderline: it is a real, well-quantified band-allocation inefficiency for the redesign tokenizer, but impact is soft (learned Linear can downweight; low-freq bands carry posture/gravity-orientation content that is HAR-discriminative, softening the 'under-covers 3-15Hz' framing) and it does not affect the current headline checkpoint, which uses the legacy spectral_temporal path (dft_size=None), not this physical-Hz filterbank. || REFUTED(repro): The finding's numeric band table reproduces exactly (D=1.0/1.5/2.0s: 27/23/21 of 32 bands with sigma<1/D; 10/7/5 sub-cycle; 12 bands <1.3Hz, 19 <3Hz, 8 in 3-8Hz, 5 in 8-15Hz), but its diagnosis and prescription are contradicted by concrete evidence. (1) Wrong code path for the headline result: small_deep_v2_4b3fdd6/best.pt was trained with feature_extractor_type='spectral_temporal' (verified in saved hyperparameters and SMALL_DEEP_CONFIG:143); the Constant-Q filterbank exists only in the untrained SMALL_DEEP_FB_CONFIG/PhysicalHzFilterbank (M4 future design). It is still a valid redesign-audit target, so I evaluated it on merits. (2) Central premise misframed: measured adjacent-band |corr| on real data (uci_har/pamap2/dsads/wisdm/kuhar) is ~0.97-1.00 across ALL frequency zones, not just low bands. Near-redundancy is a fixed property of constant-Q (gap/sigma=1.076 constant everywhere), so 'low bands specifically near-redundant' is false; the entire bank is uniformly oversampled relative to what a 1-1.5s window resolves. (3) Value claim empirically backwards: measured HAR signal energy is 48-68% below 3Hz (gait fundamentals + posture), 23-43% in 3-8Hz, 4-13% above 8Hz. The allocation (19 bands <3Hz, 8 in 3-8Hz) tracks the energy distribution rather than wasting it; calling sub-3Hz 'wasteful' and 3-15Hz 'under-covered discriminative' inverts where the signal actually lives. (4) Documented intentional deviation: design_tokenizer.md 1.2 (line 33) explicitly targets 'gait fundamentals 0.5-3Hz' as the dense log-spaced region, and 1.4 (line 56) documents the low-freq resolution limit as deliberately handled softly by the Hann window, with longer windows tightening low-band agreement (PoC-verified). (5) Prescription would harm and is cargo-culted from large-scale audio settings: cutting sub-1.3Hz bands removes the dominant-energy region, and moving them to 3-8Hz does not reduce redundancy (3-8Hz bands are equally ~0.99 correlated). The alleged capacity 'waste' is negligible at 26M scale: the tokenizer is a single Linear(65->384)=25K frozen-input projection that trivially decorrelates correlated inputs. Residual valid kernel: at 1-1.5s windows 32 bands is genuinely more than the window can resolve, so band count could be trimmed uniformly (or Q lowered / windows lengthened) with no accuracy loss — but this is a benign, documented efficiency point, not a MEDIUM defect, and the specific 'low bands wasteful / reallocate to 3-8Hz' recommendation is refuted.

### [LOW] [design:encoder-streaming] Headline temporal position encoding is integer-patch-index sinusoidal, not physical time
- **area:** positional_encoding / rate-invariance
- **issue:** The shipped headline model (use_rope=False) injects temporal order via a sinusoidal PE indexed by integer patch position. But patch duration is deliberately varied 0.75-2.5s across datasets and augmented per-sample, so the same integer index maps to different elapsed times. A model marketed as rate/duration-invariant thus has a temporal geometry that is inconsistent across datasets and even across its own augmented batches — 'patch 3' means 2.25s in one sample and 7.5s in another.
- **evidence:** positional_encoding.py:52-66 builds pe indexed by integer position; :90 slices pe[:num_patches] (no seconds). PATCH_SIZE_PER_DATASET spans 1.0-2.0s and PATCH_SIZE_RANGE_PER_DATASET spans 0.75-2.5s (train script :135,:408). encoder.py:335-336 applies this PE whenever not use_rope. hyperparameters.json for the headline confirms use_rope=None (RoPE-by-time exists only in the un-trained small_deep_fb config).
- **fix:** Index temporal position by physical seconds for the headline too (either enable the physical-time RoPE path already in transformer.py:106-113, or scale the sinusoidal argument by patch duration D=N/r). Expected: consistent temporal relative-position across rates, better cross-dataset zero-shot transfer.
- **files:** /home/alex/code/HALO/code/model/positional_encoding.py, /home/alex/code/HALO/code/model/encoder.py
- **verify:** CONFIRMED(repro): Independently reproduced from primary sources. positional_encoding.py:59-66 builds pe from integer torch.arange positions; :90/:108 slice pe[:num_patches] with no physical-time scaling. encoder.py:335-336 applies this temporal PE whenever not self.use_rope; the physical-time RoPE path (encoder.py:360-369, transformer.py:106-113) exists only under use_rope=True. Decisive ground truth is the headline checkpoint state_dict: it contains encoder.positional_encoding.temporal_encoding.pe (5000,384) and has NO rope_inv_freq buffers, proving use_rope=False for the shipped model; the PE scale trained UP from 0.1 to 0.2875, so the integer-indexed PE is meaningfully used, not decayed. Patch durations confirmed: PATCH_SIZE_PER_DATASET base 1.0-2.0s (train script :135), PATCH_SIZE_RANGE_PER_DATASET 0.75-2.5s (:408), USE_PATCH_SIZE_AUGMENTATION=True (:367). Recomputing the finding's own example: integer 'patch 3' maps to 2.25s-7.50s of elapsed time (3.3x spread) across datasets/augmented sizes, matching the claim. Note config.py default is use_rope=True, but the headline hyperparameters.json omits the key and the checkpoint confirms the sinusoidal path was used, so the finding's conclusion holds. Severity MEDIUM (not HIGH): the per-sample patch-size augmentation exposes the same integer index at varying durations, which could train the model to treat position as ordinal rather than absolute time, partially mitigating the inconsistency; it is a real design/consistency issue for a rate-invariance claim rather than a correctness bug. || REFUTED(repro): All code reads are factually correct (headline=small_deep, use_rope defaults False, integer-index sinusoidal PE at positional_encoding.py:52-66 applied at encoder.py:335-336; patch duration varies 0.75-2.5s; RoPE-by-time only in untrained small_deep_fb). The learned PE scale is a meaningful 0.2875 (not dead), so the PE is used. But the defect framing and the proposed fix are wrong, refuted by the repo's own physical-time path. (1) encoder.py:372-373 shows physical position = p_idx * D where D=N/r is a single per-sample scalar and patches are non-overlapping — so 'physical seconds' differs from 'integer index' only by a per-sample global scale of the position axis, with NO within-sample irregularity to encode. (2) Sinusoidal/RoPE attention uses RELATIVE position: integer indexing gives neighbor spacing=1 for every sample at every rate (consistent across datasets and augmented batches), whereas the proposed physical-time indexing gives spacing=D that VARIES 0.75-2.5s across samples — making relative-position geometry rate/dataset-DEPENDENT, the opposite of the claimed 'better cross-dataset transfer'. (3) Rate/duration invariance is delivered elsewhere (each patch resampled to fixed 64 tokens + z-score + rate augmentation); temporal PE encodes ORDER, orthogonal to rate — the finding conflates the two axes. (4) per_patch_prediction=True (independent per-patch classification + majority vote) makes absolute temporal position largely irrelevant to a patch's label. (5) Physical-time indexing pays off under irregular sampling / variable within-sequence gaps / long-range absolute-time dependence; here patches are regular, uniformly spaced within a sample, and sequences short — none of those hold, so enabling RoPE is cargo-culting from large/irregular-sequence settings and costs a full 400h retrain of an untrained path for a speculative, plausibly-negative benefit. Residual (different patch_size_sec across datasets means integer 'patch 2' spans different physical content) is LOW severity and mitigated by per-patch length-normalization + z-score, and is not what the fix addresses.

### [LOW] [design:text-alignment] No separability pressure on the only trainable tower: IMU embeddings collapse into a narrow cone (measured inter-class cosine 0.79-0.86)
- **area:** loss-objective
- **issue:** With the text tower frozen, only the IMU side can be reshaped, yet the objective is soft-target InfoNCE with no uniformity or angular-margin term. The result is low uniformity / modality-cone collapse on the IMU side: same-activity cosine ~0.98 but DIFFERENT-activity cosine ~0.79-0.86, a gap of only 0.13-0.19. Fine-grained zero-shot then rests on a razor-thin margin, exactly the failure the whole literature (Wang&Isola, SimCSE, Fahim'24, Ghosh'26) says to fix with a uniformity + margin loss on the trained embeddings.
- **evidence:** Checkpoint val_metrics: positive_similarity=0.9788, negative_similarity(diff-group)=0.7936, similarity_gap=0.185; train 0.990 vs 0.858. grep for uniformity|arcface|cosface|angular.?margin over model/ training_scripts/ val_scripts/ returns nothing. logit_scale learned to exp=36.9 so a 0.13 gap is only ~4.8 logits of separation.
- **fix:** Add a Wang-Isola uniformity term on the L2-normalized IMU embeddings and an additive cosine/angular margin (CosFace/ArcFace-style) in the InfoNCE for confusable pairs, so the movable (IMU) tower is forced to out-separate near-degenerate text anchors. Ablate margin magnitude to avoid the known ArcFace instability.
- **files:** training_scripts/human_activity_recognition/semantic_loss.py, training_output/semantic_alignment/small_deep_v2_4b3fdd6/hyperparameters.json
- **verify:** CONFIRMED(repro): Independently reproduced. (1) LOSS: read semantic_loss.py in full — the objective is soft-target symmetric InfoNCE (lines 186-208) with a learnable logit_scale; grep over model/ training_scripts/ val_scripts/ for uniformity|arcface|cosface|angular.?margin returns only "nyquist_margin" (an unrelated filterbank term). No uniformity or angular-margin term exists on the trainable IMU tower — confirmed. (2) CITED NUMBERS: best.pt val_metrics gives positive_similarity=0.9788, negative_similarity=0.7936, gap=0.1851, logit_scale exp=36.90 — all match exactly. But per _compute_metrics (eval_common/semantic_loss lines 295-305) these are CROSS-modal IMU-to-text sims, not the intra-modal IMU cone the headline describes. (3) INTRA-MODAL CONE (my own run on the headline checkpoint, 2000 windows each, CPU): motionsense same-class IMU-IMU cos=0.976, diff-class=0.901, gap=0.076, Wang-Isola uniformity=-0.31; realworld same=0.965, diff=0.907, gap=0.058, uniformity=-0.31. The collapse is real and if anything worse than stated — all-pairs 5th percentile cosine is still 0.82, uniformity -0.31 vs ~-4/-5 for a well-spread sphere. Downgraded HIGH->MEDIUM: the phenomenon and missing-separability-pressure mechanism are confirmed, but (a) it is a design-improvement hypothesis not a functional bug — val f1_macro=0.836 holds because logit_scale=36.9 amplifies the thin margin, and the proposed uniformity+margin fix is unproven here; (b) the finding's headline evidence "inter-class cosine 0.79-0.86" is actually the cross-modal negative similarity mislabeled as intra-modal inter-class IMU cosine (true intra-modal diff-class value ~0.90). Core claim confirmed; specific evidence framing conflates cross-modal and intra-modal quantities. || REFUTED(repro): The finding's mechanism and evidence are wrong. (1) Premise error: SBERT backbone is frozen but LabelAttentionPooling is trainable (freeze_label_bank=False), so the text tower IS reshaped. (2) Metric misread: the cited negative_similarity=0.7936 is computed in _compute_metrics as raw_sim = imu_embeddings @ text_for_metrics.T — a CROSS-MODAL IMU-to-text similarity, not the intra-modal IMU-to-IMU cosine that 'cone collapse'/Wang-Isola uniformity denotes. I measured the actual geometry: the TRAINED label-bank text tower off-diagonal is mean 0.84 (min 0.72), vs frozen SBERT mean-pool 0.32. Since positive_sim=0.98 (imu_A~text_A), the 0.79 negative cross-modal sim is inherited from the text tower's own 0.84 off-diagonal — the narrow cone lives on the trainable TEXT side, not the IMU side the finding blames. Raw SBERT anchors are NOT the cause (0.32), but neither is IMU collapse; the label-bank pooling collapses them. (3) The '4.8 logits razor-thin' figure uses the TRAIN gap (0.990-0.858=0.132 x scale 36.9 = 4.87); the checkpoint's VAL gap is 0.185 -> 6.83 logits -> ~923x softmax ratio, with strong val metrics (acc 0.850, macro-F1 0.836, MRR 0.861), contradicting a failure mode. (4) Fix misdirected/design-conflicting: IMU-side uniformity+ArcFace cannot out-separate anchors that are themselves only 0.84 apart, and directly opposes the deliberate pure-soft-target design (soft_target_weight=1.0) that trains semantic proximity for zero-shot transfer; ArcFace/uniformity are closed-set instance-discrimination tools, cargo-culted here at 400h/26M scale. Real kernel worth noting: the text-tower anchor compression (0.84 vs 0.32) and small absolute cosine gap are genuine, but that is a text-tower phenomenon the proposed IMU-side remedy would not fix. Not re-listed in the resolution log; no compensating margin term exists (grep confirms), but the claimed IMU-collapse failure is not supported. Caveat: I did not run a full forward pass to measure IMU-IMU intra-modal geometry directly, but the finding's sole evidence is the cross-modal metric, which is explained by the text tower.

### [MEDIUM] [design:text-alignment] Anchor label embeddings are never isotropized/whitened; only the soft-target similarity matrix is standardized (documented-but-unimplemented)
- **area:** anchor-geometry
- **issue:** The docs describe whitening/standardizing the frozen label anchors as the cheapest unexploited lever, but the code standardizes only the text-text similarity matrix used to build soft targets. The anchors actually dotted against the IMU embedding at train time and at recognition time are the raw normalized SBERT vectors, with no mean-centering, whitening, all-but-the-top, or per-dim z-scoring. This leaves the frozen anisotropy/rogue-dimension bias in the space that scoring happens in.
- **evidence:** semantic_loss.py:158-162 standardizes sim=soft_text@soft_text.T for soft targets, but logits=imu@all_text.T at :141 use raw anchors; eval_common.py:434 logits=emb@text_embs.T also raw. grep whiten|isotrop|all.?but.?the.?top|center.*anchor over model/ training_scripts/ val_scripts/ is empty. research_embedding_collapse.md reading-note + 5.1.1 explicitly flag this as unexploited.
- **fix:** Fit a whitening (or all-but-the-top / per-dim z-score) transform once on the frozen label matrix and apply it to anchors at BOTH train logits and eval before cosine. One matmul; expect gains on coarse and moderately-confusable pairs (concede the true-degenerate up/down, left/right axes per 5.3).
- **files:** training_scripts/human_activity_recognition/semantic_loss.py, val_scripts/human_activity_recognition/eval_common.py
- **verify:** CONFIRMED(repro): All three core claims verified from primary sources. (1) semantic_loss.py:158-162 standardizes only the soft-target similarity matrix (soft_text @ soft_text_all.T); it never touches the anchor vectors. (2) Train logits at semantic_loss.py:141 use raw all_text anchors: `torch.matmul(imu_embeddings, all_text.T) * logit_scale`. (3) Eval logits at eval_common.py:434 use raw text_embs: `emb @ text_embs.T / FINETUNE_TEMPERATURE`. Both encode() and encode_frozen() in token_text_encoder.py apply only L2 normalization (F.normalize p=2), no mean-centering or whitening. A grep for whiten|isotrop|all.but.the.top|center.*anchor across model/, training_scripts/, and val_scripts/ returns zero relevant hits — the only z-score matches are for per-patch IMU input normalization in preprocessing.py, unrelated to anchor geometry. The finding's claim is fully reproduced. || REFUTED(repro): The finding's central premise is a code-path misread. It claims the anchors dotted against IMU embeddings at both train logits (semantic_loss.py:141) and eval (eval_common.py:434) are "raw normalized SBERT vectors, with no mean-centering, whitening, all-but-the-top, or per-dim z-scoring." Verified against the headline checkpoint (small_deep_v2_4b3fdd6/best.pt): the anchors are NOT raw SBERT. Both the train logits (text_embeddings passed to InfoNCELoss comes from label_bank.encode() at semantic_alignment_train.py:967) and eval anchors (evaluate_tsfm_v2.py:118,206 and eval_common cosine acc all call label_bank.encode(..., normalize=True)) route through LearnableLabelBank -> LabelAttentionPooling, a TRAINED module: 6 learnable query tokens, a multi-head cross-attention, a LayerNorm, and a 2-layer GELU MLP (out_proj). The checkpoint's label_bank_state_dict contains non-trivial trained weights for all of these (pooling.queries std=0.132, cross_attn.in_proj_weight std=0.046, out_proj.0/2 std~0.02, norm.weight mean=1.0013), so use_mean_pooling=False for the headline model. Raw frozen SBERT mean-pool is produced only by encode_frozen(), which is used exclusively to build the soft-target similarity matrix — exactly the tensor the code DOES standardize (:158-162). Thus scoring happens in a learned, contrastively-trained transform of SBERT, not in the raw frozen anisotropic space. This directly contradicts the finding's causal claim ("leaves the frozen anisotropy/rogue-dimension bias in the space that scoring happens in"). Per the cited doc itself (research_embedding_collapse.md:39), contrastive training on the trainable side is the anisotropy fix, and for HALO's actual lexical/semantic degeneracy (doc 132-133) post-hoc linear whitening has a "ceiling." The proposed fix ("fit whitening on the frozen label matrix, apply to anchors at both train logits and eval") targets a nonexistent path — those anchors are already the learned pooled output, not the frozen matrix. Only the literal sub-claim "no explicit whiten/isotropize call exists" is true; the load-bearing claim that anchors are raw SBERT is false.

### [LOW] [design:text-alignment] ChannelTextFusion injects a content-independent per-channel constant that CrossChannelFusion then averages away; net value is small and never isolated
- **area:** channel-fusion
- **issue:** ChannelTextFusion pools each channel's text to one vector, broadcasts it identically to every patch, and adds it gated (fused = sensor + sigmoid(gate)*channel_const) - a per-channel additive bias, not temporal or content conditioning. Immediately after, CrossChannelFusion reduces channels C->1 per patch, so precisely the per-placement/per-axis distinctions the channel text is meant to carry (left/right wrist, X/Y/Z) are pooled out. Channel text also gets zero linguistic augmentation (labels do), so it is brittle. Its contribution is confounded and small.
- **evidence:** token_text_encoder.py:429 channel_embs=out_proj(...) pooled once; :433 broadcast to (B,1,C,D); :437-438 gated add. semantic_alignment.py:157-176 CrossChannelFusion collapses (B,P,C,D)->(B,P,d). RESULTS_V2.md:129 reports native+rich-text vs parity = only +3.6 F1 (33.2 vs 29.6) and it bundles rate+text (per-axis 2x2 decomposition is 'future work'); realworld swings -17.9, i.e. the effect is not a clean, reliable win. Learned gate weights are small (gate_channel.bias |mean|=0.026), so gate sits near 0.5.
- **fix:** Run a clean channel-text-only ablation (neutral text, same rate) to isolate its value. Move placement/axis to a numeric/positional side-channel (per 5.3, text cannot encode left/right or X/Y/Z), and apply FiLM-style (scale+shift) conditioning AFTER cross-channel pooling instead of an additive constant that gets averaged; augment channel text linguistically.
- **files:** model/token_text_encoder.py, model/semantic_alignment.py, docs/baselines/RESULTS_V2.md
- **verify:** REFUTED(repro): Architecture is accurately described and reproduced: token_text_encoder.py:429-438 pools each channel's text to one vector, broadcasts it identically to all patches (B,1,C,D), and adds it via a gate (fused = sensor + sigmoid(gate)*channel_const) - a content-independent-per-patch additive channel bias. CrossChannelFusion (semantic_alignment.py:157-176, called at :455 AFTER channel_fusion at train_script:581) collapses (B,P,C,D)->(B,P,d). Gate bias mean_abs=0.0258 in the headline checkpoint (sigmoid~=0.506, near 0.5) - CONFIRMED. RESULTS_V2.md +3.6 F1 (33.2 vs 29.6) and realworld -17.9 - CONFIRMED verbatim. HOWEVER, the two load-bearing evidentiary claims that make this a MEDIUM finding are FALSE: (1) 'channel text gets zero linguistic augmentation' is wrong - augmentations.py default_v2() (the preset the headline run selects) enables channel_text_phrase (synonym+template paraphrase via _paraphrase_channel) AND channel_text_dropout; the P7_no_channel_text run log even shows text_augmentation=True. (2) 'never isolated' is wrong - P7_no_channel_text_s42 and ablation_no_channel_fusion both set channel_text_fusion=False and isolate the module. That existing isolation actually shows near-zero effect on the zero-shot metric (unseen_acc 0.5367 vs headline 0.533-0.544), so the 'net value is small' intuition is if anything supported by an ablation the finding claims does not exist. One nuance: CrossChannelFusion uses MultiQueryAttention pooling, not a literal average, so 'averages away' is imprecise. Net: the architectural observation is real but the finding as framed rests on two demonstrably false premises and its requested work (isolating ablation) already exists; not an actionable defect at MEDIUM. || UNCERTAIN: The finding bundles two separable layers — a mechanistic claim that is demonstrably wrong and practical observations that are independently corroborated — so neither a clean CONFIRMED nor REFUTED is correct.

MECHANISTIC CLAIM: REFUTED.

Claim 1 — "content-independent per-channel constant": The gate is computed as sigmoid(gate_sensor(sensor_tokens) + gate_channel(channel_embs)) (token_text_encoder.py:437). gate_sensor is a learned linear of the actual per-patch sensor representation, which varies with sensor content. So the effective injection gate * channel_embs is patch-varying, not a fixed constant per channel. The base channel_embs vector is patch-constant, but the applied influence is not — the finding's label "content-independent" is imprecise.

Claim 2 — "CrossChannelFusion then averages away": The forward path is (1) encoder, (2) ChannelTextFusion modifies encoded_batch in-place (semantic_alignment_train.py:581), then (3) semantic_head receives the text-conditioned (B,P,C,D) tensor and calls CrossChannelFusion. CrossChannelFusion does NOT average channels: it applies input_proj then MultiQueryAttention — four learnable query tokens that cross-attend to the C channel vectors with a full key_padding_mask, followed by a self-attention step among queries, then concat+proj+residual (semantic_alignment.py:111-176). This is a learned, weighted, non-linear combination; calling it "averages away" is factually wrong. Per-channel identity, including the text-conditioned differences introduced by ChannelTextFusion, drives the attention weights in CrossChannelFusion. The pooling is weighted by content, not uniform.

PRACTICAL OBSERVATIONS: CORROBORATED.

(a) "no clean channel-text-only ablation": RESULTS_V2.md:129-130 explicitly acknowledges the +3.6 F1 Δ bundles rate+text and labels the 2x2 decomposition "future work." The ablation env-var (ABLATION_CHANNEL_TEXT_FUSION=0) exists in training_scripts but no isolated result appears in any results doc.

(b) "channel text gets zero linguistic augmentation": data_diversity_audit.md:58 confirms verbatim: "no augment_channel* function exists anywhere — grep confirms." Fixed 1-string-per-channel vocabulary, asymmetric with label augmentation.

(c) "small net value / brittle": data_diversity_audit.md:166 shows left-arm vs right-arm cosine similarity 0.996 (mutual nearest-neighbors), confirming side/axis distinctions are nearly invisible to SBERT — a real limitation of the text channel independent of the fusion mechanism. The +3.6 F1 figure is the only reported Δ and it is confounded.

SUMMARY: The mechanism the finding constructs to explain why channel text is ineffective (averaged away by a downstream mean pool) is wrong — it is learned attention, and the injected signal shapes the attention weights. The gate is not a simple passthrough of a static constant. However the finding's practical conclusions (no clean ablation, confounded small delta, no linguistic augmentation, SBERT-invisible side/axis) are all real and already flagged in the repo's own audit documents. The mechanism argument fails; the actionable substance survives on independent grounds. UNCERTAIN is the correct verdict: the core mechanistic claim cannot be confirmed because the code contradicts it, but the practical concern cannot be dismissed because the supporting data is genuine.

### [LOW] [design:objective] The design doc's 'dense per-patch' objective is unrealized: every patch is aligned to the single session label via repeat_interleave, so per-patch mode only replicates one label across up-to-~14 near-duplicate patches with no boundary/frame supervision, and the consistency-distillation term is dead code for the headline
- **area:** design-vs-doc
- **issue:** design_objective.md specifies dense per-frame labels, a boundary head, a smoothness term, and consistency distillation. None of that exists. labels.json maps each session to exactly one label (verified: capture24/pamap2/dsads/uci_har all list len 1), and the loop expands that single label across all valid patches (flat_frozen = frozen_text.repeat_interleave(patch_counts), semantic_alignment_train.py:986-988; flatten_per_patch_embeddings:841-862). So per_patch_prediction (semantic_alignment.py:457-461) buys no per-patch supervision benefit — it just injects redundant near-identical positive rows (several per multi-patch session) into the InfoNCE matrix, which also amplifies the queue/in-batch false-negative problem. Separately, the only additional objective present (offline->causal distillation, lines 1001-1010) is gated behind is_filterbank AND STREAM_CAUSAL_PROB>0; the headline uses a spectral_temporal encoder with stream_causal_prob defaulting to 0.0, so DISTILL_WEIGHT=1.0 never contributes — it is dead code masquerading as a multi-task term.
- **evidence:** labels.json single-label lists confirmed for 4 datasets. Session lengths measured: capture24 median 1163 rows / max 2945, pamap2 median 804 / max 1499, wisdm median 188 / max 391 (multi-patch at 2s patch size, target_patch_size=64), vs uci_har 128 and dsads 125 (1-2 patches). So multi-patch sessions get 4-14 duplicate label rows with no frame-level payoff. Distillation gate: line 1001 requires getattr(_enc,'is_filterbank',False); headline feature_extractor_type=spectral_temporal (hyperparameters.json); STREAM_CAUSAL_PROB default 0.0 (line 219).
- **fix:** Either (a) implement true frame labels + the boundary/smoothness heads the doc specifies (derive per-timestamp labels in converters for native per-sample datasets: PAMAP2/DSADS/MHEALTH/HARTH), or (b) if staying segment-only, pool the per-patch embeddings to one embedding per session for the contrastive loss to eliminate redundant duplicate rows and shrink the false-negative surface. Delete or wire up the distillation term so the loss composition is honest.
- **files:** training_scripts/human_activity_recognition/semantic_alignment_train.py, model/semantic_alignment.py, docs/v2/design_objective.md
- **verify:** CONFIRMED(repro): Every factual claim reproduced independently from primary sources. (1) labels.json is single-label per session for all 5 datasets I checked (capture24/pamap2/dsads/uci_har/mhealth, 100% length-1 distribution). (2) semantic_alignment_train.py:986-988 expands that single session label across valid patches via frozen_text.repeat_interleave(patch_mask.sum(...)); flatten_per_patch_embeddings (841-862) assigns each patch its session label — no frame/boundary supervision. (3) semantic_alignment.py:457-461 per_patch branch only reshapes+projects; there is NO boundary or smoothness head anywhere in model/ or training_scripts/ (grep found none). (4) Distillation is dead code for the headline: gate at 1001-1002 needs is_filterbank AND STREAM_CAUSAL_PROB>0; is_filterbank=(type=='physical_filterbank') (encoder.py:141) but headline hyperparameters.json = spectral_temporal, so False; STREAM_CAUSAL_PROB default 0.0 (219), DISTILL_WEIGHT default 1.0 (222). Gate never fires yet DISTILL_WEIGHT=1.0 implies an active term. (5) Multi-patch sessions confirmed: capture24 median 1171/pamap2 876/wisdm 200 rows vs target_patch_size=64, so many duplicate label rows, while uci_har=128/dsads=125 are 1-2 patches. Correction to framing/severity: design_objective.md is explicitly a REDESIGN PROPOSAL (its own section-0 header states current per-patch mode is 'secretly the segment-only supervision special case' and the dense labels/boundary/smoothness are 'three surgical changes on top' of the existing scaffold), so 'unrealized' is by-design roadmap, not a false spec claim — the 'design-vs-doc discrepancy' framing is weak. The real, valid concerns are (a) redundant near-duplicate positive rows injected into InfoNCE amplifying the false-negative surface and (b) the dead DISTILL_WEIGHT=1.0 term making the loss composition look multi-task when it isn't. Both are training-quality/honesty issues that do not produce wrong headline results, so I lower severity from MEDIUM to LOW. || REFUTED(repro): Mechanical facts reproduce (single-label sessions across capture24/pamap2/dsads/uci_har/wisdm/hhar; repeat_interleave broadcasts one session label per patch; distillation branch inert for the headline since feature_extractor_type=spectral_temporal => is_filterbank=False and STREAM_CAUSAL_PROB=0.0). But the design-vs-doc DEFECT framing is wrong on three grounds. (1) design_objective.md is a forward-looking V2 spec ('Component design for REDESIGN_PLAN.md'; plan status 'design approved, implementation starting'), not a description of the shipped headline; 'the dense objective is unrealized' just means approved work is not yet done, which is not a code defect. (2) The doc self-discloses the exact gap the finding reports — Section 0 states verbatim that today's per-patch mode is 'secretly the segment-only supervision special case: every patch...aligned to the same session-label text (frozen_text.repeat_interleave(patch_counts))'. There is no doc-vs-code contradiction, and the finding's proposed fix (a) IS the doc's own §1.4, making the finding circular. (3) The harm claims fail: per-patch mode is not gratuitous (it skips the ~9.3M-param temporal-attention+pooling head and is the streaming-inference substrate emitting one embedding/patch, an architectural purpose independent of frame labels); the 'amplifies false negatives' claim is refuted by soft_target_weight=1.0 + frozen-SBERT soft targets (semantic_loss.py:158-176), under which duplicate same-label patches share identical frozen embeddings and receive high positive soft-target mass — they are soft positives, not hard negatives; the true effect is benign loss re-weighting (multi-patch sessions weighted more), not a correctness bug. The distillation term is genuinely dead for the headline, but it is documented, gated research scaffolding for the filterbank/streaming encoder (research_streaming_design.md), and no result claims it contributes — 'masquerading' overstates it. The residual true observation (an inert, by-design distillation branch) is cosmetic, not a MEDIUM design-vs-doc defect.

### [HIGH] [design:data-strategy] Label vocabulary is a near-bijection with dataset identity: 25/38 groups are >=90% single-dataset, starving the contrastive loss of cross-dataset negatives
- **area:** corpus composition / label vocabulary
- **issue:** The contrastive objective can only learn transferable signal->semantics if a given activity concept appears in multiple datasets with different subjects/placements/hardware. In this corpus most concepts appear in exactly one dataset, so for two-thirds of the vocabulary the model never contrasts an activity against the SAME activity from a different source, and never contrasts it against a different activity from the SAME source under a different label. The alignment therefore collapses to memorizing a dataset-conditional label prior (reinforced by the channel-text fingerprint above). Zero-shot to the 6 held-out datasets is precisely the setting where the signal statistics and fingerprint differ, so this memorized structure does not transfer. This is a corpus-coverage problem that persists even with neutral text.
- **evidence:** Computed over the capped ~171k-window training corpus (label_groups.py mapping): 25 of 38 active groups are >=90% sourced from a single dataset; 11 are 100% single-dataset — household_chores/mixed_activity/vehicle/manual_work (all 100% capture24), falling (100% unimib_shar), gym_weights (100% recgym), eating/clapping/typing/stairs/grooming/folding_clothes (100% wisdm), picking_up/sit_up/push_up/talking_sitting/talking_standing (100% kuhar), elevator (100% dsads), ironing/vacuum_cleaning (100% pamap2). Only walking/sitting/standing/lying/cycling/running/stairs are genuinely multi-dataset.
- **fix:** Broaden per-concept dataset coverage rather than per-concept hours: prioritize adding datasets/free-living sources that re-cover the single-dataset concepts (locomotion+posture from more sources; free-living hand/household activities from more than just wisdm/capture24). Add explicit cross-dataset hard-negative mining so a batch pairs same-concept windows from different datasets and different-concept windows from the same dataset. Merge or drop concepts that are both single-dataset AND semantically vague (mixed_activity, household_chores) so capacity is not spent aligning to non-discriminative text. Expected benefit: forces the encoder to learn signal->semantics that survives the train/test distribution shift, directly improving zero-shot macro-F1.
- **files:** /home/alex/code/HALO/code/datasets/imu_pretraining_dataset/label_groups.py, /home/alex/code/HALO/code/datasets/imu_pretraining_dataset/multi_dataset_loader.py
- **verify:** CONFIRMED(repro): Independently recomputed over the capped corpus (170,934 windows; exact caps from semantic_alignment_train.py: wisdm~24000, hhar~33750, others uncapped; 1 window = 1 capped session). Result: 38 active groups, and 25 of 38 are >=90% sourced from a single dataset -- matching the finding's headline number EXACTLY. The genuinely multi-dataset concepts (top dataset <=55%) are walking/sitting/standing/lying/running/cycling plus ascending/descending stairs, matching the finding. The label-set structure confirms the driver: 24 group labels appear in only one dataset's labels.json, so window-weighting cannot rescue them. One discrepancy that makes the problem WORSE, not better: the finding's prose says '11 are 100% single-dataset' but I measure 24 strictly single-dataset groups (the finding's own enumeration already lists ~20, and it omitted mhealth arm/leg/waist_exercise and hapt transition_to_sitting). So the coverage claim is not just reproduced but understated. Caveat: the reproduced fact is the coverage statistic; the downstream 'collapses to a dataset-conditional label prior' claim is a plausible but not directly measured mechanism here. HIGH is warranted -- cross-dataset same-concept contrastive negatives are absent for ~2/3 of the vocabulary, directly threatening the zero-shot transfer that is the model's headline claim. || REFUTED(repro): The raw statistic is correct but the causal chain that makes it a HIGH-severity problem is broken on multiple counts.

EMPIRICAL CHECK: The 25/38 ≥90%-single-dataset claim was verified empirically at session level (38 total groups, 25 are ≥90% single-dataset, 24 are 100%). The finding's numbers are accurate.

WHERE THE ARGUMENT FAILS:

1. The zero-shot benchmark tests only 14 activity groups (mapped from 5 test datasets — harth, inclusivehar, mobiact, motionsense, realworld, shoaib). Of those 14:
   - 9 groups ARE in training and have rich multi-dataset coverage: walking (11/11 train datasets, top-dataset fraction 0.30), sitting (10, top 0.38), standing (9, top 0.40), lying (7, top 0.49), descending_stairs (7, top 0.59), running (7, top 0.74), ascending_stairs (8, top 0.64), jumping (6, top 0.34), cycling (6, top 0.61).
   - 4 groups are true zero-shot concepts absent from training entirely (ramp_ascent, ramp_descent, vehicle_entry, fall_forward_knees).
   - Only 1 tested group is single-dataset in training: falling (100% unimib_shar).
   
   The 25 single-dataset-dominated groups correspond to activities like eating, typing, grooming, folding_clothes, gym_weights, push_up, sit_up, household_chores, etc. None of these appear in the zero-shot evaluation vocabulary. The finding's claim that single-dataset memorization explains failures in "precisely the zero-shot setting" cannot be right — the zero-shot eval is almost entirely computed on the well-covered multi-dataset groups.

2. The sub-claim "never contrasts a different activity from the same source" is false. Every training dataset has 6–15 distinct label groups (capture24: 10, pamap2: 11, kuhar: 15, mhealth: 11, wisdm: 11, dsads: 12). In-batch InfoNCE with WeightedRandomSampler naturally places same-source different-activity windows in the same batch and treats them as negatives. Same-source cross-activity contrast is abundant.

3. The alignment to frozen SentenceBERT text embeddings already enforces a form of cross-dataset consistency for the well-covered groups: walking windows from all 11 train datasets are pulled toward the same text anchor regardless of sensor hardware or body placement. Explicit cross-dataset hard-negative pairing adds no information the shared anchor doesn't already supply for groups with 6-11 dataset coverage.

4. The proposed fix (broadening per-concept coverage for single-dataset concepts, hard-negative cross-dataset mining) would not plausibly improve zero-shot macro-F1 on the current benchmark: the tested concepts already span 6-11 datasets, so further broadening hits diminishing returns. The single-dataset concepts that would actually benefit from the fix aren't in the eval vocabulary, so the expected zero-shot improvement claim doesn't hold at this scale and evaluation design.

LEGITIMATE RESIDUAL CONCERN (does not restore HIGH severity): falling is 100% unimib_shar (4045 sessions) and is tested in mobiact and realworld with different placement/hardware — this is a real coverage gap. Separately, the 4 truly zero-shot concepts (ramp_ascent, ramp_descent, vehicle_entry, fall_forward_knees) reflect legitimate missing coverage, but that is a dataset-absence problem orthogonal to the near-bijection observation.

VERDICT: REFUTED. The corpus structure observation is factually accurate, but the causal claim that it starves zero-shot transfer is not supported. The benchmark's tested vocabulary is drawn almost entirely from the minority of groups (9 of 38) that have excellent multi-dataset coverage. The finding misidentifies the problem stratum and overstates the severity by two tiers.

### [LOW] [design:data-strategy] ~400h / ~171k windows / 367 subjects is a small-supervised regime for a 'foundation model' — breadth, not capacity, is the bottleneck
- **area:** scaling regime / data breadth
- **issue:** The 26M-param model is trained on ~171k labeled windows (~460k patches/epoch) from 367 subjects and effectively one free-living source. Augmentation multiplies views, not information. For a supervised multi-dataset aligner this is reasonable, but for the stated 'foundation model / zero-shot HAR' thesis it is thin: combined with the fingerprint shortcut and single-dataset label coverage above, the model sits in a regime that rewards memorizing the 11-dataset structure over learning transferable IMU->language. Capacity is not the limiter (26M is modest); subject/placement/free-living breadth is. Known wearable pretraining efforts use orders of magnitude more unlabeled breadth (e.g. ssl-wearables ~700k person-days; UniMTS synthesizes motion from AMASS).
- **evidence:** data_quantity_report.md:145-192 (~399h, ~171k windows, augmentation multiplies views not info); measured 367 unique subjects, single free-living dataset (capture24). model/config small_deep ~26M active params. Findings 1-2 show the corpus structure is largely dataset-separable.
- **fix:** Treat data breadth as the primary lever for the next submission: add the already-scoped free-living / large-cohort sources (ExtraSensory, CAPTURE-24 full streams, UK-Biobank/NHANES) to grow subject count and placement/free-living coverage, before growing model size. Where added data is unlabeled, keep the contrastive alignment for labeled sets but add a self-supervised objective on the unlabeled free-living hours. Frame the headline as real recording hours + subject/placement coverage, not augmented view counts. Expected benefit: moves the model out of the memorization-prone regime that findings 1-2 exploit, which is what actually gates zero-shot generalization.
- **files:** /home/alex/code/HALO/code/docs/v2/data_quantity_report.md
- **verify:** CONFIRMED(repro): Independently reproduced every core number from primary sources (raw labels.json, dataset manifests, checkpoint, training config), not from the report. (1) Windows: applying the actual training caps from semantic_alignment_train.py (hhar 33,750, wisdm 24,000, all others uncapped) to the real labels.json counts yields 170,934 trainable windows (finding: ~171k). (2) Hours: MEAN_SESSION_SEC * used-count = 398.7h (finding: ~399h); capture24 = 167.7h = 42.0% of the mix. (3) Free-living breadth: exactly 1 free-living source (capture24); the other 10 are scripted lab sets. (4) Subjects: using the loader's own _subject_of() parser I get 397 raw subjects across 11 sets, minus the 30 HAPT/UCI-HAR subjects that are the SAME people (HAPT is the UCI-HAR superset; I verified identical subject-id sets {1..30}) = 367 unique subjects, matching the finding exactly. (5) Capacity: the headline checkpoint's model_state_dict is 35.4M params (encoder 20.9M + semantic_head 12.9M + channel_fusion 1.6M); '26M active' is consistent after excluding the prior-sweep dead params, and either figure is modest for a 'foundation model', supporting the 'capacity is not the limiter' claim. The thesis -- ~400h/171k-window/367-subject/single-free-living-source is a small-supervised regime where breadth (subjects/placement/free-living), not capacity, is the bottleneck -- is well-supported by the data and consistent with the dataset-separability (fingerprint) findings 1-2. One secondary evidence figure is loose: the finding cites '~460k patches/epoch' but my ceil(window_dur/patch_size) estimate gives ~1.09M/epoch; this is an estimate-dependent side number, not the core claim, and does not affect the verdict. Severity MEDIUM is correct: this is a design/positioning critique tied to the leakage findings, not a code defect or a misreported result -- above LOW because it challenges the paper's central 'foundation model' framing, below HIGH because nothing is broken or falsely reported. || REFUTED(repro): The finding's diagnosis (breadth, not capacity, is the lever) is directionally sound, but as an actionable defect it is already handled and partly self-contradicting. (1) Its PRIMARY proposed fix -- add CAPTURE-24 full streams / raise free-living coverage -- is already IMPLEMENTED in code: TRAIN_HOURS_PER_DATASET in semantic_alignment_train.py uncaps capture24 (all 48,518 sessions / 167.7h), caps wisdm@85h, hhar@24h, yielding ~171k windows with free-living lifted ~15%->~42%. This is exactly what report section 8 (the lines 145-192 the finding cites as EVIDENCE) documents under the header 'Training target (~400h) -- IMPLEMENTED'. (2) The finding's third action -- 'report real hours, not augmented view counts' -- is verbatim the standing instruction in that same section (lines 188-192). (3) The remaining specific adds (ExtraSensory, UK-Biobank to TRAINING) contradict a deliberate, user-approved documented decision: ExtraSensory is reserved as a free-living phone+watch TEST set to preserve the clean cross-dataset ZS story, and UK-Biobank is access-gated and superseded by the ssl-wearables-model plan; the chosen breadth lever is virtual-IMU synthesis (UniMTS/IMUGPT), not just more free-living hours. (4) The finding's numbers are shaky: I measured 397 recoverable unique subjects (unimib_shar's ~11.7k sessions have no recoverable subject id), not '367' and not report section 1's '~250'; it also pairs the NEW 171k-window target with the OLD 85k-regime's '~460k patches/epoch'. (5) The SSL-on-unlabeled suggestion is plausible but is cargo-culted from 700k-person-day settings; at 400h/26M the higher-leverage, zero-data-cost fixes already logged (LearnableLabelBank net-negative +4-6pp, subject leakage, fake two-stage) dominate. Net: the compensating mechanism exists in code and the cited doc, so the finding does not stand as an open, actionable gap.

### [HIGH] [design:deployment-extensibility] Input contract ignores real phone/watch sensor conventions — no g-vs-m/s2 detection, no gravity-included-vs-userAcceleration handling, no frame canonicalization, and it demands exact training-format channel strings in a fixed 6-channel order
- **area:** input-contract/robustness
- **issue:** The de-facto contract (extract_tsfm_embeddings) feeds raw_data straight into forward_from_raw with no unit normalization, no gravity/linear-accel handling, and no axis/frame canonicalization; the only unit conversion anywhere is a hardcoded per-dataset ACC_IN_G_UNITS set inside a *baseline* prep script. A Core Motion / Android developer has CMDeviceMotion.userAcceleration (gravity-removed, in g) or TYPE_ACCELEROMETER (gravity-included, m/s2) with device-specific axes — the model has no way to reconcile these at inference (gravity handling is a *train-time augmentation* only). Worse, the input is a fixed canonical 6-channel layout (acc_x,acc_y,acc_z,gyro_x,gyro_y,gyro_z; DATA_CHANNELS=6) and each channel must carry the exact training string '{dataset_desc} {channel_desc} (sampled at {rate}Hz, {patch}s window)', which the developer must reproduce by hand (dataset_desc is a 2-3 sentence paragraph). The predict() contract in design_application.md:88 specifies auto-detect g vs m/s2 (warn) + internal gravity-canon, but none is implemented.
- **evidence:** val_scripts/.../eval_common.py:79 DATA_CHANNELS=6, :88 CORE_CHANNELS canonical order, :126-175 get_dataset_metadata rebuilds the exact string incl. the 2-3 sentence dataset_desc; :210-247 extract_tsfm_embeddings feeds raw data with no unit/gravity normalization; grep for 9.8/units/gravity at inference -> only benchmark_data/scripts/preprocess_limubert.py (a baseline) hardcodes ACC_IN_G_UNITS; design_application.md:88 specs the missing auto-detect+gravity-canon.
- **fix:** In predict(): auto-detect g vs m/s2 by accel magnitude and warn; accept channels=('acc','gyro') and internally map/pad to canonical order; apply gravity-frame canonicalization (consume OS gravity/linear-accel virtual sensors or a Mahony/low-pass estimate) so userAcceleration and total-acceleration inputs converge; default channel-text strings from placement/rate so callers pass placement='wrist' not a paragraph.
- **files:** val_scripts/human_activity_recognition/eval_common.py, benchmark_data/scripts/preprocess_limubert.py
- **verify:** CONFIRMED(repro): All four sub-claims independently reproduced from primary sources. (1) DATA_CHANNELS=6 and CORE_CHANNELS canonical order are hardcoded at eval_common.py:79,88. (2) get_dataset_metadata (eval_common.py:127-180) embeds a 2-3 sentence dataset description (measured: 212-235 chars/3 sentences across shoaib, motionsense, realworld) into every channel string plus a rate/window suffix, which callers must reproduce verbatim. (3) extract_tsfm_embeddings (eval_common.py:190-246) and forward_from_raw (semantic_alignment_train.py:668-712) contain no unit normalization or gravity handling whatsoever. (4) ACC_IN_G_UNITS exists only in the LIMU-BERT baseline prep script (preprocess_limubert.py:52), not in TSFM inference. Gravity handling is exclusively a train-time augmentation (augmentations.py GravityCfg). The predict()/halo/api.py described at design_application.md:88 does not exist; auto-detect g vs m/s2 and gravity-canon are unimplemented design aspirations. A caller supplying Core Motion userAcceleration (gravity-removed, g) vs Android TYPE_ACCELEROMETER (gravity-included, m/s2) would receive silently different embeddings with no warning. || REFUTED(repro): The finding's two headline mechanisms are neutralized by the model's own preprocessing, which I verified empirically. The headline checkpoint config carries normalization_method='zscore' and feature_extractor_type='spectral_temporal' (legacy path). normalize_patches applies z-score PER-PATCH, PER-CHANNEL: means=patches.mean(dim=1), stds=patches.std(dim=1) over time, independently per channel. Consequences: (1) g vs m/s2 unit scale is a global factor c multiplying both mean and std, cancelling exactly in (x-mean)/std -> the model is mathematically blind to accel units, so 'auto-detect g vs m/s2 and warn' gates on a quantity the model never sees and adds zero correctness for this checkpoint; (2) gravity-included vs userAcceleration differ by a near-DC per-channel offset over a 1s patch, removed by per-channel mean subtraction -> total-accel and linear-accel inputs converge after z-score (only intra-patch rotation leaks, second-order). The 'developer must hand-type a 2-3 sentence dataset_desc string' claim is false: get_dataset_metadata (eval_common.py:127-180) auto-assembles the full channel string from data/<ds>/manifest.json. Category error on the contract: eval_common.py is the research eval harness whose job is to reproduce training-format inputs for headline numbers, not a deployment API; the predict() contract at design_application.md:88 is an explicit FUTURE deliverable (the doc's own preamble states no predict/from_pretrained/export code exists), so faulting current code for not implementing it is misdirected. The single genuine residual — cross-axis frame canonicalization (mounting orientation redistributes gravity across axes, which per-channel z-score does not reconcile) — is real but is precisely the audited design doc's OWN rank-3 headline item (§1a: gravity-frame canonicalization + yaw-only aug, with Mahony/OS-gravity and ablation A1 already specced). So it is not novel, and the finding misdiagnoses it via unit-detection/gravity-DC mechanisms that z-score already handles. Corrected severity LOW: the actionable-and-correct kernel is already documented and prioritized elsewhere, while the finding's specific mechanistic claims and the HIGH 'input-contract bug' framing are contradicted by the code.

### [LOW] [design:deployment-extensibility] Headline released checkpoint uses the rate-dependent, aliasing spectral_temporal tokenizer; the rate-invariant PHz filterbank exists in code but is untrained and unblessed
- **area:** robustness/deployability
- **issue:** The blessed checkpoint is model_size=small_deep, whose feature_extractor_type is 'spectral_temporal': it interpolates every patch to 64 samples with F.interpolate(mode='linear', align_corners=False) — no anti-alias filter — and its rfft bins are indexed by sample count, so a fixed bin maps to different physical Hz at 50 Hz vs 100 Hz. This directly undermines the variable-rate phone/watch deployment story (in-corpus rates span 9-100 Hz). The rate-invariant fix (PhysicalFilterbankTokenizer / small_deep_fb, native-rate zero-pad, no resampling) is fully implemented but the config itself notes the DataLoader/train wiring is deferred to the M4 retrain, and no fb checkpoint is blessed — so the released weights do not have the property the design claims.
