## RESEARCH INSIGHTS

### R1. UniMTS — the strongest zero-shot raw-motion FM, and HALO's closest peer
- **finding:** UniMTS (NeurIPS 2024) pretrains a spatio-temporal GRAPH encoder (only 4.94M params, ~22.6mW on an NPU) contrastively against a FROZEN CLIP text encoder. Crucially it has almost no real IMU: it SYNTHESIZES accelerometry/gyro from HumanML3D mocap skeletons (14,616 sequences, 28.59h, 44,970 text descriptions) via physics equations, then applies uniform SO(3) random rotation + random joint masking (1-5 joints) to cover arbitrary device positions/orientations, and expands text with GPT-3.5 paraphrases (k=3). Result: zero-shot avg ~43.5% across 18 HAR benchmarks vs ~12.9% best baseline (+340%), UCI-HAR 35.2% (ImageBind 14.1%), PAMAP2 47.2%; few-shot +16.3%; full-shot 90.6%. Weights on HuggingFace (xiyuanz/UniMTS).
- **source:** Zhang et al., 'UniMTS: Unified Pre-training for Motion Time Series', NeurIPS 2024, arXiv:2410.19818
- **-> HALO:** Same CLIP-to-frozen-text recipe as HALO but wins zero-shot by mocap-synthesis + SO(3)/joint-mask augmentation + LLM-diversified text — the exact levers HALO underuses at 400h real data. Direct template for a HALO redesign.

### R2. RelCon (Apple) — SOTA self-supervised motion FM WITHOUT text
- **finding:** RelCon (ICLR 2025) uses a tiny 1D ResNet-34 (3.9M params, 256-d) on raw 2.56s @100Hz 3-axis accel, pretrained with a NOVEL relative-contrastive loss driven by a LEARNABLE motif-distance function (sparsemax cross-attention + RevIN to preserve magnitude), with rotation-invariance baked into the learned distance via accel-specific augmentations. Trained on 1B segments / 87,376 participants (Apple Heart & Movement Study). Beats SimCLR/REBAR/Aug-Pred and Yuan et al.'s 700k-subject UK-Biobank model: PAMAP2 85.4% F1 (vs 72.5%), Opportunity 69.1% (vs 57.0%), HHAR 57.6%. Code released; weights not (private data).
- **source:** Xu et al. (Apple), 'RelCon: Relative Contrastive Learning for a Motion Foundation Model for Wearable Data', ICLR 2025, arXiv:2411.18822
- **-> HALO:** Proves a text-free SSL objective on raw accel beats supervised/CLIP peers, and that rotation invariance is the key design axis. HALO has no SSL branch — a gap. Also shows 3-4M params suffice, questioning HALO's 26M.

### R3. The definitive warning about HALO's exact recipe: CLIP-to-frozen-text underperforms
- **finding:** Haresamudram/Ploetz et al. (AAAI 2025) directly stress-test natural-language-supervised (CLIP-style) HAR and find zero-shot F1 of only 10.88-31.05% vs 38-89% for supervised/SSL baselines. Root causes: (1) sensor heterogeneity across devices/rates, (2) sparse text (activity-label vocab of ~9-282 words vs billions in vision). Fixes with measured gains: SLIP (CLIP contrastive + SimCLR self-supervision) > vanilla CLIP; RoBERTa/CLIP-text encoder > DistilBERT; SIMPLE conv nets > ResNets as sensor encoder; LLM-diversified templates +5-7%; body-part/placement metadata +6-10%; adapting only the projection layers on 4 min/activity of target data +20-40% (full split +30-50%).
- **source:** Haresamudram, Beedu, Rabbi, Saha, Essa, Ploetz, 'Limitations in Employing Natural Language Supervision for Sensor-Based HAR — And Ways to Overcome Them', AAAI 2025, arXiv:2408.12023
- **-> HALO:** This paper describes HALO's configuration (per-patch CLIP align to frozen small text encoder) as the failure mode, and hands over the exact remedies: add SSL (SLIP), richer text, stronger text encoder, and a tiny target-adaptation surface. Single most actionable source.

### R4. SensorLM (Google) — hybrid contrastive+generative beats contrastive-only
- **finding:** SensorLM (2025) is a CoCa-style model (contrastive loss + caption/generative loss, λ_con=λ_cap=1) with a ViT sensor encoder scaled 3M→1.27B params, plus a hierarchical caption pipeline (statistical/structural/semantic templates). Trained on 59.7M hours / 103,643 users / 127 countries. Ablations show it RECOVERS CLIP (λ_cap=0) and Cap-only (λ_con=0) as special cases and the hybrid beats both. Zero-shot 20-class activity AUROC 0.84 (vs Gemini-2.0 0.51, near-random LLMs), F1 0.29; few-shot 50/class AUROC 0.88 (beats SimCLR/DINO/MSN). No public weights.
- **source:** Zhang et al. (Google), 'SensorLM: Learning the Language of Wearable Sensors', 2025, arXiv:2506.09108
- **-> HALO:** Strongest large-scale evidence that adding a generative/captioning objective on top of contrastive alignment helps — reinforces the AAAI'25 SLIP finding. BUT operates on 26 minute-level aggregated features, not raw waveform (different regime; see LSM insight).

### R5. Google LSM-1/LSM-2 — the scale frontier, but aggregated-feature (not raw-IMU) regime
- **finding:** LSM-1 ('Scaling Wearable Foundation Models', 2024): ViT masked-generative (imputation/interpolation/extrapolation/forecasting) on 40M hours / 165k users; found scaling laws — downstream improves MONOTONICALLY up to ~10^5 hours then diminishes, ~10^7 near plateau; total hours matter more than subject count; few-shot beats supervised; up to +38% over imputation baselines. LSM-2 (ICLR 2026 submission): 25M-param ViT-1D (d=384, 12 enc/4 dec, 10-min patches), 'Adaptive & Inherited Masking' to learn directly from incomplete data without imputation; 20-class activity linear-probe F1 0.474. Both use T=1440 minute-level, 26 DERIVED features/day (PPG+accel+EDA+temp+altimeter), NOT raw waveform.
- **source:** Google, 'Scaling Wearable Foundation Models', arXiv:2410.13638; 'LSM-2: Learning from Incomplete Wearable Sensor Data', arXiv:2506.05321
- **-> HALO:** Two lessons: (1) HALO's 400h is 2-3 orders of magnitude below the ~10^5-hour scaling knee. (2) Google's health FMs abandon raw waveform for minute-features — HALO's raw physical-Hz filterbank is the right choice for FINE motion, but HALO must scale data. LSM-2's d=384/25M is near-identical in size to HALO.

### R6. GOAT — closest prior art to HALO's text-supervised cross-dataset framing
- **finding:** GOAT (IMWUT 2024) does CLIP-style natural-language supervision using text from BOTH activity labels AND on-body sensor positions, with a Transformer activity encoder, a novel device-position encoding, and cosine-similarity loss for cross-dataset generalization. It is the paper HALO's own notes flag as the primary novelty threat. No public pretrained checkpoint reported.
- **source:** Miao et al., 'GOAT: A Generalized Cross-Dataset Activity Recognition Framework with Natural Language Supervision', IMWUT 2024, doi:10.1145/3699736
- **-> HALO:** GOAT already fuses per-position textual attributes (analogous to HALO's ChannelTextFusion). HALO's differentiation must lean on the physical-Hz tokenizer, per-patch (dense) alignment, and streamable encoder — not on 'text from sensor location', which GOAT owns.

### R7. IMU2CLIP & ImageBind — the CLIP-binding origins, and why binding alone is weak for HAR
- **finding:** IMU2CLIP (Meta, EMNLP Findings 2023, arXiv:2210.14395) aligns IMU↔egocentric-video↔text in CLIP space (Ego4D/Aria), enabling motion retrieval and boosting fine-tuned HAR; code released. ImageBind (Meta, CVPR 2023) includes an IMU encoder (18.69M params, ~702mW) bound to video/audio, but its zero-shot HAR is weak — UniMTS measured ImageBind at 14.1% on UCI-HAR (vs its own 35.2%) and ImageBind's IMU encoder is ~4x larger and ~30x more power-hungry than UniMTS's graph encoder.
- **source:** Moon et al., IMU2CLIP, arXiv:2210.14395; Girdhar et al., ImageBind, CVPR 2023; power/accuracy comparison from UniMTS arXiv:2410.19818
- **-> HALO:** Binding IMU to a frozen joint space without HAR-specific augmentation/text yields poor zero-shot and heavy encoders — validating HALO's lean/streamable direction but warning that alignment target choice and augmentation matter more than raw binding.

### R8. Apple WBM — behavioral-feature FM beats raw-biometric signal at massive scale
- **finding:** Apple's Wearable Behavior Model (2025) trains on 2.5B hours / 162k participants (Apple Heart & Movement Study), 57 health tasks, and argues aggregated BEHAVIORAL features (movement/sleep/exercise) are often a stronger health signal than raw biometrics (HR/SpO2); up to 92% accuracy on pregnancy detection in a hybrid setup.
- **source:** Apple, 'Beyond Sensor Data: Foundation Models of Behavioral Data from Wearables Improve Health Predictions', 2025, arXiv:2507.00191
- **-> HALO:** Confirms the industry split: health/behavioral FMs (Apple WBM, Google LSM/SensorLM) use aggregated features + enormous scale; motion/HAR FMs (UniMTS, RelCon, HALO) use raw waveform. HALO should not chase the health regime; it should win on fine-grained raw-motion HAR where waveform fidelity matters.

### R9. Generic time-series FMs (Chronos/MOMENT) transfer poorly to IMU HAR
- **finding:** Chronos (Ansari et al., 2024, tokenizes scalar values into a discrete vocab for a T5 LM) and MOMENT (Goswami et al., ICML 2024, masked univariate Transformer on the 'Time Series Pile') are strong general TS forecasters but are univariate/forecasting-oriented and underperform task-specific and IMU-specialized methods on HAR classification; they do not model cross-axis/cross-channel IMU semantics. Weights are open.
- **source:** Ansari et al. 'Chronos', 2024; Goswami et al. 'MOMENT', ICML 2024; comparative notes in HAR survey arXiv:2508.12213
- **-> HALO:** Justifies building an IMU-native model rather than adapting a generic TS FM: multivariate, orientation-sensitive motion needs a purpose-built tokenizer — supporting HALO's physical-Hz filterbank frontend over scalar-quantization TS-FM approaches.

### R10. Cross-cutting: orientation/position invariance is the single most repeated winning ingredient
- **finding:** Every top raw-motion FM makes device orientation/position a first-class design axis: UniMTS uses uniform SO(3) rotation + random joint masking; RelCon learns a rotation-invariant distance via accel augmentations; the HAR survey lists 'poor generalization across users, devices, positions' as the dominant failure. This invariance, not model size, drives cross-device zero-shot.
- **source:** UniMTS arXiv:2410.19818; RelCon arXiv:2411.18822; survey 'Foundation Models Defining a New Era in Sensor-based HAR', arXiv:2604.02711
- **-> HALO:** HALO's planned gravity canonicalization is aligned with this, but should be paired with explicit rotation/placement augmentation at training time (UniMTS-style) to cover the 6 held-out test datasets' unseen placements.

### R11. Cross-cutting: hybrid objectives (contrastive + generative/SSL) consistently beat contrastive-only
- **finding:** Convergent evidence: SensorLM CoCa (contrastive+caption) > CLIP-only and Cap-only in ablation; AAAI'25 SLIP (CLIP+SimCLR) > vanilla CLIP; the HAR survey names 'hybrid pretraining' as a best-practice; LSM/LIMU-BERT show masked-reconstruction SSL exploits unlabeled data. No successful FM relies on contrastive text alignment alone.
- **source:** SensorLM arXiv:2506.09108; AAAI'25 arXiv:2408.12023; survey arXiv:2604.02711; LIMU-BERT SenSys 2021
- **-> HALO:** HALO is currently pure per-patch CLIP contrastive to frozen MiniLM — the one configuration NO successful FM uses standalone. Adding a masked-reconstruction/SSL branch is the highest-consensus improvement.

### R12. Cross-cutting: text richness and text-encoder choice materially change results
- **finding:** Bare activity labels give sparse supervision; every improvement comes from enriching text: UniMTS GPT-3.5 paraphrases; AAAI'25 LLM templates (+5-7%) and body-part metadata (+6-10%); SensorLM hierarchical statistical/structural/semantic captions. Encoder choice matters too: RoBERTa/CLIP-text > DistilBERT (AAAI'25); frozen small encoders like MiniLM are the weakest link.
- **source:** AAAI'25 arXiv:2408.12023; UniMTS arXiv:2410.19818; SensorLM arXiv:2506.09108
- **-> HALO:** HALO aligns to frozen all-MiniLM-L6-v2 (a small distilled encoder) using largely label-derived text. Both the encoder and the text diversity are upgrade targets; ChannelTextFusion is the right instinct but should extend to activity-level LLM-diversified descriptions.

### R13. Cross-cutting: data scale — HALO is far below the empirically-observed knee
- **finding:** Successful FMs sit at 10^5-10^9 hours/segments: RelCon 1B segments/87k subjects, LIMU-BERT-X 1.43M hours/60k subjects/1.1k phone models (MobiCom 2025), LSM 40M hours/165k users, SensorLM 59.7M hours. LSM-1's scaling law shows monotonic gains to ~10^5 hours. HALO's 400h across 11 datasets is 2-3 orders below the knee. (UniMTS is the exception — 28.59h mocap, but MULTIPLIED via synthesis+augmentation into effectively unlimited position/orientation/text coverage.)
- **source:** RelCon arXiv:2411.18822; LIMU-BERT-X, MobiCom 2025; LSM-1 arXiv:2410.13638; UniMTS arXiv:2410.19818
- **-> HALO:** HALO must either (a) scale real unlabeled data toward 10^5h (ExtraSensory/CAPTURE-24/UK-Biobank, already in the data-strategy memo) or (b) adopt UniMTS-style mocap synthesis to manufacture unlimited labeled+text-paired coverage — or both.

### R14. Recent momentum: LLM-synthesis and one-model-cross-dataset are the 2025 frontier
- **finding:** oneHAR (IMWUT 2025) = one CDNet backbone + LLM-driven virtual-IMU synthesis to fit all datasets with no per-dataset ops; IMUGPT/IMUGPT 2.0 generate IMU from LLM text; Babel (SenSys 2025) does expandable N-modality alignment via chained binary alignments across WiFi/mmWave/IMU/LiDAR/video/depth (+12% single-modal, +22% fused, code released); SlotFM (2025) uses slot attention for a motion FM. The field is converging on synthetic data + cross-dataset unification.
- **source:** Wei et al., 'One Model to Fit Them All', IMWUT 2025 doi:10.1145/3749509; Dai et al. 'Babel', SenSys 2025 arXiv:2407.17777; IMUGPT 2.0 arXiv:2402.01049; SlotFM arXiv:2509.21673
- **-> HALO:** Confirms LLM-driven data synthesis is now standard practice for closing the IMU data gap; HALO's differentiation window is narrowing, so novelty must come from the tokenizer + dense per-patch objective + streamability rather than the text-supervision idea itself.

### R15. Released-weights landscape (reproducibility leverage)
- **finding:** Open weights/code: UniMTS (HF weights), IMU2CLIP (code), ImageBind (weights, incl. IMU encoder), LIMU-BERT (code+weights), Babel (code), MOMENT/Chronos (weights). Code-only (no weights, private data): RelCon (Apple). No public release: GOAT (no checkpoint reported), Google LSM-1/LSM-2/SensorLM, Apple WBM (all private-data models).
- **source:** Respective repos/papers: UniMTS HF, github.com/xiyuanzh/UniMTS; github.com/maxxu05/relcon; github.com/facebookresearch/imu2clip; github.com/I-ESC/Project-Babel
- **-> HALO:** The strongest directly-comparable open baselines for HALO's eval are UniMTS (zero-shot) and RelCon (SSL, code-runnable on public data) — both already in HALO's baseline plan. The Google/Apple SOTA is not reproducible, so HALO cannot be forced to match 40M-hour numbers.

### R16. Q1 — Locked (LiT) text tower wins, especially at small data
- **finding:** LiT (locked pretrained tower + trainable other tower) beat unlocking both by large margins: on their evaluation, Lu (locked-image/unlocked-text) = 70.1% ImageNet zero-shot vs Uu (unlocked both pretrained) = 57.2% vs uu (both random) = 50.6% — ~13 pts from locking one pretrained tower, ~20 pts over random. Mechanism (their Fig 4): unlocking the pretrained tower lets the limited/noisy contrastive data OVERWRITE its general representation — locked config has WORSE train loss but SUBSTANTIALLY better OOD/zero-shot. The benefit persists even at 4B pairs, so it is not merely a small-data artifact but is strongest when paired data is scarce/narrow. The strong side should stay frozen; only the weak/unaligned side is trained.
- **source:** LiT: Zero-Shot Transfer with Locked-image Text Tuning, Zhai et al., CVPR 2022, arXiv:2111.07991
- **-> HALO:** HALO already freezes the SBERT backbone (requires_grad=False) — correct per LiT. But it adds a trainable LearnableLabelBank on top of the frozen text, and the prior debug sweep VERIFIED that frozen mean-pool beats the label bank on 7/7 datasets (+5.8pp ZS-open). LiT is the theoretical justification: adapting the strong frozen text tower on ~tens-of-thousands of IMU pairs overspecializes it. Recommendation: demote to frozen mean-pool (the 'L' side truly locked).

### R17. Q2 — SigLIP sigmoid beats softmax InfoNCE at small/moderate batch
- **finding:** SigLIP's sigmoid (per-pair BCE) loss beats softmax CLIP loss 'by a large margin' when batch < 16k, is comparable near the saturation point (~32k), and softmax needs ~98k to peak yet still does not surpass sigmoid. Both saturate ~32k; batches up to 1M give diminishing returns. Sigmoid needs no global normalization (each pair independent), so it is memory-cheaper (fit 4096 vs 2048 for CLIP on the same 4 TPUv4). Critical detail for the huge negative:positive imbalance: init the learnable BIAS b = -10 and temperature t' = log(10); the -10 bias makes early predictions start near all-negative, preventing the massive negative term from dominating the gradient at initialization.
- **source:** Sigmoid Loss for Language Image Pre-Training (SigLIP), Zhai/Mustafa/Kolesnikov/Beyer, ICCV 2023, arXiv:2303.15343
- **-> HALO:** HALO trains at micro-batch ~ hundreds (DEFAULT_MICRO_BATCH_SIZE=BATCH_SIZE) — squarely in the <16k regime where sigmoid dominates softmax. HALO has LOSS_TYPE='siglip' plumbed but defaults to 'infonce'. Switching to sigmoid is directly indicated by SigLIP's small-batch result and removes the need for the memory-bank hack entirely.

### R18. Q3 — Same-label batch items are false negatives; SupCon is the multi-positive fix
- **finding:** Standard CLIP/InfoNCE assigns exactly ONE positive per anchor (the diagonal) and treats all other batch items as negatives. When many batch items share a class label, the identically-labeled off-diagonal items become FALSE negatives that the loss actively pushes apart — discarding semantic info and slowing convergence. SupCon generalizes InfoNCE to |P(a)| positives (every same-class sample in the batch is a positive, averaged in the log), and is the canonical fix; sigmoid loss handles it even more cleanly because each pair's target is independent — you simply set the target label matrix z_ij = +1 for ALL same-class pairs (no softmax normalization conflict).
- **source:** Supervised Contrastive Learning, Khosla et al., NeurIPS 2020, arXiv:2004.11362; sigmoid multi-positive property per SigLIP (arXiv:2303.15343)
- **-> HALO:** In HALO's hard-target path the targets are torch.eye(batch) — two windows with the same activity label are treated as negatives. The soft-target path (frozen-SBERT similarity) partially mitigates this for in-batch pairs (identical labels get identical high soft mass), BUT the memory-bank/queue negatives are explicitly padded with ZERO soft-target mass (semantic_loss.py:171-175) — so a queued embedding of the SAME class as the anchor is a guaranteed false negative that gets pushed away. This is textbook false-negative pushing and a likely accuracy leak.

### R19. Q3 — Explicit false-negative correction methods and their gains
- **finding:** Beyond SupCon, false-negative CANCELLATION (identify likely same-class items via embedding similarity and either remove them from the denominator = elimination, or promote them to positives = attraction) measurably helps self-supervised contrastive learning; the WACV'22 method improved ImageNet linear-probe over SimCLR/MoCo baselines. The general principle: never let an item you have reason to believe shares the anchor's class act as a negative.
- **source:** Boosting Contrastive Self-Supervised Learning with False Negative Cancellation, Huynh et al., WACV 2022, arXiv:2011.11765; Time-Series Contrastive Learning against False Negatives and Class Imbalance, arXiv:2312.11939
- **-> HALO:** HALO HAS the label information (label_texts), so it does not need to GUESS false negatives — it can mask them exactly. It already builds a same-label mask (_build_same_label_mask in semantic_loss.py) but uses it ONLY for metrics, not to correct the loss. Wiring that mask into the loss (mask same-label off-diagonals and same-label queue items out of the negative set, or set them positive) is a nearly-free correctness fix.

### R20. Q4 — Frozen sentence-encoder embeddings are anisotropic; centering/whitening helps
- **finding:** Raw BERT/SBERT sentence embeddings occupy a narrow high-similarity cone (anisotropy), which harms cosine-based semantic tasks. BERT-flow (EMNLP'20) and BERT-whitening (Su et al. 2021) transform embeddings toward isotropy and substantially raise STS correlation; whitening (subtract mean, decorrelate/scale by the covariance) is a simple closed-form post-hoc fix that also enables dimensionality reduction. Centering (mean subtraction) alone removes the dominant shared component that inflates all pairwise similarities.
- **source:** Whitening Sentence Representations, Su et al. 2021, arXiv:2103.15316; BERT-flow (On the Sentence Embeddings from Pre-trained LMs), Li et al., EMNLP 2020; Whitening-based Contrastive Learning of Sentence Embeddings, arXiv:2305.17746
- **-> HALO:** HALO's frozen all-MiniLM-L6-v2 label prototypes are anisotropic; L2-normalization alone does not remove the shared mean direction. Confusable HAR labels (walking/upstairs/downstairs) sit very close on the cone, which the debug sweep already observed as class collapse. Centering/whitening the frozen label prototypes (fit once on the label set) is a cheap way to spread prototypes before/at alignment and at eval.

### R21. Q4 — Directly-analogous result: centering + inverted-softmax gives the largest ZS-HAR gain
- **finding:** In the closest published setting to HALO (TCN IMU encoder aligned to FROZEN Sentence-BERT prototypes for zero-shot HAR): cross-modal CENTERING (subtract the global mean of each modality, then renormalize) plus INVERTED SOFTMAX (ISF: normalize each prototype's score by its accumulated test-set assignment mass, correcting hubness/prior imbalance) lifted macro-F1 from 0.42 to 0.58 — the single largest gain in the paper. Best config (contrastive + ISF) = 73.2% acc / 0.583 macro-F1 vs 58.3% / 0.34 for a label-name baseline. They also confirm label-name prototypes align poorly (mean cosine 0.30) and RICHER biomechanical descriptions HURT separability (off-diagonal cosine rose 0.29→0.50) because shared vocabulary compresses the prototype cloud — yet discriminative descriptions still won because encoder alignment quality dominates prototype geometry. They recommend macro-F1 as the standard ZSL-HAR metric.
- **source:** Closing the Modality Gap in Zero-Shot HAR: Contrastive Training and Separability-Optimized Prototypes on IMU Data, 2026, arXiv:2606.10789
- **-> HALO:** This paper essentially validates HALO's whole recipe critique. Two cheap wins HALO does NOT do: (1) cross-modal centering of frozen text (and IMU) embeddings, (2) inverted-softmax at eval to fix hubness. It also explains HALO's observed -3.27pp from rich channel text and the up/down-stairs collapse. HALO already adopted macro-F1 primary (consistent).

### R22. Q4/eval — Hubness at retrieval; inverted-softmax / CSLS / QB-Norm are the standard fixes
- **finding:** In high-dim cross-modal spaces a few 'hub' prototypes are nearest-neighbor to many queries, biasing zero-shot prediction toward majority/generic classes. Test-time normalizations fix this WITHOUT retraining: Inverted Softmax (Smith et al., ICLR 2017), CSLS = Cross-domain Similarity Local Scaling (Conneau et al., ICLR 2018, arXiv:1710.04087), and Querybank Normalisation / DBNorm (Bogolin et al., CVPR 2022) all downweight hub targets by their average similarity to a bank of queries. These are pure inference-time re-rankers.
- **source:** Smith et al. ICLR 2017 (inverted softmax); Conneau et al. ICLR 2018 (CSLS); QB-Norm, Bogolin et al., CVPR 2022; Balance Act/DBNorm, arXiv:2310.11612
- **-> HALO:** HALO's zero-shot classification is exactly a text-prototype nearest-neighbor problem. mobiact 'falls unrecognized' (F1 12.8, acc 50) is consistent with a hubness/prior failure. Adding CSLS or inverted-softmax over the eval batch of windows is a training-free eval-only change that typically recovers several points of macro-F1.

### R23. Q5 — Memory-bank staleness is worst exactly in HALO's regime (small, from-scratch encoder, no momentum)
- **finding:** MoCo's central finding: a FIFO queue of negatives only works if the key encoder changes slowly — otherwise queued features are stale/inconsistent and gradients are biased. Ablation: momentum m=0 (queue filled by the fast-changing online encoder) causes training instability and drops linear-probe accuracy to 55.1% vs ~59-60% with m=0.999; typical stable range is 0.99-0.9999. He et al. explicitly note memory-bank entries 'grow stale' because thousands of steps pass before an entry is refreshed.
- **source:** Momentum Contrast (MoCo), He et al., CVPR 2020, arXiv:1911.05722; MoCo v2, arXiv:2003.04297
- **-> HALO:** HALO's MemoryBank stores embeddings straight from the ONLINE encoder (no momentum encoder) of a SMALL model trained FROM SCRATCH that changes rapidly — the maximal-staleness setting MoCo warns against. Nuance: the TEXT half of the queue is fine (SBERT is frozen → stable), but the IMU half is stale. Queue size is only 512 (vs MoCo 65k), so it buys few negatives while injecting stale, potentially false-negative IMU vectors. HALO's own redesign (GradCache full-batch fresh negatives, QUEUE off) is the right call.

### R24. Temperature — small τ sharpens hard negatives but widens the modality gap
- **finding:** Two effects pull opposite ways. (a) A small temperature (τ≈0.01-0.07) implicitly emphasizes hard positives/negatives and tends to give slightly better small-data results. (b) But the contrastive temperature directly sets the size of the MODALITY GAP: smaller τ keeps the two modalities further apart on the hypersphere (cone effect + repulsion), and Liang et al. show that varying the gap distance materially changes downstream zero-shot accuracy and fairness. CLIP inits the learnable logit-scale at 1/0.07 and clips τ≥0.01 for stability.
- **source:** Mind the Gap: Understanding the Modality Gap in Multimodal Contrastive Learning, Liang et al., NeurIPS 2022, arXiv:2203.02053; Analyzing Learnable Softmax Temperature (OpenReview rx1QNhsNsK); To Cool or Not to Cool, arXiv:2404.04575
- **-> HALO:** HALO learns logit_scale init 1/0.07 and clamps exp∈[1,50] (τ∈[0.02,1.0]) — reasonable. But if it also adds centering to close the modality gap, it should not simultaneously drive τ→min, and it should keep macro-F1 (not accuracy) as the selection metric since the gap most affects minority classes.

### R25. Eval — Prompt/label ensembling gives free zero-shot gains
- **finding:** Averaging text embeddings over MANY prompt templates per class is a standard, inference-free zero-shot booster: OpenAI CLIP used 80 templates per ImageNet class and gained +3.5% (≈+5% with prompt engineering) at zero extra inference cost (prototypes precomputed and averaged). In the sensor/HAR setting specifically, LLM-diversified activity descriptions gave +5-7% and external body-part/movement knowledge +6-10%.
- **source:** CLIP, Radford et al., ICML 2021 (80-prompt ensembling +3.5%); Limitations of NL Supervision for Sensor HAR, Bock/Hoelzemann et al., 2024, arXiv:2408.12023
- **-> HALO:** HALO scores against each dataset's own single verbatim label string. It could ensemble multiple templates / LLM paraphrases per activity and average (or keep as multi-prototype, which HALO's loss already supports via 3D text embeddings). CAVEAT from arXiv:2606.10789: over-rich shared-vocabulary descriptions REDUCE prototype separability — prefer DISCRIMINATIVE, class-distinguishing phrasings and verify off-diagonal cosine does not rise.

### R26. HAR-specific pitfalls with frozen-text supervision
- **finding:** In sensor-HAR, natural-language supervision is bottlenecked by (1) sensor heterogeneity (sampling rate/gain/placement shift distributions, blocking zero-shot transfer) and (2) label vocabulary 3-4 orders of magnitude smaller than vision captions. Fixes with measured benefit: CLIP/BERT text encoders > DistilBERT; simple conv IMU encoders > ResNets; adapting projection layers on target data +30-50%; few-shot (100 windows/class, <4 min) +20-40%; LLM-diversified text +5-7%; the SLIP objective (contrastive + self-supervised) beat plain CLIP consistently. Temperature init 1/0.07; batch 128-512.
- **source:** Limitations in Employing Natural Language Supervision for Sensor-Based HAR — And Ways to Overcome Them, Bock/Hoelzemann et al., 2024, arXiv:2408.12023
- **-> HALO:** Validates HALO's small-batch design (128-512), its conv-style tokenizer, and per-channel placement text. Suggests an auxiliary self-supervised term (SLIP-style) alongside the contrastive loss and confirms few-shot projection adaptation as the high-yield deployment path.

### R27. Competitor grounding — UniMTS handles orientation/scale via text + augmentation
- **finding:** UniMTS aligns synthetic skeleton-derived motion time series to LLM-enriched text with a spatio-temporal graph encoder and rotation-invariant augmentation, reporting +340% zero-shot over prior baselines across 18 datasets, but retains weak granularity on fine-grained/entirely-unseen classes. It demonstrates that LLM-enriched text + orientation-invariant augmentation are the current SOTA levers for cross-device generalization.
- **source:** UniMTS: Unified Pre-training for Motion Time Series, Zhang et al., NeurIPS 2024
- **-> HALO:** Confirms HALO's redesign priorities (gravity canonicalization, orientation invariance, richer/LLM label text) are the right competitive axes; HALO's differentiator remains arbitrary raw channel sets + per-channel text + on-device, not skeleton priors.

### R28. Apple Core Motion — sampling rates & gravity separation
- **finding:** CMMotionManager vends fused deviceMotion at a max of 100 Hz per-sample in real time; its sensor fusion splits the raw accelerometer into `userAcceleration` (gravity removed, ~0 at rest) plus a separate `gravity` vector and `attitude`/`rotationRate`. Apps must set NSMotionUsageDescription in Info.plist and use exactly one CMMotionManager. This is the low-latency path production iOS HAR apps use.
- **source:** Apple WWDC23 'What's new in Core Motion' (session 10179); NSHipster 'CMDeviceMotion'; Apple CoreMotion docs
- **-> HALO:** HALO must declare which representation it consumes — raw accel (gravity-in) vs userAcceleration (gravity-out) — because Core Motion natively offers both; ambiguity here is exactly what breaks reuse.

### R29. Apple Core Motion — high-rate & background paths
- **finding:** CMBatchedSensorManager (watchOS 10, Apple Watch Series 8/Ultra only) delivers 800 Hz accelerometer and 200 Hz device motion, batched once per second at lower overhead than 100 Hz streaming, but ONLY during an active HealthKit workout session. For always-on background collection without a live workout, CMSensorRecorder records accelerometer historically at ~50 Hz, batched and crash-durable. CMMotionActivityManager is the OS's own free classifier (stationary/walking/running/automotive/cycling/unknown + low/medium/high confidence).
- **source:** Apple WWDC23 session 10179; Apple docs CMSensorRecorder / recordAccelerometer(forDuration:); Apple CMMotionActivity docs
- **-> HALO:** 50 Hz is the realistic continuous rate on Apple hardware (matches HALO's 50 Hz default). HALO competes head-to-head with the free, battery-optimized CMMotionActivity, so it must justify its cost with a strictly larger/finer label set or higher accuracy.

### R30. Android — Activity Recognition Transition API
- **finding:** Google's on-device Activity Recognition (Transition) API periodically reads short sensor bursts, runs an ML model, and emits DetectedActivity types STILL/WALKING/RUNNING/IN_VEHICLE/ON_BICYCLE with ACTIVITY_TRANSITION_ENTER/EXIT events. It is the recommended, power-efficient default and requires the runtime ACTIVITY_RECOGNITION permission (Android 10+).
- **source:** Android Developers 'Detect when users start or end an activity' (transitions); developers.google.com Activity Recognition API
- **-> HALO:** This is the Android incumbent HALO must beat. Mapping HALO's outputs onto these coarse classes (plus extras) makes it a drop-in upgrade rather than a parallel system.

### R31. Android — raw sensor conventions
- **finding:** Sensor.TYPE_ACCELEROMETER reports m/s^2 including gravity; TYPE_LINEAR_ACCELERATION is the same with gravity high-pass-removed; TYPE_GRAVITY isolates the gravity vector. Rate hints: SENSOR_DELAY_GAME ≈ 20 ms ≈ 50 Hz, SENSOR_DELAY_UI ≈ 60 ms, SENSOR_DELAY_FASTEST = 0 (device max). Units are m/s^2 (not g), device-relative axis frame.
- **source:** Android Developers 'Motion sensors' / 'Sensors overview'
- **-> HALO:** Android delivers m/s^2 while many training datasets and Apple conventions differ (g vs m/s^2, gravity in/out). HALO needs an explicit unit+gravity contract and a resample step, since 50 Hz is only a hint, not guaranteed.

### R32. Wear OS — Health Services hides raw IMU
- **finding:** Wear OS Health Services (PassiveClient/ExerciseClient) exposes only high-level metrics (heart rate, steps, distance, calories, speed) at ~1 s intervals, with unpredictable batching once the display enters ambient mode ('Don't assume any predefined or predictable batching intervals'). It does NOT expose raw accelerometer/gyroscope — raw IMU requires the standard Android Sensor API plus a foreground service, and apps must call Capabilities() at runtime because data types vary per device and per exercise type.
- **source:** Android Developers 'Enhance app compatibility across Wear OS devices' (Health Services compatibility); 'Health Services on Wear OS'
- **-> HALO:** A raw-IMU model like HALO cannot ride the low-power Health Services pipeline on watches; it forces a foreground service and manual battery management. HALO docs must tell watch devs this explicitly and provide the Sensor-API ingestion path.

### R33. Health Connect role
- **finding:** Health Connect is an on-device storage/aggregation layer (ExerciseSessionRecord, StepsRecord, etc.) for sharing data between apps — it is a write/read store for recognized sessions, not a real-time raw-IMU or inference API.
- **source:** Android Developers Health Connect docs; Sahha 'Passive Health Data Collection on iOS and Android'
- **-> HALO:** HALO's deployment value is upstream (turning raw IMU into labels); the natural integration is to write HALO's recognized activities/sessions INTO Health Connect (Android) / HealthKit (iOS), not to read raw data from it.

### R34. On-device deployment — quantization economics
- **finding:** fp32→fp16 halves size with generally no accuracy loss; fp32→int8 cuts size 4x and gives 2-4x latency on integer NPUs/ANE/Edge-TPU. Apple's Neural Engine gets extra latency wins from int8-int8 on A17 Pro/M4; weight palettization (1-8 bit) works best on ANE for memory/latency.
- **source:** Apple coremltools 'Compressing Neural Network Weights' & WWDC23 session 10047; Ultralytics 'Model Quantization'
- **-> HALO:** HALO's 26M-param headline model is ~100 MB in fp32; int8 brings it to ~26 MB — deployable but still heavy. The Tiny tier (d=192, ~5-6M) quantized to int8 (~5-6 MB) is the realistic on-device artifact.

### R35. On-device deployment — transformers are hard to quantize
- **finding:** Transformer nonlinearities (softmax, LayerNorm, attention) resist naive int8. STORM (19.7k-param 1-D HAR transformer) had to co-design a streaming CGRA for softmax to hit its budget; BabyMamba-HAR reports generic TFLite int8 parity 'degraded considerably' while a quantization-aware architecture kept >99% parity on-device.
- **source:** STORM, Electronics 15(9):1924 (MDPI, 2025); BabyMamba-HAR (arXiv 2602.09872)
- **-> HALO:** HALO's dual-branch attention encoder cannot be assumed to survive int8 as-is. Quantization must be validated per-op with accuracy deltas, and quant-aware training may be needed; this is a concrete deployment risk, not a checkbox.

### R36. On-device deployment — latency & model-size norms
- **finding:** On iPhone-class ANE, a transformer at seq-len 128, batch 1 runs ~3.5 ms at 0.45 W (or ~9.4 ms at 0.072 W in a low-power config). Production on-device models routinely run 100s of MB but are compressed hard (e.g., int4 taking a 13 GB LLM to <4 GB). Sub-tens-of-ms latency is expected for HAR-scale models.
- **source:** Apple ML Research 'Deploying Transformers on the Apple Neural Engine'; coremltools performance guide; Apple WWDC24 session 10159
- **-> HALO:** HAR windows are seconds long, so per-window latency (single-digit ms) is a non-issue on modern phones — the binding constraint is continuous-duty energy and RAM, not one-shot latency.

### R37. On-device deployment — framework landscape
- **finding:** The deployable-artifact norm is CoreML (.mlpackage) for iOS/watchOS, TFLite/LiteRT for Android/Wear, and ONNX for portability. PyTorch's on-device path is ExecuTorch (PyTorch Edge): ~50 KB runtime, torch.export pipeline, int8/int4 via torchao, and delegates to XNNPACK, CoreML, and Vulkan. A raw `.pt`/torch.hub checkpoint is NOT deployable on-device.
- **source:** pytorch/executorch (GitHub + arXiv 2605.08195); executorch.ai; Google LiteRT docs
- **-> HALO:** Shipping only a `best.pt` (HALO's current state) is a non-starter for third-party device deployment. HALO needs an export pipeline to at least CoreML + TFLite/ONNX (ExecuTorch can produce both from the existing PyTorch graph).

### R38. Continuous inference — battery budget
- **finding:** Continuous 50 Hz accelerometer HAR yields ~14 h phone battery; dynamic/adaptive sampling extends this to ~18 h and idle-gating to ~26 h. A two-stage cascade (cheap gate/decision-tree + CNN only when needed) cut average energy per inference by 67.7% at iso-accuracy on MCUs.
- **source:** 'Low Energy Physical Activity Recognition System on Smartphones' (PMC4435175); 'Two-stage HAR on Microcontrollers' (arXiv 2206.07652)
- **-> HALO:** Always-on HALO at 50 Hz will materially hurt battery. A deployable story needs duty-cycling or a two-stage design (cheap always-on gate → HALO only on motion/transition), mirroring how the OS APIs stay power-efficient.

### R39. Streaming inference — windowing & state
- **finding:** The standard streaming recipe is fixed windows with overlapping stride plus temporal smoothing: STORM uses 1.280 s windows at 0.640 s stride (50% overlap) for continuous on-node HAR; ASR-style stateful/KV-cache designs avoid recomputation across chunks for causal models. Overlapping-chunk inference is a well-established proxy for true streaming.
- **source:** STORM, Electronics 15(9):1924; 'Stateful Conformer with Cache-Based Inference' (arXiv 2312.17279)
- **-> HALO:** HALO's per-patch prediction + majority voting already fits the overlap-and-vote pattern, but its per-patch z-score normalization uses full-window statistics — not streaming-safe. Deployment needs online/causal normalization (running mean/std) and a documented window/stride/latency contract.

### R40. Sensor heterogeneity — required harmonization axes
- **finding:** Cross-dataset HAR generalization requires standardizing six things: units of measurement, sampling rate (resampling), gravity component, axis/orientation, activity-label mapping, and window size. Cross-device shift comes from differing sampling rates, sensitivity, and noise across hardware.
- **source:** 'A benchmark for domain adaptation and generalization in smartphone-based HAR', Nature Scientific Data 11:1192 (2024); 'Towards Generalizable HAR: A Survey' (arXiv 2508.12213)
- **-> HALO:** HALO trains across 11 datasets at mixed rates/channels and augments rate/gravity/channel-dropout, so it addresses these — but at INFERENCE the caller still must supply the correct sampling_rate_hz and channel semantics; the deployment API must own resampling and unit conversion so third-party devs can't get it wrong.

### R41. Sensor heterogeneity — orientation strategy (two schools)
- **finding:** Two competing inference-time orientation strategies dominate: (1) rotation-invariant augmentation to make the model agnostic to mounting orientation (UniMTS shows this is 'crucial' to its zero-shot success), vs (2) canonicalizing signals into a consistent earth/gravity reference frame using the gravity vector + attitude before inference (shown to reduce orientation-induced error).
- **source:** UniMTS, NeurIPS 2024 (arXiv 2410.19818); 'Reducing the Impact of Sensor Orientation Variability... Consistent Reference System' (PMC10346883)
- **-> HALO:** HALO must pick and document one strategy. Because Core Motion/Android can supply gravity+attitude for free, gravity-canonicalization to the earth frame is a cheap, deployable option that also matches HALO's planned 'gravity canonicalization' redesign item.

### R42. Sampling-rate robustness
- **finding:** Models can be made robust to sampling-rate variation via resampling/rate-augmentation, letting a model trained at one rate serve devices at others without retraining.
- **source:** 'Smartphone Sensor-based HAR Robust to Different Sampling Rates' (arXiv 2101.00812)
- **-> HALO:** HALO already trains with rate augmentation; the deployment layer should expose a single canonical internal rate and auto-resample any incoming stream (50/100/800 Hz Apple, ~50 Hz Android) to it.

### R43. Adoptable release — UniMTS (the gold standard)
- **finding:** UniMTS ships an Apache-2.0 license, a Hugging Face-hosted checkpoint (xiyuanz/UniMTS), a conda/pip environment, an explicit input contract (shape [N, seq_len, 6] as acc_x/y/z + gyro_x/y/z in m/s^2, first 10 s used with auto-padding, configurable --original_sampling_rate, --joint_list for placement), built-in rotation-invariant preprocessing, a compact 4.94M-param graph encoder (vs 18.69M for the ImageBind IMU baseline), and a runnable zero-shot eval script.
- **source:** github.com/xiyuanzh/UniMTS; huggingface.co/xiyuanz/UniMTS; UniMTS NeurIPS 2024
- **-> HALO:** This is the concrete packaging template HALO should match feature-for-feature: permissive license + HF hosting + explicit numeric input contract + placement/orientation handling + one-command inference.

### R44. Adoptable release — ssl-wearables / harnet (the cautionary case)
- **finding:** harnet loads with a torch.hub one-liner (harnet5/10/30 = 5/10/30 s windows at 30 Hz, 3-channel accelerometer) but ships an academic-use-only license (commercial requires negotiating with Oxford University Innovation), NO Hugging Face hosting, NO formal model card, and does not specify units (g vs m/s^2), gravity handling, or normalization. It also ships a pretrained feature extractor with an UN-trained classifier, so users must fine-tune.
- **source:** github.com/OxWearables/ssl-wearables (README + LICENSE)
- **-> HALO:** Everything ssl-wearables omits is a friction point HALO can avoid: pin a permissive license, host on HF, write a real model card, and state units/gravity/normalization numerically.

### R45. Adoption blockers — what users actually report
- **finding:** Real ssl-wearables issues: model-loading failures (#29), inability to reproduce published downstream results due to missing fine-tuning hyperparameters (#28), and Docker/dependency build failures (#18), plus recurring confusion over input format, units, and how to apply the pretrained model. High stars but few filed issues suggest many users silently bounce.
- **source:** github.com/OxWearables/ssl-wearables/issues (#18, #28, #29)
- **-> HALO:** The top blockers are unglamorous: environment that builds, documented hyperparameters, and an unambiguous input spec. HALO should treat these as first-class deliverables, not afterthoughts.

### R46. Model cards & licensing norms on Hugging Face
- **finding:** Among HF models (Jan 2024): Apache-2.0 38.1%, RAIL 24.0%, MIT 17.5%, CC 7.5%; RAIL (behavioral-use clauses) grew from 1% (Sep 2022) to 24%. Model cards (intended use, limitations, training data, eval) drive discoverability/reproducibility, but empirically their quality/completeness is low because creation is manual — underfilled cards are themselves an adoption blocker.
- **source:** 'An Empirical Analysis of ML Model and Dataset Documentation... on Hugging Face' (arXiv 2502.04484); 'Standardization of Behavioral Use Clauses' (arXiv 2402.05979); HF Model Cards docs
- **-> HALO:** A permissive license (Apache-2.0/MIT) is table stakes for industry adoption; a complete, honest model card documenting HALO's known discrepancies and eval protocol is a differentiator given how rare good cards are.

### R47. HALO-specific — the channel-text dependency
- **finding:** HALO's ChannelTextFusion requires a per-channel natural-language description at inference, and SentenceBERT (all-MiniLM-L6-v2) label/channel embeddings. Per HALO's own config, the text encoder is frozen and embeddings are precomputed so SentenceBERT is not deployed on-device — but the vocabulary of channel descriptions a third-party dev may supply is effectively unbounded.
- **source:** HALO repo model/config.py (comments on precomputed embeddings); model/token_text_encoder.py, model/semantic_alignment.py
- **-> HALO:** For adoptability HALO must ship a fixed, documented channel-description vocabulary with precomputed embeddings (so a phone app just picks 'wrist accelerometer x-axis, m/s^2' from a list), plus a documented path to register a new sensor/label — otherwise every integrator must reason about text prompts, which no production SDK will accept.

### R48. What drives cross-dataset transfer: geometry/placement >> text alignment (UniMTS ablation)
- **finding:** UniMTS zero-shot averages 43.5% Acc / 34.3 macro-F1 across 18 unseen datasets. Ablations rank the drivers by how much removing each hurts: removing the GRAPH encoder (handles sensor PLACEMENT/location) drops Acc 43.5->25.1 (-18.4pts); removing ROTATION-INVARIANT augmentation (handles device ORIENTATION) drops to 27.1 (-16.4pts); removing TEXT augmentation (label/activity diversity) drops only to 39.8 (-3.7pts). So orientation+placement invariance are ~5x more impactful than richer label text for zero-shot cross-dataset transfer.
- **source:** UniMTS: Unified Pre-training for Motion Time Series, Zhang et al., NeurIPS 2024, arXiv:2410.19818 (Table 1 ablation)
- **-> HALO:** HALO invests heavily in per-patch CLIP text alignment + ChannelTextFusion, but the strongest empirical lever is geometry: HALO's planned gravity canonicalization + orientation/placement handling should be the headline contribution, not label engineering. Text alignment is necessary for open-vocab matching but its marginal gain is small.

### R49. Geometry canonicalization is the single biggest driver (AnyMo ablation, 2026)
- **finding:** AnyMo (geometry-aware, setup-agnostic) achieves 35.7% Acc / 29.5 macro-F1 / 57.5 R@2 zero-shot across 14 unseen datasets. Removing geometry-aware simulation collapses this to 8.4% Acc / 3.8 F1 / 16.3 R@2 — a ~4x drop, the largest of any component. Per-dataset zero-shot: Opportunity 59.4/58.8, UCI-HAR 56.5/51.6, w-HAR 57.4/42.2. Trained on Nymeria (real head+2-wrist IMU + body-mesh-simulated IMU across anatomical segments).
- **source:** AnyMo: Geometry-Aware Setup-Agnostic Modeling of Human Motion in the Wild, 2026, arXiv:2605.22715
- **-> HALO:** Second independent confirmation (alongside UniMTS) that orientation/placement/geometry canonicalization dominates cross-dataset generalization. Establishes the current SOTA 'good' bar: ~30 macro-F1 averaged across many unseen sets.

### R50. Large-scale free-living SSL pretraining transfers (ssl-wearables / Yuan et al.)
- **finding:** Multi-task self-supervised pretraining on 700,000 person-days of free-living UK Biobank wrist accelerometer data yields F1 relative improvements of 2.5-130.9% (median 24.4%) over strong baselines across 8 downstream HAR benchmarks, and the gains generalize across external datasets, cohorts, living environments, and sensor devices.
- **source:** Self-supervised learning for HAR using 700,000 person-days of wearable data, Yuan et al., npj Digital Medicine 2024, arXiv:2206.02909 (OxWearables/ssl-wearables)
- **-> HALO:** Direct evidence that broadening HALO's training toward large free-living data (CAPTURE-24, UK-Biobank-scale) buys real cross-dataset robustness. ssl-wearables has released weights and is a must-have baseline; UniMTS uses it as 'BioBankSSL'.

### R51. Natural-language-supervision zero-shot HAR is currently WEAK vs supervised (Haresamudram AAAI'25)
- **finding:** CLIP-style natural-language-supervision zero-shot HAR underperforms end-to-end supervised training by 30-88 F1 points: MotionSense 38.97 vs 89.01, MobiAct 16.93 vs 78.99, HHAR 31.05 vs 55.63, PAMAP2 10.88 vs 59.43, MHEALTH 11.15 vs 48.71, Myogym 1.47 vs 38.21. Two identified root causes: (a) sensor heterogeneity, and (b) impoverished text — HAR activity vocabularies are 3-4 orders of magnitude smaller than vision (CAPTURE-24 ~176 labels; MobiAct vocab ~23 words vs ImageNet-21k ~19,200 concepts).
- **source:** Limitations in Employing Natural Language Supervision for Sensor-Based HAR — And Ways to Overcome Them, Haresamudram et al., AAAI 2025, arXiv:2408.12023
- **-> HALO:** This is the central threat AND the opportunity for HALO. HALO's per-channel natural-language descriptions (ChannelTextFusion) + per-patch alignment are exactly the mitigations this paper prescribes for the two root causes — HALO should frame itself as directly answering this critique, and must beat these NLS numbers to justify the language-alignment design.

### R52. Richer/diverse label text helps, but with diminishing returns and a separability trap
- **finding:** Overcoming impoverished text: ChatGPT-generated sentence variations of labels gave +5-7% (MotionSense 73.36->78.25 F1); adding body-part/movement descriptions +6-10%; using 33 templates vs 1 gave only modest gains. Same-dataset HHAR: base template 29.05 F1 -> ChatGPT-diversified 36.01 (+24%), body-knowledge 34.73 (+20%). BUT a separate study warns richer descriptions can REDUCE inter-class prototype separability (off-diagonal cosine 0.29->0.50), so verbose per-channel/per-class text can collapse classes together; discriminative (contrastive) descriptions and contrastive training matter more than description richness (text-sensor cosine 0.30 with bare labels -> 0.69 with contrastive training).
- **source:** Haresamudram et al. AAAI 2025 (arXiv:2408.12023); Closing the Modality Gap in Zero-Shot HAR, 2026, arXiv:2606.10789
- **-> HALO:** Supports HALO's channel-text design but flags a risk: HALO's verbose per-channel descriptions may hurt inter-activity separability. HALO should test discriminative vs descriptive phrasing and report prototype separability, not just alignment.

### R53. Label-string / prompt sensitivity is real and under-reported
- **finding:** Zero-shot text-aligned HAR is sensitive to how labels are prompted. Templates such as bare label, 'a person is {label}', 'wearable IMU motion of {label}', 'the activity is {label}' produce noticeably different class rankings with the SAME vocabulary — some templates are consistently weaker. Manually written label contexts shift text embeddings in ways not consistently beneficial, so a single hand-picked prompt can silently cherry-pick results.
- **source:** General CLIP prompt-sensitivity literature + HAR-specific template ablations reported in AnyMo (arXiv:2605.22715) and prompt-distribution work (arXiv:2410.19294); pattern corroborated by Haresamudram et al. AAAI 2025
- **-> HALO:** HALO evaluation must report robustness across multiple label phrasings (or a prompt ensemble), not one tuned prompt — reviewers at MobiCom will (rightly) suspect label-string overfitting in a language-aligned model. This is a concrete integrity item given HALO already uses frozen SentenceBERT label embeddings.

### R54. Subject leakage inflates HAR results by ~10-16 points; still prevalent
- **finding:** Random k-fold CV (subjects appearing in train+test) overestimates HAR performance by ~10% on average, rising to ~16% with overlapping sliding windows; another analysis found ~13% overestimation across 3 public datasets. Concrete case: Random Forest scores 89% under k-fold but 76% under leave-one-subject-out (LOSO) — a 13-point optimistic bias. Much published HAR still uses subject-dependent splits.
- **source:** Subject Cross Validation in HAR, Dehghani et al., arXiv:1904.02666; How Validation Methodology Influences HAR Mobile Systems, Sensors 2022, MDPI 22(6):2360; HAR Comparative Study of Validation Methods, Algorithms 2024, 17(12):556
- **-> HALO:** HALO's known 'subject leakage' finding is a documented, quantifiable field-wide problem. Strict LOSO / leave-one-DATASET-out and window-boundary hygiene are table stakes; HALO can turn rigorous leakage-free eval into a credibility asset vs prior work.

### R55. Accuracy is misleading under HAR class imbalance; macro-F1 is the honest metric
- **finding:** HAR test sets are heavily imbalanced (Opportunity NULL class alone is >75% of samples), so overall accuracy is skewed by majority classes and hides minority-class failure. Macro-F1 weights every class equally and is sensitive to minority-class degradation, making it the standard primary metric; micro-F1/accuracy emphasize frequent classes and can be gamed by a majority-class predictor. The NULL/background class specifically distorts in-the-wild HAR metrics.
- **source:** Exploring the Impact of the NULL Class on In-the-Wild HAR, Sensors 2024, PMC11207638; standard OPPORTUNITY protocol; macro-F1 rationale widely adopted (e.g., UMSNet arXiv:2205.11756)
- **-> HALO:** HALO must report macro-F1 as the headline metric and explicitly state NULL/background handling; a reviewer can dismiss accuracy-based zero-shot claims outright. This aligns with the 'metric gaming' integrity concern already flagged in HALO's audit.

### R56. Free-living vs scripted training data: lab-trained models fail in the wild
- **finding:** Algorithms trained on lab/scripted data perform poorly in free-living conditions, while models trained on free-living data recognize activity types substantially better. Scripted lab activities are cleanly segmented and homogeneous; free-living activities are intertwined (chores mix walking+standing) with nuanced transitions absent from lab data. CAPTURE-24 (~2,500 hrs wrist data in the wild, Nature Sci Data 2024) was built specifically to close this gap.
- **source:** Performance of Activity Classification Algorithms in Free-living Older Adults, PMC4833628; CAPTURE-24, Chan et al., Nature Scientific Data 2024 (s41597-024-03960-3)
- **-> HALO:** HALO's 11 training datasets are almost all scripted lab sets. Adding free-living data (CAPTURE-24 is already in HALO's train list; UK-Biobank/ExtraSensory candidates) should improve transfer to naturalistic held-out sets and is defensible as a diversity contribution.

### R57. Sensor-placement diversity in training determines cross-position transfer
- **finding:** Cross-position transfer is asymmetric and placement-dependent: with MobiAct (waist) as source, Waist->Wrist and Waist->Waist transfer succeeds but Waist->Ankle is much weaker. Models trained on a SINGLE location generalize poorly — ImageBind and IMU2CLIP, trained only on head-mounted IMU, score just 12.5% and 8.6% zero-shot Acc respectively, vs UniMTS's 43.5% (which simulates all-joint/all-placement coverage). A survey formalizes 6 escalating settings (within-group < cross-person < cross-device < cross-position < cross-activity < cross-dataset); heterogeneous domain shift can cut accuracy by 42.4%.
- **source:** Cross-Domain HAR: Few-Shot Transfer Learning, arXiv:2310.14390; UniMTS NeurIPS 2024 (Table 1); Towards Generalizable HAR: A Survey, 2025, arXiv:2508.12213 (citing Hong et al. 2024)
- **-> HALO:** Placement coverage in the training mix is a first-order driver. HALO should ensure its train datasets span wrist/waist/thigh/ankle/chest and explicitly report per-placement transfer; a single-placement bias would cap zero-shot on differently-placed test sets (RealWorld multi-position, MobiAct pocket).

### R58. Multi-dataset / data-mixture pretraining beats single-source, and mixture weighting matters
- **finding:** Cross-dataset SSL pretraining consistently beats single-dataset: CrossHAR (hierarchical SSL) outperforms SOTA by +10.83% accuracy on unseen target datasets; HAR-DoReMi (optimized data-mixture reweighting across heterogeneous IMU datasets) improves average accuracy +10.5% while using only ~30-50% of the data — i.e., HOW you weight/mix source datasets matters, not just how many you add.
- **source:** CrossHAR, Hong et al., IMWUT/ACM UbiComp 2024 (10.1145/3659597); HAR-DoReMi, 2025, arXiv:2503.13542
- **-> HALO:** Beyond adding datasets, HALO should tune the training MIXTURE (domain reweighting / balanced sampling across datasets, placements, subjects) — cheap gains and a defensible methodological contribution. Naive concatenation lets large datasets dominate.

### R59. Published zero-shot cross-dataset macro-F1 ranges — what 'good' looks like
- **finding:** Current SOTA averaged across many unseen datasets: UniMTS 34.3 macro-F1 (43.5% Acc, 18 sets), AnyMo 29.5 macro-F1 (35.7% Acc, 14 sets). Per-dataset zero-shot (UniMTS): MotionSense 33.7 F1 / 45.2 Acc, RealWorld 36.7 F1 / 43.6 Acc, Shoaib 57.2 F1 / 63.6 Acc, HARTH 41.1 F1 / 68.9 Acc, MHEALTH 61.8 F1; hard 35-class MMAct only ~10 F1. NLS zero-shot (Haresamudram) on MobiAct only 16.93 F1. Weak prior baselines (ImageBind 7.8 F1, IMU2CLIP 4.3 F1) show how low untuned CLIP-style models sit. Full fine-tuning reaches ~90% Acc (UniMTS 90.6).
- **source:** UniMTS NeurIPS 2024 (arXiv:2410.19818, Tables 1); AnyMo (arXiv:2605.22715); Haresamudram AAAI 2025 (arXiv:2408.12023)
- **-> HALO:** Concrete targets for HALO's held-out sets: on easy 6-8 class phone datasets (MotionSense/Shoaib/RealWorld) competitive zero-shot is ~35-57 macro-F1; MobiAct/HARTH are harder (~17-41 F1). HALO clearing ~40-50 macro-F1 on phone sets would be SOTA-competitive; anything near 90 zero-shot would (correctly) read as leakage.

### R60. Few-shot adaptation closes most of the zero-shot gap cheaply
- **finding:** Adapting only the projection/linear layers on target data recovers 30-50% of the supervised gap with the full split, and 20-40% with just ~100 labeled windows (~4 minutes of data). UniMTS few-shot (1-10 samples/class) is state-of-the-art and statistically dominates baselines (Wilcoxon p<<0.05).
- **source:** Haresamudram et al. AAAI 2025 (arXiv:2408.12023); UniMTS NeurIPS 2024 (arXiv:2410.19818, few-shot section)
- **-> HALO:** Pure zero-shot is a hard sell given NLS weakness; HALO should foreground FEW-SHOT curves (accuracy vs #labeled-windows) as the practical deployment story for phones/watches, where a few minutes of user data is realistic. This reframes weak zero-shot numbers as a strength.

### R61. Direct competitor landscape for a language-aligned IMU FM
- **finding:** The open-vocab/language-aligned IMU HAR space is now crowded: UniMTS (NeurIPS'24, simulation+text+graph, zero-shot SOTA), AnyMo (2026, geometry-aware, Nymeria), GOAT (IMWUT'24, natural-language supervision + device-position encoding for cross-dataset), LanHAR (LLM-generated semantic interpretations of sensor+labels for cross-dataset), IMU2CLIP/ImageBind (video/text alignment, single-placement), ZARA (2025, LLM-agent zero-shot, 2.53x macro-F1 over baseline on 8 sets), plus HARGPT/SensorLLM (LLM-prompting).
- **source:** UniMTS arXiv:2410.19818; AnyMo arXiv:2605.22715; GOAT ACM IMWUT 2024 (10.1145/3699736); LanHAR arXiv:2410.00003; ZARA arXiv:2508.04038; Foundation-Models-for-HAR survey 2026 arXiv:2604.02711
- **-> HALO:** Novelty is under pressure (matches HALO's known GOAT novelty threat). HALO's per-patch dense alignment + per-channel text fusion + gravity canonicalization must be positioned against these; the cleanest differentiators are (a) leakage-free multi-dataset eval, (b) physical-Hz filterbank tokenizer, and (c) directly answering Haresamudram's NLS critique — not 'yet another CLIP-for-IMU'.
